mr president, today is the 23rd anniversary of the tragic events which took place on 13 january 1991. at that time, the recently re-established independent state of lithuania faced the ultimate test. for several days thousands of defenceless people, in cold temperatures, surrounded the buildings which embody the independence of the lithuanian state: the parliament, the tv tower and the independent radio and television building. that night, the soviet military, with their tanks and machine guns, advanced on the singing and chanting crowds. fourteen people died from bullets and injuries caused by tanks and hundreds were injured, but no one in the crowd left. today we pay tribute to those who were killed and to all those to whom we owe our freedom. we will never forget them, and we pin a flower the colour of the sky close to our hearts. we give thanks that today lithuania, and the other baltic states, are free and independent, and are equal members among freedom-loving nations. hopefully the day will come when those who committed crimes against humanity will be brought to justice.
i congratulate the rapporteur on a really good report which highlights the problems faced by labour inspectorates in the european union and provides policy recommendations on how to reduce undeclared work and help inspectorates to work more effectively. i fully agree that action plans to combat undeclared work should be drawn up by national labour inspectorates and other relevant bodies, as this is the only way to ensure the social protection of workers. undertakings that are not covered by the general rules on the free movement of workers should be exempted from the obligation to provide information on the conditions under which they are able to exercise their right to freedom of movement. member states should therefore be much more active in combating low-wage work and shadow part-time work. i also believe that member states should improve administrative cooperation between the various institutions - labour inspectorates, tax inspectorates, municipalities and social security and social insurance services - at national and european union level, as this would help to implement eu labour law provisions, reduce undeclared work and more effectively address problems arising from the different labour market regulation in the different member states.
thank you for your interesting report. indeed, employee financial participation in the form of revenue sharing is an advanced and effective tool that not only provides employees with an additional financial benefit for working in the company, but also involves employees in the strategic decision-making of the companies. this undoubtedly increases the interest of employees in the company to contribute to the improvement of the company's performance, increases their motivation to work and, at the same time, entitles them to the benefits of a successful company. unfortunately, the practice of this enterprise management tool is still not uniformly developed in different countries, and it is therefore necessary and useful to discuss this issue at the level of the european union, to seek and propose ways of changing the situation in the member states, and which models would be most acceptable. i am of course in favour of voluntary participation only and i also think it is necessary to talk about the risks of such participation, the possible danger to workers as the weaker party in the employment relationship and the necessary measures to protect workers against possible undesirable effects.
the rapporteur. − it is indeed very important that we have the opportunity to produce a report on social protection for all, including the self-employed, in the european parliament, and i would like to thank all my colleagues, shadow rapporteurs, for their constructive cooperation in the preparation of this report. it is particularly important to discuss this issue at european union level, as the economic crisis has been a severe test for the sustainability of social protection systems. rising unemployment and falling incomes have increased the need for social security expenditure, while at the same time national budgets have been balanced by cuts in pensions and other social benefits. this has affected the most vulnerable people, some of whom have been pushed out of poverty. in this context, the report stresses that the objective of ensuring the well-being of its citizens cannot be achieved without sustainable and adequate social protection systems that promote social inclusion, social justice and income and quality social and health care services accessible to all. and with the constant evolution of our economic market, it is vital to ensure that no social group is left without social protection, which is why i call on member states to do more to increase youth employment and to ensure that young workers are not discriminated against by limiting their rights to social protection. member states should also pay particular attention to ensuring social protection for the most vulnerable groups of people (unemployed, disabled people raising children alone, young people, pensioners, young families). social services should be more accessible to all the most vulnerable people in need of long-term care, especially in rural areas. in the communication, i also call on the member states and the commission to take measures to combat all forms of discrimination in the labour market in the area of social protection, so that women's pay for the same work and social benefits, including pensions, are not lower than men's and that maternity protection is guaranteed. we also talk about undeclared work (fictitious, shadow work) which distorts competition, creates the threat of social dumping and ultimately leads to a violation of the rights of social workers. i also drew attention in the report to the social protection of the selfemployed, because there is a risk that, as the nature of employment changes and new categories of workers are increasingly confronted with situations of emergency, they are not and will not be adequately protected. i believe that social protection policy towards the selfemployed is complicated by the fact that there is no universally accepted definition of the situation in the european union. for these reasons, i urge the commission to develop a framework of concepts. and to sum up, i would like to stress that social protection systems cannot be weakened. on the contrary, they must be developed to ensure maximum coverage, sustainability and adequacy. social benefits should be seen as an investment in people, enabling them to adapt to changes in the labour market, to protect themselves against poverty and social exclusion and to help them integrate into the labour market.
for the ecr group the presidency of the council of the european union is always a major challenge, not only politically but also organisationally. it is with satisfaction and pleasure that i can say on behalf of the ecr group that the lithuanian presidency has been a high-level and successful one. the priorities of the lithuanian presidency include energy security, the eastern partnership, strengthening the eu's external borders, and cooperation between the baltic sea countries. lithuania has set itself the ambitious goal of ensuring consistency in decision-making, and more than 500 legislative requests have been planned on the eu agenda. however, the most important achievement of the lithuanian presidency was, without doubt, the agreement with the european parliament on the eu's multiannual budget for the period 2014-2020, i.e. the union's multiannual financial framework. the failure of ukraine to sign the association agreement with the eu could not be called a failure of the lithuanian presidency, as many commentators have called it, but the obvious consequence of a game, a game played by the kiev authorities and the outcome of which was predicted even before the meeting. the presidency is a great opportunity to promote the member state itself, to strengthen its role in the union and to present a positive image of the state as a reliable partner. unfortunately, the recent scandalous events in lithuania, which targeted national minorities, left a bad impression and became a bit of a teaspoon in the honey pot of the lithuanian presidency. on christmas eve, at the request of a representative of the lithuanian government, the vilnius district court, under pressure from public statements by politicians, imposed a draconian fine of 13 thousand euros on the director of the šalčininkai district municipality, boleslav dashkevič, for the use of bilingual information boards with street names in the official lithuanian and polish languages, which hang on private houses. in this region, 80% of the population is the local polish ethnic minority. the fight against indigenous minority languages, supported by the highest authorities, is nothing but an expression of extreme nationalism, which should not exist in the european union. in a special resolution on linguistic diversity last july, the european parliament called on member states that had not yet done so to sign and ratify the european charter for regional or minority languages. parliament also called for the condemnation of any action which discriminates on the basis of language or which, through explicit or implicit assimilation, violates the languages and identities of other communities. i call on the council and the commission to take immediate action in implementing this resolution and to respond to put an end to repression, discrimination and persecution of national minorities in the territory of the union. thank you for your attention and good luck to greece. (the president agreed to answer the question after the blue card had been raised (rule 149 (8) of the rules of procedure))
the question asked when the blue card is raised. first of all, are you speaking on behalf of your group or on behalf of yourself, specifically speaking about the national minorities in lithuania and allegedly certain abuses? and the second thing - you probably know very well that the official language in lithuania is lithuanian, and those fines that were imposed, they accumulated to the amount that you mentioned. and another question: do you think that a public servant can afford to disobey the laws of the state in which he works and of which he is a citizen?
answer to the question posed by the blue card. thank you very much for asking. yes, i said that i was speaking on behalf of the ecr group, and that is the view of our group, because one of the authorities in our group, the former british prime minister margaret thatcher, was the one who fought for the europe we want today, and unfortunately it is not yet in my country, because national minorities (the indigenous minorities who have lived here for 700 years) are persecuted and punished for using their mother tongue, even in areas where national minorities make up 80% of the population. these entries are written next to the official language. they were legally hung back in 1991, when the law was passed, when we, the poles of lithuania, fought for independence and won this independence. but after 20 years, that law was repealed, and the situation is the same today. this is outrageous and it cannot be.
for the efd group lithuania was the first of the three baltic states to have the honour and the difficult task of chairing the european union in the second half of last year. i am pleased that a relatively young member of the community has shown herself to be mature, politically credible and one of the most solid members of the community, capable of reaching important agreements and finding the best solutions. i congratulate you, your excellency the president. understandably, the eastern partnership was the main focus of the presidency. the fact that ukraine and the european union have not signed an association agreement and a free trade agreement is sometimes seen as a defeat for lithuania. i think that is a fundamentally wrong assessment. we have to admit that neither ukraine itself nor the institutions of the european union have done everything they could to put all the dots on the partnership agreement. however, it should not be forgotten that during this period, lithuania's presidency in the eastern partnership achieved other important things: an agreement on a visa-free regime for moldova, the signing of four other agreements. under the lithuanian presidency, agreement was reached on a seven-year budget. the commission has also been active in the area of the fight against fraud and corruption. in the past six months, an agreement was reached with the european investment bank and the structural funds on the possibility of financial resources to support small and medium-sized enterprises. but when we talk about really important achievements and good results, we should not forget that the european union today stands at the crossroads between federation and national unity, and the decision on which direction to take is inevitable. perhaps not for this parliament, perhaps not for another, but for the one elected after 2020, it will be inevitable. but until then, we must make some changes to some of the increasingly vocal political positions in the community. if the slogan 'more europe' means fewer freedoms and rights for the nation states, it will not be good. let us turn our policy today towards a union of nation states, because it is strong states with their own identity, culture and religion that are the basis of a strong europe.
i welcome and rejoice in the excellent results of the lithuanian presidency and particularly in the agreement on the european union's multiannual financial framework, which will ensure that all eu-funded programmes are implemented on time. the lithuanian presidency, after long negotiations, finally agreed on european union programmes with a social dimension, which means that the european union budget will be people-oriented and our citizens will reap tangible benefits. i would like to congratulate them on the agreements reached on the european social fund, the european globalisation adjustment fund and the fund for the support of the most deprived, because all these funds contribute to the well-being of our people and are dedicated to people affected by the crisis. the youth guarantee initiative and the erasmus+ programme for education and training, adopted under the lithuanian presidency, are of great importance in this context, as together we have achieved that the solution to the problem of unemployment should focus on increasing youth employment. moreover, it is indeed a good achievement that the budget for education and training has been increased by as much as 40% compared to the previous financial programming period. in fact, lithuania's presidency succeeded in finding common compromises, in adopting common decisions on the common banking supervision and resolution mechanism, on the deposit guarantee scheme directive, and on the long-debated tobacco directive. i am also very pleased about the additional pension rights for mobile workers and the solid progress that has been made, and about the directive on posted workers and about many other issues that are actually making the european union more reliable, growing and open. and in conclusion, mr president, i thank you and your team, i thank ambassador karoblis, i thank deputy minister vytautas leskevičius, who has really worked very well with the european parliament, and the entire lithuanian government and all the people here, because i know how hard they have worked, how many nights they have not slept, how much they have been nervous, but the result is really good.
as the representative of lithuania, i would like to thank all my colleagues from the european people's party and other political groups who have contributed to the success of the lithuanian presidency. to all of you who have worked hand in hand with us in this chamber, thank you for your understanding, your support and your cooperation. a few things. lithuania's presidency of the european union has succeeded in focusing europe's attention on closer relations with the eastern partnership countries, and the european union has lost its illusions about moscow's policy objectives after russia's pushback. the refusal of ukraine to sign a historic agreement with the european union at the vilnius summit is only a short-term and very costly victory for russian and pro-russian forces in ukraine. we must admit that our eastern partnership programme started out as a bureaucratic and technical programme, but it has now become clear that this is a geopolitical issue and we must approach it accordingly. i would note that russia has completely destroyed its image in the world: many people have realized that russia does not offer friendly partnership, but seeks dominance. russia, by playing hardball, may win short-term victories, but in the long run, the nation will seek to break free from russian influence. the lithuanian presidency was successful in particular because it made the eastern neighbourhood a fully-fledged part of the european union's agenda. finally, although the eastern partnership is not one of greece's priorities, i believe that it is this policy that we must pay particular attention to in the next six months.
it is very gratifying to hear so many compliments on lithuania and it is gratifying to hear that lithuania has surprised many with its well-organised presidency. the largest number of agreements was reached involving both the banking union and the multiannual budget, as well as programmes implementing them, especially in terms of reducing youth unemployment. the vilnius meeting was a key event in the strengthening of relations with the eastern partnership countries. now we have to take into account the games of the great actors, we must not leave the ukrainian people alone. the eu also wants to consolidate as soon as possible the agreements reached at the summit with those countries which are firmly on the path towards rapprochement with the european union. i think we have achieved the objectives of the presidency, which were energy security, integrated border management, the european union's baltic sea strategy, the eastern neighbourhood policy. i would like to emphasise, in response to the statements made by some of my colleagues, that the rights of national minorities in lithuania are fully in line with international agreements. this has been confirmed on several occasions by both the osce and other international institutions. nowhere in the world, and particularly in the case of the polish minority, do the polish minority have so many rights and opportunities to develop their culture, social life and the rest of their lives. so please stop harassing my colleagues and dragging them into political games. 23 years ago on january 13th, the people of lithuania stood in front of the parliament defending it with a song and with a firm belief. we were facing the soviet army. and it is perhaps a miracle that today, 23 years later, we are celebrating the successful lithuanian presidency of the european union. i would therefore like to thank the entire lithuanian team, i would like to thank my fellow members of the european parliament and i hope that we will have many opportunities in the future to cooperate and agree on issues of common concern.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. from 2014, member states will be required to develop national and/or regional smart specialisation strategies. the development of these strategies is a precondition for receiving erdf support for research, technological development and innovation. the past has shown that, in the absence of a common strategy, eu investment in research, technological development and innovation in member states or regions has not produced tangible results. the strategies will include specific requirements to allow financial resources to be concentrated on a limited number of research and innovation priorities, to encourage private investment, and to ensure a better control and monitoring system. without a strategy, a member state or its region will not be able to benefit from erdf funding.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this document because the development of a smart specialisation strategy will be a necessary precondition for erdf support for research, technological development and innovation in the 2014-2020 programming period. the past has shown that without a comprehensive overall strategy, eu investment, including in research, technological development and innovation (r & td), has often not achieved the desired results: support is too broad, solutions are not adapted to the specific situation in a given place, and there is too little involvement of actors and representatives at administrative level. if a national or regional strategy for research and innovation in advanced specialisation is not developed at all or if there is no action plan ensuring the implementation of the strategy by 2016 when the action programmes are adopted, no erdf payment for the thematic objective of r&d&i shall be made.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. it should be noted that smart specialisation is a key element of the new eu cohesion policy and a prerequisite for the use of the european regional development fund. it is therefore essential that smart specialisation strategies are developed in partnership with the private sector, researchers and the innovation community. helping regions to maximise their innovation potential is essential resources should therefore be focused on priority areas for smart growth with competitive advantage. in particular, it is necessary to actively involve representatives of the business, research, education and innovation communities and other stakeholders. regions should be active in the implementation of the knowledge economy. in preparing the strategy, particular attention should be paid to the setting of priorities. the region must take account of its own strengths and comparative advantages. close cooperation and interaction between all relevant actors at all political levels should also be encouraged.
in writing. − i voted in favour of a proposal to discuss how the ep gives its consent in procedures where this is required by european union law. the 1986 european act sets out the approval procedure in two areas: association agreements and accession treaties. it is now a special legislative procedure under the treaty of lisbon. the procedure for the consent procedure in parliament's committees varies considerably. the aim is therefore to clarify the approval procedures and to make them more transparent and efficient.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution, which obliges commercial vehicle manufacturers to produce greener vehicles and also reduces the maximum average co2 emissions of new commercial vehicles from 203 g/km to 147 g/km, to be applied from 2020. global temperatures could rise to critical levels if fossil fuel combustion and the production of co2-emitting cars continue at this rate.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. after long and difficult negotiations, an agreement was reached in 2011 for an average emission ceiling target of 147 g co2/km to apply from 2020. i think we have to stick to this agreement in principle and provide clarity for the car industry without changing this objective. i agree with the rapporteur that the special credit system must be maintained. special loans are an important means of encouraging the introduction of low-emission vehicles on the market and providing incentives for manufacturers to develop low-carbon technologies. as it is not yet known which low-emission technology will be the first in the future, the special credit scheme should be maintained for light commercial vehicles. this does not require any additional financial resources and does not impose any burden on taxpayers.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is parliament's acceptance of the commission's proposal to confirm the ambitious environmental targets set back in 2011 to reduce co2 emissions from new light commercial vehicles. in recent interinstitutional discussions, doubts were raised about the technical feasibility of such an objective, but the commission confirmed that it was technically possible to achieve the objective, and parliament therefore supported it. the environmental objectives were reaffirmed in parliament also because there have been no changes in environmental or economic conditions since the 147 g/km target was agreed which would justify a change in the target value and because manufacturers need planning certainty to take account of the long development cycle of light commercial vehicles.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is necessary to reduce co2 emissions from new light commercial vehicles. the established rate of 147 g co2/km shall not be changed, i.e. neither increased nor decreased, in order to provide clarity for car manufacturers. in my view, the special credits must also be preserved, because they are additional credits for low-emission vehicles, they encourage the development of low-emission technologies. for each low-emission vehicle emitting less than 50 g co2/km, an additional credit shall be granted to the manufacturer's fleet. this scheme will expire in 2018. these credits are included in the commission proposal for co2 emissions from passenger cars but not for co2 emissions from light commercial vehicles.
in writing. − the european union is one of the most progressive regions in reducing co2 emissions and thus contributing to pollution reduction. the eu has a coherent approach to reducing co2 across a wide range of industrial sectors. this is a question that is of particular concern to the automotive industry. it is understood that passenger cars are not and cannot be an exception to reduce co2 emissions, but at the same time i agree that the requirements must be realistic and therefore the co2 emissions targets must not be changed. it is important that we not only be ambitious in this area but also understand the real potential of the industry to reduce co2 emissions from passenger cars, as it is necessary to remain competitive in the global market.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to 'develop a financial framework for eu action in the field of consumer policy for the period 2014-2020, thus maintaining a high level of consumer protection'. the consumer programme will be supported by eu consumer policy. the programme shall aim to put consumers first in the single market, in particular by ensuring greater product safety, by improving consumer awareness, education and knowledge of rights, by strengthening consumer rights and by ensuring effective remedies, in particular through alternative dispute resolution and by ensuring the enforcement of rights at cross-border level. i support the suggestions made to give greater attention to information and education initiatives to make consumers, especially young people, aware of their rights. further development of the evidence base is necessary to improve both eu and national consumer affairs policies, as well as the consumer markets scoreboard, which identifies consumer failing markets in europe. it is also important to implement legislation to enhance consumer rights as soon as possible and to complete the development of an alternative dispute resolution system.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament's support for the eu consumer programme 2014-2020, with certain of its own proposals and amendments. under this programme, the eu commits to continue to invest in consumer protection, education and information, with a particular focus on the safety of goods and services offered on the market. one of the innovations proposed by parliament in the area of consumer protection in the coming year is the creation of websites providing consumers with comparable, reliable and easily accessible data on prices, quality of services and sustainability. the communication also proposes to provide financial support to european consumer organisations, while enhancing capacity and promoting the exchange of good practice, consumer research and consumer information for consumer organisations at local, regional, national and european levels.
in writing. − i have endorsed the proposal to establish a financial framework for the european union's action in the field of consumer policy for the period 2014-2020 in order to maintain a high level of consumer protection. the programme aims to enhance product safety by improving consumer awareness, education and rights awareness, by strengthening consumer rights, by ensuring effective remedies, by implementing alternative dispute resolution and by ensuring the enforcement of rights at cross-border level. eu consumers must be able to access goods and services with the assurance that effective market monitoring is being carried out in the eu. eu consumers must be fully informed about their rights and how to exercise them.
in writing. - i approve this document. i am convinced that consumer protection must play a greater role in the functioning of the internal market. although the funding provided is not large, i believe that if the funds are allocated properly, they will benefit the consumer. the priority areas for funding should include not only consumer information and education but also training and upskilling of professionals dealing with consumer complaints. these people must be professionals in their field in order to provide the affected consumer with appropriate assistance.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, the purpose of which is to align council regulation (ec) no 774/94 opening and providing for the administration of certain community tariff quotas for certain meat and cereal products with the provisions of the treaty of lisbon.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it aligns the 1994 european community regulation opening and providing for the administration of certain community tariff quotas for certain meat and cereal products with the provisions of the treaty of lisbon as regards the procedural aspects of the establishment and administration of the quotas. these procedural changes are due to the modification of certain commission working and procedural rules by the treaty of lisbon and to the enhanced role of the european parliament.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to update regulation (eec) no 1601/91 laying down common rules for the definition, description and presentation of aromatised wines, aromatised wine-based drinks and aromatised wine-based cocktails. the aromatised wine sector is of major importance to the european union, with around 90% of the world's total production of this type of wine produced in the eu. the reform of the wine sector aims to lay down the definitions, descriptions, presentation, labelling and protection of geographical indications of aromatised wine products. it also provides for the improvement and clarification of the application of eu legislation on aromatised wine products and the introduction of a new well-defined quality policy for such products. i support the proposals made to include a clear indication that the labelling of aromatised wine products may refer to the ecological character of the products used in their production. it is also important to provide for rules requiring the indication of all the operations characterising the production of aromatised wine products: aromatisation, possible addition of alcohol, sweetening and the use of colours
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it aims to update the 1991 regulation laying down common rules for the definition, description and presentation of aromatised wines, aromatised wine-based drinks and aromatised wine-based cocktails. in the process of simplifying the common agricultural policy, it is also appropriate to restructure the way the eu wine market is managed, with a view to phasing out ineffective and costly intervention measures and redistributing costs in order to meet consumer needs as closely as possible and to make european wine more competitive. aromatised wine products represent a relatively large part of the european wine sector and are considered to be of major importance in terms of quality and quantity on the domestic and international markets. the eu produces around 90% of the world's aromatised wine and many member states already have a long tradition of producing this wine.
in writing. −in order to reduce global co2 emissions at the lowest cost, it is essential to implement carbon capture and storage (ccs) technologies. these technologies may continue to use fossil fuels, but would have a lower global warming impact. i agree with the rapporteur that, although the financial support of the member states is of particular importance for the development of ccs, the european union itself must nevertheless be involved in promoting the development of this technology and in ensuring the exchange of good practice. in order to stimulate innovation, reduce costs and ensure that this technology is accepted as a viable option, the development of pilot ccs projects should be supported.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. carbon capture and storage (ccs) is currently the only large-scale demonstration technology available for capturing carbon dioxide from the combustion of fossil fuels to produce low-carbon electricity. the eu was hoping to become the world leader in the deployment of carbon capture and storage (ccs) five years ago, but its measures have not produced significant results in this area. although the ner300 financial mechanism has been set up for this purpose and has been allocated around eur 1 billion, the majority of this amount has not been used to date. the main problem remains the lack of a specific business model in the eu and its member states to provide investors with a clear regulatory and funding environment to develop these technologies efficiently. i agree that ccs technologies must be integrated into the eu's long-term energy strategy, with clear financing mechanisms and a level playing field for different low-carbon energy technologies and fair competition between them.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it sets out the possible direction and the necessary measures to be taken by the european parliament to encourage the accelerated deployment of carbon capture and storage technology in the european union. the deployment of this technology will be crucial to achieving a significant reduction in global co2 emissions by 2050 at the lowest cost, as it would reduce emissions from large industrial installations and allow the continued use of some fossil fuels with a lower environmental impact. unfortunately, in the five years since the pilot projects were announced, almost none of these projects have been successful, and it is therefore considered that there is a need to review the governance of the projects, as well as to develop business models that encourage private investment. the report also proposes to require eu legislation to require each member state to publish indicative strategy papers explaining how they will achieve the target of reducing co2 emissions by almost 20% by 2050.
in writing. − i supported the need to develop an eu-wide strategy for carbon capture and storage (ccs) and identify sources of funding. five years ago, the european union was expected to become the world leader in the deployment of carbon capture and storage. however, the majority of the eur 1 billion allocated to ccs pilot projects under the european energy programme for economic recovery in 2009 has not yet been spent and will not be reallocated. one study after another shows that the cost of one tonne of co2 avoided by ccs will be lower than the cost of offshore wind and solar. the main problem remains the absence of a specific business model in the eu and its member states that would provide investors with a clear regulatory and funding environment to develop these technologies efficiently. i believe that ccs technologies must be integrated into the eu's long-term energy strategy and that clear funding mechanisms must be established.
in writing. − we have been discussing issues related to these technologies since 2009. i then raised the question of the effective protection of the environment through the use of ccs technologies. this question remains relevant today. on the other hand, advances in science and new methods make the use of this technology much safer. on the other hand, i would like to reiterate my point made earlier that we should not talk about these technologies in the first place, but about reducing carbon dioxide emissions.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. in particular, the deployment of capture and storage technology is essential to reduce global co2 emissions by 2050 at the lowest cost. it should be noted that the application of this technology will not only reduce emissions from large industrial installations, but will also allow the continued use of some fossil fuels with a reduced global warming impact. the absence of ccs in a long-term energy strategy will severely constrain efforts to combat climate change at national, union and global levels. this is why it is essential that each member state submits indicative strategy papers explaining how they will achieve the 2050 target. it is important that the commission and the member states make greater efforts to remove the main regulatory, financial and social barriers to the use of carbon capture and storage technology. member states should be prepared to actively ensure the development of this technology as well as the proper observance of environmental standards.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the resolution on the ehealth action plan 2012-2020. e-health is a tool and a more universal way to improve the quality and efficiency of healthcare in the 21st century. while the right to quality healthcare is recognised as a fundamental right, the increasing demand for healthcare services as a result of an ageing population, the impact of chronic diseases, the mobility of patients and healthcare professionals, together with the increasing expectations of citizens for the quality of healthcare and the decreasing budgets for healthcare, do not always ensure the quality of the provision of these services. i agree with the rapporteur that there is a huge potential market in ehealth. in other words, facilitating access to healthcare for people living in remote or sparsely populated areas, improving working conditions, reducing waiting times and ensuring a safe, efficient and high-quality delivery process for healthcare services possible measures to tackle healthcare system problems.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. the ongoing economic downturn, the increasing mobility of people and the reduction of national health budgets pose major challenges to national health systems and the proper delivery of such services. the main purpose of the health system in each member state is to provide safe, high-quality and effective healthcare. the provision of remote healthcare could become one of the ways to provide healthcare services in a flexible and efficient manner to certain targeted patient groups, such as those receiving home care. i support the call for member states to integrate health services into existing medical systems and for eu-wide standards to be established for the development of eu-wide ehealth systems and for ways to harmonise them across member states.
in writing. − i voted for this report because it sets out parliament's view of a possible eu ehealth action plan, or telemedicine, by 2020 to ensure innovative healthcare in the 21st century. e. health a measure to improve access to healthcare for people living in remote or sparsely populated areas, to improve working conditions, to reduce waiting times and, above all, to help ensure safe, effective and quality healthcare. however, to achieve these objectives, healthcare providers need to cooperate with each other on a wider scale than is possible due to competence and language barriers. this requires technical standardisation and interoperability of eu healthcare systems and the establishment of certification and identification schemes at eu level. of course, data protection, confidentiality, privacy or liability are also very important issues that need to be addressed if we want to successfully deploy ehealth.
in writing. − i voted in favour, as the economic downturn has reduced health budgets in member states and it is therefore necessary to find ways to increase the efficiency and sustainability of health systems. e-health can be part of the solution to these problems a means of improving access to healthcare for people living in remote or sparsely populated areas, improving working conditions, reducing waiting times and, most importantly, helping to ensure safe, effective and quality healthcare. i believe that standards should be set for the development of ehealth systems used at eu level and that they should be harmonised across member states to ensure their effectiveness.
in writing. − e-health becomes a means of improving the quality of healthcare, while also providing access to healthcare in more remote areas. in supporting this report, i hope that the measures it proposes will contribute to improving patient health literacy and access to healthcare. i believe that the provisions contained in the report will bring positive developments in this new and dynamically developing area.
in writing. − i support the ehealth action plan presented, which extends the ehealth action plan adopted in 2004. the plan's focus on improving the quality and accessibility of healthcare, reducing healthcare costs and ensuring greater equality for european citizens is welcome. i think it's a tool and a more universal way to improve the quality and increase the efficiency of health care in the 21st century. it can improve access to healthcare for people living in remote areas, improve working conditions, reduce waiting times and help to ensure safe, effective and quality healthcare. however, it is necessary to ensure technical standardisation and interoperability of eu healthcare systems, and to establish certification and identification schemes at eu level. the successful deployment of these services requires the resolution of issues related to data protection, confidentiality, privacy or liability. the opinion of doctors and healthcare professionals, patient associations, should be taken into account before and during the development of healthcare applications. it is necessary to guarantee appropriate support for smes in order to ensure a level playing field in this sector.
in writing. - i voted in favour of a resolution on the food crisis, fraud in the food chain and its control. despite transparency being one of the key elements of the food safety control concept of the european commission and the member states, fraud in the food sector is frequently reported. example: the horse scandal, sold as beef. this is usually because the potential financial benefits of fraud are high and the risk of getting caught is low. such actions not only undermine consumer confidence in the food chain but also have a direct negative impact on the food sector. the agri-food sector is one of the largest economic sectors in the eu, employing some 48 million people and generating a turnover of €715 billion a year. the commission must take all necessary measures to prevent fraud and the control authorities must cooperate better at international level.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. in fact, several recent food-related scandals have damaged consumer confidence in the eu's ability to control food fraud. despite the eu's strong focus on food safety, there is still a lack of clear regulation on food fraud. the eu has so far only a general requirement that labelling, advertising, presentation and packaging must not mislead consumers, although the practical application of this provision varies widely from country to country and the number of controls in this area is very limited. for this reason, fraud in the food sector is not usually detected, especially if there are no consequences for public health or food safety. i agree that the situation needs to change, in particular by defining the concept of food fraud, by strengthening the role and resources of the food and veterinary office, by improving cooperation between the member states and europol and by increasing the penalties for fraud.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is the european parliament's study of the problem of food fraud, the factors that influence its occurrence and possible solutions. in recent years, the problem of food fraud has received increasing attention due to cases of unfair food labelling and other food fraud. examples such as the use of road salt in food, the marketing of conventional eggs as organic and the recent horse scandal show that this can be a continuing or structural problem. among other things, this also has a negative impact on consumer confidence in the food chain, creating a very paradoxical situation: food is safer than ever before, but consumer confidence is low.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the proposal as the problem of food fraud is gaining attention due to cases of unfair food labelling and other food fraud affecting the eu food chain, such as the use of road salt in food, the marketing of conventional eggs as organic and the recent horse scandal. these cases of food fraud have already had a negative impact on consumer confidence in the food chain. the eu has so far only a general requirement that labelling, advertising, presentation and packaging must not mislead consumers, although the practical application of this provision varies widely from one country to another and the number of control measures in this area is very limited. therefore, fraud in the food sector is not usually detected, especially if there are no consequences for public health or food safety. this also makes it very difficult to establish the actual extent of food fraud in the european union. the situation needs to be changed, in particular by defining the concept of food fraud, by strengthening the role and resources of the food and veterinary services, by improving cooperation between member states and by increasing the penalties for such fraud.
in writing. − one of the key principles of eu food safety policy is the control of consumer safety, proper information and traceability of products. indeed, recent developments show that it is not always possible to ensure that the end-user receives what he or she reads on the label. i therefore support the strengthening of the control system and better coordination of such controls and call on the commission to speed up the adoption of measures to allow for closer cooperation between the competent authorities of the member states in preventing such fraud.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. i believe that the prevention of food fraud must be an integral part of eu policy, as such fraud has had a negative impact on consumer confidence in the food chain. in order to ensure adequate consumer protection and to restore consumer confidence in the common food market, consumers need to know where the food they buy comes from. in order to combat this fraud, more attention should be paid to better traceability of ingredients and products throughout the food chain. it is necessary to provide for stricter controls on imports from third countries. i think that a special group should be set up to investigate such fraud cases, to carry out unannounced inspections. the development of private sector anti-fraud programmes must be encouraged across the eu. it should be noted that the economic benefits of fraud are further enhanced by the relatively low fines and the wide variations in the procedures in force in the member states, as well as by the strict austerity measures. therefore, in order to facilitate the effective fight against fraud in the food supply chain, a clear, legally valid, eu-wide definition of food fraud is necessary.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. the eu is increasingly confronted with undeclared work and the shadow economy. in the eu-27, undeclared work accounts for 18.8% of total gdp, and in some countries it is even above 30%. at present, labour inspectorates in many member states are facing difficulties in combating undeclared work by all means, as the savings in labour inspectorates have led to a reduction in funding. one of the main problems for labour inspectorates is cross-border labour relations. it is difficult for national authorities to obtain relevant information from the authorities of the country of origin and to understand the employment relationship. i support the commission's initiative to create a platform for european labour inspectors to help exchange good practice, to introduce a european social security card with all the necessary data, such as working hours and social security, which would allow labour inspectors to have all the necessary information immediately. i doubt the need for a separate eu agency. i think that this is an excessive proposal and it would be appropriate to strengthen the role of labour inspectorates by the means mentioned above.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is the european parliament's way of drawing attention to the role of labour inspection systems in combating undeclared work in the european union. this is important because the economic and social changes of recent decades have had a major impact on the labour market. the globalisation of the world economy has had a negative impact on traditional safe and fair labour relations. subcontracting, contract work, low job security, work intensity and job insecurity are all damaging the quality of jobs in europe. this erosion of labour relations is accompanied by a lack of respect for labour law and workers' rights. meanwhile, labour inspectorates in many member states are currently facing difficulties in combating undeclared work in any way possible, as their funding has been reduced as a result of budgetary savings. this should be brought to the attention of the member states.
in writing. − i supported the need to review the role of labour inspection systems in combating undeclared work in the european union, the problems they face and to provide policy recommendations on how to tackle undeclared work and help inspections work more effectively. the eu is increasingly confronted with undeclared work and the shadow economy. in the eu-27, undeclared work accounts for 18.8% of total gdp, and in some countries it is even more than 30%. at present, labour inspectorates in many member states are facing difficulties in combating undeclared work by all means, as the savings in labour inspectorates have led to a reduction in funding. one of the main problems for labour inspectorates is cross-border labour relations. it is difficult for national authorities to obtain relevant information from the authorities of the country of origin and to understand the employment relationship. it is proposed to introduce a european social security card, which would record all the necessary data, such as working hours and social security. this would enable the labour inspector to review all the necessary data immediately. sweden has already introduced such a card in the construction sector. the inspectors receive detectors that allow them to read the information on the cards very quickly and easily. in january 2013, luxembourg introduced a similar scheme.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. it is worth noting that undeclared work, the shadow economy, labour law and the lack of respect for workers' rights in the eu have damaging consequences for the whole economy, society and businesses. it is therefore essential to give high priority to the role of labour inspection systems in combating undeclared work in the eu. it is necessary to establish guidelines for cross-border cooperation between national authorities and for new legal initiatives. the regulation of new forms of work and non-standard work by establishing a clear and binding definition of worker at eu level is another effective means of combating fictitious self-employment. i believe that labour inspections should cover all categories of workers, with particular attention to the protection of exploited workers. in addition, innovative systems for cooperation with other public authorities and systems for the registration of mobile workers should be established. it is noted that there is no enforcement of the social anti-dumping rules at eu level. i support the proposal to set up a european agency to deal with all types of crossborder issues, and it is also appropriate to introduce the european social security card.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. employee financial participation (efp) in company management is not a popular measure in eu companies, although it does involve employees more in the decision-making process in the companies where they work, helps to strengthen good governance and ensures the sharing of the benefits of a financially successful company. it is much more popular in the us, but in the eu it is difficult to implement due to the disparity in tax incentives, as well as the lack of regulatory incentives and clear practical implementation guidelines. i agree that this measure could be further developed and applied in the eu, especially by small and medium-sized enterprises, which would encourage their development. the eu should provide a framework concept and common guidelines on key principles and best practices to inform national and company-level decision-making on dfd systems.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it seeks to highlight the positive role that employee financial participation systems can play in developing employee participation in the management and in contributing to the success of the financial undertakings in which they work. the strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth europe 2020 highlights the need for inclusive growth, which empowers citizens through, inter alia, employment, investment in skills, the fight against poverty and the modernisation of labour markets and social protection. employee financial participation (efp) is a concrete tool for developing these issues, involving employees more closely in the decision-making process in the companies where they work, helping to strengthen good governance and ensure the sharing of the financial benefits of a successful company. employee financial participation through innovative financing mechanisms can also strengthen smes, encourage employees to invest in the shares of the company where they work and, at the same time, capitalise on growth. developing long-term employee investment in one's own company can also provide stability and certainty, strengthening smes and protecting jobs.
in writing. - i voted for this statement. employee financial participation is an effective tool to increase employee involvement in decision-making and to develop new forms of corporate financing. the measure also contributes to strengthening good governance and ensuring the sharing of the benefits of a financially successful company. although the benefits of financial employee involvement are clear, participation in the implementation of the measure is still low. this requires greater emphasis on the positive role of the system in developing employee participation in the management and in contributing to the success of the financial undertakings in which they work. the measure should also be sustainable in the long term and based on the principle of voluntary participation. i believe that the commission should provide a framework for this system and common guidelines on the basic principles and best practices that would apply across the eu. in addition, guidelines should be established to help businesses, workers, social partners and national authorities to better design and implement this measure.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. europe has always been characterised by a high level of social protection and is an integral part of the european social model. however, the ongoing crisis has also affected this sector, with cuts in social benefits and a reduction in the number of people on social assistance. the economic and financial crisis has brought a number of changes and is forcing us to look for new ways of increasing employment while maintaining the stability and sustainability of social protection systems. people who are out of work or have lost their jobs should be encouraged to take up new forms of work, such as self-employment, which provides greater flexibility. however, in promoting self-sufficiency, we must ensure the social protection of the people involved, particularly in terms of protection against disability and old age, maternity and paternity, unemployment, access to healthcare services, which would allow the people involved to feel secure and encourage this very important form of employment.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report, which focuses on the social protection of the selfemployed, because there is a risk that, as the nature of employment changes and situations become more frequent, new groups of workers are not and will not be adequately protected. more than 30 million people are selfemployed in the european union, or about 15% of all those in employment. most people become self-employed because they cannot find another job or because they cannot find flexible working conditions. unfortunately, we do not have a uniform and universally accepted situation in the european union, which raises a number of problems in relation to the labour rights and social protection of self-employed persons. for example, in many countries, self-employed persons are only partially involved in the pension system, and as a result, they are at high risk of poverty in old age (low contributions mean low pensions). the same applies to invalidity pensions. self-employed persons are also less protected in the case of maternity/paternity. even fewer selfemployed persons are protected in the event of unemployment. in my report, i therefore urge member states to ensure that the situation is not used as a means of preventing workers from benefiting from social and labour guarantees or as a means for employers to circumvent labour and social security law. i also propose that member states provide all citizens who want to work for themselves with information on their rights in relation to social protection.
in writing. − i supported the need to review social protection systems in the eu and to ensure social protection for all, including the self-employed. social protection is the cornerstone of the european social model, with the main function of promoting social inclusion, social justice and ensuring income and access to quality education and health care for all. the situation has been rapidly expanding and taking on new forms, but there is no universally accepted definition of the situation in the eu. the situation must be recognised as a form of work that helps to create jobs and reduce unemployment. member states must develop social protection in the event of old age, disability, maternity (fatherhood) and unemployment in order to make it more appropriate to the needs of the self-employed and to bring it up to the standard of the wage-earning population. the commission and the member states should link social protection and related rights not to an employment contract but to a person, thus ensuring adequate social protection for all, including self-employed persons and employees, regardless of their contract type or employment status.
in writing. − the european union is facing unemployment problems. one way to reduce unemployment and dependence on social benefits is through self-employment (individual activity, situation). although many professionals in different fields work in this way, they very often face a lack of social protection. although social protection has traditionally been a member state competence, i believe that it is necessary to promote cooperation and the dissemination of good practice in this field between member states and to seek a standard that ensures an adequate level of social protection. this will protect the social interests of those who take up individual activities, while encouraging them to take up individual activities and thus contribute to reducing unemployment.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to analyse the readiness of the member states for the next phase of cohesion policy funding. in the current economic climate, national, local and regional budgets are increasingly burdened. thus, for many member states and regions, cohesion policy funding is becoming increasingly important. it is therefore necessary that member states and regions take the necessary steps to draw up partnership agreements and action programmes in an efficient and timely manner so that funding can be mobilised as soon as possible. member states are at very different stages of preparation for the next programming period. however, many of them are making efforts to analyse what has worked and what has not in the 2007-2013 programme in order to develop new strategies to ensure the effective use of cohesion policy funds. i support the proposals put forward to give greater emphasis to the involvement of local and regional authorities and to give the regions greater flexibility in setting priorities in accordance with their territorial needs.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it provides parliament with an overview of the eu member states' readiness to start the new programming period for cohesion policy in an effective and timely manner. in the current economic climate, national, local and regional budgets are increasingly burdened and, as a result, cohesion policy funding is becoming increasingly important for many member states and regions. this highlights the increased need to ensure that money is spent efficiently so that citizens can enjoy all the benefits of european funding. in this context, it is essential that member states and regions take the necessary steps to prepare partnership agreements and action programmes in an efficient and timely manner, so that funding can be mobilised as soon as possible once agreement is reached on the common provisions regulation. the communication notes that member states have reached very different stages of preparation for the next programming period, partly for objective reasons, such as member states making certain changes in their preparation for the next programming period compared to the previous funding stage, such as structural changes to the operational programmes; new administrative structures are in place; discussions are underway on focusing thematically on several specific objectives.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the proposal to analyse the readiness of the member states for the next phase of funding of cohesion policy. the aim is to conclude as soon as possible the negotiations on the cohesion policy legislative package, the partnership agreements and the action programmes. two thirds of the outstanding commitments accumulated at the end of the 2007-2013 mff relate to the financing of cohesion policy, and the aim is to find a credible solution to reduce the risk of the implementation of eu programmes being blocked by late payments. i also agree with the need to focus more on the involvement of local and regional authorities, giving the regions more flexibility in setting priorities according to their territorial needs.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. in order to ensure an effective and timely start to the new programming period of the fp, it is important that member states and regions take the necessary steps to prepare partnership agreements and operational programmes in an effective and timely manner. the implementation of these measures would further reduce existing disparities between eu regions, promote growth and strengthen economic, social and territorial cohesion. it should be noted that the principle of multi-level governance is essential to ensure the effective management of the new cohesion policy. for many member states and regions, cohesion policy funding is becoming increasingly important. to achieve a greater impact of eu assistance, it is therefore necessary to adopt an integrated approach and to coordinate the various sources of funding available, as well as to ensure policy and regulatory coherence. in addition, the management of union funds, access to support procedures, procurement rules and the supervision procedure should be simplified in order to increase transparency. clear and accessible information on the beneficiaries of funds and funding opportunities must be made available to all interested parties. we must ensure that money is spent efficiently and that citizens can enjoy all the benefits of european funding.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the resolution on plastic waste in the environment. the european plastics industry has an annual turnover of around eur 300 billion and employs 1.54 million people, but the data on waste from this industry is still not consolidated and ranges from 25 million tonnes of waste (european commission data in 2008) to 13 million tonnes (european environment agency data in 2010). in this context, we call for the elimination of the most hazardous plastic materials from the market by 2020 and for a reduction in the use of plastic bags. i believe that the plastic waste in the environment has a direct negative impact on both human health and the environment itself. the world is accumulating huge amounts of plastic that have been around for centuries and are damaging the environment. in addition, the eu has no specific rules for plastic waste and common waste legislation is poorly implemented in eu countries. it is therefore essential that at least 80% of plastic waste is collected and sorted. it should be noted that the economic potential of recycling plastics is not fully exploited, i.e. only a quarter of plastic waste is recycled.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish a european strategy to reduce plastic waste in the environment. in 2010, some 95 billion plastic bags were placed on the market in the european union. today, the eu plastics industry employs 1.6 million people and global plastics production could triple by 2050. however, data on waste from this industry are still not precise and uniform from 25 million tonnes of waste to 13 million tonnes. in the absence of reliable and comparable data on the production, collection, sorting, recycling, energy use and disposal of plastics, it is difficult to develop targeted and effective policies, in particular actions, against illicit waste streams. the proposal aims to remove the most hazardous plastics from the market by 2020 and to reduce the use of plastic bags. also reduce the use of single-use, non-recyclable, non-biodegradable and non-compostable plastic bags and ensure that no waste is sent to landfill that can be recycled and reused by 2020. i support the proposals put forward to provide for rules that would allow the characteristics of products placed on the market to be clearly defined: consumers need to know whether the plastic they buy is recycled, compostable, biodegradable or recyclable to facilitate sorting. it is also important to raise public awareness through public information campaigns.
in writing. − i voted for this report because it is a way for parliament to draw attention to the problems of plastic waste in the environment. the european plastics industry has an annual turnover of around eur 300 billion and employs 1,54 million people, but data on waste from this industry are still not consolidated and properly assessed. in the absence of reliable and comparable data on the production, collection, sorting, recycling, energy use and disposal of plastics, it is difficult to develop targeted and effective policies, in particular actions, against illicit waste streams. clearly, the environmental impact of plastics is still underestimated. in this report, parliament also calls for eu legislation to regulate the management and recycling of plastic waste, to provide for relevant targets and to set stricter implementation of existing rules. currently, 40% of waste is made up of packaging, although the only eu legislation currently in force since 1994 stipulates that only 22.5% of plastic must be collected. updates and additions to the legislation are therefore necessary.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the proposal as it proposes to remove the most hazardous plastics from the market by 2020 and to reduce the use of plastic bags, to reduce the use of single-use, non-recyclable, non-biodegradable and non-compostable plastic bags, and to ensure that no waste is sent to landfills by 2020 that can be recycled and reused. i agree that more efforts are needed to ensure transparency of information, to clearly define the characteristics of products placed on the market: consumers need to know whether the plastic they buy is recycled, compostable, biodegradable or recyclable to facilitate sorting. it is important to raise public awareness through public information campaigns to reduce the amount of plastic in the environment.
in writing. - plastic waste one of the longest-lived in the environment. we need to take action as soon as possible, both to reduce their production and to increase reuse and recycling. this requires not only a review of existing eu legislation but also the effective functioning of plastic waste collection systems in the member states. in supporting this resolution, i therefore call on the commission to take more stringent measures to monitor and ensure the proper functioning of member states' plastic waste collection systems.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish regional quality labels. regional quality labels indicate that products and services belong to a specific single region in which those products are produced. they are important for the regions concerned and for the rural economy, and promote sustainable tourism development and help to increase the competitiveness of the regions. i support the proposals made to continue to organise cooperation and partnerships between all the actors concerned in the development of regional quality labels. it is also important to establish common conditions for the award of the label and criteria with which the apartments are accompanied by tailor-made programmes (financing, training, etc.) in order to provide the necessary incentives for local authorities and businesses to implement them effectively.
in writing. − i voted for this report because it highlights the growing importance of rural development policy. the report notes that the urban/rural divide is increasingly blurred and that suburban agriculture is becoming more popular. it is therefore important that regional policy, as an annex to rural development policy, also covers rural areas in order to support innovative and structural projects in these areas. the communication also highlights that the rural development programming period 2014-2020 will offer member states a wider range of measures, such as support for the production of quality food, support for cooperation between territorial entities to exploit all the resources of quality goods and services, strengthening of producer organisations, and encouraging innovation and economic diversification in rural areas.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. regional quality labels are of particular importance for the regions concerned and for the rural economy. these signs promote sustainable tourism development and help to increase the competitiveness of regions. they are also a comprehensive tool used to better involve operators, organise their activities and bring products to market. it is important to note that these signs not only promote the economic development of productive regions, but also improve the competitiveness and attractiveness of rural areas. it is therefore essential that adequate funding is provided for its establishment. cooperation and partnerships between all actors concerned should be continuously promoted in order to develop and promote regional quality labels. regional quality labels should provide essential information on local and regional identity. they would also contribute to the preservation, transmission and promotion of local and regional traditions, history and regional characteristics. in order to help maintain the image of europe as a destination for high-quality tourism, common conditions and criteria for the award of the label should apply. local authorities and enterprises should be given the necessary incentives and support to implement the programmes effectively.
draft opinion of the committee on industry, research and energy. − first of all, i would like to congratulate my colleague, mr marco tarabella, on the great work he has done in the imco committee and in the negotiations with the council to preserve the principled aspects of public procurement endorsed by the itre committee. with regard specifically to the directive on the procurement by entities operating in the water, energy, transport and postal services sectors, it should be noted that the separate regulation of the provision of municipal services has been maintained. these sectors still have a high degree of market concentration and low level of competition and, therefore, given the importance of these public services, it is appropriate to apply specific rules that reflect the specific nature of these services and ensure the conditions for the provision of high quality social services. the revision of these directives has provided a new and fairer framework for more transparent procurement procedures in the future. one of the most important achievements could be the promotion of electronic public procurement. the proposal already provides for the mandatory transmission of notices and the availability of procurement documents in electronic form and an obligation for member states to move to full electronic correspondence within two years. i believe that in the future all public procurement must be moved to the digital space, which will allow for transparent and more efficient public procurement systems in the member states. the european parliament has expressed a clear commitment to reform and amend the public procurement rules, and the commission will therefore have a special task in monitoring the way in which member states transpose and implement the provisions of these directives in the future.
madam president, we fully acknowledge that there is an increasing mismatch between the legal openness of the eu’s public procurement markets, the eu’s ambitions and interests in this area and, on the other hand, restrictive practices by major trading partners. the eu’s on-going efforts to open up public procurement markets for eu companies in third countries are to be welcomed, and that is exactly what the commission is trying to do. the commission’s proposal aims to create a new instrument which seeks to redress the lack of leverage in the eu’s international trade negotiations and to gain substantial market access commitments for the benefit of eu business. the amended proposal, suggested by our rapporteur daniel caspary and voted in the committee on international trade (inta), is a further step in improving the original text of the commission, which gives the commission a major role in determining the exclusion of foreign bidders and introducing more legal certainties. at the same time, the amended proposal is a real alternative to the original one. i will vote in favour of the amended proposal and i suggest to my colleagues that they also support it.
i would also like to congratulate my colleague, monica macovei, on her really good work on the hercule iii programme for the next seven years. but i would like to mention very briefly that we have actually been using this programme for 10 years, and not all countries are taking full advantage of the opportunities that are trying to be absorbed or take advantage of the opportunities of this programme. i mean even lithuania itself, that not all lithuanian politicians know that such a program exists. and in the baltic states in particular, cigarette smuggling is very evident in some of our regions up to 40% of the cigarettes used are contraband. and i think the commissioner knows this very well, which is why i am proposing that in future this programme be promoted more and even coordinated, as my colleague mr liberadzki has said, so that the same equipment is purchased in each region, so that it is not possible to go around the borders, etc.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it replaces the powers and responsibilities of the committees set out in parliament's rules of procedure, taking account of the changes brought about by the treaty of lisbon. these amendments aim to clarify and narrow the powers of parliamentary committees and to eliminate any duplication of powers. the proposed changes include the transfer of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs to deal with the establishment and operation of the eppo, the more explicit assignment of the union's foreign policy coordination to the committee on foreign affairs, and the further strengthening of the role of the internal market committee in the implementation of the internal market and consumer rights. the amendments shall enter into force at the beginning of the next parliamentary term.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to replace the programme prevention, preparedness and response to terrorism and other security-related risks (cips) with the national security fund. the current cips special programme is set for the period 2007-2013. it aims at preventing, preparing for and protecting persons and critical infrastructure against terrorist acts and other security incidents. the programme will be replaced by the internal security fund for the period 2014-2020.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it supports the abolition of the programme 'prevention, preparedness and consequence management of terrorism and other security related risks' which was in force from 2007 to 2013. it was designed to prevent, prepare for and protect persons and critical infrastructure against terrorist acts and other security incidents. the programme will be replaced by the internal security fund (isf police measure) for the period 2014-2020. given that the cips special programme was adopted on the basis of a dual legal basis (ec, euratom), its abolition requires a separate legal act.
