in writing. − i voted in favour of the proposal to change the programme 'prevention, preparedness and consequence management of terrorism and other security-related risks' to 'the internal security fund'. the current specific programme 'prevention, preparedness and consequence management of terrorism and other security related risks' (cips) covers the period 2007-2013. it aims at preventing, preparing for and protecting persons and critical infrastructure against terrorist acts and other security incidents. for the period 2014-2020, this programme will be replaced by the 'internal security fund'. given that the cips was approved on the basis of a dual legal basis (ec, euratom), its repeal requires a separate legal act.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to renew the eu-russia agreement on scientific and technological cooperation for five years. science and technology is one of the sectors in which the eu and russia cooperate most dynamically, given their common interests in promoting innovation and economic competitiveness. the legal framework for cooperation is set out in the eu-russia agreement on scientific and technological cooperation, which was concluded in 2000 and renewed for five years in 2009. this decision renews the agreement for a further period of five years. the content of the renewed agreement would remain identical to that of the agreement which expired on 20 february 2014.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it expresses parliament's support for the renewal of the eu-russia agreement on scientific and technological cooperation for another five years. this eu-russia agreement, which is renewed every five years, was first concluded in 2000. intensive cooperation in research and technological development, including a large number of joint projects and other cooperation actions, is a crucial element of the eu-russia strategic partnership, contributing to the objectives of the partnership for modernisation. the agreement on scientific and technological cooperation is also important in the context of the ongoing negotiations on an eu-russia visa facilitation agreement. visa facilitation could have a positive impact on research and academic mobility and facilitate the effective implementation of the agreement on scientific and technological cooperation, as well as contributing to the broader objective of strengthening political, economic and cultural links between the eu and russia.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the proposal to renew the eu-russia agreement on scientific and technological cooperation for five years, as science and technology is one of the sectors in which the eu and russia cooperate most dynamically, in the light of the common interest in promoting innovation and economic competitiveness. the legal framework for cooperation is set out in the eu-russia agreement on scientific and technological cooperation, which was concluded in 2000 and renewed for five years in 2009. this decision renews the agreement for a further period of five years. the content of the renewed agreement would remain identical to that of the agreement which expires on 20 february 2014.
in writing. − in 2000, an eu-russia agreement on scientific and technological cooperation was concluded, which was to be renewed every five years. the agreement has been renewed twice so far. the european parliament has therefore decided to extend the agreement for a further five years, considering that the scientific and technological field is one of the most intensive areas of bilateral cooperation between the eu and russia. i hope that this agreement will contribute, above all, to russia's efforts to move closer to international and european standards in the fields of environment and nuclear safety, and to cooperate in the search for scientific and technological solutions to problems that have an impact on the eu.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the new version of the regulation on the denominations and technical specifications of euro coins intended for circulation. in 1987, the commission decided that all acts which had been amended no more than ten times should be codified, and stressed that this requirement was minimal and that the commission departments should endeavour to produce codified versions of the texts for which they were responsible within a shorter timeframe in order to ensure that their provisions were clear and easily understandable. the commission initiated this proposal for codification, however, in the legislative process the european central bank (ecb), in its opinion on the proposal for codification, recommended incorporating actual amendments to the technical specifications. since the proposals to amend the annex would indeed imply the need to make substantial changes, and would therefore go beyond the limits of simple codification, it was decided that the codified version of the regulation should be replaced by the new wording.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal because, in the opinion of the consultative working party of the legal services of the european parliament, the council and the commission, the proposal does not contain any substantive changes other than those mentioned and because, as regards the codification of the unchanged provisions of the earlier acts and of the amendments mentioned, the proposal seeks only to codify the existing texts without changing their substance.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. the association of southeast asian nations (asean) is now one of the strongest regional organizations in the world, both in terms of economic development and geopolitical dynamics. promoting prosperity, cooperation and peace is a key political objective of eu-asean cooperation, and trade issues are the main focus of this relationship. i agree with the rapporteur that while trade and investment are very important, cooperation must also cover areas such as human rights and the environment. in other words, asean member states need to focus more on human, social, labour and economic rights.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to strengthen the eu's relations with asean. asean is the association of southeast asian nations, established by the 1967 bangkok agreement. it is a political, economic, and cultural union of countries that includes the philippines, indonesia, malaysia, singapore, thailand, brunei, vietnam, laos, myanmar, and cambodia. asean's purchasing power and economic size are already on par with china's, and it is therefore essential to develop stronger economic ties with it, combined with a more appropriate social, political and institutional framework. it is very important that the eu negotiates seven partnership and cooperation agreements between the eu and individual asean member states. the establishment of a formal european-asean parliamentary assembly would further improve relations between eu and asean member states. i support the proposals set out to encourage national and foreign companies operating in asean countries to adhere to the core labour standards of the ilo and the un basic principles on business and human rights. i also agree that the eu should provide more aid and cooperate more in the fight against corruption.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is a way of drawing attention to the need to strengthen relations between the eu and the association of southeast asian nations (asean), which are often unduly relegated to the background by the eu's relations with china, japan and india. this situation persists despite the fact that asean's purchasing power and economic size are already equal to china's purchasing power and economic size and despite the fact that asean's political influence is increasing. the eu and asean share many similarities, which open up many opportunities for interaction and mutual learning. the report examines how stronger economic links could be combined with a more appropriate social, political and institutional framework and relationships. it also sets out how bilateral relations between the regions could be improved. the evaluations and recommendations are presented in the context of the ongoing process of ambitious intra-asean integration, new political forces and the challenges of democracy.
in writing. - i voted in favour because the report seeks to give due weight to eu-asean relations, which are being pushed into the background by the eu's relations with china, japan and india. asean is the association of southeast asian nations, established by the 1967 bangkok treaty. it is a political, economic, and cultural union of these countries, including the philippines, indonesia, malaysia, singapore, thailand, brunei, vietnam, laos, myanmar, and cambodia. asean's purchasing power and economic size are already matching china's purchasing power and economy, and asean's political influence is growing. the eu is also looking at ways of combining stronger economic ties with a more appropriate social, political and institutional framework and opportunities for the eu to contribute more to the asean integration process. the commission has also received comments from the goc and the goc's representatives on the implementation of the wto agreement on trade-related aspects of intellectual property rights (trips) and the wto agreement on trade-related aspects of intellectual property rights (trips). i welcome the negotiation of seven partnership and cooperation agreements between the eu and individual asean member states. the parliamentary dimension of the relationship needs to be strengthened. the establishment of a formal european-asean parliamentary assembly would further improve relations between eu and asean member states. i think the eu should provide more aid and cooperate more in the fight against corruption.
in writing. − asean is an emerging international political and economic actor. the asean economic community aims to create an internal market of 600 million people by 2015, on par with other major world markets. the rapidly developing asean countries, which have been relatively successful in coping with the global economic crisis, face a variety of challenges, including the consequences of extreme natural disasters, high levels of air pollution, which can become an unconventional security problem. i believe that rapid economic growth must reach all segments of society, and that a strong focus on poverty reduction and improving the situation of the most vulnerable segments of society is necessary. as the eu and asean share common interests and a common political objective to promote prosperity, cooperation and peace in their respective regions and throughout the world, parliament calls for closer cooperation with the organisation and for a more comprehensive framework for political and economic cooperation with asean.
in writing. − i voted against the proposal to reject the council's position adopted at first reading. the council supports the objective of achieving better implementation of the social rules relating to road transport and reducing administrative burdens by making full use of new technologies and new regulatory instruments. tachographs are used in road transport to monitor compliance with driving and resting time rules in order to ensure road safety, appropriate working conditions for drivers and fair competition between transport undertakings. i support the proposals for the introduction of smart tachographs, which would help to reduce the administrative burden associated with the obligations imposed on transport undertakings using the tachograph. the smart tachograph will have two new functions: a remote communication function for control and a location data recording function. at the same time, it is necessary to provide for enhanced data protection and privacy guarantees.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the regulation replacing previously adopted eu legislation in the field of road safety. tachographs are used in road transport to monitor compliance with driving and resting time rules in order to ensure road safety, appropriate working conditions for drivers and fair competition between transport undertakings. the proposed regulation, which will replace the current regulation adopted in 1985, aims to make fraud more difficult, to improve the implementation of social rules and to reduce administrative burdens by making full use of new technologies and introducing certain new regulatory measures. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of electronic data-processing techniques, such as the use of electronic data-processing machines, and to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of electronic data-processing techniques.
in writing. − smart tachographs will help to improve the implementation of legislation and reduce administrative burden. therefore, in order to prevent fraud and to improve the implementation of social rules, it is necessary to ensure that the tachograph is effective and accessible to all interested parties. the free access to intellectual property rights should be open to all, and the control mechanism should be strengthened. in order to ensure road safety, appropriate working conditions for drivers and fair competition between transport undertakings, it is appropriate to extend the functionality of tachographs. i support the proposal to gradually introduce such innovative tachographs with the aim that, 15 years after the smart tachograph has been placed on the market, it should be installed in all vehicles. it should be noted that these tachographs will allow the vehicle's speed and position to be recorded using satellite navigation systems and will enable the real-time transmission of this data to control authorities so that they can control the vehicles from a distance. the new tachographs would also record the weight of the car by being linked to the sensors that record it.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. public procurement accounts for a very large proportion of economic activity in the eu. public procurement and concessions are the most common forms of public procurement. concessions are a very important area, since infrastructure projects are financed with private capital and state resources. there are very clear rules on public procurement. however, works concessions are subject only to certain public procurement provisions, while services concessions are subject only to the general principles of the treaty and to a large number of separate national laws, which creates legal uncertainty and complicated regulation of concessions. i agree that clear principles and rules on works and service concessions need to be established at eu level to help public authorities wishing to take advantage of additional means to develop and modernise public services. this would enhance competition within the eu. increasing the transparency of procedures would thus allow for a more effective fight against favouritism in the award of public contracts and for the purpose of ensuring an effective, consistent and pragmatic legal framework.
in writing. − i voted for this report because it is the first time that parliament has supported the regulation of concessions at eu level. public procurement accounts for a very large proportion of economic activity in the eu. public procurement and concessions are the most common forms of public procurement. public procurement is governed by very clear rules, whereas works concessions are governed by the main provisions of the existing directives on public procurement and service concessions by the general principles of the treaty and a wealth of case law. the advantage of the legislation on concessions is that it clarifies the current legal framework in relation to the ecj case-law and enhances legal stability and certainty in the light of legal differences in the eu regarding the non-application of uniform treaty principles. clear rules on works and service concessions will help public authorities wishing to have additional means to develop and modernise public services. this will enhance competition within the eu. finally, greater transparency in procedures will make it possible to combat favouritism in public procurement more effectively.
in writing. − i supported the proposal because a legislative initiative at eu level on concessions is necessary. the lack of legal certainty, the continuous conclusion of contracts under private law, the fundamental principles on which the eu internal market was founded, and the legal differences between the various member states in this area are reasons why a legal framework should be established at european level. however, this system must not be a substitute for a 'lighter' approach, which sets clear, consistent, understandable and effective rules.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the resolution on the amendment of the public procurement rules. it is important to note that public sector spending on goods, services and works accounts for around 19% of the eu's gross domestic product (gdp). i support this reform in the hope that public procurement tenders will be more transparent and that the administrative burden on small and medium-sized enterprises will be reduced. the current 'minimum price dictatorship' has been replaced by the 'minimum price dictatorship' under the public procurement reform, but the need for quality assurance has arisen. in other words, contracting authorities will have to take account not only of the price of the bid but also of the economic, environmental and social benefits. it is also important that the new procurement rules restrict the participation of companies that have violated labour laws. they also provide for the possibility for contracting entities to require suppliers to disclose subcontractors in advance. the directives also allow for the rejection of tenders from suppliers who have poorly performed previous contracts. i also voted in favour of this resolution because experts estimate that the implementation of the reform will reduce the administrative burden on companies participating in public procurement by up to 80%.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. public procurement in the eu represents a relatively large part of the economy, about 19% of the union's gdp, and it is therefore necessary to establish clear and transparent procurement principles at eu level and to maximise the potential of public procurement. the new version of the directive introduces certain innovations aimed at ensuring efficient and socially sustainable procurement procedures, such as the exclusion of any economic operator from participation in a procurement procedure who has not fulfilled the obligations under social, labour and environmental law laid down in national and eu legislation and in collective agreements concluded in compliance with union law. contracting authorities will not be able to award the contract to the highest bidder if the economic operator is unable to provide up-to-date information on the payment of social security contributions. the concept of the lowest price is removed from the directive and replaced by the concept of the most economically advantageous tender. this will allow the most economically advantageous tender to be identified, taking into account both the price and the tender best suited to the specific needs of the contracting authority. the new rules will also benefit smes, which will have more opportunities to participate in public procurement on a subcontracting basis, allowing them to become more active at eu level.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it supports the modernisation of the current public procurement directives. the aim of this legislation is to strike a balance between simplified public procurement rules and sound and efficient procedures based on innovation and sustainability criteria for the award of contracts, while ensuring greater participation by smes and procurement procedures conducted online. public procurement represents a relatively large part of the economy (around 19% of union gdp), and the successful revision and implementation of the rules on the organisation of public procurement will make a significant contribution to the recovery of investment in the real economy and to overcoming the crisis in europe.
in writing. − i supported the proposal as the reformed eu public procurement rules package will allow the public sector to choose not only the cheapest bid when purchasing goods or services, but to assess its overall economic, environmental and social viability. public authorities spend up to 19% of the eu's gross domestic product on public procurement, making it an important tool for achieving certain public objectives. the preliminary agreement reached with the eu council in july 2013 on three public procurement directives (the 'classical directive', the 'utilities directive' and the 'concessions directive') will allow public authorities to assess the environmental and social benefits and costs of the proposals. for example, a customer may want to ensure that a nursery or hospital is supplied with high-quality food and that the supplier employs people with disabilities. the new rules will also reduce administrative burdens, facilitate participation of small enterprises in public procurement and strengthen the rules on the examination of abnormally low-priced tenders and the transparency of subcontractors in order to ensure compliance with labour laws and collective agreements. in view of the importance of water as a public good, the concession directive will not apply to this sector. the new rules on international public procurement will aim to open up foreign procurement markets to eu companies, while giving foreign companies access to the eu's analogue market.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. i was the shadow rapporteur on this matter in the committee on industry, research and energy. the new version of the directive clarifies the basic definitions in the light of the relevant case law of the european court of justice, such as the concept of procurement, a public entity, etc., removes the traditional distinction between priority and non-priority services, and provides for the possibility of choosing the form of procedures, the use of an innovative procurement form in certain cases, and the use of a six-way procurement technique, which will allow contracting entities to carry out e-procurement more easily. one of the most important achievements in the modernisation of public procurement is, in my view, the promotion of electronic procurement. the introduction of mandatory electronic procurement systems will lead to considerable savings, improve procurement outcomes and provide all stakeholders, in particular smes, with much more transparent access to public procurement not only in their own member states but also in other member states. the proposal already provides for the mandatory transmission of notices in electronic form, the mandatory availability of procurement documents in electronic form and a transitional period of two years for member states to switch to fully electronic correspondence, in particular e-tendering, in all procurement procedures.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this document because it is necessary to modernise the public procurement directives, but this would require a balance between simplifying the rules on the one hand and fair and efficient procedures based on award criteria linked to innovation and sustainability on the other, as well as ensuring greater participation by smes and generalising public procurement online. efforts should be made to exploit the full potential of public procurement in the single market to promote sustainable growth, job creation and social inclusion. given that public procurement represents a significant part of the economy (around 19% of union gdp), the reform and successful implementation of procurement rules would make a significant contribution to the resumption of investment in the real economy and to overcoming the european economic crisis.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the resolution on access to the public procurement markets for goods and services. the eu's main external trade partners are the united states, canada, japan, india and china. until now, the eu public procurement market has been fully open to them. at the same time, europeans faced various artificial obstacles when they wanted to participate in their public procurement tenders. we encourage other countries to open their procurement markets to our business. the european commission will have the right to exclude from european competitions those countries which are acting unfairly towards the european union.
in writing. − i voted in favour of postponing the vote on this proposal in order to agree a tripartite agreement. this proposal aims to strengthen the eu's negotiating position on access for eu companies to third country procurement markets and to clarify the legal position of foreign tenderers, goods and services on the eu procurement market. this proposal establishes a comprehensive eu external procurement policy governing access to the eu procurement market for foreign goods and services and provides mechanisms to encourage eu trading partners to enter into negotiations on market access. eu law establishes the principle that goods and services subject to market access obligations are subject to the same conditions as eu goods and services in the eu internal procurement market. i support the proposals made to introduce a review clause obliging the commission to assess the impact of the regulation after it has been in force and applied for two years and to adjust it accordingly.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it supports the commission's proposal for a regulation restricting access to the eu's public procurement market to third countries which do not provide reciprocal access to their markets. this regulation also lays down procedures to support negotiations on access for union goods and services to third country procurement markets. the objective of the proposal is to encourage bilateral trade negotiations with third countries on the opening of public procurement markets. many third countries are reluctant to open their procurement markets to international competition or are not opening them more than they have done so far. as a result, eu operators are faced with restrictive practices in public procurement in many of the union's trading partners.
in writing. − the european union, as a free market participant, has created favourable conditions for foreign business entities to participate in public procurement. unfortunately, eu businesses do not always have equal access to public procurement in third countries because of the restrictions that apply, sometimes of a protectionist nature. for these reasons, business operators suffer considerable losses and market distortions occur. i believe that these amendments create a more favourable environment for negotiations with third countries, while at the same time ensuring more favourable conditions for trade. only by ensuring mutual commitments between the eu and third countries in the field of public procurement will free trade be promoted, creating a competitive market in third countries.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the motion to postpone the vote by referring the matter back to committee. the purpose of the proposal is to provide for rules to clarify the status of pollen in honey. the proposal aims to provide an accurate and correct description of pollen, to amend the rules applicable to honey and to bring the honey directive 2001/110/ec into line with the lisbon treaty. the eu produces 13% of the world's honey. for the purpose of this proposal, pollen is defined as a natural component rather than an added ingredient. the fact that the label of honey would indicate that the added ingredient is gm pollen would undoubtedly have a negative impact on the reputation of honey as a natural product. if pollen were to be referred to as an added ingredient, consumers might think that it is a separate product added to honey, when it is not, because pollen enters the hive through the activity of bees and therefore appears in honey without any intervention by beekeepers. i agree with that position.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it supports the parliament's proposed amendment to the eu honey directive. those amendments are necessary in order to adapt that directive to the new procedures provided for in the treaty of lisbon. also to ensure that the labelling of honey products clearly indicates that pollen in honey is an ingredient of honey and not an added ingredient. this is because honey is a natural substance produced by bees, and no nutritional ingredients can be added to it.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at protecting the financial interests of the european union. in the context of the global financial crisis, it is crucial to protect the european union's revenues and ensure that citizens can make the most of eu funds. the european commission adopted two proposals for the hercule iii and pericles 2020 programmes at the end of 2011. the programmes will be implemented for seven years from january 2014. hercule iii is designed to combat fraud, corruption and any other illegal activities detrimental to the eu's financial interests. the main focus will be on cooperation between the commission, acting through olaf, the competent authorities of the member states and other european institutions and bodies. this programme provides technical and operational support to member states' law enforcement authorities in the fight against illegal cross-border activities and in the field of vocational training. the pericles 2020 programme is an exchange, assistance and training programme to ensure the protection of euro banknotes and coins in europe and worldwide. it will also finance a seminar on 'a community strategy to protect the euro in the mediterranean', training courses on counterfeiting in latin america and staff exchanges between eu and non-eu public authorities.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this document because the protection of the european union's financial interests is of particular importance at this time. at the end of 2011, the commission adopted two proposals for the hercule iii and pericles 2020 programmes. the programmes will be implemented for seven years from january 2014. hercule iii is designed to combat fraud, corruption and any other illegal activities detrimental to the eu's financial interests. the main focus is on cooperation between the commission, acting through olaf, the competent authorities of the member states and other european institutions and bodies. the programme aims to ensure equivalent protection in the member states and in all eu institutions, bodies and agencies. the hercule iii programme provides technical and operational support to law enforcement authorities in the member states in their fight against illegal cross-border activities and vocational training. the previous hercule programme had significant results, including the implementation of 70 technical assistance projects, which financed the purchase of sophisticated technical equipment for law enforcement agencies to combat fraud, and anti-fraud training for over 5 300 law enforcement personnel. pericles 2020, meanwhile, is an exchange, assistance and training programme designed to ensure the protection of euro banknotes and coins in europe and worldwide.
in writing. − the amendments proposed by the s&ds concerning tax avoidance were not included, but as i would not like the whole proposal to be suspended, i voted to refer the proposal back to the committee on economic and monetary affairs for reconsideration. simplified customs clearance systems shall not collect statistical information at customs level on goods for which customs clearance is carried out. in order to ensure the quality of intra-eu trade statistics, this proposal aims to collect information on the transport of these goods through the intrastat system. moreover, the exchange of confidential data between the relevant authorities of the member states could benefit the quality of eu statistics.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to combat wildlife crime. wildlife crime is an international organized crime business with an annual turnover of 19 billion usd. it is the fourth most serious crime in the world, after drug trafficking, counterfeiting and human trafficking. i support the proposals put forward to the commission to draw up an eu action plan to combat wildlife crime and the illegal trade in wild fauna and flora, including clear targets. it is important to declare a moratorium on the import and export of ivory until poachers no longer pose a threat to wild elephant populations. it is also important that the eu prioritise the fight against wildlife crime in its development aid programming. i support calls for both the eu and the member states to support the international consortium on wildlife crime, which is made up of cites, interpol, the un office on drugs and crime, the world bank and the world customs organization.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it calls on the commission to draw up an eu action plan to combat wildlife crime and the illegal trade in wild fauna and flora without delay. wildlife crime, including poaching and illegal extraction, in consumer countries constitutes a major international organised crime business with an annual turnover of at least usd 19 billion, and is the fourth largest criminal activity in the world after drug trafficking, counterfeiting and human trafficking. these crimes not only threaten the welfare of individual animals and the conservation of the animal and plant species concerned, but also the illegal trade in wild fauna and flora is often linked to international organised crime networks and used as a source of income for such networks and militant rebel groups. unfortunately, the eu is the most important destination market for such illicit trade in the world. if demand for wildlife products remains high and law enforcement efforts are weak, legal trade will continue to be a cover for illicit trade and will encourage poaching, and i therefore fully support the need for a coordinated global response at the highest political level, the strengthening of law enforcement authorities and effective law enforcement and criminal justice systems.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because wildlife crime is a major international organised crime business with an annual turnover of 19 billion usd and is the fourth largest crime in the world after drug trafficking, counterfeiting and human trafficking. it is therefore necessary to establish an eu action plan to combat wildlife crime and the illegal trade in wild fauna and flora, including clear targets, and to declare moratoriums on the import, export, domestic trade and acquisition of ivory until poachers no longer pose a threat to wild elephant populations. in the programming of development assistance, financial instruments shall include the fight against wildlife crime as a priority. strong support is needed for the international consortium on wildlife crime, which comprises cites, interpol, the un office on drugs and crime, the world bank and the world customs organization, as well as financial resources and expertise.
in writing. − the conservation of biodiversity is one of the key objectives of eu environmental policy. i therefore support this document, which calls for stronger measures to combat poaching and illegal trade in protected or endangered species. i call not only on the eu institutions but also on the member states to review the legislation on liability for such crimes and to ensure that it is properly implemented.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. it should be noted that the eu is also a major market and transit route for illegal wildlife trade. the illegal trade in endangered species generates between eur 18 and 26 billion a year. it is therefore essential that the eu is subject to the eu action plan to combat wildlife crime and the illegal trade in wild fauna and flora. currently, high profitability and low fines for trafficking create favourable conditions for organised crime groups to commit crimes. in this context, it is necessary to use all existing relevant eu and domestic instruments to combat organised crime, corruption and money laundering. wildlife crime should be punishable by penalties commensurate with the seriousness of the crime. i believe that the fight against wildlife crime should be included as a priority in the programming of development finance. in addition, there is a need to strengthen international policies and legal systems, increase law enforcement capacity, meet intelligence technology needs and develop effective judicial systems to better combat wildlife crime at local, national and regional levels.
mr president, the issue we are now debating is difficult to understand and define in correct terms. the practice whereby european citizenship is sold and bought for money entirely compromises the whole eu and is one more blow against the union and more fuel for the eurosceptics. a union of values is what we used to say. we should stop kidding ourselves in such a situation, where citizenship of the union is offered to anybody from any non-democratic or anti-democratic country who has money for a bribe. what if, as some say, it is nothing to do with the responsibilities of the member state? i do not think so. we should at least kindly ask malta to denounce this step; to denounce such citizenship as a moral mistake of anti-european content and give the money received as a bribe back to such a client.

answer to the question posed by the blue card. i am glad that you live in the country you like to live in. my statement was made on 10 december and it is still valid. it is directed against the resolution of edith estrella, the resolution of december 10, on the interference of the state institutions in the education of children.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to strengthen eu industry by encouraging investment in new technologies, improving the business environment and access to markets and facilitating access to finance. the crisis had a major impact on the economies of european countries. it has caused 3.8 million people to lose their jobs in the industrial sector. the eu therefore needs a comprehensive growth strategy to meet these challenges. in 2012, the commission presented a communication on industrial empowerment, which proposes to create the right conditions to stimulate investment and focus on six priority areas: clean manufacturing technologies, markets for biological products, sustainable industrial policy, construction and raw materials, clean vehicles and smart electricity grids. improving the functioning of the internal market for goods, promoting entrepreneurship in the digital single market, protecting intellectual property rights and further promoting the internationalisation of eu smes worldwide are important. it also provides for an increase in the capacity of lending to the real economy through a more efficient mobilisation and allocation of public resources, including from the structural funds and the european investment bank. human capital and skills are also important. it is important to better anticipate the capabilities of human capital and to adapt them to market needs.
in writing. − i supported the proposal because of the need to strengthen eu industry by encouraging investment in new technologies, improving the business environment and access to markets and facilitating access to finance. the right conditions need to be created to encourage investment and to focus on six priority areas with huge potential for economic growth and job creation in europe: smart, clean manufacturing technologies, markets for biological products, sustainable industrial policy, construction and raw materials, clean transport including ships and smart electricity grids. the commission should focus on those actions where significant progress can be achieved quickly: improving the functioning of the internal market for goods, promoting entrepreneurship in the digital single market (which is expected to grow by 10% per year by 2016), protecting intellectual property rights and further promoting the internationalisation of eu smes worldwide. the capacity to lend to the real economy needs to be increased through a more efficient mobilisation and allocation of public resources, including from the structural funds and the european investment bank. in the future, it is important to better anticipate skills needs and matching so that workers can better adapt to industrial change.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. the eu needs a strategy to overcome the economic and financial crisis and to accelerate the new dynamism of the economy. european industry has a crucial role to play and is an important part of the solution to this crisis. the future of europe as an industrial area depends on a modernisation drive that will strengthen innovation centres and close the development gap in industrially and structurally weak regions. european industrial policy therefore needs a strong financial structure to stimulate investment. a more active anti-dumping policy should be pursued to prevent unfair export subsidies in certain third countries. moreover, a real exchange rate policy is needed to protect the eu's commercial interests. it is of particular importance that the social and solidarity-based economy, as well as the circular, recycling and processing economy, be developed. reasonable targets and precise timetables for their implementation should be set. the development of sustainable business models should in particular support smes and the traditional manufacturing sectors. the principle of fair trade, based on mutual respect for social, environmental and cultural norms and standards relating to respect for human rights, must be strictly respected throughout the eu in international trade.
in writing. − i have approved the proposal because the purpose of this measure is to compensate producers in the french overseas departments for the competitive disadvantages caused by their remoteness, isolation, small size, difficult topographical and climatic conditions and economic dependence on several products, in particular the value chains of sugar, cane and rum, since the stability and combination of these factors severely limit the development of these regions. the commission proposes to allow france to apply reduced rates of excise duty and gst for up to 50% of the corresponding standard rates for the period 2014-2020, but the total reduction in excise duty and gst should not exceed 50% of the total rate of duty for alcohol set in article 3 of directive 92/84/eec. this is intended to stimulate the economy of the outermost regions.
in writing. − i have agreed to the proposal as it proposes to recast the relevant articles of the rules of procedure in order to make their structure more consistent and their content more in line with recent case law and the practice of the committee responsible. the immunity of a member is not his personal privilege but a guarantee of the independence and immunity of parliament as a whole and of its members. the rules of procedure should therefore cover this matter without legal loopholes and in sufficient detail to protect the importance of the institutions directly representing union citizens.
in writing. − i have endorsed the proposal as it calls on the commission not to propose authorisations for any new gmo species or renewals of old authorisations until risk assessment methods have been significantly improved and calls on the council to reject this commission proposal. no gmo has been authorised for cultivation in the eu since 2010, when the potato variety 'amflora' was authorised. this authorisation was annulled by the general court of the european union on 13 december 2013. it is widely recognised that the long-term effects of gmo cultivation and the impact on non-target organisms have not been adequately addressed in risk assessments to date. the council and the european parliament recognise the need to strengthen the long-term impact assessment of gmos, to carry out an independent study of the potential risks associated with the deliberate release or placing on the market of gmos and to ensure that independent investigators have access to all relevant material. in the 2010 special report 354 of the eurobarometer, a majority of consumers are concerned about food produced from genetically modified organisms. i agree that genetically modified maize line 1507 does not provide any benefit to consumers.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. in my view, the free movement of persons is one of the fundamental and most important freedoms in the eu today, as more than 14 million eu citizens study, work and live in other countries. i see the free movement of workers as a positive social and economic phenomenon, as it ensures eu integration, economic development of countries, social cohesion, increased personal development in the professional field and more. respect for labour rights is particularly important to ensure a level playing field for all eu workers and fair competition between companies. the proposal to limit the number of eu migrants must be rejected as it is contrary to the principle of free movement of persons enshrined in the eu treaty. labour mobility is one of the factors driving the competitiveness of the eu economy.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal because the right to free movement is one of the four fundamental freedoms of the eu, enshrined in the treaty on the functioning of the european union as a cornerstone of european integration and as directly linked to eu citizenship. free movement is one of the eu's core values, as it gives its citizens the choice of where to live and work, and promotes mobility and development in the labour market, education and other areas. eu citizens see free movement as the right most closely linked to eu citizenship and as a positive achievement of the eu that brings economic benefits to their country's economy. the benefits of free movement of eu mobile workers in the host country's development are seen across europe, particularly in the fields of health, agriculture and construction. the free movement of workers is a positive social and economic example for the eu and its member states: it is a cornerstone of eu integration, economic development, social cohesion, personal professional development, tackling the negative effects of the economic crisis and of the union as a stronger economic power ready to face the challenges of global change. calls on the member states to respect the treaty provisions on free movement and the eu rules on free movement and to ensure that the principles of equality and the fundamental right to free movement are respected in all member states; member states must not discriminate against eu mobile workers by falsely linking the right to free movement for work purposes to alleged abuse of social protection systems.
in writing. − freedom of movement is a fundamental eu value on which the eu treaties are based. this is one of the most essential and attractive guarantees of eu citizenship. the parties should cooperate more closely with each other and with the european institutions to address emerging problems and effectively prevent abuses. at the same time, however, politicians and governments must refrain from linking generalised and irregular labour migration to the abuse of social benefits systems, anti-immigration rhetoric, and the shifting of responsibility for the economic hardships of countries onto the shoulders of immigrants, who undoubtedly contribute to the host country's economy. populism based on the problems of this area is particularly dangerous because, among other things, it undermines the mutual respect and solidarity of people united by eu citizenship.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. i am very concerned about the programmes in malta and in some countries of the european union which allow third-country nationals, directly or indirectly, to buy their citizenship and at the same time their eu citizenship. the maltese parliament is seeking to sell citizenship for €650,000, thus issuing a passport. in other words, foreign nationals will have the opportunity to purchase a maltese passport under a special 'investor programme' and under specific conditions which do not require permanent residence. under the eu treaties, residence and citizenship are matters of exclusive competence of the member states, and i therefore expect each eu member state to respect and seek to preserve the common values and achievements of the eu. these values, one of which is citizenship, are invaluable and cannot be priced. eu citizenship entails not only rights but also obligations. i also hope that member states will act wisely in their competence in this area and take into account possible undesirable side effects. the european commission, for its part, should clearly assess whether these programmes comply with the provisions of the eu treaty, the schengen and non-discrimination rules.
in writing. − on 12 november 2013, the maltese parliament adopted amendments to the maltese citizenship act which will allow maltese and therefore eu citizenship to be sold to third-country nationals for €650,000. the amendments provide that the names of third-country nationals acquiring citizenship will not be published and that they will not be required to live, work or invest in malta or to enter malta or any other member state. several other member states offer citizenship by investment, unlike malta, where money is paid directly to the government. the maltese government will receive approximately eur 30 million for the sale of 45 citizenship in the first year. it is estimated that between 200 and 300 applications for citizenship will be received each year. malta is a member of the eu and the schengen area. eu citizens have the right to move freely within the community. in addition, there are no border controls in the schengen area. the opposition has strongly criticised these plans, complaining that candidates do not have to live and invest in malta. the nationalist party is therefore afraid that malta will soon be compared to a tax haven in the caribbean. it does not rule out the possibility of holding a referendum against the sale of citizenship. i voted in favour of the obligation to clearly indicate whether these citizenship sales schemes are in conformity with the eu treaties. the rights granted by eu citizenship are based on human dignity and should not be bought or sold, and money should not be the main criterion for granting eu citizenship to third-country nationals.
in writing. − it is important that serbia is moving towards normalisation of relations with kosovo, which has declared independence. progress on this one of the most important issues is visible and welcome. the implementation of the agreements reached, the corresponding changes and approaches, and in the legal area, such as the constitution, which still declares kosovo to be an integral part of serbia, are now important. we have long been concerned about the business environment and the protection of foreign investment. i trust that the problems that arise will be properly addressed. there is still much work to be done in many other areas, including the fight against corruption and economic strengthening. the eu's accession partners need not simply transpose the acquis into national law, but often the challenge is much greater in terms of its proper implementation and capacity building. i agree that the accession negotiations are a very strong and comprehensive monitoring instrument. at the same time, it is necessary to involve and inform both parliament and the widest possible public in a timely and appropriate manner. i want to highlight the aspects of justice and historical memory as essential to further democratization and reconciliation. the research and analysis of the archives of the special services of the socialist republic of yugoslavia must be facilitated and encouraged, ensuring free access to these archives and, where necessary, their sharing with interested parties.
in writing. − the european parliament supports the stabilisation and association negotiations with the republic of kosovo. however, not all eu countries have established diplomatic relations with the country. for kosovo, the rapprochement with the eu is one of the strongest drivers of normalisation, internal reforms and political and economic consolidation in relations with its neighbours.
in writing. − iceland fulfils the copenhagen criteria and accession negotiations with iceland were opened on 27 july 2010, following the council's approval. negotiations were opened on 27 chapters, and 11 of them were provisionally closed. the newly elected icelandic government has suspended the eu accession process and an assessment of the accession negotiations and developments in the european union is currently being prepared in the light of the discussions in the icelandic parliament (altinge) on these issues. the icelandic government has stated that accession negotiations will only resume after a referendum has been held. i support the prospect of a new member state with a long-standing tradition of democracy and strong institutions and civil society. iceland has a good overall capacity to assume the obligations of membership, in particular as a result of its participation in the european economic area. the european union remains fully committed to continuing and completing the accession negotiation process. the latest opinion polls show that a majority of icelanders want to conclude the accession negotiations. i hope that a referendum will be held soon.
in writing. − the newly elected icelandic government has suspended the eu accession process and announced its intention to hold a referendum on eu membership. the european union, for its part, remains fully committed to continuing and completing the accession negotiation process. iceland is undoubtedly an exceptional candidate, with a deep democratic tradition and strong institutions and civil society, as well as a long-standing involvement in the european economic area, with good overall capacity and capacity to assume the obligations of membership.
in writing. - i support the resolution expressing grave concern at the outbreak of violence in south sudan. this nascent state, which declared independence in 2011, faces enormous challenges due to its high illiteracy rate, high levels of poverty, rampant corruption, difficult relations with its neighbours and decades of conflict. the situation is already very fragile, and the recent resurgence of conflict between two political actors, each controlling certain military elements, is hampering peace negotiations, fuelling ethnic discord, further atrocities and gross human rights violations. we must have an immediate ceasefire and an agreement. strengthening the young state, fulfilling the aspirations of its people and peace must be the top priority.
in writing. − i supported the proposal because all human beings are born free and equal in dignity and rights and because it is the responsibility of the member states to support and ensure the realisation of these rights. homelessness is a violation of human dignity and human rights. housing is a basic human need and a prerequisite for a decent life and social inclusion. under the europe 2020 strategy and its flagship initiative, the european roadmap against poverty and social exclusion, as well as the eu social investment packet, unemployment has become a priority for the eu's poverty reduction policy. however, the current levels of poverty and social exclusion risk undermining the europe 2020 target of reducing the number of people living in poverty and social exclusion or at risk of poverty and social exclusion by at least 20 million. unemployment is one of the most extreme forms of poverty and deprivation and has increased in recent years in almost all member states. i agree that there should be an eu homelessness strategy or something like that. the eu homelessness strategy should be implemented in full compliance with the treaty, which affirms 'a significant role for national, regional and local authorities and a wide margin of manoeuvre in the provision, commissioning or organisation of services of general economic interest that are as responsive as possible to the needs of consumers'. the eu homelessness strategy should help member states to assume this responsibility as effectively as possible, in full respect of the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. i think that we should have an eu strategy on homelessness as soon as possible. this strategy should help member states to take responsibility for tackling homelessness in full respect of the principle of subsidiarity. it should be noted that homelessness should be integrated into all relevant eu policies. particular attention should be paid to youth, cross-border homelessness and the prevention of homelessness. the european homelessness field needs to be monitored regularly. in addition, the eu should promote social innovation in homelessness policies and services, and research and cooperation are being developed to enhance mutual learning and exchange of best practice and to develop a common policy approach. it is essential that member states organise regular round tables in this area. it should be noted that effective prevention policies should reduce evictions and effectively combat all forms of discrimination against homeless people and the exclusion of entire communities. i believe that the use of the fund for european aid to the most deprived and other programmes should be further encouraged to improve the situation of homeless people and to develop a plan for social inclusion and integration into the labour market.
