in writing. one of the eu's founding principles subsidiarity. however, ms lunacek's report is in fundamental breach of the principle of the division of competences between the eu and the national states. questions such as life, marriage and the family are the exclusive competence of the national states. however, the report contains provisions which require member states to recognise the civil status of different marriages, to adapt to changing family patterns and to interpret reproductive rights. all these and similar provisions presuppose special conditions and privileges for one group of people. although the new text proposed by the various political groups is much more moderate, it still contains provisions which allow the member states to impose certain provisions. no one is questioning the fundamental human rights enshrined in many documents, but we must leave the member states to decide on the issues of value.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council amending council regulation (ec) no 2368/2002 as regards greenland's participation in the kimberley process certification scheme, as it is closely linked to the proposal for a council decision laying down the rules and procedures for greenland's participation in the kimberley process certification scheme. both proposals seek to allow greenland to participate in the kimberley process certification scheme (kpcs) for rough diamonds in cooperation with the european union. the scheme proposed would allow the import and export of rough diamonds from greenland to and from the union and to other countries participating in the certification scheme only if all imports and exports of rough diamonds are subject to verification by the union authorities and, in the case of exports, to the issue of a certificate in accordance with the requirements of regulation (ec) no 2368/2002 implementing the kimberley process certification scheme for international trade in rough diamonds. this is an important, innovative measure to stop the flow of conflict diamonds (crude diamonds) used by rebel movements to finance wars with legitimate authorities. the european union is actively involved in this process and seeks to play an even more effective role.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal for a council decision laying down the rules and procedures for the participation of greenland in the kimberley process certification scheme, as it is closely linked to the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council amending council regulation (ec) no 2368/2002 as regards greenland's participation in the kimberley process certification scheme. both proposals seek to allow greenland to participate in the kimberley process certification scheme (kpcs) for rough diamonds in cooperation with the european union. the scheme proposed would allow the import and export of rough diamonds from greenland to and from the union and to other countries participating in the certification scheme only if all imports and exports of rough diamonds are subject to verification by the union authorities and, in the case of exports, to the issue of a certificate in accordance with the requirements of regulation (ec) no 2368/2002 implementing the kimberley process certification scheme for international trade in rough diamonds. this is an important, innovative measure to stop the flow of conflict diamonds (crude diamonds) used by rebel movements to finance wars with legitimate authorities. the european union is actively involved in this process and seeks to play an even more effective role.
in writing. - i have supported the proposal to move from national credit transfer and direct debit schemes to harmonised sepa credit transfer (hereinafter referred to as 'sct') and sepa direct debit (hereinafter referred to as 'sdd') schemes, inter alia by providing union citizens with a unique international bank account number (iban) which can be used for all euro sct and sdd transactions. the regulation provided for the transition to sepa by 1 february 2014, but a large group of member states lags far behind the planned transition rates. therefore, it is unlikely that all market participants will be in compliance with the sepa rules by 1 february 2014. a transitional period should therefore be provided for to allow for the continued parallelisation of the different payment formats. the only additional transitional period of six months is considered appropriate. during the transitional period, member states should not impose penalties on payment service providers who execute non-compliant payments and on payment service users who have not yet switched to sepa.
in writing. - i voted for this motion. it should be noted that the regulation setting technical and commercial requirements for credit transfers and direct debit operations in euro is an important element in the implementation of the single euro payments area (sepa). however, the deadline it sets for national and intra-european credit transfers and direct debits in euro to become sepa credit transfers and sepa direct debits is questionable. in some member states, the transition to this system is too slow. furthermore, until the full transition to sepa is completed, there is a risk of payment incidents leading to late payments or market disruptions. in view of this legal problem and the possible consequences for citizens and businesses, i consider it appropriate to extend the sepa transition period by at least six months.
in writing. - i voted for this report. the number of new infringement procedures has decreased in recent years, which is to be welcomed. however, there is cause for concern that in the last few years there has been an increase in cases of late transposition into national law, in particular in the areas of fundamental rights, the environment, the internal market and property rights in the areas of the environment, the internal market, transport and taxation. i think the commission should do more to ensure that petitions to parliament and complaints to the commission are treated with the same care. parliament shall have the right to receive detailed information on specific acts or provisions which raise problems of transposition into national law, as well as on the number of complaints against specific acts or provisions. particular attention should be paid to the 'eu pilot' system. it is a platform for bilateral discussions between the commission and the member states. it is necessary to strengthen its implementation in order to ensure transparency for complainants, to provide citizens with more information about the system.
in writing. - immunity is a special guarantee and privilege. this guarantee ensures the uninterrupted execution of the mandate and, at the same time, the effective functioning of the institutions. a member must be protected against unjustified persecution, while ensuring the continuity of his or her work and that of parliament, their autonomy and independence. the waiver of immunity is a highly sensitive issue, as a member of parliament is often the target of political intrigue, rumours, settling of accounts and, ultimately, potential unfounded criminal charges. but on the other hand, it's not a personal privilege. in each case, the rule of law and the separation of powers must be respected in the decision-making process.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because it is of great benefit to eu citizens. it removes bureaucratic obstacles and implements the principle of mutual trust. it reduces taxes for the benefit of citizens and companies, while at the same time creating a system of administrative assistance that can better protect against fraud. this proposal does not in any way harmonise the fundamental law of the member states in the relevant areas. no indication that a member state is obliged to recognise a certificate issued in another member state. this is the abolition of additional crossborder formalities. the formalities for legalising and apostillating documents should be abolished. in case of doubt, the authorities may either request administrative assistance or require the original or certified copy of the document to be produced. in this case, a copy certified in another member state must also be accepted. the exemption applies to entries in registers for which the state is financially responsible for the accuracy. for certified translations: it is proposed to accept jointly uncertified translations. in case of doubt, an official or certified translation may be required. a copy certified in one member state must be valid in all other member states. multilingual standard eu forms ten additional multilingual eu forms are being developed to cover as widely as possible all problem cases.
in writing. - one of the eu's characteristics is the free movement of persons, which is essential to ensure the proper functioning of the eu. however, the field still faces a variety of administrative formalities that make it difficult for eu citizens to benefit from the opportunities offered by the free movement of persons, such as the requirement for certified translations or copies, the non-recognition of certified translations carried out in another eu country, etc. the proposals put forward simplify the administrative burden for eu citizens in relation to the recognition of public documents, and i therefore support this report. i believe that the proposal to use multilingual forms when justifying legal facts (date of birth, divorce, etc.) would simplify administrative procedures and reduce the discrepancies between different eu countries, while at the same time facilitating the use of the opportunities offered by the free movement of persons.
in writing. - i voted for this report. we need to do more to promote the free movement of citizens and businesses. the proposal to simplify the recognition of certain public documents throughout the eu is welcome. it is worth noting that the new order will make life and freedom of movement easier for millions of europeans living outside their home countries and for thousands of businesses. it should be noted that the current eu regime is flawed and creates an unnecessary bureaucratic burden. both natural and legal persons are experiencing enormous difficulties, wasting time and incurring financial losses. in this context, i believe it is appropriate to legalise simplified document recognition throughout the eu. it is to be welcomed that the proposed amendment to the regulation also provides for a safeguard mechanism to help prevent possible counterfeiting. i agree with the commission's proposal that if doubts arise about the authenticity of a document, the internal market information system should be used to clarify the matter. in addition, common multilingual key document formats should be used to simplify the recognition of public documents.
in writing. - i voted for this proposed rule change. the introduction of new regulatory measures is necessary to ensure better protection of workers' health and safety. however, the new provisions should not restrict the potential for the creation and development of smes and their job creation. good health and safety standards should not be seen as constraints. these are fundamental rights and must apply without exception to all sectors of the labour market and to all types of enterprises, irrespective of their size. it is essential to maintain a high level of protection in the eu. in the light of technological progress, existing provisions should be kept under review and adapted to changing conditions, while ensuring the necessary level of health and safety protection in the case of exposure to hazardous substances and mixtures in the working environment. particular attention should be paid to workers in certain professions who frequently work in environments where dangerous chemicals and mixtures are present. it is essential that the commission properly and timely implement the functions entrusted to it and develop interpretative guidelines, in particular for smes.
in writing. - intellectual property and its opportunities are considered to be an under-exploited area in the eu. the management of collective copyright and related rights covered by the communication is often subject to a well-earned criticism for its lack of efficiency and transparency. i therefore support the gallo report, which proposes greater transparency, better management and implementation (by reducing the cost of procedures, simplifying the licensing process in some cases, etc.). i support the proposal to create a new instrument to ensure a better functioning of the online music sector in europe, including the provision of multi-territorial licences for the use of musical works online. i believe it is important that the eu becomes a leader in collective rights management, that the administrative burden on users and rights holders is reduced and that a mechanism is put in place to ensure that artists' rights are properly protected.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in particular, the new copyright rules for musical works will make it easier for businesses selling music online to obtain a licence to use these works in several eu countries at once. this will encourage a wider availability of music works online and ensure better protection of copyright. it is to be welcomed that the european parliament has succeeded in ensuring that the requested organisation is obliged to administer the repertoire represented by the applicant organisation under the same conditions as it administers its own repertoire. this will allow rightholders to freely choose the organisation that best represents their rights. all organisations will have to ensure that, in the exercise of their functions, authors receive adequate remuneration for the use of their works in a timely manner. in this context, it is essential that these organisations are subject to appropriate requirements for transparency, accountability, governance and transparent use of revenues. high standards of governance, transparency and reporting must be established across the eu.
in writing. - i voted in favour of a report on rules to simplify the licensing of music and to ensure better protection of the interests of rights holders. this will facilitate the online distribution of music across the european union, and allow creators to participate directly in the management of their rights and to receive payment more quickly. an independent study has shown that, if the copyright tax system were to be solved, the digital single market would bring an additional €500 billion or 4% of gdp to europe. the eu online music market is currently fragmented due to a complex and uncoordinated regulatory framework, which is hampering the entry of new providers. it is therefore proposed to simplify the provision of multi-territorial licences for the use of musical works online. it is important that the new arrangements benefit music composers and lyricists. their royalties will be collected more fairly and distributed more equitably.
in writing. - i voted in favour, as the aim is to establish criminal sanctions for the most serious market abuse offences in the european union. the current sanctions against market abuse are not effective and sufficiently dissuasive, and therefore the directive is not implemented effectively. the offences of insider dealing or market manipulation which constitute criminal offences vary considerably between member states. in this crucial area, the commission has for the first time used the powers conferred by the treaty of lisbon to harmonise criminal law provisions in order to implement harmonisation measures effectively.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in order to prevent market manipulation offences, the same criminal sanctions should apply throughout the eu. this will restore confidence in financial markets and protect investors. it should be noted that the lack of a union-wide criminal sanction regime currently allows market abusers to benefit from leniency in some member states and avoid liability. it is therefore necessary to provide for more severe penalties, including imprisonment, for such serious offences. i therefore support the proposal to harmonise maximum sentencing periods for these offences at eu level. furthermore, the introduction of criminal sanctions for market abuse offences will act as a deterrent for potential offenders. it is important to note that market manipulation can cause serious harm to the lives of millions of people. the current problems and gaps have a major impact on market confidence and can cause significant losses for investors and distort the real economy.
in writing. - i voted for this proposal because it aims to make the relationship between demand and supply more transparent. member states are required to report to the commission every two years data and information on investment projects relating to the construction, modernisation or decommissioning of oil, natural gas, electricity production and transmission and storage capacities. this information shall include oil, natural gas, electricity, biofuels and carbon dioxide emissions from the use of these resources. under the regulation, energy companies are required to provide their member state with the necessary data. the commission would be kept accurately and regularly informed of investment projects in eu energy infrastructure in order to fulfil its energy tasks. it will carry out every two years a study of the evolution and prospects of the structure of the eu energy system in the various sectors, as well as any other special studies required. this will allow identifying possible future supply and demand gaps and barriers to investment.
in writing. - i agree. this regulation provides that member states agree to provide the commission with information on their investments in energy infrastructure every two years in order to achieve the objectives of the eu energy policy and the establishment of the eu internal energy market. the ep also stresses that the information should be provided on infrastructure investments involving third countries. the information procedure established and the commission's commitment to provide an inquiry will help to ensure that funds are invested in projects that meet priority objectives, to assess the real situation, to identify potential obstacles to investment, to promote transparency for market participants, to better balance supply and demand, and to avoid production and transmission infrastructure shortfalls in the future.
in writing. - i voted for this report. i think it is appropriate to receive information from the member states on a systematic basis on certain types of investment projects relating to the creation, modernisation or decommissioning of generation, transmission and storage capacities. this will make the forecasted demand and the current supply more transparent. i do not think that the new requirements will impose an additional administrative or financial burden on energy companies. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of personal data. it is also appropriate to extend the scope of this regulation to include coal as an important element in the energy mix with implications for eu energy policy. it is also essential to provide information not only on projects within the eu but also on infrastructure investments in third countries that have an impact on the european energy market. it is worth noting that the production of energy by smaller entities will become an important part of the eu's energy production, and it is therefore appropriate to reduce the threshold related to the capacity of onshore wind power plants.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal because the legislation proposed and adopted at european union level should be simple, effective and efficient, should provide clear added value, should be easily understandable and accessible in all the official languages of the member states, and should provide full benefits at the lowest cost. the eu institutions must respect the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality when adopting legislation. better legislation the principle thinking of the little ones first should be the most important part of the whole process.
in writing. - the eu is working to reduce administrative burdens and increase the involvement of eu citizens in decision-making, but the eu is still criticised for its lack of democracy. there are several reasons for this. one of them is the insufficiently flexible application of the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality, which are inherent in the area of shared competence and involve national parliaments in decision-making. secondly, the time limits for national parliaments to submit comments and proposals are often too short, leading to criticism that the eu institutions do not hear the voice of the member states. that is why i voted in favour of this report, which makes proposals and suggests taking measures such as the 2003 interinstitutional agreement on better law-making, because i believe that this will facilitate legislative procedures in the area of shared competence, make them clearer to eu citizens and, at the same time, contribute to the spread of democracy in the eu.
in writing. - i voted against this document which aggressively attempts to impose the ideological dictates of homosexuals on the citizens of european countries. i think we cannot allow this to be imposed on all citizens of european countries. the report calls for the granting of special rights to homosexuals and people with gender identity problems, giving them privileges over all other citizens of european countries. i cannot agree with the substance of the provisions of this report, and the assertion that 'member states which have adopted legislation on de facto cohabitation, registered partnership or marriage between same-sex couples should recognise similar provisions adopted by other member states' is an unacceptable ideological imposition. i think that this aspect of relations between member states is unacceptable. the requirements of this document are certainly not going to help protect the rights of all eu citizens, and its content is inexcusably contrary to the principle of equality. we must respect the rights of all citizens, not just those of a select group.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the document because consumption is one of the main drivers of growth in the european union, consumers have the greatest influence on the eu economy and consumer protection and their rights are one of the eu's core values. i agree that the aim must be to ensure a high level of consumer protection throughout the european union and to strengthen consumer confidence in the single market, while guaranteeing a high level of legal certainty for businesses and reducing barriers to cross-border trade. the development of the digital economy and technological devices has fundamentally changed the way companies buy and advertise and sell their goods and services. some businesses, especially smaller ones, and many consumers are still not sufficiently aware of their rights in europe. the role of consumer associations needs to be strengthened and they need to be given the opportunity to strengthen their capacity. i believe that the objective must be to ensure that all economic operators in the union, in particular consumers and smes, are adequately protected against fraudulent or dishonest practices, thereby enhancing confidence in the single market.
in writing. - i support the speaker's position. the independence of the judiciary is considered an integral and necessary feature of democracy, but at the same time we must strive to ensure that the judiciary not only remains independent but also functions effectively and with quality, and that the judiciary is stable and predictable, as this is the only way for citizens and businesses to have confidence in the institutions of the judiciary. in order to achieve these objectives and to ensure the proper application of eu law, it is necessary to ensure common standards and effective judicial remedies. i also believe that it is important to promote alternative dispute resolution mechanisms which would ensure faster resolution of disputes and partly reduce the burden on the courts.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal because, with rising energy prices, climate change and new social and economic challenges, all eu regions will have to find ways to ensure a stable and secure supply of energy that is affordable for all citizens, while respecting environmental and sustainability criteria. in addition to ensuring security of electricity supply and reducing dependence on fossil fuels, advanced networks provide a range of other benefits to local communities, such as increased employment and the creation of green jobs. the renewable energy sector has weathered the current economic crisis and can continue to create jobs in regions where they are most needed, such as in coastal and rural areas. the success of leading regions and communities is often determined by openness and transparency on the part of local authorities, businesses, industry, local communities and citizens. member states and local and regional authorities are therefore encouraged to invest in smart local networks as early as possible.
in writing. - the development of smart grids is essential not only for the development and promotion of renewable energy sources across the eu, but also at local and regional levels. good practice shows that energy independence of individual settlements is possible through the use of smart grids. on the other hand, an individual consumer, by being connected to such a network, can freely switch from being a consumer to being an electricity producer and reduce his electricity bills. that is why i support the efforts to develop smart grids not only at the macro level but also at the micro level.
in writing. - the european union must continue to expand and make better use of existing renewable energy sources in order to become independent of foreign energy suppliers. however, in many regions, the most feasible indicators have already been achieved and we are approaching the limit of the capacity of the existing infrastructure to incorporate green energy into the electricity transmission grids. i voted for this report because it shows the way forward for eu countries and regions to tackle this problem. the report shows how eu regions can become energy exporters rather than energy importers. this will boost the regional economy and create new jobs. i agree that the regions should make the most of eu funds to modernise existing electricity networks. the development and improvement of advanced networks is an inevitable next step. but this process cannot be done from the top down, it must be discussed in depth and coordinated with local communities so that all the people in the regions feel the real benefits of change.
in writing. - i supported the proposal because small farms are not only productive but also provide for the livelihoods of millions of people in europe, prevent poverty, and preserve traditions and customs, but they are given little attention in the eu's common agricultural policy. member states and the european commission are therefore invited to take into account the specific needs of small, family-run farms, which are an important part of the european agricultural model and the basis for multifunctional rural development: to strengthen the competitiveness of small farms, to create jobs and to slow down the process of rural depopulation. measures to halt the process of rural depopulation and depopulation are urgently needed to create the right conditions and long-term prospects for small farms in their current location. whether this will be achieved will depend not only on the documents adopted in brussels, on national legislation, but also on concrete actions, which i believe must be taken immediately here in lithuania.
in writing. - support for small-scale agricultural producers is a particularly important part of support for rural areas. however, measures must be taken not only in the context of the distribution of eu support, but above all at national level. member states shall, by means of their laws, regulations and administrative provisions, facilitate the activities of small farmers in the growing, production and marketing of their products. protection against unfair competition by large market players must also be ensured.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because the eu is the second largest steel producer in the world, producing over 177 million tonnes of steel a year, or 11% of world steel production. trade policy can play an important role in promoting the development of the steel industry. maintaining a competitive steel production in europe is of strategic importance for european economic growth and job creation. over the last few years, more than 65 000 jobs have been lost in the european steel industry as a result of plant closures. the recovery of domestic demand is unlikely to recover in the near future and the share of european companies in the global market is declining. the survival of the european steel industry is threatened by a decline in competitiveness. it is therefore necessary to support the strategic development of key steel-using sectors by improving investment conditions (including in research and innovation and skills development), by creating incentives for efficient and fair production processes (e.g. through standardisation and public procurement policies), by strengthening the internal market and by pursuing european infrastructure development projects. as steel will continue to be a key material in european industrial value chains, it is proposed to increase its demand in the key steel-using sectors, including new industries with high growth potential. better market monitoring, measures to reduce the social impact of industrial restructuring, including the use of eu funds.
first of all, i would like to thank mr fjellner, the author of this important report, for his cooperation and efforts to reach a compromise acceptable to the majority. as the lithuanian representative, i note that lithuanian producers strongly support the european commission's legal initiative to modernise the community's trade defence instruments. i am fully convinced that active and open trade requires effective measures to combat unfair trade practices, harmful dumping or subsidising practices. i will note that the main causes of unfair trade are artificially distorted prices of raw materials and energy resources. in this context, the removal of the advantage of the lesser duty rule is of particular importance. this is also the case for lithuanian producers in the chemical, fertiliser, plastics and other sectors, which suffer from, for example, russian export duties on raw materials or double pricing of gas. the shortening of the duration of anti-dumping and anti-subsidy investigations is also important. it is clear that unfair trading prevents all market participants from being able to ensure a level playing field and thus directly affects the competitiveness of european union enterprises. i fully support the inta committee's position on this matter, as expressed in the vote of 21 january, and i urge my colleague to reject all the new amendments.
in writing. mr president, the regulation of the european parliament and of the council establishing a community control system to ensure compliance with the rules of the common fisheries policy is a good step towards better protection of this economic sector, which is of particular importance to the baltic sea countries such as lithuania. unfair competition, which has wiped out many small and medium-sized enterprises, is a persistent problem in the new community countries. and this could very quickly become a global problem, because today, almost 12% of the world's population lives off of fishing, processing and trade. this number is a call to action. we must do everything we can to keep our jobs. we need to have thoughtful fishing regulations. this will improve the management of this sector within the european union. we cannot focus on the european union's territorial seas alone, because the restrictions will have an even greater impact on european fishermen. this needs to be discussed internationally as soon as possible. it is not small boats in the baltic sea that are a threat to fish, but giant trawlers. their activities should be restricted rather than dismantled, and the family fishing business should be strengthened and developed. they are a guarantee of rational fishing decisions. the elimination of these in the new member states, even at eu money, benefits only the big companies. it's an attempt to eliminate competition in this market. therefore, a good plan for the future will be to support small-scale fishing rather than to eliminate it.
in writing. it is welcome that lithuania, during its presidency, has concluded negotiations and reached agreement on a directive laying down the conditions for the entry and residence of third-country nationals for the purpose of seasonal work in the european union. this proposal for a directive sets out the conditions under which seasonal workers would be granted the right to reside and work for a maximum of six months in any calendar year and also defines the rights of seasonal workers. the directive should apply to the agricultural, horticultural and tourism sectors, as the activities related to these sectors are seasonal and increase the demand for labour during the season. this directive aims to prevent the exploitation of seasonal workers, by reducing their remuneration and working conditions. the directive also aims to prevent illegal immigration and illegal work in the european union. the european parliament proposes to include information for seasonal workers in the directive to reduce the risk of exploitation. it is proposed that prospective seasonal workers and employers should be provided with information to enable them to make a decision on whether to become seasonal workers or to hire seasonal workers. the european parliament also proposes to include in the proposal monitoring and verification systems in the member states to ensure that the rights of seasonal workers are fully respected and that the provisions of this directive are enforced.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish a common procedure for the entry and residence of seasonal workers from third countries in the european union and the rights of such workers. about 100 000 seasonal workers arrive in the eu each year. the proposal aims to prevent the exploitation of these workers and the conversion of short-term work into permanent employment. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the new rules and will continue to monitor the implementation of the new rules. member states will be able to take them on for between 5 and 9 months, during which they will be able to renew their employment contract or change employers at least once. i agree that each member state should have the right to decide on the number of seasonal workers it will take on. i agree with the proposals that the application for seasonal work in the eu should be accompanied by an employment contract specifying the salary and working hours. it is also important that the application be accompanied by appropriate documentation proving the accommodation conditions. it is important that seasonal workers have the same rights as eu citizens in areas such as minimum working age, pay, dismissal, working hours, holidays and health and safety requirements at work.
in writing. - over 100 000 seasonal workers from third countries arrive in the european union each year. i voted in favour of rules that will make their working and living conditions easier. for the first time, seasonal workers will be able to benefit from the right to adequate accommodation, as well as the right to extend their stay and change employers. in addition, provisions on non-discrimination, protection against dismissal and labour inspection have been strengthened. this would help to ensure that third-country seasonal workers are not exploited. although many people in europe are out of work, certain areas of the eu need workers from third countries. we therefore need to ensure that this process is carried out in a wise and appropriate manner. if these standards are not met, we will face social dumping and exploitation of workers. then dishonest employers will likely resort to illegal employment, leading to increased illegal immigration and human trafficking. so while these rules may not solve all problems overnight, they are an important step in the right direction.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to repeal regulation (ec) no 827/2004 prohibiting the importation of bigeye tuna originating in bolivia, cambodia, equatorial guinea, georgia and sierra leone. iccat (an international organisation of which the european union is a contracting party) has decided to lift the ban on imports of atlantic bigeye tuna and products thereof from bolivia and georgia. in accordance with its international obligations, the eu should transpose that decision into union law and consequently repeal that regulation.
in writing. - i voted in favour of repealing regulation (ec) no 827/2004 banning the import of bigeye tuna originating in bolivia, cambodia, equatorial guinea, georgia and sierra leone. the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas (iccat) has decided to lift the ban on imports of atlantic bigeye tuna and products thereof from bolivia and georgia. in accordance with its international obligations, the eu should transpose that decision into union law and consequently repeal that regulation.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at the renewal of the protocol to the fisheries partnership agreement between the european community and the gabonese republic. as a result of the lack of updating of the protocol, eu fishing vessels have not been active since 2011. the eu will pay gaon eur 1 350 000 per year for fishing rights: eur 900 000 for access to fishery resources and eur 450 000 for the development of gabon's fisheries policy. i agree with the suggestions that the european union should make greater efforts to check that the principles necessary for the use of aid are respected. it is important to improve the regular monitoring of catches by european union vessels in the gabonese fishing zone and to collect up-to-date and more accurate scientific data on fish stocks and total fishing effort in gabonese waters.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish cooperation between the european union and greenland and denmark for the period 2014-2020. it will replace the agreement covering the period 2006-2013. the agreement covers the main areas of cooperation: education and training, minerals, energy, tourism and culture, research and food supply. the financial contribution from the european union to greenland for the period 2014-2020 shall be eur 217,8 million. i support the proposals set out below to remove some references to exploration and exploitation of natural resources from the text of the agreement in order to ensure the neutrality of the agreement and the right of the greenlandic authorities to freely decide which sector of cooperation will receive eu financial support. it is important that natural resources are used in accordance with strict environmental sustainability criteria. i also believe that eu financial support should be allocated on a pro gramme basis to programmes aimed at the social development of the people of greenland.
in writing. - i have endorsed the proposal on relations between the european union and greenland and the kingdom of denmark, which sets out the framework for cooperation between the two countries for the period 2014-2020. this decision will replace council decision 2006/526/ec covering the period 2006-2013. the decision builds substantially on the provisions and achievements of the previous decision and will contribute to strengthening the excellent relations which the european union currently enjoys with greenland and the kingdom of denmark. the areas of cooperation covered by the agreement are: education and training, minerals, energy, tourism and culture, research and food supply. eu financial assistance should be directed to programmes aimed at the social development of the people of greenland, giving priority to the economic development of the territory, as the significant potential for the exploration and exploitation of natural resources, including gas and oil, will undoubtedly attract the necessary private investment or european investment bank loans and will not require a contribution of state capital. however, it is necessary to take all precautionary measures to ensure that greenland's natural resources are used in accordance with strict environmental sustainability criteria in order to protect the fragile ecosystem of the region.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to improve preparedness for natural and man-made disasters. over the past decade, europe's land temperatures have risen by an average of 1.3 degrees celsius compared to pre-industrial temperatures, while the global climate has warmed by 0.8 degrees celsius. we've had some extreme weather. it is estimated that each euro spent on flood protection will save six euros, as losses will be avoided accordingly. without adaptation, the eu will suffer annual losses of €100 billion in 2020 and €250 billion in 2050 due to the impacts of climate change. the european commission has put forward a two-part package to tackle this problem: an eu strategy for adaptation to climate change and a green paper on the prevention of natural and man-made disasters. the documents set out the framework and measures for implementing the eu's preparedness for the current and future impacts of climate change, and open a wide-ranging public debate on the adequacy and sufficiency of existing safeguards. i support the proposals set out to encourage all member states to develop comprehensive climate change adaptation strategies and to provide financial support to strengthen climate change adaptation capacity. it is important to integrate climate change adaptation into policies in vulnerable sectors such as agriculture, fisheries and cohesion. it is also necessary to increase the resilience of eu infrastructure and to promote protection against natural and man-made disasters.
in writing. - i supported the proposal because the situation on the eu insurance market is not uniform, because member states face different risks and different natural and man-made disasters, and the predictability of these disasters depends on different factors, and natural and man-made disasters are financial risks, no matter where they occur. natural disasters are affected by climatic and geographical factors, while man-made disasters are caused by misconduct or poor risk management. the consequences of certain natural disasters are sometimes greater because governments, local authorities and citizens do not take adequate precautions. in the case of man-made disasters, compliance with and optimisation of safety rules are of crucial importance for disaster prevention purposes. prevention is the most important factor in protecting people and avoiding losses caused by unforeseen events. the eu therefore has a crucial role to play in creating a more responsible society with a sufficient focus on precautions and a culture of prevention that would help raise citizens' awareness of both natural and man-made disaster risks. member states should share best practices and experience to protect citizens from unforeseen events and establish a network for the exchange of information and agree on cross-border coordination and management.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the delegated regulation as it is important to ensure that the delegated regulation on the european partnership code of conduct for the european structural and investment funds enters into force as soon as possible, given the urgent need to apply this code of conduct to the ongoing preparation of the partnership agreements and programmes for the period 2014-2020.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at an agreement between the european union and the swiss confederation to ensure more effective enforcement of competition law. switzerland is the eu's third largest economic partner and the second largest recipient of foreign direct investment. the two economies are closely interlinked and cross-border trade is affected by a number of distortions of competition. following the conclusion of cooperation agreements with the usa, canada, japan and south korea on competition issues, the european commission agreed on the first so-called second generation agreement with switzerland. unlike previous agreements, this agreement also provides for the exchange of evidence obtained by competition authorities in the course of the same case, which will help to ensure more effective enforcement of competition law.
in writing. - i agreed to the proposal as it provides for the exchange of evidence obtained by competition authorities investigating the same case and will help to ensure more effective enforcement of competition law. this is a ground-breaking eu-swiss agreement. switzerland is the eu's third largest economic partner and the second largest recipient of foreign direct investment. the two economies are closely interlinked and cross-border trade is affected by a number of distortions of competition. the establishment of similar practical rules in the eu and switzerland will facilitate the implementation of the agreement. in a world of globalised companies and global value chains, distortions of competition, such as the abuse of a dominant market position or cartel activity, are also considered to be non-tariff barriers to trade. in this context, it is essential that greater efforts are made to promote international cooperation and to combat these distortions of competition in the regulation of trade and foreign investment. wto competition rules are no longer enough.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to assess the benefits of european union cooperation agreements in the field of competition policy. the purpose of the cooperation agreements is to implement the competition rules in an increasingly globalised economic activity in which cartels operate on a cross-border basis. the prosecution and punishment of serious transnational crime by international cartels under these agreements is more effective. i support calls for the establishment of secure mechanisms for the use and transmission of confidential information. the commission should promote more actively the implementation of competition policy at international level, in particular in multilateral fora such as the wto, the international competition network (icn) and the organisation for economic cooperation and development (oecd). i also welcome the 2013 memorandum of understanding with india on cooperation in the field of competition policy implementation activities and the ongoing negotiations on the second generation agreement with canada and the free trade agreement with japan on cooperation in the field of competition.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, cooperation agreements aimed at implementing competition rules in an increasingly globalised economic activity where cartels operate on a cross-border scale and mergers often involve several jurisdictions are very important and i hope that the prosecution and punishment of international cartels for serious cross-border crime will be more effective under this agreement. the commission should actively promote the implementation of competition policy at international level, in particular in multilateral fora such as the world trade organisation (wto), the international competition network (ict) and the organisation for economic cooperation and development (oecd). this would be the most effective form of cooperation, as investigations often have to be carried out in a number of jurisdictions where not all parties have bilateral agreements or where the terms of those agreements differ. the oecd and the octs should develop instruments to promote international cooperation and keep guidelines of good practice up to date.
in writing. - i voted for this motion, which seeks to ratify the arms trade treaty. unlike wmd and mines, the trade in conventional weapons is not subject to any legally binding global measure. the arms trade treaty is intended to fill this gap. the arms trade treaty was adopted in 2013 by a un assembly resolution. three countries voted against the adoption of the treaty (north korea, iran and syria) and 22 abstained (including china, india and russia). the treaty sets the standard for regulating international trade in conventional arms and, to some extent, ammunition, parts and components. i support calls on the commission to develop codes of conduct for private entities involved in the trade in military goods in line with the un guiding principles on business and human rights. at the same time, member states should commit themselves to ensuring transparency and public disclosure of their annual arms trade reports. it is essential that the eu and the member states provide support to third countries that need it to fulfil their obligations under the treaty.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because, unlike wmd and landmines, the trade in conventional weapons is not covered by any legally binding global measure. the arms trade treaty is intended to fill this gap. after lengthy negotiations, the arms trade treaty was adopted on 2 april 2013 by a resolution of the united nations general assembly. while the existing export regulations in europe for conventional arms are used as a basis for un negotiations as best practice, the existing regulations in europe set much higher standards and have a wider scope than the att. rapid ratification of the treaty in all eu member states will send a signal to third countries that have not yet ratified the treaty, such as the united states. in order to ensure the swift and effective implementation of the treaty, the full-fledged arms trade treaty secretariat should facilitate cooperation between states in the areas of institutional capacity building, legal advice, technical, material and financial assistance. the european union cannot sign and ratify the arms trade treaty, as only member states can be parties to the united nations treaties. however, the council has authorised the commission to negotiate certain provisions of the att which fall within the exclusive competence of the union, i.e. fall within the scope of trade policy. the purpose of this proposal for a council decision is therefore to authorise the member states to ratify the treaty in respect of matters falling within the exclusive competence of the union.

in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because i agree that the european union needs effective anti-dumping measures. this issue is also of great relevance to lithuania, as it is about ensuring fair competition on the eu market. at present, eu producers often have to compete with much cheaper products imported from countries with much lower energy prices and labour standards. international trade brings many benefits, but it should not encourage a race to save more at the expense of labor or conservation. i believe that the anti-subsidy and anti-dumping regulation adopted by parliament will help to defend eu industrial policy and eu workers' rights and will allow for a quick and effective response to complaints from eu companies about unfair competition from third countries. the european parliament has also called for the eu to be more transparent in its approach to the issue of asylum. the wto provisions must be implemented, but additional, stricter commitments must be undertaken with great caution in the face of a crisis.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to bring regulation no 1224/2009 establishing a community control system for ensuring compliance with the rules of the common fisheries policy into line with the treaty of lisbon.
in writing. - i voted in favour, as the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon on 1 december 2009 and the re-allocation of powers under it require legal harmonisation of eu legislation and therefore a redistribution of powers granted to the commission under the former comitology procedure is necessary.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to align the nine product directives with the new legal framework of the ntas. the new legislative framework creates a simplified regulatory environment for products and facilitates a more consistent implementation of technical standards this will help improve the functioning of the single market by ensuring a level playing field between non-compliant products and economic operators. the alignment of the nine product directives with the nacs will increase confidence among both producers and consumers. the obligations of economic operators will be more clearly defined and the authorities of the member states will be given more effective means to carry out market surveillance, thus reducing the number of non-compliant and unsafe products on the market. i agree with the proposals made to ensure that instructions, safety information and labelling are reliable, understandable and transparent. it is essential to modernise the current procedures by allowing the submission of eu declarations of conformity not only in paper format but also in electronic form and it is also proposed to reduce the bureaucratic burden on economic operators in relation to their identification. it is important to establish an obligation for member states to provide the ec with annual information on their market surveillance activities and to encourage them to allocate sufficient funds to their market surveillance authorities.

in writing. - i supported the amendment of the harmonisation of the laws of the member states relating to equipment and protective systems in potentially explosive atmospheres, as the harmonisation of the nine product directives with the new regulatory framework (ner) is an important step towards completing the eu single market. the new legal framework creates a simplified regulatory environment for products and facilitates a more consistent implementation of technical standards this will help to improve the functioning of the single market by ensuring a uniform approach to non-compliant products and economic operators, as well as a uniform assessment of notified bodies across the eu market. the harmonisation of the nine product directives with the nsis will increase confidence among both producers and consumers by clarifying the obligations of economic operators and providing member states' authorities with more effective means of market surveillance, thus reducing the number of non-compliant and unsafe products on the market. the commission's proposals in the harmonisation package are based on extensive consultation with stakeholders, including several hundred smes, and their experience with the commodity package.
in writing. - ensuring consumer rights is one of the eu's key priorities. the 2004 air passenger rights regulation was one of the successful examples of eu legislation. however, in recent years, with the increase in passenger traffic and the liberalisation of the european air market, the shortcomings of the 2004 regulation have become apparent. they relate to the uneven application of the rules in the various member states, which arises from the 'grey areas' contained in the regulation. i believe that the new text adopted by parliament will help to define more clearly the rights and obligations of passengers and airlines. from now on, airlines will only be able to refuse compensation to passengers for delayed or cancelled flights in the cases specified in the regulation. the law also aims to ensure that airlines provide clearer information to passengers about the price of tickets, rather than adding additional services such as customer service at the last moment. the new rules will also harmonise the hand luggage requirements of airlines and prevent passengers from being denied re-entry on return flights if they have missed a flight ahead.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to modernise the european rules on the insolvency of crossborder companies. each year, 50,000 eu companies become liable in crossborder bankruptcy cases. the provisions of european bankruptcy law are laid down in regulation (ec) no 1346/2000 on bankruptcy proceedings. the regulation lays down rules on jurisdiction, recognition and the determination of the applicable law and provides for the coordination of the examination of bankruptcy cases brought in several member states. the adoption of the new rules will make cross-border bankruptcy proceedings more efficient and effective. the proposal aims to restructure firms in difficulty and to create a favourable business environment. clear rules on jurisdiction will be established and close cooperation between the courts dealing with bankruptcy cases in different member states will be ensured where the debtor is subject to several bankruptcy proceedings. creditors will be better informed as member states are obliged to publish key decisions, such as those on the opening of bankruptcy proceedings. i support the proposals for a european union register to enable creditors and courts to determine whether bankruptcy proceedings have been opened in another member state. the register should be integrated into the european e-justice portal. it is also important to designate a coordinator who will not only identify and develop recommendations for a flexible bankruptcy coordination procedure but also provide a group coordination plan that identifies, describes and recommends the implementation of a comprehensive set of measures necessary to address the difficulties in the event of bankruptcy of group members.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because it aims to modernise eu bankruptcy law, focusing not only on liquidation but also on giving companies a second chance. this is an important initiative on insolvency proceedings under eu company law. the report also notes that the list of areas of insolvency law where it is worthwhile and possible to harmonise legislationis still relevant today. it cannot be ignored that 'differences in national insolvency laws give rise to competitive advantages or disadvantages and difficulties for undertakings operating in more than one country and may impede the successful restructuring of insolvent companies'. the idea of creating an eu register to enable creditors and courts to identify whether bankruptcy proceedings have been opened in another member state is welcomed. the register should be integrated into the european e-justice portal. the register should contain all relevant information on cross-border insolvency proceedings opened. such a register is particularly important in order to increase publicity and transparency. it also lays down the minimum criteria that an undertaking given by the insolvency practitioner to local creditors must meet in order to be enforceable and binding. it is also clarified that local creditors may challenge any decision to postpone or refuse to bring a secondary action.
the situation in ukraine, madam president, ladies and gentlemen, requires that we take the problem seriously and that we assess it properly. first of all, it is worth saying that we, the european union, have contributed to the tense situation in this country by not doing all the homework properly. it should also be acknowledged that ukraine itself did not do its homework in such a way that the partnership discussions ended with the signing of the agreement. today, neither sanctions nor visits to kiev by politicians will solve this problem. the european union is committed to the principle of subsidiarity and the principle of subsidiarity must be respected. but the primary objective today is to convince all parties to the conflict to stop provocations and violence, arrests of persons and seizures of buildings. this should be an indisputable condition, the implementation of which would allow the preparation of the referendum and the new elections, in which the osce could carry out an observer mission.
mr president, what is it that needs to be proposed for ukraine by the more efficient european bodies, including this house? an interim government should be established until elections can be held at the earliest possible date, and the first change should be to replace the current minister for the interior with a person able to undertake the restructuring and reinstructing of the berkut special unit to end its hostility towards, and fascist-type hatred of, civilian protesters against the current government. a list of berkut senior officers responsible for atrocities such as torture and killings should be in preparation now, in readiness for targeted european sanctions. also, if possible, agreements with moldova and georgia must be reached during the sochi games – and not after them when mr putin will have his hands free for even greater destruction.
we are re-examining the situation in ukraine, because we can no longer be blind and create for what is happening in our neighbour. it was a long time, though. they may not be blind and deaf, but they're not inherently indifferent. it took the sacrifices of the vilnius summit and euromaidan, the sos signals from ukraine, for the whole of the european union and ourselves, the european parliament, to fully appreciate both the essence of russian policy and objectives, and the complexity of the economic situation in ukraine, and the high price that eastern europeans have to pay for their rapprochement with the european union, with the democratic world. in this context, the slogan of a strategic partnership between russia and the eu based on shared values sounds like a joke. if the eu and russia were to pursue a strategic partnership, there would be no need for euromaidan and ukrainian mothers would be crying for their dead children. ukraine is europe, my friends.
in writing. - i voted for this bill to strengthen the rights of airline passengers. today, only 2 percent of airline passengers who file complaints with airlines receive compensation. stronger consumer protection is necessary in this area. it is important that passenger rights do not exist only on paper. the new rules will improve complaints procedures and enforcement measures to ensure that passengers can effectively exercise their rights and obtain compensation. passengers stranded at airports will have better opportunities to receive information, assistance and be diverted to other routes. if the passenger's complaint is not answered within 2 months, it means that the air carrier accepts the compensation requested. baggage restrictions must be clearly indicated at ticket purchase and boarding. the aim is also to oblige carriers to take out insurance or to hold funds in special funds from which, in the event of bankruptcy, they would be reimbursed for tickets or paid for the return of passengers. at the same time, the supervision of air carriers by national and european authorities is being strengthened.
in writing. - the aviation industry has undergone major changes in recent years, with the increase in passenger numbers and flights in a liberalised european market. however, this broadly positive change is accompanied to some extent by unfair practices and a deterioration in the quality of services. carriers and national authorities are not fully and uniformly implementing the current regulation, creating confusion for both passengers and industry. in addition, passengers are not informed about their rights and are dissatisfied with the circumstances and costs involved in enforcing those rights. the figures show that in reality only a small proportion of passengers who would theoretically be entitled to benefits and compensation apply for or receive benefits. the most important factor is that the air carriers operating the flights do not automatically recognise the relevant passenger rights. customers feel insecure about the complexity of the process. many passengers are reluctant to take legal action because of the high costs involved. i supported the proposals which, taken as a whole, aim to strengthen passenger rights.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the future development of european climate and energy policy. i agree with the rapporteurs that the greatest challenge will be to achieve a successful balance between climate protection, affordable energy prices, security of supply and industrial competition. a recent impact assessment of the strategy published by the european commission shows that its implementation would create one million new jobs and significantly reduce the eu's dependence on imported energy sources. the €573 billion paid by eu states in 2011 for the import of fossil fuel resources could be used to stimulate the production of green energy and energy saving mechanisms. it is important to note that the lithuanian population, like the rest of europe, has clearly expressed its support for renewable energy sources and energy efficiency. in the latest eurobarometer survey in lithuania, 53% of respondents believe that renewable energy sources should be given priority, with 25% giving priority to improving energy efficiency. only 15% would prefer nuclear energy and 10% would prefer fossil fuels.
in writing. - i voted for the resolution on ukraine, which calls on the authorities to restore the 2004 constitution, restoring a semi-presidential or parliamentary republic, reforming the electoral law, prosecuting those responsible for the violence in the maidan, and continuing to consider sanctions against ukrainian officials who used force against peaceful protesters. ukraine is a deeply divided country - ethnically, linguistically, and economically. the only way to reduce unrest is through effective dialogue between the authorities, the opposition and civil society. ukraine must resolve this conflict on its own, its fate will not be decided in brussels, moscow or even more so in washington. i agree with the view that the european union can help to bring the situation in kiev under control more quickly only by working together, not by confronting russia. europe should not put ukraine in a position of either the eu or russia.
in writing. - this is an unusual summit in eu-russia relations, lasting only 3 hours. russia is a difficult strategic partner for the eu, but we see the direction we need to go. president putin's proposal to negotiate a new cooperation agreement and a free trade area from lisbon to vladivostok is to be welcomed. the first step is to build trust between brussels and moscow. i voted in favour of this resolution, which stresses that moscow's political and economic pressure on the eu's and russia's neighbours is unacceptable. russia joined the wto in 2012, but it has not fulfilled all its obligations. russia has adopted a number of protectionist measures in the eurasian customs union. the resolution also highlights the persistent gaps in human rights. i agree that it is important to promote dialogue between the eu and russia in the areas of culture, heritage and history, and to promote exchanges and mobility of students, teachers, lecturers and researchers in order to facilitate people-to-people contacts. this would create a visible, tangible and sustainable partnership, which is a community of values created in the long term.
as we look to the new term, we should take a closer look at the past term, and we can celebrate the achievements, but it wasn't as good as we hoped, and we have to admit that. many of the good things written in directives, resolutions and proposals have remained there, unfulfilled in real life. we have an agreement on growth and jobs, but unemployment has not gone up very much, especially among young people. i think the european commission has not done enough to turn this agreement from words into flesh. investment, growth - that's the way of the future. on the other hand, when we talk about the new ep semester, we must fundamentally decide on the future of europe itself: how and which way europe should turn. i believe that only democratic, free, equal and strong national states are the foundation of a strong european union. on the economic side, poverty and social exclusion must be reduced, and i would suggest that we consider establishing a uniform minimum income in the eu countries, which should be in line with the current average of the community countries. although that is what should be recommended to the states.
unemployment in the european union remains high, especially among young people, and we need to take action as soon as possible, for example by concluding a european pact for youth employment, which would start implementing the measures long agreed. in addition, additional funds and new measures should be allocated to tackle youth unemployment by reducing the number of young people who are not in employment, education or work. i would also like to urge the member states to implement the youth guarantee as soon as possible, which must be translated from words into action. alongside the problems of youth unemployment, we cannot forget the elderly, especially when we raise the retirement age, and member states must therefore ensure that older workers also have access to employment, by providing lifelong learning, working conditions and adequate wages. the commission and the member states must ensure that people have access to necessary and adequate social protection, health and social services, and should increase the participation of vulnerable groups in the labour market and reduce poverty, especially child poverty.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. since 2009, the financial crisis and the economic downturn have seriously undermined financial stability and economic growth in the european union as a whole, and in particular in the member states receiving external assistance. macroeconomic adjustment programmes have been developed for these countries in coordination with the european commission, initially implemented in seven member states (cyprus, greece, hungary, ireland, latvia, portugal and romania) and currently still providing financial assistance to cyprus, greece, ireland and portugal. the capacity of these member states to invest in both the public and private sectors has declined and it has become more difficult to use the resources available for growth and competitiveness. for these reasons, it is necessary to promote the facilitation of simplified management of european union funds in order to stimulate and accelerate investment in the member states receiving financial support and the implementation of projects necessary for social and economic recovery and the development of the sector. the aim of this commission proposal to amend regulation (ec) no 1198/2006 is to help those member states currently receiving financial support and those that could benefit from adjustment programmes to continue to benefit from financial instruments for the implementation of their projects under the fisheries sector policy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the amendments needed to ensure that eu funds can continue to reach countries in financial and economic difficulty. these member states are still subject to strict budgetary constraints, which create liquidity problems and make it more difficult to implement the operational programmes, in this case the operational programmes to absorb the european fisheries fund. the activity of the fisheries sector may therefore be affected and there may be a risk of a downturn. the main amendment made by this document is to extend the continuity of the increased funding provided for during the crisis without changing the appropriations allocated.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its consent to the necessary procedural changes in the european union's legislation relating to the use of the regulatory procedure with scrutiny. the regulatory procedure with scrutiny has been replaced by the so-called delegated acts in the treaty of lisbon, which amend or supplement non-essential acts in the case of delegated acts, the legislator empowers the commission to adopt acts of a legislative nature. however, the regulatory procedure with scrutiny shall continue to apply for the adoption of measures based on the legislation in force. the document adopted today examined whether the previously existing regulatory procedure with scrutiny was justified in specific cases.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. the objective of this proposal is to reduce co2 emissions from new vehicles. the emissions target for the new car fleet is 160 g co2/km from 2006, 130 g co2/km from 2015 and 95 g co2/km from 2020. i believe that it was necessary to provide clarity and predictability for the transport sector by setting specific and defined objectives, giving the sector greater certainty and clear legal regulation in planning long-term investment in sustainable technologies and ensuring competitiveness. in order to allow the automotive industry to make long-term investments and innovate, a target for 2025 should be set. it should be based on the emissions that need to be reduced in line with the union's long-term climate change objectives and should accelerate the development of cost-effective technologies for reducing co2 emissions from passenger cars. i welcome the commission's report and its proposal for standards that are consistent with the union's long-term climate change objectives for 2050 in a cost-effective manner and that ensure continued leadership of the european union in fuel efficiency and co2 emissions.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the regulation setting out the ways in which the target of reducing co2 emissions from new passenger cars by 2020 can be achieved. this objective will be achieved by the application of modern drive concepts. these measures will result in higher costs for the larger premium car manufacturers than for the smaller car manufacturers. however, as the most advanced technologies are generally first introduced in larger cars, this regulation proposes a realistic incentive framework to encourage the development and use of new, environmentally friendly drive concepts.
in writing. - i am pleased that the european parliament has finally adopted a coordinated position on co2 emissions from new cars. it is, of course, regrettable that the negotiations on this agreement have taken too long, have not always been very transparent and have resulted in certain concessions that are not in the interests of the environment. however, i welcome the agreement itself, as the new standards, which will come into force in less than 10 years, will allow for further reductions in air pollution from passenger cars.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to simplify the conditions for obtaining trade marks and to ensure more effective protection of them. eu trade mark registration systems should become cheaper, faster, more reliable and more predictable. this would improve the conditions for companies to innovate and benefit from more effective protection of trademarks against counterfeiting, including counterfeit goods transported across the eu. the community trade mark system and the office for harmonization in the internal market (ohim) have been in operation for more than 15 years and it is therefore appropriate to review the existing rules and improve the system in the light of today's realities.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the improvement of the european trade mark system. the community trade mark system and the office for harmonization in the internal market have been in place for more than 15 years, and it is therefore appropriate to review the existing rules in order to improve the very successful system. the current review implies that changes in the governance of the office for harmonization in the internal market will be necessary in order to continue to ensure independence, user-friendliness and competence. some aspects of the management of the agency will be improved, with clarifications on definitions, cooperation with member states, fees for the eu trade mark system and the use of revenue generated by them.
in writing. - i agree with the position that the development of the internal market requires the improvement of the legislation relating to it. uniform and uniform protection of intellectual property throughout the eu is an important element of such improvement. however, i would point out that the new regulatory provisions must also ensure a balance between the protection of intellectual property and access to it.
in writing. - the role of trademarks in terms of market success and commercial value is enormous; the importance of trademarks to competition and eu business is crucial. with the increase in the role of the community trademark, eu legislation should be improved as well. in order to make eu businesses even more competitive, and foster innovation and economic growth all over the eu, the community trademark system should be modernised while the inconsistencies within the existing regulatory framework have to be reduced. a set of measures proposed in the mentioned proposal for a regulation aim to lower costs and increase the speed, predictability and security of the legal system. such improvements in the trademark system can not only create many benefits for eu businesses but will make the functioning of the euʼs internal market more efficient. i welcome this proposal aimed at making the legal framework for the community trademark more harmonised and the trademark system itself more efficient and accessible for eu businesses. i believe that when all the sets of measures have been applied, each member state, including lithuania, will be able to benefit from this modernised legal framework.
