in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to strengthen the fight against the proceeds of crime and to introduce new measures to that end. the eu is making huge profits from organised crime. for example, it is estimated that the eu generates €100 billion a year from the sale of illicit drugs. while a large proportion of this illicitly obtained money is legalised and reinvested in the legal economy, less than 1% of the proceeds of crime are currently frozen and confiscated. the commission has proposed new rules to make the large-scale confiscation of proceeds of crime and other property more effective the nca will be able to confiscate assets transferred to third parties more effectively, criminal assets will be easier to confiscate even in cases where the suspect is in hiding, and the possibility for competent authorities to temporarily freeze assets that may disappear if no action is taken will be ensured.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it was the report parliament adopted in favour of the directive on the freezing and confiscation of the proceeds of crime in the european union. the directive lays down minimum rules for member states on the freezing and confiscation of criminal assets, by means of direct seizure, confiscation of property of appropriate value, extended seizure, seizure not based on an indictment and seizure from third parties. the adoption of such minimum rules will harmonise the freezing and confiscation procedures in the member states, thus contributing to the strengthening of mutual trust and facilitating cross-border cooperation. it will also be a step forward in strengthening the mutual recognition of freezing and confiscation orders, which is an important aspect of the fight against transnational serious and organised crime in the european union.
in writing. - the eu has had some success in fighting organised crime, but there are a number of problems with the transfer of illicitly acquired assets to third parties, i.e. most of the proceeds of crime that are frozen or confiscated are not passed on to third parties and are reused for organised crime. in supporting this report, i hope that the proposals it contains will not only contribute to the fight against organised crime, but also facilitate cooperation between member states. the communication also provides for measures to enable member states to apply freezing and confiscation procedures to proceeds of crime also in cases where assets are acquired illegally in third countries and attempts are made to conceal or legalise those assets in member states.
in writing. - the main objective of the proposal to amend directive 2008/95/ec of the european parliament and of the council of 22 october 2008 on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to trade marks and of the related regulation, which forms part of the same package, is to harmonise trade mark registration systems in all eu member states so that they are more efficient, cheaper, simpler, faster, more predictable and provide greater legal certainty. this could greatly accelerate innovation and economic growth. the directive, which harmonises certain aspects of the trade mark law of the member states of the european union, has been in force for more than 20 years. the current review is an opportunity to learn from best practice and further strengthen the harmonisation of the substantive trade mark law and procedures applied by national trade mark offices.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the improvement of the directive on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to trade marks. this directive has been in force for more than 20 years. the current review is an opportunity to learn from best practice and further strengthen the harmonisation of the substantive trade mark law and procedures applied by national trade mark offices.
in writing. - as the european union develops its legal framework for the protection of intellectual property, it is inevitable that we will have to adapt the substantive law applicable in the member states. however, such improvements in the harmonisation of the law must be proportionate and must not be used as a pretext for restricting citizens' access to intellectual property.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, which aims to increase the efficiency of the eu law enforcement agency europol in collecting and analysing information and sharing the results of that analysis with the un. the proposal also provides for increased accountability of europol to the european and national parliaments and for stricter protection of personal data, for mandatory reporting to europol by the nsa and for targeted financial support for investigations. europol shall provide annual reports on the quantity and quality of data provided by the nsas. the data processing structure will be fundamentally changed. the european data protection supervisor shall be responsible for the external data protection supervision of the data processed by europol. this regulation merges europol and cepol into a single agency, to be located at europol's headquarters in the hague, in order to avoid duplication of the support activities of the two agencies.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the improvement of the european police office (europol) and resolves the debate in the eu over the merger of europol and the european police college by leaving them as separate agencies. the european police office was established in 1995, but since 2009 the council, representing the member states, has been asking for its powers to be extended to enable it to perform more effectively the tasks assigned to it in the prevention of cross-border crime and the fight against transnational organised crime, which is on the increase in the eu. a 2013 europol assessment found that serious and organised crime is an increasingly dynamic and complex phenomenon in the eu, that the impact of globalisation on society and business is creating favourable conditions for the emergence of various new forms of criminal activity, that criminal networks are exploiting loopholes in the law, the internet and the conditions created by the economic crisis to obtain illicit profits with little risk. terrorism also remains one of the greatest threats to the security of the european union, as european society continues to suffer from terrorist attacks. for all these reasons, the improvement and expansion of the european police office was almost unanimously approved by the european parliament this week.
in writing. - europol is making a significant contribution to reducing organised crime, but the ways and means of organised crime are changing and improving, and we need to strengthen europol's powers to ensure the safety of eu citizens and to fight crime effectively. i support the measures envisaged in the communication to improve the governance of europol, to strengthen data protection standards, and to encourage member states to provide information to europol more effectively, while implementing the provisions of the lisbon treaty. the implementation of these changes will make europol more effective in the fight against organised crime.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, which aims to make the eu more attractive to foreign students and researchers. more than 200 000 foreign students and researchers take advantage of the opportunity to come to the eu on a temporary basis every year. the current rules for issuing student visas or residence permits are too complex and unclear, the process is long and uneven. the commission has proposed to facilitate the entry of students, researchers and other professionals from third countries to the eu to work there for more than three months. the new legislation will clearly set the time frame for national authorities to process applications, provide new opportunities for access to the labour market and facilitate movement between eu countries and procedural safeguards, in particular by setting a 60-day period for the authorities of the vt to take a decision on an application for a visa or residence permit. simpler and more flexible rules will increase the mobility of researchers, students and paid trainees within the eu. it will also facilitate access to the labour market by allowing students to work at least 20 hours a week to earn a living. both researchers and students will be allowed to stay in the country for an additional 12 months after the end of their studies or research period, under certain conditions, in order to find a job or set up their own business.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the newly drawn up directive which will henceforth regulate the conditions and procedures for admitting third-country nationals for the purposes of research, studies, pupil exchanges, unpaid traineeships or voluntary service. this new wording of the directive also adds two new categories: paid trainees and au pair nannies. these two categories have not been included in the system governing their situation until now. their inclusion in this directive will help to prevent exploitation and work without documents.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution establishing the european voluntary humanitarian aid corps (eu aid volunteers). the objective of this corps is to express the union's humanitarian values and solidarity with people in need by promoting an effective and recognised european voluntary humanitarian aid corps, which contributes to strengthening the union's capacity to respond to humanitarian crises and helps to build the capacity and resilience of vulnerable or disaster-affected communities in third countries.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the establishment of a european voluntary humanitarian aid corps. the objective of the establishment of this corps is to express the union's humanitarian values and solidarity with people in need by promoting an effective and recognisable european voluntary humanitarian aid corps, which contributes to strengthening the union's capacity to respond to humanitarian crises and helps to build the capacity and resilience of vulnerable or disaster-affected communities in third countries. eu aid volunteers will be deployed in humanitarian aid operations in third countries in the event of man-made crises or natural disasters, except in cases of armed conflict and internal disturbances.
in writing. - the eu has for many years been the world's largest humanitarian aid donor, contributing almost 50% of all humanitarian aid. i think that unpaid volunteer work in humanitarian missions is a very encouraging thing. this not only contributes to the resolution of humanitarian problems at crisis points, but also promotes solidarity among eu citizens with victims of disasters in third countries, based on humanity, common human compassion and a willingness to help through personal, real action.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. the eu fund for the poorest eu citizens will be extended until 2020 and will amount to €3.5 billion. the new programme, which will run from 2014 to 2020, will replace the food distribution programme, which has allowed the redistribution of surplus food stocks generated by agriculture. the new fund will support food and material assistance and also finance social inclusion measures to provide increased assistance to the eu's poorest citizens.
in writing. - the european union has set itself the target of reducing the number of people at risk of poverty and social exclusion by at least 20 million by 2020. however, the economic and financial crisis has led to an increase in poverty and social exclusion across the union. in 2011, almost 120 million europeans - an increase of six million in two years - were at risk of poverty or social exclusion, and now account for almost a quarter of the total population. one of the main signs of material deprivation is the inability to obtain sufficient food of adequate quality. children are particularly sensitive to malnutrition and poor nutrition patterns can negatively affect brain development and learning ability and their future health. the fund will focus on people at risk of material deprivation and food shortages, in particular the homeless, children and households with children, as well as supporting social inclusion measures, tackling the most severe and severe forms of deprivation, and strengthening existing and planned eu efforts in these areas.
in writing. - i agree with this report and i am glad that we have managed to reach a unanimous agreement to increase the operating budget of the fund by a full billion, i.e. to 3.5 billion for the years 2014-2020. this will help to combat poverty, and in particular child poverty. statistics from the world health organization show that as many as 43 million europeans cannot afford to eat meat, chicken or fish or even vegetarian substitutes every other day. this shows that the eu needs to tackle the problems of poverty in society by supporting vulnerable social groups with food, basic goods such as hygiene products, clothing, etc., and thus reduce social exclusion. i would also like to stress the importance of involving ngos in the work of the foundation, as they work indirectly with people who are suffering from poverty and social exclusion. the procedures for receiving food from the food fund will also be facilitated in the next financial period. i very much hope that the new programme will achieve its objectives.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution, which seeks to amend the technical regulation on the placing on the market and use of biocidal products. on 22 may 2012, the european parliament and the council adopted regulation (eu) no 528/2012 concerning the making available on the market and use of biocidal products. the regulation represented a major revision of the existing legislation on biocidal products by harmonising the rules for the placing on the market and use of biocidal products, while ensuring a high level of protection of human and animal health and the environment. since the adoption of the regulation, the ec has identified a number of technical errors and i therefore support the commission's proposal to make the necessary corrections to ensure the necessary legal clarity and certainty for the ngos, industry and the general public.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the correction of technical errors and unintended consequences of the regulation on the placing of biocidal products on the market and their use adopted in 2012. the biocidal products regulation has represented an important and fundamental revision of the existing legislation on biocidal products by harmonising the rules for the placing on the market and use of biocidal products, while ensuring a high level of protection of human and animal health and the environment. it is therefore important to adopt this amending regulation in order to make the necessary corrections and adjustments as soon as possible, taking into account that it has been applicable since 1 september 2013.
in writing. - accurate scientific data on the environmental impact of regulated products and technologies is one of the key factors determining the development of eu environmental policy. legislation adopted by the eu institutions must be based on scientific evidence and any errors in this must be regarded as a threat to the environment and human health. i therefore support the clarification of the legislation governing the use of biocides to correct the errors that have occurred.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it calls on the member states not to sacrifice the fight against and prevention of gender-based violence, at the expense of balanced budgets in the wake of the economic crisis. violence against women is a widespread violation of human rights and a form of gender discrimination. it is a major cause of gender equality, as it prevents women from participating fully in economic, social, political and cultural life. women who experience violence suffer from severe psychological and physical trauma over a long period of time. in this report, parliament called on the commission to submit a proposal for a regulation on measures to promote and support action by member states in the field of prevention of gender-based violence and also called on the member states to develop measures to prevent and combat gender-based violence against women and girls.
in writing. - every day, women and girls are beaten, sexually abused, just because they are girls, women. this is not only happening outside the european union, but unfortunately also in the eu countries. statistics show that 45 percent of women report having experienced physical violence, 10 percent sexual violence, and 15 percent domestic violence. seven women die every day because of it. this is unacceptable. the european parliament has repeatedly asked the european commission to develop an eu-wide strategy to prevent and combat violence against women. no such strategy has been developed so far. i voted in favour of this report because it calls for the eu to establish a legal basis on this issue. i also agree that member states need to exchange information, to take action on the prevention plan. the commission could set up a service for the prevention of gender-based violence, declare a year for the elimination of violence against women. we call on the commission to submit a report on violence against women by the end of this year.
in writing. - women must not be beaten, violence against women must not be perpetrated - this is a violation of human rights. i agree with this report. as many as 25% of women in the eu suffer from physical violence. we should not leave the hands of the violent until all the countries have ratified the istanbul convention. we must fight this terrible phenomenon in our lives. unfortunately, violence against women is not considered a problem in some parts of society. it is therefore necessary to change the mentality of society to turn to women and their families who suffer violence. the victims of trafficking in human beings should also be kept in mind they are also subject to violence. we need to invest in prevention measures that can help curb violence. i would like to congratulate my colleague, mrs parvanov, on her excellent report.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. it aims to summarise the work done in the eu institutions at administrative and political level over the past four years on delegated and implementing acts, with particular attention to the activities of the legislative institutions and the commission, and to prepare for the inclusion of these issues in the forthcoming review of the interinstitutional agreement on better law-making in 2003.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it seeks to review the application in practice of the new legislative procedures introduced by the treaty of lisbon and to provide practical guidance on the use of delegated and implementing acts. the high expectations expressed in the previous reports on the delegation of legislative powers and the implementing regulation have not been met to the extent expected. these measures were designed to further improve the scrutiny of secondary legislation by the legislative authorities and thus to increase the democratic legitimacy of such acts. they also aimed to ensure greater efficiency of legislation at the european union level and further simplification of the legislative process. the main objective of this report is to summarise the work done at administrative and political level in the eu institutions over the last four years on delegated and implementing acts, with particular attention to the activities of the legislative institutions and the commission, and to prepare for the future review of the interinstitutional agreement on better law-making.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to assess the 2014 annual growth survey. real gdp growth in the euro area is gradually picking up again and is expected to reach 1,1% in 2014, with unemployment expected to decline and inflation to remain well below 2%. despite the optimistic forecasts, the commission acknowledges that even under the most optimistic scenario, most of the europe 2020 targets will not be achieved. i therefore support the european parliament's request to include in the specific recommendations for the eu and the eu as a whole, including the recommendations for economic adjustment programmes, the issue of not only fiscal consolidation but also structural reforms to promote real, sustainable and socially balanced growth, employment, increased competitiveness and increased convergence. the adoption of legislation on 'convergence guidelines' which would set a very limited number of targets for the most urgent reform measures for a given period is necessary, and parliament reiterates its call on the member states to ensure that national reform programmes are drawn up on the basis of the said convergence guidelines and verified by the commission.
in writing. - a year ago, some people thought that the euro would collapse and eventually the european union. today's situation proves those prophets wrong. eu countries are showing signs of economic recovery. latvia joined the euro area. without eu aid, greece, cyprus, ireland and portugal would have gone bankrupt and faced their greatest social challenges. however, national budgets should not be boosted at the expense of increasing social inequalities. the situation of unemployment, especially among young people, remains difficult in some eu countries. 35% of people in the eu live below the poverty line. this is primarily a consequence of austerity policies. it has recently emerged that 20 member states have reduced their expenditure on education. i voted in favour of this report because it calls for more than just austerity policies, but for investments that will ensure the economic growth of the eu countries and the implementation of sustainable reforms.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution, which assesses the employment and social aspects of the 2014 annual growth survey. i support the european parliament's demands to hold a meeting of euro group employment and social affairs ministers before the euro area summits, to make the submission of national employment and youth plans mandatory in the 2014 country-specific recommendations, to tackle poverty among the working population and to implement labour market policy measures aimed at ensuring fair wages for workers.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, in which we expressed our position on the need to strengthen the social dimension of the economic and monetary union of the european union. 26.2 million europeans are currently unemployed, and almost one in four is at risk of poverty and social exclusion. on the other hand, social investment also has a positive impact on economic growth by helping people to adapt better to changes in the labour market, to work more productively, to ensure a healthy demand, etc. it was social protection policies, in particular unemployment benefits, support for the minimum income and progressive taxation, that helped to reduce the recession and stabilise labour markets and consumption during the economic crisis. it is therefore necessary to integrate employment and social policies more closely into the european economic semester, which culminates in country-specific recommendations. the european parliament welcomed the fact that the commission had for the first time presented a summary of the eu's employment and social policy performance in the context of the annual growth survey. we have called for the inclusion of additional indicators, in particular the level of child poverty, access to health care, homelessness and the decent work index, which would allow a more appropriate assessment of the social situation in the countries. the european parliament also called for more investment to promote sustainable growth and the creation of quality and sustainable jobs. and they must be used first and foremost to reduce youth unemployment.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, which aims to launch a thorough discussion and study of the situation of plant breeding in european and world agriculture. food demand is projected to increase by 70% over the next 30-40 years, posing major challenges for eu agriculture. i did not agree with the provisions that genetically modified varieties should be increased in agriculture and that the agricultural sector should be transformed by technology. i believe that traditional farming should be encouraged and that people should be encouraged to work in agriculture, exploiting the potential of this sector.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it contains parliament's recommendations on plant breeding. the plant breeding industry is one of the most important elements in the productivity, diversity, health and quality of agriculture, horticulture, food and feed production and our environment. in the future, high population growth will place tremendous demands on agriculture, particularly in terms of increasing productivity to meet the high growth in demand for food. the parliament therefore stressed the crucial importance of an efficient and competitive plant breeding industry to respond to future challenges such as future food needs and climate change. it is also important to develop species that are resilient to conditions likely to be encountered in the future, such as higher rainfall and estimated higher prevalence of plant diseases.
in writing. - meeting the world's and the eu's food needs is an important challenge. i agree with the provisions in the adopted document on the possibility of preserving the biodiversity of crops and on the need to devote more attention and resources to the selection of new varieties of peas. i also support the view that legal protection of plant species should not disproportionately restrict the distribution of breeding material to small and medium-sized farmers. i would also like to point out that the problem of food shortages should be tackled, wherever possible, by selecting plants and not by artificially modifying their genetic material.
for the efd group i would like to draw your attention to the fact that there is no consensus among member states on the 4th package proposed by the european commission, in particular on the scission of railway companies. i am convinced that the forced divestment of well-functioning integrated companies will reduce the efficiency of the railway sector, increase costs, increase the sector's dependence on the state budget and cause other negative consequences. i would like to emphasise that our region, which includes lithuania, is characterised by fierce competition for the transit of european union and cis freight between railway companies, freight terminals and seaports in estonia, latvia, lithuania, belarus, ukraine, russia and elsewhere. i believe that the institutions of the european union must concentrate on the essential issues of ensuring transparent, fair competition in the railway sector, increasing the efficiency of railway operations. it is very important to remember that european railway undertakings are not only competing with each other but also with railway undertakings in eastern third countries, which are, i stress, very strong economically and large integrated undertakings. unfortunately, some colleagues - representatives of the european commission and members of the european parliament - believe that the dismantling of integrated rail systems in the european union countries is not related to the interests of third countries and the threats they pose. we, the lithuanian representatives, see that the dismantling of the national railway company under the 4th package would open up wide possibilities for the cis countries to take control of the essential freight flows of the national railway company into their own hands and transfer the effect currently obtained by the national company profits from the provision of transport services to third countries.
in writing. in pursuing the integrated single market in the european union, we cannot bypass the transport sector, which has a particularly high potential to stimulate economic growth. i therefore support the commission's initiative to complete the removal of the remaining obstacles to improving the quality and efficiency of rail services. however, the principle of subsidiarity remains equally important for all of us, and it is therefore essential to maintain a balance between common rules, common regulation and what the member states can set for themselves in order to achieve the best model for their markets. the commission and parliament have a clear desire to establish a common regulatory framework, to ensure transparency in the allocation and use of funds and to maintain a high level of security. however, some of the proposed amendments to the 4th railway package go beyond the regulatory threshold. a single model of management of railway undertakings should not be imposed, as markets are different in size and each region has its own characteristics in terms of passenger and freight transport. we must create an international railway market, but we cannot violate the principle of subsidiarity, which is to allow the member states to decide for themselves what they can best do.
in writing. i am pleased that member states, including lithuania, have taken an active approach to implementing the youth guarantee adopted last year. at a time when up to 14 million young people under 29 are not in employment, education or training in europe, the swift and smooth implementation of the youth guarantee is more important than ever. the youth guarantee initiative is a set of measures, jointly funded by the eu and the member states, to help young people get into the labour market. this initiative will aim to ensure that the young person is offered a job, industrial placement, traineeship or further training within four months of graduation or registration in the labour market, taking into account individual needs and experience. i would like to support the call by my colleague for member states to pay particular attention to those young people who are economically and socially disadvantaged. this is the only way to prevent the transmission of poverty from generation to generation. i am pleased that the implementation plans prepared by lithuania for the youth guarantee initiative mainly include measures to provide vocational guidance for young people, partnerships between academic institutions and business organisations and employers, both in the formulation of training programmes and in the search for cooperation opportunities for young people to undertake industrial placements, internships, and a great deal of attention is paid to promoting youth entrepreneurship. i believe that all these measures will have concrete and tangible benefits and that we will avoid losing the opportunities that our young people provide.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to maintain due regard for the functioning of the eu single market and to comment on the updated position on the content of the next annual growth review, on the priority actions at national and eu level, and on the recent efforts of the commission on a number of single market governance measures. the commission communication of 8 june 2012 on better governance of the single market calls for a renewed commitment to ensure that the single market contributes effectively to economic growth and that the tangible results are felt by eu citizens, consumers and businesses. this call was reiterated in the 2013 annual growth survey and the accompanying 2013 state of single market integration report. the coordination of the priorities of the single market in the european semester should be achieved through a deeper democratic process, with greater involvement of national parliaments, respect for the eu's exclusive rights and sufficient scope for the social partners and all other stakeholders. better protection of citizens' rights - both for consumers and workers - is essential to enhance confidence in the single market and to promote responsible consumer behaviour.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it sets out parliament's recommendations on the governance of the single market in the context of the european semester 2014. parliament called on the commission to strengthen the governance of the single market by establishing an annual cycle of governance of the single market, which would include a summary of the internal market performance, an annual report on the integration of the single market as part of the annual growth review, recommendations from the european council to the member states, national action plans to implement the single market guidelines and country-specific recommendations. parliament agreed with the commission that the services, financial services, transport, energy and digital market sectors remained crucial to the full integration of the single market. however, it considers that a renewed implementation of a coherent and integrated industrial policy is necessary to restore growth. parliament also noted that these actions should include strengthening the promotion of citizens' rights, including consumer and workers' rights, protection and a competitive model based on knowledge and innovation. at eu level, investment, research and technological development and support for higher education should be promoted more effectively.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to assess the 7th and 8th progress reports on economic, social and territorial cohesion. the reports from the member states provide an overview of the social and economic challenges they face and the implementation of programmes financed by the european union. some member states are lagging far behind in areas such as innovation, research and technological development, it services and broadband, energy and capacity building. the 2007-13 programmes have robust mechanisms for controlling cash flow and use, but the instruments for setting, monitoring and evaluating targets are weaker. the use of funds is therefore a strong point in the reports, but they have difficulty in presenting quantitative information on progress towards the objectives in a convincing way. i agree with the suggestions made that the european commission and the member states should make more full use of monitoring and evaluation tools (increased focus on results, selection of performance indicators specific to programmes and a clear performance framework) in order to increase transparency of reporting, quality of programming and its implementation.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it is a contribution by parliament to the evaluation of cohesion policy in the past. cohesion policy is the main mechanism for implementing the eu strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth europe 2020 and it is therefore necessary to assess the achievement of the objectives of the previous programming period (2007-2013). this is particularly important as this period coincided with the economic, financial and social crisis, and it is therefore necessary to analyse how cohesion policy and the funds have helped to combat the crisis and how they have so far contributed to boosting economic growth. the role of cohesion policy continues to grow as a result of fiscal consolidation in many less developed member states and regions, cohesion funding accounts for more than half of their public investment. this is why the new cohesion policy framework 2014-2020[n] must focus on the need to focus investment at regional and local level on key areas such as job creation, smes, employment (in particular youth employment), labour mobility, training and education, research, technological development and innovation.
in writing. - eu cohesion policy 2007-13 and the lessons learned from past mistakes must serve as a basis for the implementation of the cohesion policy in the new financial perspective. we have to use the funds as efficiently as possible, because the problems are increasing and the funds, unfortunately, are not. i therefore call for funds to be directed first and foremost to our own future: to developing the skills of young people and reducing unemployment by creating and promoting new jobs for young people. it is the younger generation that will have to ensure that eu social policy is implemented in the future.
in writing. - the 7th and 8th progress reports on the eu cohesion policy and the strategic report 2013 on programme implementation 2007-2013 tackle the issue of regional development and the best ways for it to increase cohesion and economic growth within europe and achieve sustainability. these reports also seek ways to improve cohesion policy while creating more jobs and generating growth opportunities at local and regional level. one such means of improvement is investment at regional and local level in areas such as job creation, smes, training and education. another way, as is stated in the reports, is by ensuring efficiency and synergies between the eu budget and the national budgets and the reinforcement of the territorial dimension of the governance system of cohesion policy. i welcome the importance of this report for the future of cohesion policy and i believe that all these steps are vital for achieving its best application. i also believe that lithuania can contribute to these objectives by improving reporting systems, better introduction of indicators and better use of the monitoring and evaluation tools available in the context of the current legislative framework.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to promote the development of the outermost regions. the proposal aims to open up new areas of investment policy in the outermost regions and to create opportunities for their sustainable development. the aim is to facilitate the access of the outermost regions to the various union funds. i agree that the european union's policy, inter alia, through specific fiscal and customs measures, should contribute to the economic development of the outermost regions and to job creation. in the context of the implementation of the 'horizon 2020' programme, the 'energy 2020' strategy, the life+ and erasmus programmes, the trans-european energy, transport and telecommunications networks, the outermost regions should no longer be separated from the priorities and objectives of the programmes. it is important to take into account the geographical, economic and climatic particularities of the outermost regions when implementing the common agricultural policy, the common fisheries policy and the internal market and when laying down the rules for their functioning. in the implementation of the union's trade, external and development policies, the overseas regions should be as important as the rest of the eu.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is a contribution by parliament to the debate on the renewal of regional cohesion policy. the aim of this parliament's report is to propose a different approach to the vision that has been set out and is now coming to fruition, so that investment in the outermost regions can be extended to new areas and can be developed in a sustainable way. the communication calls not only for the benefits of the union's overseas regions to be welcomed and recognised, but also for them to be exploited in practice. in the context of the implementation of the 'horizon 2020' programme, the 'energy 2020' strategy, the life+ and erasmus programmes, the trans-european energy, transport and telecommunications networks, it is proposed that the outermost regions should no longer be separated from priorities and objectives. it is noted that the geographical, economic and climatic particularities of the outermost regions will have to be taken into account in the future in the implementation of the common agricultural policy, the common fisheries policy, the internal market and the rules for their functioning. without the cap, the cap and the internal market, the outermost regions will not be able to integrate sustainably and in a coherent manner into the european union.
in writing. - the outermost regions are a strong asset for the eu. the support of the outermost regions by the eu can be beneficial for the whole eu as it can create a basis for overcoming the challenges that the eu is facing now in terms of sustainability and regional development. the outermost regions open many perspectives to the eu and contain a lot of potential for increasing cohesion and economic growth throughout the eu. that is why the proposed set of measures aimed at creating synergies between the european structural and investment and other european union programmes is needed. such synergies can unveil the potential of the outermost regions and can open up many opportunities for them. i welcome this report and its importance for the development of the outermost regions. many issues mentioned in the report are also relevant for lithuania. synergies and international cooperation have to be promoted in order to diminish the substantial economic and social disparities between the regions. long-term investment should be encouraged and investment promoted in order to ensure that the outermost regions become regions of excellence. in that case the eu will be able to benefit from it as a whole.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to present an evaluation of the union's financial performance. the budgetary discharge instrument has been supplemented by a new instrument, the performance-based evaluation of the union's finances. the commission shall prepare an annual report in this respect. this evaluation report focuses on the financial results. this change will facilitate the transition from activity-based to results-based budgeting. the 2011 communication only summarises the evaluations of the various programmes and periods and does not provide a comprehensive assessment of the results achieved by the commission in implementing the policy in 2011. the evaluation carried out by the ec should be a source of information for the court of auditors. i agree that the court should carry out an annual audit of the evaluation process by the ec. it is also important that the commission defines in its management plan a range of objectives and indicators that meet the requirements of the court of auditors in terms of relevance, comparability and reliability of information, thus creating a new organisational culture based on results.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is a contribution by parliament to the development of a new instrument for the discharge of the eu budget: the performance-based evaluation of the union's finances. in its communication, parliament stresses that activity-based budgeting remains the fundamental principle to be followed in drawing up the union budget. regrets, therefore, that, for example, the proposals for the 2014-2020 programming period on agriculture and cohesion are still largely cost-based and therefore still prioritise compliance with the rules over performance; reiterates its call on the commission to use specific information on the achievements of the member states in its assessment of the union's financial achievements; only in this way will the budgetary authority be able to obtain a clear picture each year of the actual extent to which the union's fundamental objectives have been achieved.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it was the report in which parliament gave its support to the introduction of a european emergency call system in all vehicles, operating on the basis of common european standards using the 112 telephone number. under today's decision, the 'ecall' will be activated automatically when the vehicle's sensors detect a serious collision. the activated system will automatically dial the european emergency number 112, establish a telephone link with the relevant emergency centres and transmit the accident data, including the time of the accident, the exact location of the vehicle involved and the direction of travel, to the rescue services. the ecall system can also be activated manually by pressing a button in the car, for example by a witness to a serious accident.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal for the signing of a political dialogue and cooperation agreement between the european community and costa rica, el salvador, guatemala, honduras, nicaragua and panama. the text aims to strengthen political dialogue and the process of economic cooperation, and to promote sustainable development in both regions, by promoting a development partnership involving all stakeholders, including civil society and the private sector. the agreement also seeks to improve cooperation in the field of migration and to pave the way for new initiatives with common objectives and a common framework in areas such as regional integration, poverty reduction and social cohesion, sustainable development, regional security and stability, conflict prevention and resolution, human rights, democracy, good governance, migration and the fight against corruption, illegal immigration, the fight against terrorism, drugs, small arms and light weapons.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the conclusion of the political dialogue and cooperation agreement between the ec and costa rica, el salvador, guatemala, honduras, nicaragua and panama. the eu-central america political dialogue and cooperation agreement aims to develop the previous cooperation established under the 1993 eu-central america framework cooperation agreement. it commits the parties to seek the conclusion of an association agreement, which was signed in june 2012[n] after five years of negotiations. however, the association agreement currently applies only to the trade sector. the remaining sectors require the agreement of all eu member states and are still awaiting approval.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal for the signing of a political dialogue and cooperation agreement between the european community and costa rica, el salvador, guatemala, honduras, nicaragua and panama. the text aims to strengthen political dialogue and the process of economic cooperation, and to promote sustainable development in both regions, by promoting a development partnership involving all stakeholders, including civil society and the private sector. the agreement also seeks to improve cooperation in the field of migration and to pave the way for new initiatives with common objectives and a common framework in areas such as regional integration, poverty reduction and social cohesion, sustainable development, regional security and stability, conflict prevention and resolution, human rights, democracy, good governance, migration and the fight against corruption, illegal immigration, the fight against terrorism, drugs, small arms and light weapons.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the conclusion of the political dialogue and cooperation agreement between the ec and costa rica, el salvador, guatemala, honduras, nicaragua and panama. although the cooperation agreement is broader in scope, covering various aspects such as political dialogue, social and economic cooperation, cultural exchanges, cooperation in education and training, consumer protection, health protection, data protection, scientific and technological cooperation, cooperation in the fields of environment and biodiversity, participation of civil society in cooperation, cooperation on gender equality, cooperation involving indigenous peoples and other central american ethnic groups, this document is aimed at obtaining parliament's consent only on the issues mentioned in article 49 (3) of this agreement the mutual readmission of illegal migrants.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it was the report parliament adopted in favour of the commission's proposal for guidelines for the employment policies of the member states. article 145 of the eu treaty states that the member states and the union shall develop a coordinated strategy for employment, in particular by developing a skilled, professionally prepared and adaptable workforce and labour markets that are responsive to economic change. the commission proposal states that member states should continue to make every effort in the following priority areas: increasing labour market participation and reducing structural unemployment; developing a skilled workforce adapted to labour market needs and promoting good quality work and lifelong learning; improving the functioning of education and training systems at all levels and increasing participation in tertiary education; fostering social inclusion and combating poverty.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish a common system of value added tax. the european commission estimates that vat accounts for around 21% of the national tax revenues of the eu member states and, given that 12% of vat is not collected annually, a more efficient vat system is needed to adequately prevent fraud. the introduction of the standard vat return will reduce the administrative burden of vat collection by up to eur 15 billion per year. i welcome the fact that the commission's proposal aims to increase the efficiency of vat collection and at the same time to reduce bureaucratic obstacles and the related costs for member states' tax authorities and taxable persons. i agree that the information requirements should be limited to the information strictly necessary for the control and collection of vat. by allowing undertakings to submit to the tax authorities of the member states in which they operate a maximum of twenty-six completed standard information boxes using a common, preferably electronic, vat return format, the completion of the vat return could save time for all those involved and help to avoid errors.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the introduction of a standard vat return form. according to european commission estimates, vat represents about 21% of the national tax revenues of the eu member states and, given that 12% of vat is not collected annually, a more efficient vat system is needed to adequately prevent and eliminate the diversity of vat fraud. the introduction of the standard vat return is expected to reduce the administrative burden of vat collection by a maximum of eur 15 billion per calendar year.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to extend the preferential rate of excise duty applied to portugal in the autonomous region of madeira. the 2009 decision authorises portugal to apply until the end of 2013 a reduced rate of excise duty to rum and liqueurs produced and consumed locally in the autonomous region of madeira and to liqueurs and spirits produced and consumed locally in the autonomous region of the azores. the reasons for adopting this measure were the small size of the agricultural holdings, their fragmentation and the low degree of mechanization. in addition, the introduction of certain raw materials and packaging materials into the islands, which are not produced locally, generates additional costs that are not incurred by the introduction of the final product only. i am in favour of extending this authorisation.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the extension of the period of the decision on the application of a reduced rate of excise duty to rum, liqueurs and spirits produced and consumed locally in the autonomous region of madeira and the autonomous region of the azores. the reasons for the reduced rate are the small size, fragmentation and low level of mechanization of agricultural holdings. the 75% preferential tariff does not provide for anything that is not necessary to compensate for the additional costs incurred by economic operators as a result of the specific characteristics of the outermost regions of madeira and the azores.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to introduce a dock levy and apply a differentiated tax regime to france until 1 july 2014 by allowing the exemption from dock levy for certain local products produced in the french overseas departments of guadeloupe, guyana, martinique, mayotte and réunion. the reasons for the special measures are: remoteness, isolation, small market and low exports, difficult topography and climate, economic dependence on a few products and sometimes even natural phenomena such as cyclones, volcanic eruptions and earthquakes. i support the differentiated tax regime in mayo.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the amendment to decision 2004/16/ec authorising france to exempt from dock dues or to reduce them until 1 july 2014 for the local products listed in the annex to that decision produced in the french overseas departments of guadeloupe, guyana, martinique, mayotte and réunion. the reasons for these special measures are remoteness, isolation, a small market and low exports, difficult topography and climate, economic dependence on a few products and sometimes even natural phenomena such as cyclones, volcanic eruptions and earthquakes. the remoteness of these regions and their dependence on air and sea transport, as well as on raw materials and energy, has a so-called domino effect on production costs. all these inconveniences add up to a net increase in the price of locally produced goods, which would not be able to compete with goods produced elsewhere without the special measure. if local products could not compete, it would be impossible to support local production, which would have a negative impact on the employment of the population of those areas.
in writing. - indonesia's economy is the largest in southeast asia. it is also the fourth most populous country in the world and one of the poorest countries in southeast asia. in 2009, the eu and indonesia signed a framework agreement on comprehensive partnership and cooperation (cpc), the eu ratification of which is currently being finalised. the ppp aims to strengthen eu-indonesia relations by developing political and economic cooperation in the fields of trade, industrial policy, energy, science, tourism, migration, counter-terrorism, etc. i support calls for eu member states to extend mutual legal assistance in their anti-corruption relations with indonesia and for indonesia to develop its own national plan for the implementation of the un basic principles on business and human rights. it is also important that the eu and indonesia consider opening negotiations on a free trade agreement with a view to gradually eliminating the main barriers to trade, as well as strengthening consultations on wto compliance, promoting the application of international standards on technical barriers to trade, improving the protection of intellectual property rights, increasing the transparency of trade rules, developing customs cooperation and promoting non-discriminatory investment treatment.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the conclusion of a framework agreement between the ec and indonesia on comprehensive partnership and cooperation, except for readmission issues. the comprehensive partnership and cooperation agreement is the first of its kind between the european union and indonesia and aims to strengthen political, economic and sectoral cooperation of mutual interest, as well as to further strengthen bilateral and regional cooperation on global issues.
in writing. - indonesia's economy is the largest in southeast asia. it is also the fourth most populous country in the world and one of the poorest countries in southeast asia. in 2009, the eu and indonesia signed a framework agreement on comprehensive partnership and cooperation (cpc), the eu ratification of which is currently being finalised. the ppp aims to strengthen eu-indonesia relations by developing political and economic cooperation in the fields of trade, industrial policy, energy, science, tourism, migration, counter-terrorism, etc. the readmission provision establishes a mutual obligation between the eu and indonesia to take back their nationals, which means that their respective national authorities must first establish their identity. i support the suggestions that the commission should provide regular information to the ep at all stages of the preparation, negotiation, conclusion and implementation of eu readmission agreements. the commission should ensure that persons who are persecuted, tortured or punished in indonesia are not returned. it is also important to have control mechanisms in place to ensure that the fundamental rights of returnees are not violated when they return and settle in indonesia.
in writing. - indonesia's economy is the largest in southeast asia. it is also the fourth most populous country in the world and one of the poorest countries in southeast asia. in 2009, the eu and indonesia signed a framework agreement on comprehensive partnership and cooperation (cpc), the eu ratification of which is currently being finalised. the ppp aims to strengthen eu-indonesia relations by developing political and economic cooperation in the fields of trade, industrial policy, energy, science, tourism, migration, counter-terrorism, etc. the readmission provision establishes a mutual obligation between the eu and indonesia to take back their nationals, which means that their respective national authorities must first establish their identity. i support the suggestions that the commission should provide regular information to the ep at all stages of the preparation, negotiation, conclusion and implementation of eu readmission agreements. the commission should ensure that persons who are persecuted, tortured or punished in indonesia are not returned. it is also important to have control mechanisms in place to ensure that the fundamental rights of returnees are not violated when they return and settle in indonesia.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at an agreement between the european union and turkey on the readmission of persons residing without authorisation. turkey is considered to be the main transit country for illegal refugees travelling to the eu. in order to return migrants who have entered the eu illegally, the eu and turkey began negotiating a readmission agreement in 2002. this agreement obliges its parties to take back their nationals and, where appropriate, third-country nationals residing without authorisation in the country concerned, as well as stateless persons. turkey has only initialled the agreement so far, as the turkish side unexpectedly linked the signing of the agreement to the requirement for the eu to develop an action plan on visa liberalisation. the commission's action plan on the gradual liberalisation of the visa regime linked such liberalisation to legal and administrative reforms in turkey related to the protection of personal documents, cooperation with eu border protection authorities and the safeguarding of fundamental rights. i support calls on turkey (after the eu has presented an action plan on visa liberalisation) to keep its promise and sign the agreement without delay. it is also important to link the preparation of the visa liberalisation action plan to strict criteria.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the conclusion of an eu-turkey agreement on the readmission of persons residing without authorisation. the agreement would make a significant contribution to reducing illegal immigration to the european union via turkey. at the same time, it would contribute to the fight against cross-border crime, in particular trafficking in human beings, and relieve a large part of the burden on greece and, at the same time, on the european union as a whole. a readmission agreement obliges its parties to take back their own nationals and, where appropriate, third-country nationals residing without authorisation in the country concerned, as well as stateless persons.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is a report in which parliament makes use of the possibility, provided for in its rules of procedure, of extending the transitional periods for the use of irish, maltese and croatian in parliamentary activities and for ensuring the translation of parliament's documents into these languages. such an extension of the transitional periods until the end of the next term of office is necessary because, despite all the appropriate prior measures, the irish, croatian and maltese translation capacities are not expected to be such as to enable the provision of full interpretation services into these languages from 1 july this year.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the more frequent use of roll-call voting in parliament. following a study commissioned by the committee on constitutional affairs entitled 'democratic control, transparency and voting procedures in national parliaments and in the european parliament', it is proposed to amend the rules of procedure in order to align parliament's methods with the best practices envyed by other european union parliaments. the proposed amendment will also increase transparency and democratic accountability of members to the electorate and citizens. voters have the right to know how their elected representatives vote and to ask them to account for this.
in writing. - i approved this report, but with certain reservations. i supported the alternative proposal, which would allow for the organisation of integrated railway management in a holding company without requiring full decommissioning or unreasonably strict requirements for the interaction between the holding companies. the european parliament supported this alternative, thus giving the member states of the european union the right to decide for themselves on the model of management of railway undertakings, taking into account the specificities of the railways of a particular country, and recognising that the efficiency of railways is determined not by their fragmentation but by the efficiency of work organisation, geographical, technical and technological and other relevant circumstances.
in writing. - unfortunately, some representatives of the european commission and members of the european parliament consider that the dismantling of integrated rail systems in the countries of the european union is not related to the interests of third countries and the threats they pose. i believe that the dismantling of the national railway undertaking in accordance with the provisions of the fourth railway package would open up a wide range of possibilities for the countries of the union of independent states to take over control of the essential freight flows of the national railway undertaking and to transfer the effect currently obtained by the national companies in providing transport services to third countries. this is unacceptable. it is very important to note and point out that, apart from the economic losses in the eu, the transfer of control of the operation of the transport system to third countries would give them a good opportunity to influence the political processes and crisis management in the eu countries.
in writing. - i voted for this report. the cross-border rail passenger market has been open since 1 july 2010. however, domestic passenger transport markets in many member states are still largely closed. public passenger rail transport, like other passenger transport: bus, tram and metro services cannot usually be provided on a commercial basis. instead, they are provided under public service contracts. these service contracts include compensation for public service obligations. the most important element of the commission's proposal is the abolition of the possibility of directly awarding public service contracts for rail transport, as provided for in article 5 (6) of regulation (ec) no 1370/2007/eu. i supported the european parliament's position that the possibility of directly awarding very small-scale rail passenger services contracts should be maintained. in order to ensure fair competition and to avoid the misuse of compensation, the principle of reciprocity should also apply. that principle should apply not only to undertakings established in the member states and in the union but also to undertakings from third countries wishing to participate in public procurement procedures in the union.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal in favour of the exception for the baltic railways, which is dominated by the 1520 mm wide crayfish. this will allow the authorisation of rolling stock and the certification of railway undertakings by the national safety authorities of these states, which have the capacity to assess this specificity and to take decisions contributing to the safety of rail transport.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, which aims to improve rail safety in the eu. the european union agency for railways (era) should develop guidelines which, inter alia, provide best practices in member states and examples of cross-border activities. in order to protect human life (staff, passengers, persons living near the line, third parties), public health and the environment, specific solutions to the issues related to the risks of dangerous or polluting goods are necessary on the basis of a proper and consistent interpretation of the relevant directives (1996/49/ec and 2008/68/ec). it is necessary to set clear time limits for the execution of the procedural steps (decisions to be taken within 3 months from the date of the request at the latest) in order to ensure predictability and efficiency on the basis of the agreement reached on the first railway legislative package (directive 2012/34/eu). the agency should also establish a system for the easy and anonymous communication of potential safety risks, thereby drawing the attention of the agency and the national safety authorities.
in writing. - i agree with the ep's position, as i believe that the reform of the railway sector must be completed as soon as possible. however, i do not think that it is necessary to delegate all powers to the european railway agency in order to regulate certain railway matters (e.g. the operation of rolling stock only in the territories of the member states). this could be in breach of the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to strengthen and improve the active system of aviation accident prevention by rapid analysis and exchange of information. the substantial increase in air traffic by 2030 requires the strengthening of preventive aviation safety measures to avoid an increase in aircraft accidents in line with the increase in traffic. in some member states where whistleblowers are not adequately protected, aviation workers still fear legal action or penalties from their employers. the enhanced protection of the reporting person will allow for the strengthening of this trust. it is envisaged that staff tasked with collecting, assessing, processing, analysing and storing reports will work separately and independently from other parts of the organisation to ensure confidentiality of data and anonymity of the reporting person. the appeal mechanism will now be available to employees who have been penalised for information disclosure. the member states and the european aviation safety agency will be able to better exchange the information collected through the use of an anonymous and secure database european central repository. it is important to enable member states to know the precise circumstances of an incident in national airspace.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the strengthening of preventive aviation safety measures. the increase in air traffic is projected to continue until 2030[n], and therefore the strengthening of preventive aviation safety measures is necessary to avoid an increase in aircraft accidents in line with the increase in traffic. this regulation extends in principle the protection of the whistleblower, as in some member states where whistleblowers are not sufficiently protected, workers in the aviation sector still fear legal action or penalties from their employers. this regulation also strengthens the rules on confidentiality, so that by ensuring the anonymity of staff, for example by limiting the number of experts with access to personal data, the information collected will be used for the sole purpose of enhancing aviation security.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to introduce new rules requiring car manufacturers to install an ecall automated emergency call system in new car models from october 2015. in the event of a serious accident, this system would automatically collect the single european emergency telephone number 112 and transmit the incident information to the emergency services. this would allow rescuers to reach the scene of an accident faster, save lives, and reduce the severity of injuries. each year, emergency services have to respond to traffic accidents, which in 2012 killed 28 000 people and injured 1.5 million. since this system is a public service, i agree that it should be provided free of charge. i also agreed to other rules requiring the european union countries to have the infrastructure ready to receive ecall signals by october 2015.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is the report by which parliament endorses the mandatory introduction of ecall in new vehicles from 2015[n 1]. ecall will be used to ensure that the communication system of the vehicle involved in an accident establishes a connection with the emergency services. in the event of an accident, the system will automatically collect the single european emergency telephone number 112 and transmit the vehicle's location to the emergency services. in order to ensure efficient transmission of ecalls, member states and mobile network operators will need to modernise their infrastructure. it is estimated that installing ecall in a new car will cost less than €100, but will save around 2500 lives a year.
in writing. - i voted to postpone this report in order to refer it back to the committee on economic and monetary affairs. the purpose of the notification is to enhance consumer protection in financial services. it is proposed to introduce new consumer-friendly investment information standards, stricter standards for advice and certain rules for investment funds to make funds safer. more than 70% of insurance products are sold without proper advice. the aim of this proposal is to improve the quality of information provided to consumers intending to invest. the aim is to inform consumers in an easily understandable form by setting a new innovative product information standard information will have to be presented in short and simple words, thus making it more understandable to the consumer. information will be provided on the main features of the product and the risks and costs of investing in that product. it is proposed that the obligations and liabilities of the depositary (the entity that safeguards assets) be clear and uniform across the eu. it is important to harmonise the requirements for the sale of insurance across the eu and to prevent the provision of incorrect and misleading information.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to introduce new ambitious health and consumer programmes. the new programmes will be implemented in the period 2014-2020. the health programme is allocated eur 446 million and the consumer programme eur 197 million. the aim of the proposed programmes is to develop healthy, active, informed and self-aware european citizens who can contribute to economic growth. the objective of the 'health for growth' programme is to develop innovative and sustainable health systems, to increase citizens' access to better healthcare and to improve health and prevent disease. the consumer programme will be supported by eu consumer policy: by ensuring greater product safety, by improving consumer awareness, education and rights awareness, and by strengthening consumer rights and ensuring effective redress mechanisms. it is important to avoid duplication of programmes and to make the best use of the financial resources allocated to them. i agree that the ageing population and age-related diseases should be better integrated into the health programme.
in writing. - i'm in favor of this document. while health protection is primarily a matter for the member states, i am convinced that some action is also needed at eu level, especially when it comes to funding or the high research potential. i am pleased that the document adopted as the eu's health priority contains some really important elements: a greater focus on the diagnosis and treatment of neurodegenerative diseases, as well as promoting well-being and health education.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish guidelines for broadband networks and digital services infrastructure in the telecommunications sector, i.e. the objectives and priorities envisaged in the context of the european infrastructure networks instrument. the guidelines identify projects for the deployment of broadband networks and digital services infrastructure of general interest. these projects will promote the interconnection and interoperability of national networks and will also promote the development of the digital single market. the objective is to achieve the targets set in the digital agenda for europe by 2020: universal coverage and 30 mbps or at least 50% of households using speeds above 100 mbps. it is essential to pursue the objective of the digital agenda for europe to ensure that the digital single market based on high-speed internet delivers sustainable economic and social benefits. it is also necessary to emphasise the importance of cybersecurity in ensuring consumer protection.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to introduce rules to ensure that public authorities' websites are accessible to all. the rules would make it easier for over 100 million eu citizens to access public services online, such as looking for a job, registering a car, submitting a tax return and submitting documents for a passport or driving licence. the proposal sets mandatory standardised accessibility conditions for 12 types of websites in the eu. the compulsory accessibility condition would apply to key services provided by public authorities, such as social insurance and health-related services, job search, university applications and the issuance of identity documents and certificates. the 80 million europeans with disabilities and the 87 million europeans over 65 would benefit the most. visually impaired persons will be able to listen to the visual descriptions using a screen reader, and hearing impaired persons will be able to see the information in the written audio files. only one third of the 761 000 websites of the european public sector and public authorities are fully accessible. i support the proposals set out to extend the scope of the directive and to implement additional measures in the field of monitoring, reporting and implementation. i agree that member states should designate a competent authority responsible for monitoring, which would be tasked with taking action on complaints from users about non-compliance with the web accessibility requirements of the websites concerned.
in writing. - in the age of the internet, more and more important services are being provided electronically. the web design industry, which employs around one million people, has a turnover of around eur 144 billion. more than 761 000 public sector websites in the eu provide access to information and services, but less than 10% of them are accessible to people with disabilities. a previous declaration adopted by the eu council stated that all public websites should be accessible by 2010. although member states have already adopted legislation or other measures on website accessibility, barriers to the internal market remain, resulting from very different national approaches across europe. i agree with the rapporteur that public sector bodies and all entities providing essential services to the public need to have integrated web accessibility practices in order to fulfil their obligations to all citizens. i voted in favour of this resolution because it calls on eu member states to establish a comprehensive list of types of websites operated by entities providing essential services to the public and to gradually implement the eu directive at national, regional and local level by 2020.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it is a contribution by parliament to the debate on the long-term financing of the european economy. long-term investment is crucial for economic growth and job creation. without them, it is impossible to successfully launch or carry out major projects in the fields of infrastructure, research and technological development and innovation. almost all european countries are currently pursuing a policy of fiscal consolidation in order to manage their debt. reliable fiscal policy instruments are the key, but it is crucial for europe to start developing a sustainable growth trend that would strengthen its competitiveness vis-à-vis other regions of the world and ensure that jobs are created. commercial banks, whether private, savings or cooperative banks, will remain one of the main sources of financing in most countries, but alternatives are needed to fill the financing gap and complement the traditional banking intermediation process. a regulatory framework should be established to create investment vehicles that focus and channel reliable and sustainable funding from multiple sources towards long-term investment.
in writing. - 42 million people, mostly women, are engaged in prostitution worldwide. some eu countries see prostitution as rooted in gender inequality, economic exclusion and poverty. prostitution is the trade in women and children. other eu countries regulate that women can do whatever they want with their bodies. studies show that few women choose to become prostitutes. young women are often trafficked into the eu prostitution network, where they are forced to use drugs and are exploited economically. there are not many statistics. the rapporteur, meri hanibol, a social democrat, calls on the eu countries to adopt the swedish model, where the purchaser of sexual services would also be a criminal. it is the only legally effective means of combating prostitution.
