in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to ensure a responsible role for business in the extractive industry. the exploitation of valuable natural resources, including oil, gas, minerals and timber, is a source of many conflicts worldwide. a global strategy is needed to address the key causes of conflict in mining and to better manage the environmental, social and human rights impacts of mining. mining activities have a negative social impact. forced evictions are commonplace in mining operations. mining activities compete for space with other land uses such as farming, which may affect the livelihoods of local communities, etc. international corporations should take social responsibility for this. i support the proposals for action to regulate the risks posed by european companies extracting natural resources from other conflict-affected and high-risk areas. the existing certification schemes for minerals are a step forward towards more transparent supply chains involving mining and processing companies. the eu must adopt legislation requiring european businesses to carry out thorough supply chain checks to ensure that they do not contribute to the financing of conflicts or human rights abuses through the extraction and trafficking of natural resources.
the political crisis in ukraine, which has escalated into bloody clashes between the authorities and the public, has become a test of policy options a test of our policy. today we should not be afraid to admit that the blood of the dead is also being spilled on the insufficiently resolute policies of our union. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are protected. the peace in ukraine today remains fragile and efforts by all the institutions of the union are needed to do everything possible to prevent a renewed outbreak of fighting. violence, no matter from which side it arises, can have no justification. european politicians must not speak but act to bring down tensions in ukraine and to start the process of unification. representatives of the european parliament and other institutions could form a special working group to act as an impartial mediator and adviser in order to form a new government in ukraine without oligarchs, as maidan wanted and wants. europe must be active at international level in order to preserve the territorial integrity of ukraine.

in writing. mr president, the european union and some member states still have a high level of violations of fundamental rights, as evidenced by the european commission's reports. all citizens of the european union must be guaranteed their fundamental freedoms. the protection of national minorities and national and regional languages in the enlarged union is a major problem today, as, despite the signed conventions, multilingualism is being combated in some countries. the report raises a number of important points which are to be welcomed. for example, the call to prohibit discrimination against persons belonging to national minorities and to fight against linguistic discrimination. the rapporteur expressed concern that members of minority communities face obstacles in the areas of justice, education and culture, which result in violations of their rights. moreover, they are treated as second-class citizens by the public authorities of their home country. the author of the report rightly calls on the member states to ensure that persons belonging to national minorities are not discriminated against in the national legal system. calls also for appropriate measures to promote relevant international standards based on the council of europe framework convention for the protection of national minorities. and, most importantly, it called on the commission to develop policy standards for the protection of national minorities, including indigenous and linguistic communities, given that they represent over 10% of the eu population. it stressed the need for a comprehensive union system for the protection of traditional national minorities, regional language groups, together with a mechanism to monitor its functioning. the abovementioned entries in the report shall be put to the vote.
i'm here about the european arrest warrant. i welcome the rapporteur's position that the procedures for implementing the european arrest warrant must be clearer and that the countries should follow the same standards. it was precisely the disparate standards that led to the painful case a few years ago, when one eu country refused, on procedural grounds, to extradite a suspected perpetrator of crimes against humanity and war crimes to another eu country. i am referring to the case of mikhail golovatov and the fact that he was not extradited to the state of lithuania, where he allegedly committed crimes against humanity on 13 january 1991. it is regrettable that the report does not address the appropriateness of the exception whereby extradition under the european arrest warrant is only carried out in cases of crimes committed after 2002[n 1]. in my view, this exception should not apply, in particular, to serious crimes, i.e. crimes against humanity and war crimes, which are not subject to the statute of limitations under international law. the european commission has also invited the member states to consider this, and croatia, the new member state of the european union, has been obliged not to apply this exception. i would like to ask commissioner reding, if i may, what do you think about the time limitation that the crimes only apply after 2002?
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to create a common european law on sales contracts. differences in the contract law of member states create obstacles for traders and consumers wishing to engage in cross-border commercial transactions in the internal market. the barriers created by these differences have discouraged traders, especially smes, from engaging in cross-border trade. this proposal envisages the creation of a single european law on sales contracts, which will harmonise the national contract law of the eu, not by requiring the replacement of the existing national contract law, but by creating in the national law of each eu a second contract law framework for the contracts falling within its scope, which will be uniform throughout the eu and will operate alongside the existing national contract law rules. the common european law on sales contracts will be applied voluntarily to crossborder contracts by explicit agreement between the parties. i support the proposals made to make it clear that european contract law is the second legal system operating in each eu legal system. it is also important to clarify fully that the agreement to apply the common european law of sales contracts cannot be confused with a choice between two legal systems, but is a choice between two different contract law systems provided for by the same national law.
in writing. - the european arrest warrant is considered an important eu achievement in the area of criminal justice, facilitating cooperation between eu member states. while the report emphasises the need for greater clarity in the european arrest warrant procedures and for uniform standards in the member states, it must be acknowledged that there are many problems with the practical application of the european arrest warrant. on the one hand, the european arrest warrant is used frequently and disproportionately in some countries. on the other hand, there are eu countries which invoke the exception of non-application of the european arrest warrant procedures for offences committed before 2002. such a misuse limits the effectiveness of the european arrest warrant and, at the same time, raises legitimate doubts about the credibility of the european arrest warrant and the eu criminal justice system. i believe that no exceptions can be made for particularly serious crimes, such as war crimes or crimes against humanity.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to update the rules on the production and sale of tobacco in order to reduce the attractiveness of its products to young people. smoking remains the leading preventable cause of death in the eu, killing around 700 000 people a year. over the past decade, the number of smokers in the eu has fallen from almost 40% in 2002 to 28% in 2012. the proposal aims to ban all elements in cigarettes that are described as characteristic of their smell or taste. products with a characteristic smell or taste are chocolate, fruit or mint tobacco products. the use of other additives, such as vitamins, which may give the impression that the product is useful or not so harmful to health, is also prohibited. it also requires the use of visual and text warnings covering 75% of the front and back of all cigarette packs. the use of descriptions such as 'flat cigarettes', 'natural', 'organic' and misleading colours is prohibited. a single pack of cigarettes should contain at least 20 cigarettes. the use of lipstick-shaped packages would be prohibited for these measures. it is proposed to set the minimum size of a tobacco bag for rolling cigarettes at 40 g. many stricter provisions on traceability and security to tackle the problem of illicit trade in cigarettes.
in writing. - the new tobacco control measures are a big step forward. i agree with this report. this will help to prevent a new generation of smokers. it is no secret that many children and young people start smoking at an unconsciously early age: the vast majority of smokers start before their 18th birthday. the tobacco directive is the least we can do for the younger generation. i also support the banning of the production and sale of tobacco with flavours harmful to health. women are now the largest consumers of flavoured tobacco products, especially menthol. finally, it was officially recognised that flavoured tobacco products increase addiction to tobacco products, are more harmful and are more likely to encourage smoking. i agree that e-cigarettes should be subject to the same advertising restrictions as all tobacco products. i believe that this new long-running battle in the european parliament over tobacco production and distribution will reduce the number of deaths from tobacco, which is currently huge in the eu - as many as 700,000 a year. the agreements currently reached are, first and foremost, an important step towards a healthier society.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it reiterates parliament's call on the commission to further increase the capacity of solvit and to enable citizens to obtain more and more detailed information on their rights in the european union. the solvit online network not only provides individuals and businesses with more detailed information about their rights in the eu single market and the rules in force in the various member states, but also provides them with the necessary assistance in dealing with cross-border problems. analysis of the cases examined by solvit reveals numerous examples of over-regulation, late or incorrect implementation of legislation or national rules that are contrary to eu law. it is through solvit that citizens and businesses often bring cases of poor enforcement to the attention of public authorities. the potential of solvit to ensure the smooth functioning of the single market as a whole is therefore very high, and parliament, in its report, calls on the commission to take more active action to take advantage of this knowledge and findings.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it was the report parliament adopted in favour of the directive on the european investigation order in criminal matters. the european investigation order (eio) is a comprehensive system for the collection of evidence in cases of a cross-border nature. the eu instruments adopted so far in cross-border criminal cases, such as the preservation order or the european evidence order adopted in 2008, have not created a coherent and clear framework. the directive adopted by parliament today will create a single and coherent mechanism for all investigative measures aimed at gathering evidence. the european investigation order will be issued for one or more specific investigative measures to be carried out in the executing state and will involve the taking of evidence already in the possession of the executing authority. the european investigation order is an important part of the area of freedom, security and justice, which in a certain sense complements the area of free movement of persons in the european union with elements of security and justice.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the amendment of the regulation establishing the list of third countries whose nationals must be in possession of visas when crossing the external borders and the list of third countries whose nationals are exempt from this requirement. this amendment adds moldova to the list of countries which are exempt from the visa requirement when crossing the external borders of the eu. the commission has proposed to lift visa requirements for moldovan citizens, given that the country has made significant progress in its dialogue with the eu on visa liberalisation over the past three and a half years. at the eastern partnership summit in vilnius in november 2013, moldova initialled the association agreement with the eu and became the first eastern partnership country to fully implement the requirements of the visa liberalisation action plan, which provides the conditions for the implementation of visa liberalisation.
in writing. - today, the european parliament adopted a very important decision, approving a visa-free regime for moldovan citizens with biometric passports. moldova has become the first eastern partnership country to successfully implement the visa liberalisation action plan and its citizens will soon be able to travel visa-free to the schengen area. this is a very important step towards closer relations between the eu and moldova. i would like to congratulate moldova for completing all its homework quickly and with confidence. i hope that the remaining approval procedures on the eu side will be carried out without delay and that soon ordinary moldovan citizens will benefit from the visa-free regime and that the remaining eastern partnership countries with which visa dialogues are ongoing will follow moldova's example.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its consent to the conclusion of an agreement with indonesia on forest law enforcement, forest management and trade in timber products imported into the eu. the objective of this agreement is to establish a legal framework to ensure the traceability of timber products imported into the eu, and to establish a verification procedure to confirm that timber products brought to the eu market are lawfully sourced, harvested, transported and exported, and to ensure good governance and legal exploitation of indonesian forests. this agreement also establishes the eu forest law enforcement, governance and trade (flegt) action plan. the conclusion of this agreement is an important step for the eu in its efforts to improve sustainable forest management worldwide, halt deforestation and forest degradation, and promote systemic change in the forestry sector by rewarding conscientious operators who source their timber from legitimate and reliable sources and protecting them from unfair competition.

in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it contains parliament's recommendations to the commission on improvements to the european arrest warrant system, which has been in place since 2004. the purpose of the european arrest warrant is to speed up and facilitate the prosecution of suspects and accused persons by replacing the previously complex extradition procedure, which included political actors, with a new, more rapid system of surrender based on the interaction of judicial authorities. in the application of the european arrest warrant, the surrender is accelerated by setting strict time limits and by limiting the application of the reciprocal penalty to provide a ground for refusal to execute the warrant. however, despite the generally positive evaluation over the last decade, weaknesses in the functioning of the system have been identified, which parliament calls for to be addressed in this report. this is necessary to ensure that the credibility of the european arrest warrant and of eu criminal justice initiatives in general is not undermined.
in writing. - the european arrest warrant is considered an important eu achievement in the area of criminal justice, facilitating cooperation between eu member states. while the report emphasises the need for greater clarity in the european arrest warrant procedures and for uniform standards in the member states, it must be acknowledged that there are many problems with the practical application of the european arrest warrant. on the one hand, the european arrest warrant is used frequently and disproportionately in some countries. on the other hand, there are eu countries which invoke the exception of non-application of the european arrest warrant procedures for offences committed before 2002. such a misuse limits the effectiveness of the european arrest warrant and, at the same time, raises legitimate doubts about the credibility of the european arrest warrant and the eu criminal justice system. i believe that no exceptions can be made for particularly serious crimes, such as war crimes or crimes against humanity.

in writing. - i voted for this report because it is a report by parliament which seeks to consolidate knowledge, recommendations and initiatives relating to the social and economic role of women in the fisheries and aquaculture sector. despite the social and economic importance of women in the fisheries and aquaculture sector, there is little specific reference in plans, programmes and fisheries legislation to the need to ensure equal opportunities for women in fisheries-related jobs. women's work in the fisheries sector and related activities is often invisible and complementary to the work of partners, as economic conditions for family businesses become increasingly difficult. in such cases, women receive neither a fixed salary nor social benefits. in fact, for some women, it is their only source of income. one of the recommendations made by parliament is to ensure that women and young people working in the fisheries sector are able to participate in continuing vocational training in order to acquire more knowledge and opportunities for advancement in the fisheries labour market and to participate in all the organisations representing them.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to improve statistics on the carriage of goods by inland waterways. the regulation on the carriage of goods lays down general rules for community statistics on inland waterway transport. the proposal aims to align the provisions of the regulation with those of the treaty of lisbon. a successful policy to enhance the role of inland navigation in the transport sector should be based on objective, comparable and reliable statistical data. i agree that it is important to support support the increased use of inland waterways for freight and passenger transport, as this is the cleanest form of transport. ships used on inland waterways are often old and would have an additional environmental benefit if they were refurbished or renewed. statistics should be necessary to allow better assessment of the fleet condition and renewal indicators. data on waterway infrastructure should also be provided.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish new rules to ensure high quality comparable statistics for the macroeconomic imbalances procedure (mip). these rules will aim to ensure that the data used by the mdp are comparable, reliable and of the highest quality, so as to enable the early and effective identification and correction of macroeconomic imbalances in the member states. eurostat shall monitor the quality of the statistical data transmitted by the member states. the european commission's proposal also provides for penalties in the event of any member state intentionally providing incorrect mdp data. if there is any doubt about the compliance with eu statistical standards, eurostat will be able to visit a specific member state. states that intentionally provide misleading mip statistics may be subject to a dissuasive penalty not exceeding 0,05% of their gdp.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to ratify the nagoya protocol on access to genetic resources and the fair and equitable sharing of benefits arising from their utilization. the nagoya protocol has already been signed by 92 countries, including 26 eu member states. the parties undertake to ensure benefit-sharing, the fair and equitable sharing of the benefits arising from the utilization of genetic resources and the subsequent application and commercialization of the results. the benefit may be monetary, such as a percentage payment for the use of the property, or non-monetary, such as the sharing of research results or the transfer of technology. the nagoya protocol will enter into force shortly after it has been ratified by 50 states.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this recommendation because it is important that the european union and all its member states ratify the nagoya protocol as soon as possible in order to maintain its leading role and to participate immediately in the negotiations that will take place during the other countries' conference. the nagoya protocol of 2014 is an international treaty on access to genetic resources and the fair and equitable sharing of benefits arising from their use. it has been signed by 92 countries, including the european union and 26 member states. it establishes an international framework to support one of the three objectives of the convention on biological diversity - sharing the benefits arising from the utilization of genetic resources, in order to conserve biological diversity and sustainable use of resources. this important international agreement on biodiversity management is the result of lengthy negotiations following the adoption of the convention on biological diversity in rio de janeiro in 1992. it is an agreement at a time of global biodiversity crisis, which is deepening with the loss of living organisms. one in three species is threatened with extinction today, and ecosystems, complex systems that are important to humans for their functions, are increasingly affected by human activities. to reverse this trend, it is necessary to mobilise the necessary resources, in particular financial resources, at international and local levels, in order to preserve biodiversity that is important to humans. the nagoya protocol contributes to this objective.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to allocate eur 840 000 for the integration into the labour market of workers made redundant in 198 enterprises active in the production of textile products in spain. in 2013 spain submitted an application egf/2013/008 eu/comunidad valenciana, textiles for a financial contribution from the egf, following redundancies in 198 enterprises active in the textile industry in the region of valencia in spain. the expiry of the wto's 10-year mandatory agreement on textiles and clothing in 2004 opened the eu textile market to global competition, in particular from china and other far eastern countries. over the period, imports of textiles into the eu increased by 17%, while exports of textiles from the eu to the rest of the world decreased by 3%. the package of tailor-made services aimed at re-entering the labour market for redundant workers will include information meetings, profiling, career guidance, training, entrepreneurship support, etc.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to strengthen the rules on anti-money laundering. a united nations report estimates that money laundering flows worldwide currently account for 2.7% of global gdp (us$1.6 trillion in 2009). the proposed regulation aims to increase the transparency of all types of transfers of funds and to facilitate the tracking of funds by law enforcement authorities. the updated anti-money laundering rules propose to publish the true owners of companies and funds registered in eu countries in public registers. i agree that this should make it more difficult to conclude dubious transactions and prevent tax evasion. i agree that the new rules should also apply to the casino business, but the inclusion of other services in the gambling sector in these rules should be left to the eu member states. i believe that the new rules will successfully strengthen the supervision of banks, financial institutions, auditors, lawyers, accountants, notaries, tax advisers, asset managers and real estate agents.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to strengthen the rules on anti-money laundering. the new directive aims to improve the current system to prevent the proceeds of crime from being converted into legal tender through the financial system. i agree that the functioning of business registers should be improved. in order to prevent money laundering and terrorist financing, it is essential to identify the beneficial owner of the business or the beneficial owner of the business transaction. the company must know who its customer is and identify who the ultimate beneficiary is. it is also important to ensure the interoperability of registers. the registers should be interconnected and accessible to public authorities. it is also important that the preventive approach is targeted and proportionate and does not become a general control system for the whole population. the fight against money laundering and terrorist financing must be conducted in compliance with data protection standards.
in writing. - the eu economy loses €1 trillion a year to tax avoidance. this report provides guidance on how eu countries can better identify and tackle money laundering and terrorist financing threats. i voted for the report because it proposes to require banks, financial institutions, auditors, lawyers, accountants and estate agents to monitor suspicious transactions from customers more closely. these rules would also apply to the casino business, while eu countries could decide for themselves whether to extend them to other gambling services. the directive also requires companies with business links to politicians to obtain the approval of their management for transactions with them and to take measures to trace the origin of their assets or funds. this is currently only applicable to international politicians. the aim is now to include the most important figures in the state - heads of state, ministers and their deputies, judges of the supreme and constitutional courts or parliamentarians, as well as their family members.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to introduce stricter vehicle inspection rules to improve road safety. more than 5 people die every day on european roads in accidents caused by technical failures. technical failures account for 6% of all car accidents, killing 2,000 people a year and injuring many more. studies show that up to 10%[n] of vehicles on the road at any given time are in a state of repair that would not meet the requirements of a roadworthiness test. directive 2009/40/ec lays down minimum standards for the periodic roadworthiness test of motor vehicles. the new proposals aim to save more than 1 200 lives and prevent more than 36 000 accidents related to technical failures per year. i support the proposals to introduce mandatory roadworthiness tests for scooters and motorcycles and to increase the frequency of roadworthiness tests for older vehicles. it is also important to establish a more frequent inspection of very large cars and vans. they should be inspected in the same way as other vehicles travelling many kilometres, such as taxis, ambulances, etc. i support the proposals to improve the quality of vehicle inspections by establishing common minimum standards for defect assessment, equipment and inspectors. it is important to take strict measures against counterfeiting of the readings of the row counter.
in writing. - i voted for this proposal, which is about road safety. it lays down minimum requirements for roadworthiness tests for motor vehicles, their registration documents and for roadworthiness tests for commercial vehicles. directive 1999/37/ec on the registration documents for vehicles lays down requirements relating to the issue of registration certificates, their general recognition and minimum harmonisation of their content. i agree with the proposals to define more precisely the place of registration of vehicles and to provide for the suspension of the registration certificate. suspension ensures that vehicles posing a direct risk to road safety are not allowed to be used on the road, i.e. their registration is suspended until they have been successfully subjected to a subsequent roadworthiness test.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to introduce stricter vehicle inspection rules to improve road safety. more than 5 people die every day on european roads in accidents caused by technical failures. technical failures account for 6% of all car accidents, killing 2,000 people a year and injuring many more. studies show that up to 10%[n] of vehicles on the road at any given time are in a state of repair that would not meet the requirements of a roadworthiness test. directive 2009/40/ec lays down minimum standards for the periodic roadworthiness test of motor vehicles. the new proposals aim to save more than 1 200 lives and prevent more than 36 000 accidents related to technical failures per year. i support the proposals to introduce mandatory roadworthiness tests for scooters and motorcycles and to increase the frequency of roadworthiness tests for older vehicles. it is also important to establish a more frequent inspection of very large cars and vans. they should be inspected in the same way as other vehicles travelling many kilometres, such as taxis, ambulances, etc. i support the proposals to improve the quality of vehicle inspections by establishing common minimum standards for defect assessment, equipment and inspectors. it is important to take strict measures against counterfeiting of the readings of the row counter.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to simplify the rules for the production of union rail transport statistics. regulation (ec) no [nbsp ]91/2003 lays down common rules for the production of union rail transport statistics. the data collected under this system shall include freight and passenger transport as well as traffic flows on the railway system and traffic events. the proposal simplifies regulation (ec) no [nbsp ]91/2003 by leaving only the detailed reporting requirements and abandoning the current concept of simplified reports, thus reducing the burden on member states, but without compromising the quality of rail freight and passenger data. i support the proposal to remove the requirement to collect traffic accident data, as these statistics are also collected by the european railway agency.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at the use of invoices in public procurement. the use of e-invoices is an important step towards electronic european public administration (e-government). the use of e-invoicing in public procurement across the eu could save up to eur 2.3 billion. the cost of receiving invoices can be reduced from eur 3050[nbsp] to eur 1 by replacing paper invoices with electronic invoices only. the draft directive on the use of electronic invoices in public procurement proposes to establish a european standard for electronic invoices, which should increase the interoperability of individual electronic invoicing systems. i agree that it is essential to remove the legal uncertainty, excessive complexity and additional operating costs for economic operators who now use different electronic invoices in different member states. the use of e-invoicing in europe needs to be encouraged, as it is currently very low: e-invoicing accounts for only 4¢15[nbsp] % of all invoices. in order to move towards fully electronic procurement, it is necessary to ensure that the use of e-invoicing in public procurement is the rule rather than the exception.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the european investment bank (eib) remains the main institution for shaping and developing european union (eu) policies, ensuring continuous public involvement and offering investment capacity, while ensuring the inclusion and implementation of the europe 2020 flagship initiatives as far as possible. the eib supports the key instruments to promote the growth and jobs goals of the europe 2020 strategy, such as growth-enhancing infrastructure, smart innovation and competitiveness. welcomes the eib's annual report 2012 and calls on the eib to continue to support the eu's long-term priorities of economic and social cohesion, growth and employment, environmental sustainability, climate policy and resource efficiency, by further developing new financial and non-financial instruments aimed at addressing short-term market failures and longer-term structural weaknesses in the eu economy;
in writing. - during the economic crisis, the unemployment rate in the european union rose by millions. in 2012, 26% of women and 23.9% of men lived below the poverty line. millions of women were either unemployed or on half-time, their wages, health and pension contributions were cut. the entry of young women into the labour market has become even more difficult (in some countries one in three young women was not working or studying). many women, especially young ones, had to emigrate in search of a better life. hundreds of thousands of women in the industrial, commercial and social services sectors, where work schedules are intensive and wages are low, are experiencing poverty even while working. i voted in favour of this report because it calls for a more effective fight against discrimination against women at work on grounds of sex, including pay, and for women to be given better opportunities to combine work and family life. i also support the rapporteur's call for increased funding for organisations that fight against poverty and social exclusion of women, prostitution and trafficking in women, and support women who have experienced violence.
in writing. - the economic crisis has put millions of women in europe under the stress of unemployment, reduced earnings and, in some countries, pension and child benefit cuts. poverty and unemployment increased. women had a harder time entering the labour market, and work was often short-term, forcing many women, especially young women, to leave their homelands and go to work abroad to support themselves and their families. these problems are still relevant in lithuania. and phenomena such as poverty and unemployment in my country have triggered a wave of violence against women. in lithuania, 42.6% of married or partnered women have experienced violence at least once by their husbands. compared with the results of studies in other countries, it turned out that in lithuania women are more likely to experience violence not in public places, but in their own home, in the family environment! report by the european institute for gender equality on the beijing platform for action on violence against women. victim support (2012) showed that the economic crisis has triggered an upsurge in violence against women. it is therefore clear that the economic crisis, the increase in poverty and unemployment have triggered a gender equality crisis. it was very important to acknowledge that. i support this report and congratulate my colleague, inês zuber, on her excellent work.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the creation of the european system of financial supervisors (esfs) has improved the quality and consistency of financial supervision in the internal market. this is a continuously evolving process, during which the values and interests of the european union should be the primary focus of the members of the supervisory board. the economic and financial crisis has shown that poor risk management, inefficient, uneven and divergent supervision of financial markets have increased financial instability and have undermined consumer protection in financial services. the european parliament supported the creation of the european supervisory authorities (esas), envisaged giving the esas more powers of coordination and direct supervision, and considered that these institutions play a key role in creating more stable and secure financial markets. however, the european parliament believes that the european union needs stronger and better coordinated supervision at eu level. the european parliament has therefore made detailed recommendations on how to strengthen the functioning and efficiency of the european financial supervisory systems.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because transparency is essential for a democratic union of citizens, in which they can fully participate in the democratic processes. transparent administration is in the interests of citizens, the fight against corruption and the legitimacy of the union's political system and legislation. the eu must continue to work to increase the transparency of its administrative and legislative processes. citizens are demanding more transparency. new technologies have given rise to many interesting new initiatives that provide a glimpse into the workings of the eu institutions. however, too often the public has to rely on leaks and whistleblowers to find out about institutional misconduct. it's a very bad thing for trust. as the case law shows, the institutions often still make exceptions to transparency and do so in a general way rather than on specific grounds. the possibility of partial access to documents is often not considered. official documents are also often overly classified and institutions request closed meetings without a good reason. the independent body should be entrusted with the task of supervising the classification of documents and requests for the preparation of closed meetings. a quick and easy procedure for objecting to a refusal of access to documents should therefore be provided for, in order to reduce the time and cost of legal proceedings.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to evaluate the activities of the committee on petitions in 2013. since 2009, the committee has received and registered some 10 000 petitions. about 60% of them were found acceptable because they fall within the eu's field of activity. in 2013, the number of registered petitions approached the three thousand mark. that's an increase of nearly 45 percent since 2012, and double that since 2011. the high number of inadmissible petitions is a cause for concern. i agree that the original filter should be re-introduced when the petition is registered in the dg of the directorate-general, as was done before, in 2012. in 2013, a large number of pending petitions accumulated, of which only 30% had been assessed at the time of adoption of this report. i agree that it is important to strengthen administrative capacity as much as possible and to set clear deadlines for the ep's consideration of petitions.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the committee on petitions is a useful and transparent instrument for citizens and residents of the european union, which helps to exercise democratic control and oversight over many aspects of the european union's activities, in particular those relating to the implementation of eu legislation by national authorities. the committee, on the basis of the petitions received, can further contribute to a more coherent and coordinated application of eu legislation on the one hand and to the improvement of future eu legislation on the other, by drawing on the experience gained from the substantive examination of the petitions received. during the current term of office of the european parliament, the committee on petitions has dealt with the challenges of meeting the expectations of the citizens of the european union, and it is therefore important to emphasise that citizens should be directly involved in the work of parliament and present their problems, proposals or complaints to the specific committee members. the committee on petitions, together with other institutions and bodies such as the committees of inquiry and the european ombudsman, performs the functions of an independent and clearly defined point of contact for all citizens. these institutions, together with the european citizens' initiative, are the key tools for building a democratic eu and a pan-european society, and their proper access and sound functioning must therefore be ensured.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the arrangements for the fruit and vegetables sector are part of the common agricultural policy and aim to restore balance in the food supply chain, promote the fruit and vegetables sector, increase competitiveness and support innovation. there are growing difficulties in plant protection, pest control and eradication, which can have a negative impact on the diversity of european agriculture and the quality of vegetables. the european parliament therefore stresses the importance of promoting the eu horticultural sector and enabling it to compete better on the world market by promoting innovation, research and development, energy efficiency and security, climate change adaptation and mitigation and measures to improve marketing. furthermore, efforts should be continued to eliminate the disproportionate relationship between operators and suppliers by increasing the level of concentration in the sector.
in writing. - i voted for this motion to end torture in the world. the eu guidelines on torture continue to be insufficiently implemented. i support the calls for the european external action service (eeas) and the member states to re-encourage the implementation of these guidelines, in particular by setting priorities, best practices and public diplomacy. i support calls for the emcdda, the human rights working group (cohom) and other relevant actors to jointly study eu support for the development and use of npms (independent and effective national prevention mechanisms) and identify best practice. it is important that the prevention of torture is integrated into all actions in the area of freedom, security and justice at eu level.
in writing. - i voted for this report because we must condemn the torture and other ill-treatment that still exists in the world. such heinous crimes must continue to be prohibited anywhere in the world at any time and can never be justified. the european parliament is concerned about the torture of human rights defenders, including community activists, journalists, human rights lawyers and bloggers, in prisons. the most common victims of unlawful imprisonment, intimidation, torture and threats to the family are people, mainly those involved in the struggle for human rights and democracy. parliament called on the eu to combat the various forms of human rights violations, especially those affecting children, which amount to torture. in particular, combating crimes related to trafficking in children, child pornography, child soldiers, child prisoners of war, child labour, cyberbullying, including in orphanages, detention centres and refugee camps. it is therefore necessary to implement effective safeguards to protect children as soon as possible.
in writing. - physical or psychological torture is the worst form of human rights abuse. the number of victims of this uncivilized, inhuman treatment around the world, especially in third countries, is enormous. the report condemns in particular the most cruel form of torture, namely the mutilation of the genital organs. i agree with the rapporteurs that specific european union mechanisms are needed to combat this phenomenon. i voted in favour of this report because it calls on the european union, in cooperation with the united nations, to put pressure on third countries that practice this. they must enact laws prohibiting torture. it is important to protect the most vulnerable sections of society, including women and children. the eu should also combat torture on eu territory. we should start a public awareness campaign about torture.
in writing. - saudi arabia is an important trading partner of the european union, as well as a counter-terrorism partner. the country is one of the world's largest oil consumers. the eu, meanwhile, is saudi arabia's largest trading partner. so there's a lot of interconnectedness and interdependence, and it's growing. cooperation between the two countries has intensified in recent years. in pursuing its interests, the eu should bring human rights violations in saudi arabia to the attention of the saudi leadership. the protection of religious freedom, the discrimination against women must be emphasised. in this country, women will not be able to vote in municipal elections until 2015, and they are not even allowed to drive cars. saudi arabia still carries out the death penalty, including publicly. it is also applicable to minors and foreigners. sadly, torture is also common in this country. the eu, together with international organisations, should put pressure on all these issues. i voted in favour of this report because it calls on the eu to take a more active role in the fight for human rights in saudi arabia, in particular by ensuring the rights of women, who are widely discriminated against and excluded from public life there.
in writing. mr president, the european union is planning to develop a modern and comprehensive framework for the protection of personal data, which should ensure a high level of transparency. this is particularly important in times of rapid technological development and high levels of online activity. let us not forget that an effective system for the protection of personal data in europe should above all contribute to strengthening the security of every european citizen. modern technology allows for the exchange and collection of data on an unprecedented scale. the provision of personal data by natural persons in a public and electronic manner is becoming increasingly common. the union must therefore have a clear say on the quality and extent of the use of the data collected and on the way in which they are processed. several of the issues raised by the proposed directive require further clarification. in particular, the rules on the protection of personal data need to be clearly defined. the specific purposes of the processing of personal data should be clear, compliant with the law and defined at the time of their collection, and limited to the minimum necessary for the purposes of their processing. furthermore, personal data should be processed only where that purpose cannot be achieved by other means. it is essential to prohibit judicial authorities from transferring personal data to private entities in the union, except in situations where a special situation requires it. however, personal data should not be transferred to authorities outside the european union in any way. that way we avoid creating an orwellian society.
in writing. although the free movement of workers within the european union is based on the principle of non-discrimination in the labour market, including the right to receive remuneration for work performed, social, health and labour protection rights, and the right to have family members accompany the worker, in many cases, workers still face discrimination or violations of social rights in real life. one of the shortcomings is that illegal and unlawful work is still too widespread in the european union, and in this case eu workers moving from one member state to another are particularly at risk because of their lack of knowledge of the language and the law. this results in a breach of workers' rights and social guarantees and a loss of resources from the budgets of the member states, thus weakening the possibilities for social policy. in order to avoid exploitation of workers in the labour market, member states must ensure that workers are employed legally, by means of employment contracts and in compliance with all legal provisions laid down by law. member states should also ensure that all relevant information on conditions, social benefits, housing, healthcare, tax benefits and disincentives for the partner of the worker, taxes and benefits relating to childcare and pension rights is widely available and easily accessible in the relevant languages to workers, employers and other interested parties in other member states.
the situation in ukraine today is so tense that at any moment there could be a frenzy that historians will later call war. and we talk too much and we do too little. political dialogue is important, but at this point, i am convinced, it should also take place at the next, the very highest level. i would urge the leaders of the european union to convene a special g8 conference just for that purpose. and the situation calls for solutions that will preserve peace. bilateral or trilateral negotiations are unlikely to yield results. it's been going on too long. for too long, all that has been expressed has been concern. i appeal to the leaders of the nations: the future of the world depends on your decisions. i hope those decisions are wise.
i want to emphasize three points. first, russia's actions in crimea constitute a clear violation of the norms of international law regarding the territorial integrity and sovereignty of the countries. and we have to make that clear once again to the people of ukraine and to the world. secondly, yes, we have to act wisely, seriously, in a coordinated way, to look for opportunities for negotiation, but it is time to take more effective diplomatic, economic and political measures and even sanctions against russia in order to bring peace to the region. we have to ask ourselves whether the suspension of the negotiations on the eu-russia trade agreement and visa regime is really an effective and adequate measure, given that these negotiations have not been held in practice for some time. unfortunately, we have to admit that energy dependence on russia and economic interests often take precedence over peace. thirdly, the urgent need for financial support from the european union to the people of ukraine.
in writing. putin's russia has become a violator of international law, breaking its own agreements and commitments. we cannot allow anyone's imperial ambitions to be put above peace, democracy and the sovereignty of states. putin is using yanukovych as a puppet - perhaps in the same way he dreams of controlling ukraine. this person is publicly mocked, but at the same time, it is said that he is still legitimate. in the meantime, the russian side has no doubts about the legitimacy of the new crimean authorities, which were elected after the armed seizure of the crimean parliament. i think that the relevant international institutions should give their assessment of the law on the annexation of territories to the russian federation that is being prepared in the duma. former so-called 'minister of foreign affairs' of the so-called 'lugansk people's republic'. all possible means are being used to spread putinist propaganda. false rumors were spread that 'euromaidan fighters' were being prepared in lithuania and poland. the eu and media freedom organisations must contribute effectively to the strengthening of media freedom throughout ukraine. sanctions are required against those who act against the territorial integrity and sovereignty of ukraine. and those who continue to trade in military technology must consider whether they are acting against themselves and their allies. i welcome the financial and economic measures contributing to the ukrainian government's reforms and economic rescue, as well as the commission's recent decision to offer economic incentives worth eur 500 million. i think we should have similar tailor-made measures for moldova and georgia.
in writing. russia has violated international law, breached its own signed commitments. all speakers in the european parliament agree. eu leaders and the governments of its member states have taken measures, including financial assistance for ukraine. today, unfortunately, it is not enough to tighten visa requirements or freeze offshore bank accounts. the european union needs to fundamentally rethink its policy towards russia. it is clear that there is no benefit for either side in changing the established economic relationship between the eu and russia, but we must make some sacrifices to bring about real change, otherwise the consequences will cost the eu even more. the eu needs to take decisive and effective decisions on energy relations with russia, and especially on arms trade. ukraine is on the brink of a real war and its security must be the priority for all of us, which is why today we need quick and firm decisions at the level of heads of state.
in writing. - the european parliament notes the efforts of the pakistani government to strengthen relations with the eu, which is seeking to develop cooperation with the country on development and strengthening regional security. the eu has allowed pakistan to benefit from a preferential trade regime since 2014, thus contributing to the strengthening of the pakistani economy. the ep also notes the importance of the may 2013 elections for the consolidation of democracy and civil governance in the country. however, much remains to be done to achieve sustainable democracy and a pluralistic society, to ensure greater social justice, to eradicate extreme poverty, to increase the level of basic education, to improve the education and position of girls and women, to prepare for the effects of climate change, and to strengthen regional cooperation in the fight against trafficking in human beings, drugs and goods. pakistan can and must play a significant role in the fight against terrorism and radicalisation at home and abroad. pakistan's intention to engage in trade in nuclear weapons is of particular concern.
in writing. - it is to be congratulated that we have made significant progress in strengthening bilateral relations. the adoption of the five-year cooperation plan and the launch of the eu-pakistan strategic dialogue demonstrate the commitment of the two sides to further strengthen political and security cooperation. however, it is essential that pakistan properly implements international conventions in the field of human rights, labour rights, as well as environmental and good governance. i believe that we need to continue strengthening democratic institutions, the rule of law, civil control in all areas of public administration, promoting internal and regional security, implementing governance reforms, increasing transparency and intensifying the fight against organised crime. it is also essential to take more effective measures to reduce social injustice and prevent any violation of human rights. only by implementing the provisions of international law will pakistan play a constructive role in strengthening regional stability.
in writing. - in the current context, where russia, in violation of international law and its own commitments, is engaged in military and information aggression against its closest neighbour and a european state, and is conducting unannounced military exercises, including the use of tactical weapons, on the borders of eu and nato member states, cooperation between the eu and the us is of even greater strategic importance. i believe it is high time to strengthen this transatlantic link and cooperation within nato in every way possible. the ballistic missile shield must become one of the key elements of european security and provide a single protective shield for all nato members in europe, without any gaps. i would also like to draw attention to the need for some eu countries that are members of nato to increase the amount of their defence budget to bring it at least closer to the agreed level. unfortunately, the reality is that spending on defence and security is no longer a luxury but a necessity. i also believe that the security impact of cyberattacks should be considered and prepared for.
in writing. - it is essential that member states are protected against possible ballistic missile attacks in the event of a threat. in this context, the strategic partnership with nato in the field of ballistic missile defence should be further developed. it is necessary to ensure a uniform level of security in all member states. i believe that the issue of ballistic missile defence should be addressed at the highest level and included in future security strategies, studies and white papers.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is necessary to develop and strengthen the european union's fisheries sector. the sector faces a number of challenges, including rising fuel costs, labour shortages and quota restrictions. the processing sector suffers from the limited supply of raw materials and competition with products imported from third countries. the aquaculture sector also faces competition from imported products, low prices for certain species, strict environmental rules and conflicts over land use. the eu is the world's largest importer of fishery products. the european union's fisheries and processing industries are vital to ensuring food supplies for eu citizens and livelihoods for coastal communities. securing their future is and must be an important eu policy objective.
in writing. - it is essential that imported fishery products which may disrupt the production and the market of these products in the eu are considered sensitive products. therefore, these products should be subject to strict and consistent rules of origin without any reservations and their accumulation should be strictly limited. furthermore, imports of canned tuna and other fishery products from thailand should be subject to the same conditions of competition as eu fishery products to the greatest extent possible and should continue to be subject to the current customs tariff. on the other hand, it is essential that, where appropriate, a rigorous impact analysis and assessment is carried out. in order to strengthen bilateral cooperation, thailand needs to demonstrate strong respect for human rights, environmental protection, conservation and sustainable use of fisheries resources, the fight against illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing, and compliance with eu sanitary and phytosanitary rules.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because of the importance of nutrition for human health. poor nutrition can be a major cause of obesity, anorexia, heart disease, diabetes, stomach problems, and other ailments. to avoid the enormous and difficult costs of treating diseases and pathologies caused by poor nutrition, even in the most developed societies, it is therefore necessary to integrate nutrition knowledge, taste laboratories, knowledge of nutrition-gastronomy and nutrition-gastronomy culture into the education systems of the various countries. gastronomy and culinary arts have also become an increasingly important form of artistic and cultural expression; that nutrition and proper food are one of the most important foundations of social and family relationships. finally, it is clear that gastronomy has become a very important aspect of tourism promotion and that, in the end, the gastronomic offer is very important for the traveller and the passenger, in addition to other attractive factors. however, for nutrition and food and drink to play their role in modern societies, it is essential to introduce appropriate education and culture in this area.
in writing. - i voted for this report. it is noteworthy that in developed countries, nutrition has become a major cause of diseases and pathologies due to lack of culture and education. poor diet is a major cause of obesity, anorexia, cardiovascular disease, diabetes, stomach problems, and other health problems. to avoid the high costs of treating diseases and pathologies caused by poor nutrition, it is necessary to integrate nutrition knowledge, taste laboratories, knowledge of nutrition-gastronomy and nutrition-gastronomy culture into the education systems of different countries. it is worth noting that gastronomy and culinary arts have become an increasingly important form of artistic and cultural expression. gastronomy has also become a very important aspect of tourism promotion, and the gastronomic offer is of great importance to the tourist and the passenger. therefore, for nutrition and food and drink to play their role in modern societies, it is essential to introduce appropriate education and culture in this area. i think that education in food and gastronomy should be integrated into the education systems of all european countries. it is essential that the eu develops policies to promote education in the field of nutrition and gastronomy.
in writing. - rapid technological advances and the expansion of the range of services provided online have created a need to protect personal data. it is clear that the standards of protection of personal data vary between member states and that it is therefore necessary to seek a common standard that not only allows for adaptation to the changed needs of the digital world, but also enhances consumer confidence in the use of online services by providing more precise information on the reasons for the request for certain personal data, the protection of the data provided and the rights. i support this report and at the same time hope that the amendments it proposes will not only ensure better protection of personal data and consumer rights, but also contribute to strengthening the eu's internal market, reducing the administrative burden in the field of personal data protection.
in writing. - i agree with the report. the updated rules on the protection of personal data will further restrict the transfer of such data to non-eu countries and give them greater control over the processing of their data. i think it is appropriate to tighten the penalties for non-compliance. in addition, companies should be obliged to explain their privacy policy to their customers in a clear and comprehensible manner and should obtain the consent of the individual in each case to process his data. particular attention should be paid to limiting profiling. in order to prevent any discrimination, people should not be profiled solely on the basis of their nationality or race, political or religious beliefs, sexual orientation, genetic data or minor offences. it is essential that the provisions of this regulation ensure that any profiling can only be carried out with the consent of the individual.
in writing. - i support this report on the amendments to the eu's personal data protection rules, as they aim to strengthen citizens' rights to privacy online. the internet, smartphones, and social networks have made it easier than ever to share personal information. it also increases the likelihood that it will be found and used in illegal ways. if the council of the eu approves the proposed amendments, the same data protection rules would apply across the eu. among them is the 'right to be forgotten', which will allow people to better manage the information they have put online in the past. under the new rules, authorities will be allowed to collect no more personal data than necessary for security reasons. the data of europeans will also be better protected against requests from foreign governments, and their provision to third parties without explicit consent will be strictly restricted.
in writing. - i agree with the recommendation. i believe that it is necessary to extend the application of the pericles 2020 programme to the member states which have not adopted the euro as their single currency, as its importance, usefulness and effectiveness are beyond doubt. it should be noted that this programme contributes to enhancing the efficiency of the union's economy and ensuring the sustainability of public finances. the implementation of these measures not only across the eu but also beyond will further strengthen the protection against euro counterfeiting. it should be noted that the effective implementation of the programme will be achieved only through the establishment of a comprehensive and flexible implementation framework. it is gratifying to see that the programme has been implemented effectively and has achieved its intended objectives. its further application and development would therefore ensure greater effectiveness in preventing and punishing euro counterfeiting and would increase the awareness of eu citizens. on the other hand, the results achieved by the programme depend not only on effective action but also on external factors. it should therefore be ensured that the programme is assessed in the light of all aspects affecting euro counterfeiting and fraud.
in writing. - i support the european parliament's decision to give its consent to the conclusion of the eu-azerbaijan visa facilitation agreement and the directly related eu-azerbaijan agreement on the readmission of persons residing without authorisation. these agreements are among the most important in the strengthening of eu relations with azerbaijan, which is a participant in the eastern partnership programme. they will help to strengthen relations not only between countries but also between people.
in writing. - i voted for this recommendation. i believe that it is appropriate to simplify the procedure for issuing visas to citizens of the european union and the republic of azerbaijan. the free movement of people and closer ties between nations are among the key advantages of the eastern partnership programme. the agreement on visa facilitation is a crucial precondition for the stable development of economic, humanitarian, cultural, scientific and other relations. it is noted that azerbaijan is a very important partner of the eu and that this cooperation needs to be strengthened on the basis of common interests. however, visa facilitation should not be an incentive for irregular migration. therefore, particular attention should be paid to security and readmission.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the conclusion of the agreement between the european union and the republic of azerbaijan on the readmission of persons residing without authorisation. this agreement is important in terms of policy and procedures to ensure effective action against illegal immigration. it is noted that the process of strengthening eu-azerbaijan relations will help to expand the boundaries of the area of freedom, security and justice. this agreement will be a step forward in cooperation on mobility. in addition, simplified and faster procedures will help to develop lasting and strong friendly relations. it should be noted that the readmission obligations are based on full reciprocity. both parties should therefore ensure that the agreement is applied in a manner that respects human rights and the obligations and responsibilities set out in the relevant international instruments applicable to the parties.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it is very important to protect children in armed conflicts. children are separated from their families, recruited as soldiers, forced to kill, and exploited in all forms of conflict, with devastating effects on their physical and mental development that affect them for life. while the primary responsibility for the protection of civilians lies with the state, it is also important to emphasise the responsibility and obligation of armed non-state actors to protect civilians under international humanitarian law. this is why the neutral and impartial humanitarian organisation geneva call was set up in march 2000 with the aim of obtaining a commitment from non-state actors to respect the provisions of international humanitarian law. the geneva call, which has been recognised as a success story by the un secretary-general, promotes a constructive dialogue with armed non-state actors to persuade them to change their behaviour and commit to specific humanitarian law, and monitors and supports the implementation of these commitments.
in writing. - recent armed conflicts have increasingly involved one or more non-state actors fighting against government forces or other armed groups, with the civilian population, and especially children, suffering the most from these wars. in addition to the irreparable damage caused by armed conflict to the lives, psychology and development of children and adolescents, the most acute problems are the recruitment of minors and adolescents into armed forces and their exploitation for participation in conflicts, sexual violence, which amounts to war crimes and crimes against humanity, and the death and maiming of children by landmines. the international community must seek and ensure that international norms are respected by all parties to the conflict, whether state or non-state actors.
in writing. - i voted for this report. participation in armed conflict has a significant negative impact on the physical and mental development of children. they are also at greater risk of various and repeated violations of their fundamental rights. in this context, armed non-state actors should undertake greater commitments and sign action plans for the protection of children in armed conflict as soon as possible. the eu has also committed to supporting the implementation of the un charter and the un declaration on the rights of persons with disabilities. they should make greater efforts to prevent the recruitment and forced recruitment of children under the age of 18. on the other hand, eu member states should also join efforts to protect schools from attacks by armed actors and military use. it is very important that more support is given to ngos. in addition, particular attention must be paid to political, diplomatic and financial measures to protect children.
in writing. - one of the principles of eu food policy is the clarity and transparency of the information provided to the consumer. a few years ago we adopted the main piece of legislation in this area - the regulation on consumer information. it will be launched at the end of the year, with some provisions still to be finalised by the european commission. however, such a clarification should not be contrary to the objective of the regulation itself, as we see in the present commission proposal. i therefore support the resolution which seeks to prevent legal contradictions in the field of food policy.
in writing. - i support the motion. in particular, the eu needs to ensure a high level of consumer health and interests protection for final consumers and to allow them the right to choose products on the basis of correct information. in order to ensure that consumers are properly informed, all ingredients in the form of engineered nanomaterials should be clearly identified in the list of food ingredients. it is also essential that the definition of this concept be based on technical and scientific progress. it should be noted that food additives may be present in the form of nanomaterials. in this context, the commission should present a new delegated act which takes into account the position of the parliament and does not confuse safety issues with the general labelling requirements to inform consumers.
in writing. - the european commission has already expressed the need to create a common data protection framework and to harmonise the different systems in the eu member states, as this would contribute to better protection of personal data and, at the same time, to the creation of a common internal market. however, the rapporteur suggests that data protection standards should be tightened in some cases, so that law enforcement agencies are not always able to properly carry out the functions assigned to them due to overly strict restrictions. it is understood that personal data must be collected and used in a transparent manner, avoiding the collection of excessive personal data, but restrictions must not become limiting factors in the activities of law enforcement authorities.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in order to facilitate the work of competent authorities, a uniform high level of protection of personal data and a consistent and uniform application of the rules on the protection of fundamental rights and freedoms of natural persons with regard to the processing of personal data should be ensured in the eu. personal data must be processed properly and used only for specific purposes and only with the consent of the person concerned or on other legitimate grounds established by law. the scope of such data should not exceed the necessary purposes for which they are processed. in view of this, the collection of such data and the period for which they are to be stored should be strictly limited. the processing of personal data should be lawful, fair and transparent with regard to the data subjects concerned. every person has the right to have access to and rectification of personal data concerning him or her.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is important to implement the reform of air navigation services. the single european sky initiative was launched in 2000. the commission notes that the airport services agreement with ryanair was signed on 20 june 2012 and that the agreement was signed on 1 july 2012. the initiative has its origins in the implementation of the enlargement policy and the active neighbourhood policy. the commission's proposal on the implementation of the single european sky measures thus aims to increase the independence of national supervisory authorities, strengthen the role of the network manager, redefine the concept of functional airspace blocks and thus allow companies in the sector to play a more important role. in addition, to support the provision of air navigation services based on competitive public procurement procedures. the european parliament suggests in the report that the concept of the single european sky itself should be better understood and allow for more flexible use of airspace, and that the actors in this area should be encouraged to cooperate more actively across the boundaries of their activities. they should have equal opportunities to expand the geographical and operational scope of this regulation and to conclude appropriate agreements with neighbouring third countries, including fab agreements.
in writing. - i voted for this report. the benefits of the single european sky are undeniable. it contributes to growth, employment and competitiveness. this initiative has led to the integration of air traffic management and navigation services into the common transport policy and the creation of a european aviation market. in this context, it is necessary to further develop and improve the implementation of this initiative and to accelerate the reform of air navigation services, in line with the initial objectives and principles. european airspace must not be congested and fragmented. particular attention should be paid to improving the structure and efficiency of the overall european route network. it is also essential that the reform in progress helps to reduce navigation charges and ticket prices and the cost of average flight duration or fuel consumption and to remove the unnecessary burden on airspace users and passengers caused by the deficiencies in the eu aviation chain.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is important to achieve and maintain a high uniform level of civil aviation safety in europe and to establish the structure of the european union aviation agency (management). this regulation is of particular importance for two reasons: first, because of the need to adapt to the new rules on the single european sky and the reform of governance to implement the interinstitutional agreement on eu agencies signed in july 2012. the european commission, the european parliament and the european council reached agreement on a common approach to eu agencies. although the agreement does not have binding legal effect, it is based on the principle that each individual case must be examined individually. the second reason is the advanced nature of many aspects (conditions for the establishment of agencies, choice of location, management, operation, use of resources, control).
in writing. - i voted for the proposed rule change. it is necessary to achieve and maintain a high uniform level of civil aviation safety throughout the eu and to establish a governance structure for the eu aviation agency. it is essential to continuously improve flight safety while maintaining effective international aviation relations. in particular, the issue of security should be addressed in greater detail. the international role of the agency needs to be strengthened. it should be noted that the reform of the management and operation of the agency should take into account the specific nature of the agency. aviation safety should remain a priority objective of the agency. in addition, the international role of the agency should be continuously strengthened. on the other hand, the structure of the executive board of the agency should ensure that no state enjoys privileges or disadvantages compared with others and that all states are represented.
in writing. - the creation of the eu single market is inseparable from the proper protection of consumer rights, and it is therefore necessary to improve the protection of travellers and to increase competition in this area. i support this communication, which will contribute to a better protection of consumers when they buy package holidays. the amendments extend the organiser's liability and give consumers additional rights, such as: withdrawal from the contract and refund in the event of an increase in the price or a significant change in the duration of the trip, the organiser's obligation to provide the consumer with more information on the provision of services, the characteristics of the trip for disabled persons, etc. i believe that these amendments will better ensure consumer rights while responding to the rapidly changing needs of the market.
