in writing. - i voted for this report. as the travel market is constantly evolving, the scope of the directive should be adapted to its needs and help to strike a balance between consumer and economic interests. it is necessary to ensure a level playing field and adequate consumer protection in the eu. in particular, a broader definition of packages will help to address the problems raised by the customization of packages. the new directive will also prevent market abuse and help to maintain efficient business models that benefit consumers. i believe that member states should do more to ensure that the directives are properly enforced and to impose strict penalties on companies that do not comply with their provisions. however, the provisions of the directive should not create an excessive regulatory burden for electronic commerce in travel services. on the other hand, while ensuring greater transparency, it is necessary to maintain a high level of consumer protection.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, i congratulate the rapporteur and i think that the directive really needed to be updated, because the tourism market has changed a lot since 1990. the tourism sector throughout europe, including lithuania, plays a very important role. the updated directive complements the existing legislation. we all know that there are many different offers for us consumers on the internet today, and that is why greater consumer protection is particularly important in this important area. citizens should be given full details before purchasing a package, especially when certain important and consumer-relevant information is written in fine print in the contract. consumer protection must therefore be further strengthened. the good news is that under the updated directive, consumers will have the right to opt out of a service if the service provider fails to comply with its obligations. consumers will also not be left to their own devices if the package provider goes bankrupt during the journey.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because we need to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. the montreal protocol, considered the most successful international environmental agreement, has achieved a global phase-out and reduction of the most ozone-depleting substances, including chlorofluorocarbons and hydrochlorofluorocarbons. the european union (eu) has called for a reduction in greenhouse gas emissions of 80% to 95% by 2050 compared to 1990 levels. therefore, additional legislation is needed now to prevent emissions from rising for the next few decades. sustainable alternatives are already on the market and are being used, and have been shown to be safe, economical and energy efficient. the phasing out of ozone-depleting substances will increase sustainable growth, stimulate innovation and reward european companies for investing in green technologies.
in writing. - i voted for this report. i believe that member states should make greater efforts to implement the roadmap to a competitive low carbon economy by 2050[n]. in order to reduce the emission of fluorinated greenhouse gases, training programmes should include information on the technologies used to replace and reduce the use of fluorinated greenhouse gases. in addition, the use of consistent and qualitative data is necessary for the effective monitoring of the emissions of fluorinated greenhouse gases. i think that there should be prohibitions on the placing on the market of new refrigeration, air conditioning and fire-fighting equipment containing or operating on such gases. it should be noted that the gradual reduction of the quantity of hydrofluorocarbons that may be placed on the market is the most effective and cost-effective way of reducing the long-term emissions of those substances. in view of this, marketing quotas should be set for certain entities in order not to exceed the overall quantitative limit for the placing on the market of hydrofluorocarbons.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because we must ensure the effective free movement of workers in the european union. unfortunately, there are still many problems for workers in the eu when they move, and many workers are very often unaware of their rights to free movement. these workers continue to be discriminated against on grounds of nationality when they move from one member state of the european union to another. workers also experience the most severe consequences of discrimination on grounds of racial or ethnic origin, religion or belief, disability, age or sex. there is therefore a gap between the legislation and the practice of applying it, and this problem needs to be addressed. this gap also persists for some vulnerable groups, such as domestic workers, people with disabilities or illiterate people. specific measures should therefore be put in place to enable those groups to exercise their right to work in another member state. the european parliament calls on the commission and the member states to set up a network of european national contact points to help exchange best practice and improve cooperation between member states in facilitating the free movement of workers. in addition, member states should promote more dialogue with the social partners and relevant non-governmental organisations which, in accordance with national law and practice, have a legitimate interest in supporting the fight against discrimination on grounds of nationality by promoting the principle of equal treatment.
in writing. - i voted for this report. the free movement of workers is one of the fundamental freedoms of union citizens and an important element in the creation of a genuine union labour market, as it enables workers to move to areas where there is a shortage of labour or where there are more employment opportunities. in order for citizens to enjoy this right properly, it is necessary to prevent discrimination on grounds of nationality as regards access to employment, employment and working conditions, in particular as regards pay, dismissal and tax and social benefits. in addition, any unjustified restrictions and administrative obstacles must be removed and the procedures for union citizens living, working and travelling in other member states must be simplified. the implementation of the envisaged measures will facilitate access to eu labour mobility support services for workers, prevent abuse and at the same time increase employment opportunities across the union.
in writing. - free movement of workers is a fundamental freedom of eu citizens laid down in the treaty and in eu legislation. free movement of workers gives citizens the right to move freely to another member state and to work and reside there for that purpose. however, this right needs to be fully empowered in the eu as there is still discrimination based on nationality within the eu. that is why eu workers who face such discrimination must be guaranteed their rights and must be granted means of legal protection. this legislation aims at clarifying and better applying those rights under existing eu legislation. i welcome all the steps proposed in the directive that aim at improving the functioning of the single market and do not impose new obligations on member states. i consider that all these steps are necessary to ensure security in the labour market. the eu and its member states need to act together to improve the efficiency of labour market policies and boost employability. lithuania, like other member states, can make all these target measures more efficient by improving cooperation with each other in enforcing the rights of workers from other eu countries.
in writing. - the document adopted today is the result of lengthy interinstitutional negotiations. unfortunately, its text is much more lenient than the original position of the european parliament, including on the legal regulation of liquid hydrocarbons. however, in the light of the complexity of the eu legislative procedure, even this text can be seen as a step forward in the transparency of the environmental impact assessment system.
in writing. - it should be noted that during the 28 years of application of the directive, it has been implemented with some success in terms of harmonising the principles of environmental assessment at union level. in order to continue to achieve the objectives pursued, i believe that it is necessary to include shale gas and shale oil within the scope of the directive. it should be noted that the exploitation of these unconventional gas sources poses significant environmental and social risks. the environmental impact assessment of projects must therefore be carried out on a case-by-case basis, as any human intervention is harmful to the environment, especially when drilling to depths of 2 kilometres or more, when using chemical agents to erode rocks and when using land resources for the construction and operation of drilling sites. another important aspect is the simplification and harmonisation of administrative procedures. i think it is appropriate to extend the selection criteria for projects. the 'one-stop-shop' approach to eia should be applied more effectively in the member states in order to align the procedure with possible environmental impact assessments. i believe that member states should be allowed to set stricter rules to protect the environment and human health.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the aim of the eastern partnership is to enhance the political, economic and cultural integration of the eastern partners into europe, based on shared values and shared interests. the vilnius summit has highlighted the need to strengthen the strategic dimension of the eastern partnership. the current momentum in relations with the eastern partners should be used to encourage the peoples of the eastern partnership countries to pursue further democratic reforms. the eastern partnership countries that are most committed to strengthening relations with the eu and are willing to undertake the necessary reforms and implement them at both political and economic levels should therefore be given due consideration and support, thus providing an incentive for further european integration. the key to building a strong and lasting partnership with the eastern partnership countries is a process of democratic transition based on the rule of law and respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms. civil society also plays an important role in the democratic transition and reform processes and in political dialogue in the eastern partnership countries.
in writing. - i'm backing the message. the time has come for a comprehensive review of the eastern partnership policy in order to make it more effective. the clear prospect of eu membership would undoubtedly provide a major impetus for reform. the commission has already put forward some good and operational solutions adapted to the situation in moldova and ukraine. i think the commission should review all the instruments available and the council should provide the political and strategic direction. i would point out that the ep supports joint research between the eu and the eastern partnership countries on the legacy of nazism and communism, totalitarian regimes and a common european history, including through the europe for citizens programme and in cooperation with the platform of european memory and conscience. i believe that a common discussion of the recent past, which has much to do with a common approach to current events, would make a significant contribution to better mutual understanding and would also help to understand the root causes of russia's actions today. much of the blackmail and pressure on the eastern neighbourhood countries is carried out through the information field. the eu must therefore pay greater attention to media and information dissemination issues. in addition, given the growing number of refugees from occupied crimea, the eu should contribute to ensuring their resettlement by providing humanitarian assistance.
in writing. - to strengthen and develop the eu's relations with the eastern partnership countries, a more effective european neighbourhood policy and a more flexible use of the eu's instruments are needed. in particular, a genuine multilateral dimension to the eastern partnership should be developed in order to improve good neighbourly relations, strengthen regional cooperation and overcome bilateral differences. the process of transition to democracy by the parties shall be based on the rule of law and respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms. greater attention should be paid to citizens of the eastern partnership countries, especially young people. another important aspect is energy security and cooperation. it is worth noting that the societies of the eastern partnership countries need strong, proactive and urgent eu support. this support should take a variety of forms and policy sectors and cover a wide range of aspects, from financial support to visa facilitation programmes. corruption remains a widespread problem that requires urgent action. the partners should do more to combat this problem.
in writing. - the eu's relations with the eastern partnership countries are becoming increasingly important. the issues of this partnership were raised frequently in the eu member states, especially during the lithuanian presidency. the decision by the ukrainian leadership not to sign the association agreement with the eu at the vilnius summit in november and the subsequent revolutionary events in ukraine have brought the region's concerns to the agendas of eu institutions and national governments almost daily, and to the headlines of european newspapers. i voted in favour of this report because it makes ambitious proposals for developing the european union's relations with its eastern neighbours. the rapporteur calls for the eu member states to provide tangible financial support to the eastern partnership countries, to remove barriers to trade, to introduce a visa-free regime in order to develop people-to-people relations, despite the economic crisis. the eu should also focus on the youth of its eastern neighbours, ensuring that their leaders have a future linked to the eu.
in writing. - this very important report provides some incentives for the eastern partnershipʼs political approach in different sectors of cooperation between eastern partnership countries and the eu. the eu as a community based on eu values and principles needs to show consistency in its eastern partnership policy and needs to take the lead in ensuring that the aforementioned policy functions well. the messages reflected in this report are relevant in the current context of relations with the eastern partners and are crucial to the credibility of the eu as a player in external relations which supports the eu aspirations of its eastern neighbours. i welcome this report and agree on its core messages underlining the euʼs proactive approach in its relations with its eastern partners. i consider the eastern partnership project needs to be assessed on its effectiveness, especially in light of recent events. further reflection on its comprehensive vision and functioning with regard to its eastern neighbours is needed. taking into consideration the lessons learnt after the eastern partnership summit in vilnius, the eu needs to reflect further on the strategy and steps that could lead to an improvement of the aforementioned policy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is important to develop a common approach between judges and prosecutors to eu criminal law and to strengthen mutual trust. the commission should progressively extend the scope of the justice scoreboard to a stand-alone and comprehensive justice scoreboard, which uses objective indicators to assess all areas of justice, including criminal justice, and all horizontal issues related to justice, such as the independence, efficiency and integrity of judges, their careers and the safeguarding of the procedural rights of parties. in addition, the commission should involve all relevant actors and build on the positive and negative experience gained, as well as on the work already carried out in this field by the council of europe bodies and the european union agency for fundamental rights in relation to the evaluation of the rule of law and justice systems.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is essential to ensure non-discriminatory, transparent and open access to the internet for all users and providers of audiovisual services. in fact, net neutrality the internet based on the principle of best efforts, non-discriminatory access to and non-discriminatory transmission of any audiovisual content ensures the pluralistic provision of information and the diversity of opinions and cultures. therefore, an aspect of integrated media integrity, similar to the principle of mandatory transmission, is necessary. the european parliament therefore calls on the commission to ensure that the principles of net neutrality are respected, as this is necessary in view of the convergence of media. the availability and accessibility of audiovisual services for the general public is becoming a key issue in the integrated environment. net neutrality is a guarantee of non-discriminatory transmission of all content. end-users should have access to the content and services they want, and access providers should not be able to restrict consumers' freedom of choice. however, the mere transmission of non-discriminatory content is not sufficient. in future, it will be important to ensure that programmes are not only accessible on individual transmission channels but also that they can be found in content access areas.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because all citizens of the european union need to know their rights and how to exercise them. i welcome the commission's initiatives to raise awareness of citizens' rights through the 'europe direct' information centres and the 'your europe' portal. i also think that more information on european citizenship could be provided on 9 may[n] on the occasion of europe day. member states could give greater emphasis to political education on eu affairs in their school curricula and adapt teacher training accordingly, and provide the necessary practical experience and resources to this end. accessible education plays a key role in the development of future citizens, providing them with a solid common knowledge base, promoting personal empowerment, solidarity and mutual understanding, and strengthening social cohesion. it is also important enough that national, regional and local authorities promote a better understanding of eu citizenship and explain its practical benefits to each individual.
in writing. - european union citizens represent the core of the eu and are the symbol for the future development of the eu. that is why it is extremely important for the eu that all citizens are aware of their rights and do not face obstacles in exercising them. to achieve that, the eu should take many steps to provide their citizens with all practical information on economic, administrative, legal and cultural issues and to promote a better understanding of eu citizenship. i welcome this report aimed at strengthening the direct link between eu citizens and the eu. i strongly believe that all the measures addressed in the report are extremely crucial in bringing eu citizens closer to the eu. the more eu citizens acknowledge their rights and make full use of them, the more the eu can benefit from it. lithuania is still facing a serious lack of citizens’ initiatives and participation in the eu decision-making process. that is why lithuania and other member states need to take more measures to enable citizens to participate more closely in the decision-making process. closer coordination and exchange of best practices between member states can be one such measure.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the establishment of the european public prosecutor's office is a step forward in the cooperation between member states in the field of criminal justice. for example, coordination with eurojust, olaf and europol will be useful and complementary to criminal prosecution, in order to ensure full judicial cooperation in criminal matters and to concentrate important national expertise in one institution, with the participation of all member states where possible. the european parliament notes the need to review the measures of jurisdiction applicable to the determination of the competent court, the means of investigation of offences falling within the secondary competence of the public prosecutor's office, the admissibility of evidence, as well as the termination of the investigation of cases. it also recalls certain principles and rights in the field of procedural guarantees which enhance the protection of suspects without weakening the investigation of and sanctions for criminal offences. finally, it is proposed to create an institution with a flexible and uncomplicated structure, which would ensure high standards of independence, experience and professionalism and be able to meet the demands of thorough investigation, rapid decision-making and knowledge of the realities of the countries where crimes are committed.
in writing. - cooperation between eu member states in the field of criminal justice is essential, and the establishment of a european public prosecutor's office would allow member states not only to better fight fraud and protect the eu's financial interests, but also to ensure full legal cooperation by coordinating their activities with olaf, eurojust and europol. i support this report and hope that the creation of the european public prosecutor's office will ensure independent and professional investigations in the field of criminal law and promote the dissemination of good practice in the eu.
in writing. - during the lithuanian presidency, after a three-year break in the eu accession negotiations with turkey, a new negotiating chapter was opened. turkey is a strategic partner of the eu and the potential of the eu-turkey relationship can best be realised through an active and credible accession process. i support the ep's position and the proposals it makes on further reforms and the work needed to strengthen the partnership.
in writing. - i voted for this report because the eu and its member states make a very important contribution to research in the arctic. large research projects in the region supported by eu programmes, including the new horizon 2020 framework programme, and the european structural and investment funds, also benefit the people and economies of the arctic countries. the european parliament draws attention to the economic potential of europe's arctic and sub-arctic regions and to various sectors such as tourism, maritime industry and shipping, renewable energy, environmental and clean technologies, gas and oil, offshore industry, forestry and timber industry, mining, transport services and communications, information technology, fisheries and aquaculture, as well as agriculture and traditional livelihoods such as reindeer farming. it is therefore essential to recognise their impact and importance for the region and europe as a whole, by focusing on the activities of european business, research and development actors.
on behalf of the ppe group. – mr president, we understand how important it is to speak with one voice against human rights violations in order to achieve the best possible result and how important it is to strengthen cooperation and enhance coordination among member states and among eu institutions. in our draft resolution, we reiterate our belief that elections to the united nations human rights council need to be competitive and we oppose uncontested elections by regional groups. i would like to express my deep concern about human rights abuses in a number of newly-elected members of the human rights council, namely cuba, vietnam and russia. the last, for instance, just weeks ago violated international law by invading the sovereign country of ukraine. i would like to stress the importance of standards for human rights council membership as regards commitment and performance in the human rights field, and i urge the member states to insist on such standards when defining the candidates they will vote for. human rights council members are required to uphold the highest standards in the promotion and protection of human rights. i also believe that strong and transparent criteria for reinstating suspended members are necessary. on behalf of the epp, i would like to commend the united nations high commissioner for human rights, navi pilley, for her continued efforts in the treaty body strengthening processes. at the same time, we regret the fact that the space for interaction between civil society and the human rights council is shrinking and that ngos are being offered fewer opportunities to speak at the sessions. the eu and the human rights council should ensure that civil society is allowed to contribute as fully as possible to all united nations human rights mechanisms. i look forward to our resolution being adopted tomorrow.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr[nbsp ] president, minister, today we are not only debating a technical resolution on the eu priorities for the 25th[nbsp ]session of the united nations human rights council, we are also expressing our views regarding current events in the various corners of the world, reflecting on the past and creating a future vision, and trying to strategise and shape the very core of our union’s efforts and values: the promotion of human rights in the world. the un human rights council should be the prime forum for effective high-quality discussion and for decisions based on full and morally uncompromised dedication to the protection of human rights. i, like colleagues from my political group, am concerned about the human rights abuses in a number of newly-elected member countries of the un human rights council, including algeria, china, cuba, morocco, russia, saudi arabia and vietnam, and we call on these countries to do their utmost to improve their human-rights situation at home. we have submitted an amendment to that end and would urge all other political groups to support it. in russia the human-rights situation has become virtually as dire as it was at the time of the soviet union. developments such as the foreign agent law, the repression of freedom of expression, association and assembly, and the anti-lgbti laws are all huge steps backwards, in contrast to the positive efforts which russia made during the first post-soviet years. on another note, i was pleased to see that a reference to caste-based discrimination was included in the resolution. let parliament keep talking about it, let the eu keep talking about it and, most importantly, let us finally do something to resolve this human-rights abuse which is so serious and so widespread yet so sadly and unacceptably neglected by the international community. lastly, let us not forget tibet. tibet is not one of the eu priorities for this session of the un human rights council, but it should be. the precarious situation of the tibetan people is an absolutely vital question which should be at the forefront of eu human rights policies. i urge the eu to ensure that realpolitik and relations with the big strategic partners do not lead to the fate of a whole nation being swallowed up and forgotten.
for the efd group human rights and freedoms the building blocks of society. one of the most important, by the way. the world is striving to guarantee to every individual freedom of belief and speech, free democratic elections. unfortunately, these rights and freedoms are often not only brutally mentioned but also subtly restricted. the resolution adopted in this house on 6 february on bosnia and herzegovina's 13th annual progress report. in other words, if the ruling of the european court of human rights is not implemented, the legitimacy of the elections of the last 14 years will be called into question. i am talking about bosnia and herzegovina, which is a country that is not a member of the european union. i believe that the european parliament should defend human rights not only in the candidate countries but also in the member states. the principles of democracy must never be ignored.
in writing. - i welcome the commission's proposal to set up an asylum and migration fund, which will help achieve the union's strategic objectives and bring a clear added value to europe. it is essential that the fund be based on simplified operating rules, which will speed up procedures and reduce administrative burdens. the new mechanisms for the provision and use of funding should ensure easier and faster access to funding for key beneficiaries on the one hand, and a more operational, efficient and flexible response to emergencies on the other. the new financial instrument must be more effective, flexible and comprehensive. a more results-oriented approach should be established, enhancing the coherence of the different asylum-related measures through the implementation of the mechanisms in place. it is essential to ensure that the actions financed have a clear added value for europe and contribute to the achievement of objectives consistent with union policies.
in writing. - i agree with the report. the 'three' helped greece, portugal, ireland and cyprus to overcome the crisis, but its structure and methods of operation have put national governments' efforts to manage the crisis into the background. it should be noted that all this had a negative impact on the transparency of the process, confused accountability and had a negative impact on the labour markets of these countries. in this context, it is essential that the proposed economic reform measures are reviewed in the near future and that the troika's actions to rescue indebted eu countries are assessed. in addition, budgetary control mechanisms need to be strengthened. moreover, commitments to structural reforms remain a prerequisite for financial support in the euro area. i think it is appropriate to create a single financial support instrument that would apply to all eu member states. all of this would enable the union and its institutions to address, in a rapid, effective and duly democratically legitimised manner, challenges that could pose a threat to the economic, financial and social stability of the euro area and its member states in the future.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in particular, it is necessary to guarantee the sustainability of public finances and to ensure adequate social protection for citizens. the particularly severe economic and financial crisis in greece, ireland, portugal and cyprus and the adjustment policies implemented by these countries have led to an increase in unemployment and job losses. it should be noted that the expectations of restoring economic growth and job creation through internal devaluation measures to restore competitiveness have not been fulfilled. it is a pity that the youth unemployment rate is still the highest. therefore, the fight against poverty, in particular child poverty, should remain one of the objectives pursued by the member states. i think there is a lack of effort to identify the weaknesses in health systems and in the decisions to cut the overall health budget. the drop in the number of out-of-school children in these four countries is encouraging, but there are no positive trends in the quality of education systems, mainly due to cutbacks in public funding. i believe that the commission should carry out a thorough study of the social and economic consequences of the economic and financial crisis in these countries and of the adjustment programmes implemented to overcome it, and should pay greater attention to social imbalances and their correction.
in writing. - i voted for this report. i agree with the proposal that an integrated approach is needed to address the problems arising from the scale of migration and the number of asylum applications and to address issues relating to the management of the union's external borders, ensuring full respect for international and human rights law, including in the case of actions in third countries, showing solidarity among all member states and recognising the need to fulfil national obligations while ensuring clear targets. it is essential that the new financing structure in the field of home affairs simplifies the rules applied and reduces the administrative burden on beneficiaries. however, in all cases an effective control mechanism must be maintained. it is also essential to ensure sound financial management and effective implementation, while ensuring transparency, legal certainty, access to funding and a level playing field for beneficiaries. in this context, it is appropriate to define forms of support and harmonised eligibility conditions for expenditure, including simplified payment modalities.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is important to ensure a high overall level of security of network and information systems. as early as 2010, the digital agenda for europe called for the establishment of legal instruments aimed at developing a policy of high levels of network and information security. a major disruption of the interfaces between network and information systems in one member state may have a negative impact on other member states and the european union as a whole. the reliability and stability of network and information systems, as well as the continuity of essential services, are crucial aspects of the smooth functioning of the internal market, in particular in view of the further development of the digital single market. therefore, in order to increase the effectiveness of the proposed measures, the european parliament considers that this directive should initially apply only to certain operators, it should help to ensure the security of investments already made in network and information systems and avoid duplication of institutional structure and obligations imposed on market participants. furthermore, this directive should support the development of trust-based relationships and exchanges between public and private sector actors and should encourage a culture of risk management instead of a temporary counter-productive culture of compliance.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is necessary to establish a regulatory framework for the placing on the market, free movement and putting into service of radio equipment and telecommunications terminal equipment in the european union. the directive, as presented by the commission, lays down essential requirements relating to health and safety, electromagnetic compatibility and the prevention of harmful interference. as in other new approach legislation, these requirements form the technical requirements that belong to the non-binding harmonised standards. the number of mobile and wireless devices has increased dramatically in recent years. there is therefore a risk of interference between different products. therefore, the efficient use of radio spectrum is of paramount importance. the european parliament welcomes the commission's proposal for this directive. however, it also proposes to include horizontal and vertical amendments to avoid unnecessary administrative burden, which would have a particularly significant impact on smes, while ensuring a very high level of consumer protection, the most efficient use of radio spectrum, improved market surveillance and greater coherence with the new legal framework and to remove possible textual inconsistencies that could give rise to legal uncertainty.
as a co-author of this resolution, i am very pleased that parliament has finally taken a rather firm stand on the issue of russia's invasion of ukraine. putin's russia is unpredictable, it violates international agreements and international law, it does not allow diplomats to enter crimea, it does not allow observers from international organizations. blocking the channels, the information space. i want to draw attention to the pressure on the crimean tatars. they are the people who are for territorial integrity. their houses are marked with signs like in 1944 when stalin had them deported to siberia. it's also a propaganda thing. the russian people are forced to live in a world war ii mood. the euromaidan, europe is called fascist, nazi, anti-semitic. sanctions are required against those who threaten the territorial integrity of ukraine. and i am very pleased about one very important provision in this resolution, the arms embargo. those countries that sell arms to russia should seriously consider whether they are not acting against themselves and against europe and the world.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the european parliament's decisionmaking powers have changed with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. the purpose of this report is therefore to assess the implementation of the treaty of lisbon, in particular by analysing the impact of the main changes it has brought about on relations between the european parliament and the european commission since the treaty entered into force. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful substances. this report provides an assessment of the european parliament and the commission's activities and cooperation in these areas. it can be said that the european parliament has succeeded in gaining recognition for its new powers and its role as one of the responsible legislative bodies, and that considerable progress has been made in its relations with the commission. however, a number of issues and shortcomings remain, in particular in relation to information sharing, delegated and implementing acts, impact assessment, approach to legislative initiatives and parliamentary questions - all of which need to be addressed more closely and in which more vigilance is required.
in writing. - the lisbon treaty has given the european parliament greater political power in the legislative and eu governance processes. the 2014 european parliament elections and the approval of the new european commission the time when these powers will have to be put into practice and the president and commissioners of the european commission will have to be appointed in the light of the results of the european elections. i welcome these developments and encourage the governments of the member states to take them into account.
in writing. - i believe that, without prejudice to the strengthening of parliament's powers of scrutiny, excessive parliamentarisation of the system should be avoided and the principle of separation of powers respected. i am in favour of establishing a strictly equivalent system of rotation between commissioners with a portfolio and commissioners without a portfolio, taking into account the demographic and geographical coverage of all member states. all members of the commission should retain the same legal status. parliament has succeeded in gaining recognition for its new mandate and role, and there has been significant progress in its relations with the commission, but a number of issues and shortcomings remain, in particular in relation to information sharing, delegated and implementing acts, impact assessment, approach to legislative initiatives and parliamentary questions, which need to be addressed more closely.
in writing. - i voted for this report because ensuring land rights and greater equity in land use creates a secure basis for livelihoods, economic opportunities and, in rural areas, for household food production. the universal declaration of human rights states that 'everyone has the right to own property, both individually and in association with others', and that 'no one shall be arbitrarily deprived of his property'. yet, 1.2 billion people worldwide are enjoying property without formal rights, living without permanent homes or access to land. in addition to private land ownership, a variety of other legal forms of land use should be recognised, including the use of customary land-use systems, in order to legally protect rights to private land, agricultural land and natural resources, as called for in the united nations programme on human settlements.
in writing. - i voted for this report. i believe that ensuring land rights and more equitable use of land creates a secure foundation for livelihoods, economic opportunities and domestic food production. it is essential that, in addition to private land ownership, various other legal forms of land use should be recognised and rights to residential land, agricultural land and natural resources legally protected. it should be noted that the security of legal land use by smallholders stimulates the local economy, increases food security, reduces migration and slows down the development of slums in cities. we need to work together as quickly as possible to bridge the gap between the rule of law, legality and practice. i condemn the practice of land grabbing. land reform should be flexible, adapted to local, social and cultural conditions, empower the most vulnerable, recognise the legality of customary land use arrangements and prevent the expropriation and abuse of land rights. the eu must work to ensure that people have access to social protection and insurance systems and can protect their livelihoods and assets in the event of an accident or a shock. the commission should set a clear budget line and focus not on a narrow perspective but on long-term land management reform; there is a need to strengthen policy instruments to ensure equal access to property for women and men in developing countries.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because there should be greater coordination of member states' policies and programmes in order to avoid fragmentation and duplication. moreover, better coordination and coordination of policies would reduce administrative burdens and related costs. the european parliament therefore calls on the commission to respect its commitments in the fields of development and human rights and recalls their role in promoting and coordinating union policies in various fields. it also considers that the commission should actively promote the idea of a coherent and modern human development in order to achieve the millennium development goals and respect the commitments. it also calls for better alignment of migration management with development policies of the eu and partner countries. however, this requires a strategy that takes account of political, social, economic and cultural circumstances and that aims at a general revival of the union's relations with its immediate neighbours.
in writing. - i agree. the eu has for many years been a leader in providing development cooperation across the world. we can do even more if we properly implement development cooperation policy, reduce fragmentation and duplication of member states' policies and programmes. this requires better coordination of eu member states' development policies and aid programmes. it is estimated that the reduction in transfer costs alone could save almost eur 800 million per year. proper coordination would reduce the administrative burden and related costs. this would provide an opportunity to make an even greater contribution to the global challenges of poverty, food insecurity and climate change, with a view to accelerating the achievement of the agreed millennium development goals.
in writing. - i voted for this report. policy coherence for development has become a common policy instrument aimed at preventing inconsistencies arising from union policies in order to avoid adverse consequences for developing countries. i believe that the eu, the member states and the partner institutions should seek to include in the new post-2015 development goals the policy coherence for development objective, which allows for the development of reliable indicators to assess the progress made by donors and the southern partners and the impact of policies in different areas on development. the commission's impact assessment system needs to be improved, ensuring that development becomes the fourth main element to be examined, alongside economic, social and environmental impacts. the method and mechanisms for achieving optimal compatibility should be given the utmost attention in order to avoid any inconsistency. the eu must have an independent body to formally collect and deal with complaints from citizens and communities affected by the various union policies.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution because the european union and its member states should strive to take a united stand and speak out against human rights violations in order to achieve the best possible results and, in this context, should further strengthen cooperation between member states and the eu institutions and improve the organisational arrangements and coordination of their activities. the european parliament seeks to increase the effectiveness and visibility of the eu's human rights policy and to help implement the strategic programme and the action plan on human rights and democracy. parliament called for the fight against corruption to be stepped up in the public and private sectors, as these actions promote and increase inequalities and discrimination which impede the equal enjoyment of civil, political, economic, social and cultural rights. the report also calls for action to combat discrimination against women and people with disabilities, to protect children from all forms of exploitation and to combat violence against women and girls.
in writing. - i voted yes. coordination of the eu's position at the un summit is important, as nine eu countries are currently members of the council. however, i would like to note that russia, which has just been elected to the un security council, is a member, its aggression against ukraine is difficult to understand in the context and can discredit this international institution, reduce its effectiveness. it is also regrettable that the text of the resolution does not address the issue of human rights violations in tibet.
in writing. we often hear in our own countries that the issues dealt with by the european parliament are far from the people. i would disagree with that. this document we're looking at is just a demonstration of how the decisions we make affect people's lives. the first decisions at eu level on the limitation of roaming charges were welcomed in 2007, but now, from a modern perspective, when we are considering the total abolition of roaming charges, they seem hopelessly outdated. by making roaming and data communications as good as local calls, we are making a major contribution to both the free movement of persons and better protection of european consumers. i congratulate the rapporteur and welcome one of the best examples of how we in the european parliament make consumer-friendly decisions.
in writing. the stockholm programme has made good progress towards the goal of creating an area of freedom, security and justice, but it is important to note that in some areas more needs to be done to protect the rights of our citizens. the stockholm programme aims to ensure human rights and facilitate the free movement of citizens and residents of the european union, while protecting and respecting all rights and obligations related to the european area of justice. judicial cooperation is a key tool to achieve this objective and the continued efforts of the eu institutions and member states to make progress towards universal support for the international criminal court as one of the key actors in ensuring justice for victims of crimes under international law and promoting respect for international humanitarian and human rights law should be welcomed. the establishment of adequate accountability procedures is therefore essential for the effective protection and promotion of human rights and the implementation of a legitimate and effective security policy based on the rule of law. the commission should propose an accountability mechanism to strengthen the capacity of the eu and the member states to prevent human rights violations at eu level.
in writing. mr president, the european union's approach to external action is important in the development of a common policy on international aid. major geostrategic changes are taking place in many countries of the world, in particular with a view to creating multilateralism on an international scale, which increases the interdependence of countries and continents. in the face of such geopolitical moods, the european union must preserve its values and promote them internationally. member states must better fulfil their obligations by actively promoting external relations with third countries requiring support and assistance. this is particularly important in the context of humanitarian aid. it is important to remember that compliance with the rules of humanitarian aid (independence, impartiality, neutrality) requires special attention from us. the security of personnel of humanitarian organisations providing humanitarian and humanitarian assistance in crisis depends in particular on the management of the area covered by the assistance. for that reason, they must be independent of biased political influences. the capacity of a humanitarian organisation to provide assistance and humanitarian assistance itself shall not be used for political purposes in any way. to reach the masses, it must not only be neutral but also visible. only an integrated approach can ensure the necessary assistance to countries and support the fight against poverty.
in writing. - i supported the proposal because, in the light of an ageing population, changing working conditions, persistent incentives to retire early, the financial crisis and changes in production patterns, as well as the need to increase competitiveness, a strong response is needed to enable older workers to remain active in the labour market, including when they are already at retirement age. ensuring the employability of older workers and keeping a large proportion of older people in their jobs for longer is crucial to tackling demographic challenges and achieving the eu's target of increasing employment of 20-64 year olds to 75% by 2020. calls for the removal of all obstacles to the promotion of active ageing and for support for lifelong learning, in particular in the acquisition of new skills and technical abilities such as computer skills and foreign languages. stresses that lifelong learning for older people should be firmly integrated into working life and that these policies should be continuously monitored, evaluated and improved. the commission should identify specific measures for disadvantaged groups or categories of vulnerable workers to help member states better define key priorities, strategies and sustainable projects eligible for esf funding for the coming period 2014-2020.
in writing. - i agree. in this recommendation, the european parliament, one of the leading institutions in the field of human rights and freedoms, advocates that the european union should introduce targeted restrictive measures against the specific individuals responsible for the death of sergei magnitsky and the subsequent impunity of the officials involved. in the light of the magnitsky act adopted by the united states, the impact of sanctions is likely to be stronger if applied in a coordinated manner. s. and magnitsky's case, particularly painful, illustrates the self-imposed bias and ambiguity that has taken hold throughout the russian legal system. as a member of the council of europe, the organisation for security and cooperation in europe and the united nations, russia has committed itself to protecting and promoting human rights, fundamental freedoms and the rule of law, but we have recently seen a sad trend of systematic breaches of commitments. this is the position taken by the european parliament to the european council. i hope that the foreign affairs council will take appropriate and immediate decisions in line with this position of the ep.
in writing. - i agree. the eu, which is not a member of the un, has to define positions on the most important climate change, development cooperation, the protection of women's and children's rights and other key issues, guidelines that all eu member states participating in the un general assembly agree to follow. a united position of the eu countries in the un general assembly on the issue of russian aggression against ukraine is necessary. the fact that a permanent member of the un security council has grossly and recklessly violated not only bilateral agreements and international commitments but also the principles of the un charter is the biggest shock to the international system in recent years, the foundations of which are laid and the observance of which should be ensured by the un, and this must not be ignored.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal because the independent european broadcasting media offer a common vision to the european and global community, support certain values enshrined in the eu treaties, such as human rights and fundamental freedoms, including freedom of expression, and promote pluralism, independent thinking, cultural diversity, pluralism of opinion and the fundamental values of non-violent conflict resolution, tolerance and respect, both in the eu and in external relations. the european broadcasting media can have a significant impact internationally, worldwide, by spreading information about the values of democracy, media freedom and human rights, explaining different national perceptions, shaping a genuine european perspective and providing a reliable, high-quality information to the audience, and above all, by a firm, unwavering commitment to journalistic objectivity. i agree that eu external assistance funding for projects should be allocated to broadcasters that meet the highest standards of journalistic independence, accuracy and balance and that follow and promote eu values, and to projects that enable those broadcasters that fall short of the standards of independence, accuracy and sustainability to achieve those standards, given that in the future any funding should be conditional on performance evaluation using measurable criteria.
in writing. - in today’s developing world european media broadcasting is an important tool for supporting and promoting european values and freedoms such as human rights, freedom of expression and journalistic pluralism. in addition, radio and online media are also effective ways to communicate eu policies, to reach a wide audience in the eu and beyond and to ensure objective news coverage. this recommendation to the council, the commission and the european external action service underlines the importance of broadcasting inside and outside the eu and proposes to make the broadcasting strategy an integrated tool of the common foreign and security policy. i welcome this useful proposal that puts an emphasis on effectively ensuring the broadest outreach to the public audience in the eu and in third countries. i believe that a competitive media environment is vital for developing society in various member states, including lithuania. the access to independent and diversified broadcasting media is a precondition for a conscious society in the eu and its vicinity. diversified broadcasting media can also help every citizen to stay in touch with the changes of the globalised world and to be at the forefront of the digital revolution.
in writing. - i supported the establishment of limit values for vehicle categories m1 to m3 (passenger vehicles) and n1 to n3 (freight vehicles) in three stages, the first starting on 1 january 2017. for example, the limit value for passenger cars will be reduced to 68 db over time. the commission will also carry out an assessment of the conditions and information for the labelling of air pollution and noise levels and the impact on consumers, and manufacturers will have to make efforts to provide information on vehicle noise levels at the point of sale and in technical promotional material. i also agree with the importance of protecting blind and other vulnerable people in electric vehicles by installing appropriate audible vehicle warning systems. such systems should be mandatory. it is agreed that this will start to apply within three years of the date of application of the regulation for new types of vehicles and within five years for all electric and hybrid vehicles. the importance of road surface issues and their impact on reducing vehicle noise levels is also important. the commission should develop informative guidelines on road quality improvement technologies and best practices for road classification, based on information provided by member states.
in writing. - in the context of the application of regulation (ec) no 1760/2000 establishing a system for the identification and registration of bovine animals and regarding the labelling of beef and beef products and repealing council regulation (ec) no 820/97 and in accordance with the provisions of directive 64/432/eec, the member states have set up computerised databases containing information on bovine animals present on their territory. to date, these databases do not take into account the fact that the technology for electronic marking of bovine animals has been developed. in the context of the review of regulation (ec) no 1760/2000 and in order to allow for the gradual and harmonised development of this new technology, it is appropriate to adapt the principles for the collection of these data and to align them with the other two means of identification of bovine animals. directive 64/432/eec should therefore be amended accordingly. i voted in favour of the amendments proposed by the commission to the provisions relating to computerised databases. this review, which is a direct consequence of the amendment of regulation (ec) no 1760/2000, is necessary and appropriate.
in writing. - i supported the proposal as the eu currently lacks a horizontal legislative framework to enforce its rights under the wto and other international trade agreements. in a world of increasing retaliation, the legitimate aim of the proposed measure is to better ensure the enforcement of the eu's trade rights, to enhance the eu's credibility and, ultimately, to convince our trading partners to adhere to the rules of conduct set out in trade agreements. this proposal is also part of the strategy of the european union to open up new markets and brings its decision-making process into line with the provisions of the lisbon treaty. this new measure should also be understood in the context of the multilateral (mro), regional and bilateral dispute settlement rules framework, under which the complaining party already has the right to take extreme measures, i.e. provisional defensive retaliation against the infringing party, if the latter does not comply with the rules laid down by the dispute settlement body. the measure does not therefore pursue any protectionist objective.
in writing. - i support the speaker's position. measures to enforce wto and other international trade agreements are often lacking at eu level. the provisions on the application of retaliatory measures are an important instrument of eu trade policy. while they will be applied only as a last resort, they will allow for a rapid response to problems that arise, while encouraging third countries to comply with international trade rules. only by creating a free, open and predictable trading system can we ensure sustainable growth and competitiveness in the eu.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because it aims to update european legislation in the light of technological and scientific progress and to address recent concerns about certain aspects of patient safety. the hundreds of thousands of different types of medical devices currently on the market in the european union - from patches, syringes, catheters and blood sampling devices to complex implants and life support technologies - are required to be safe not only for patients but also for healthcare professionals who use or handle them and for non-specialists who come into contact with them. i am convinced that it is necessary to ensure high standards of quality and safety of medical devices, thereby ensuring a high level of protection of health and safety of patients, users and other persons.
in writing. - i have agreed to these proposals, which will help to substantially improve patient safety. ivd medical devices are not a small item but one of the most important medical devices and can be the basis for all therapies, including pharmaceuticals and surgery. without proper diagnosis, there is no proper treatment or prevention of disease. unfortunately, the current directive does not ensure that low-quality iud medical devices are not placed on the market. this proposal therefore seeks to remedy this situation.
in writing. - i supported the proposal because the new accounts will contribute to the further development of more detailed indicators to develop better public debates and shape policy. as the national statistical institutes are currently facing financial and staff constraints, it is now important to focus on improving quality and to encourage the use of the already existing modules and those proposed by the commission in the current proposal. it is important that the additional administrative burden be limited to the most essential aspects. the current pace of change and the uncertainties surrounding possible future trends call for further action to ensure that policy in the union continues to be conducted on the basis of sound knowledge of the state of the environment and of the possible response alternatives and their consequences. to ensure that reliable quality data and indicators are produced and that they are more accessible, measures should be put in place. it is important that such data are provided in an understandable and accessible form
in writing. - this report, which is the result of an awareness-raising campaign involving representatives of the eu institutions, the economic and social committee, the committee of regions, concord and civil society, underlines the need to put the spotlight on the eu’s global role in international development. at a time of economic recession and economic and political crisis, it is important to acknowledge that there are more than 100 million poor people in the eu. that is why strong coordinated action by the eu and its member states is required. the european year of development 2015 will provide such an opportunity to create public awareness of poverty and can become the first year dedicated to a global theme. i welcome this report which puts an emphasis on tackling the major challenges of anti-poverty targets. the european year of development 2015 can be a year for excellent cooperation between the eesc, the eu institutions and civil society, and a year that will gather broad support from people inside and outside the eu. for that, coordinated action by all 28 member states in this field will be required to make 2015 a year of success for the eu’s development policy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because union law provides for requirements on food and food and feed safety at all stages of production, including rules aimed at ensuring fair trade and the provision of information to consumers. they also lay down requirements for the prevention and control of infectious animal diseases and zoonoses, as well as for animal welfare, animal by-products, plant health and plant reproductive material, plant variety protection, the placing on the market and use of genetically modified organisms and plant protection products and the sustainable use of pesticides. union law also provides for official controls and other official activities aimed at ensuring the effective implementation of those requirements and their compliance. the general objective of union law in those areas is to contribute to a high level of human, animal and plant health throughout the food chain, a high level of consumer protection and information and a high level of environmental protection, while promoting competitiveness and job creation. to achieve that overall objective, adequate financial resources are needed. it is therefore necessary that the union contribute to the financing of measures taken in the various fields to achieve that general objective. in addition, in order to make more efficient use of expenditure, specific objectives and indicators to assess how those objectives will be achieved should be set.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal as it establishes a flegt licensing scheme (forest enforcement, forest management and trade in timber) to combat illegal logging and the related trade. under the flegt scheme, the partner countries commit to ensuring that there are checks and controls to help determine whether the timber products are made from legally harvested local timber or from timber that has been legally imported into the partner country. the placing of illegally obtained timber on the eu market is an infringement.
in writing. - i support this report. to remain competitive in clinical research, we need to maintain high standards and promote the application of the latest research in this field. i also welcome the fact that the measures envisaged in the report aim to reduce bureaucratic burdens and provide for the establishment of national compensation schemes to encourage research into rare diseases. clinical trials are the area where we need to be transparent and clear.
in writing. - i supported the proposal to strengthen the current regulatory framework for nuclear safety in the community. the importance of preventing incidents and accidents and of responding effectively should an incident or accident occur should be emphasised. accident preparedness and coordination should be the cornerstone of nuclear safety. following the 2011 fukushima nuclear accident in japan, there has been a renewed global focus on the measures needed to reduce the risk of nuclear accidents and to increase nuclear safety, as well as on testing to assess the risks and safety of nuclear power plants in the european union in detail.
in writing. - the fukushima accident has prompted a careful examination of whether we have done everything we can to ensure the highest standards of nuclear safety across the eu. the european parliament, by this decision, endorses the need to continue the development of the eu nuclear safety framework, to promote continuous improvement of nuclear safety in all eu countries, ensuring it at all stages of the operation of the relevant installations, and to strengthen the resilience to man-made or natural hazards. by strengthening nuclear safety standards in the countries of the european union, the eu contributes to the highest international nuclear safety standards and to the development of a nuclear safety culture at international level. unfortunately, even if we ensure the highest standards of nuclear safety in our own countries, we cannot be sure that our citizens will not be exposed to nuclear accidents from third countries until such standards are also applied there. the development of unsafe power projects in the eu's neighbourhood, primarily in belarus, which, despite repeated requests from affected eu countries, does not comply with international procedures and does not provide requested project information, remains a major concern.
in writing. - i agree that ensuring fair taxation for all income groups is not only a moral imperative, but also a highly economically beneficial way of ensuring that the taxes imposed are collected and fully used. the additional revenue would allow member states to make new investments in job creation, growth programmes and infrastructure without imposing any new taxes. we cannot afford to sit idly by while europe is still recovering from the crisis. the current eu efforts to close existing tax loopholes caused by differences in member states' national tax systems, double taxation and extreme forms of undertaxation are leading to significant revenue losses for member states and are creating conditions for unfair competition between companies operating in the single market. in order to ensure fairness, transparency, competition and economic efficiency, the eu must make every effort to ensure that the tax gap is closed without delay.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the european union should promote the highest level of protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms for all. the human rights chapter of the stockholm programme is in line with this objective, but despite the progress made, more needs to be done to implement this chapter. the european parliament therefore considers that more attention should be paid to responding to the particular situation of vulnerable groups and to strengthening the fight against racism, xenophobia, anti-semitism and religious intolerance. parliament called for a stronger fight against violence against women and children and for more aid to be given to victims of such and other crimes. parliament therefore welcomes the directive setting minimum standards for the rights, support and protection of victims of crime, with particular emphasis on the protection of women and children. it is important to highlight the frequent victimisation of these groups in the context of all forms of violence, including domestic violence.
in writing. - the stockholm programme defines the principles and actions that will create an area of freedom, security and justice for the benefit of citizens. in order to ensure an open and secure european union, it is necessary not only to consolidate the achievements in the areas already identified, including through enhanced cooperation in civil and criminal matters and the mutual recognition of judgments and documents, but also to take account of new challenges. in my view, and as the ep has repeatedly confirmed, one of these challenges is the need to ensure the security of the eu's digital and information space. the exchange of information and good practice in this area must be fully supported, and this requires a stronger coordination of initiatives and actions at eu level. i believe that the macro-regional initiatives currently under discussion, which seek to strengthen cooperation in this area in the baltic region, should be supported by the eu both politically and financially. the openness, reliability and security of the information space is not only a matter of geopolitical importance for the baltic states, but also a matter of concern for the eu as a whole.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal as it is important to continuously improve the efficiency and consistency of the eu's export control regime for strategic goods, ensuring a high level of security and adequate transparency, without creating barriers to competitiveness and to legitimate trade in dual-use goods. the system needs to be modernised and more harmonised in order to take account of new threats and the speed of technological change, reduce distortions, create a genuine common market for dual-use goods (level playing field for exporters) and continue to be an example of export controls to third countries.
in writing. - i supported the agreement reached with the eu council to exempt international flights from the allowance for emissions (atl) scheme by 2017[n 1]. the eu's emissions trading system is fully justified. although the aviation sector currently accounts for around 3% of all greenhouse gas emissions, its pollution is increasing rapidly. the international civil aviation organisation (icao) estimates that the amount of co2 emitted by aviation-related activities almost doubled between 1990 and 2006. for comparison, the amount of co2 emitted from fossil fuels increased by 34%. it can be assumed that by 2020 by which time the eu has committed to reducing its emissions by 20% compared to 1990 the level will have increased by 63% 88%. the problem of global warming can only be effectively addressed by an international approach. neither carbon pollution nor climate change transcends national borders. the same climate policy objectives should therefore be endorsed globally. this is only possible after an agreement has been reached with icao.
in writing. - i agreed to the proposal because consumers are often unaware that payment cards (such as visa or mastercard) are subject to a fee for merchants, which depends on the type of card, the issuing bank and the country. this tax, which costs eu businesses some €10 billion a year, is often passed on to consumers. the draft new rules stipulate that the fee charged by banks for credit card settlement will not exceed 0.3% of the amount transferred, and for debit card settlement 7 euro cents or 0.2% of the amount transferred, whichever is lower. these tax limits would apply to both local and international settlements within the eu. it is expected that in the long term the reduction of this tax will make goods and services cheaper.
in writing. - with the increase in non-cash transactions, the problem of interbank charges is becoming more frequent, which imposes additional costs on consumers, creates barriers and restricts competition. in supporting this report, i hope that the introduction of caps on interbank charges for domestic and crossborder transactions in the eu will contribute to improving the functioning of the internal market and the implementation of the eu principle of free movement of goods, persons, services and capital, and, finally, that these amendments will take greater account of consumer rights.
in writing. - i agree that the situation in ukraine requires urgent decisions. ukraine has been going through a dramatic political period in recent months and the country's new government needs strong and urgent support from the european union to counter external pressure and overcome economic and financial difficulties. the above suggestion is very helpful and understandable. the proposal will help ukrainian enterprises to increase their export volumes and diversify their export destinations by temporarily unilaterally reducing the trade barriers (tariffs, quotas or tariff quotas) to ukrainian goods imported into the eu to the level agreed by both parties under the eu-ukraine comprehensive and universal free trade area (cefta) agreement. in other words, these unilateral concessions will help ukraine to save approximately several hundred million euros in the coming months. for these reasons, the precarious balance of payments situation in ukraine will improve and more foreign currency will enter the country, which will help restore economic and financial stability and reduce the risk of currency devaluation. the eu is giving ukraine every opportunity to restore sound economic conditions in the country through swift and decisive action.
in writing. - the situation in ukraine requires a swift and urgent eu response and assistance. i congratulate the rapporteur on an ambitious communication in which he proposes to reduce the trade barriers (tariffs, quotas or tariff quotas) imposed on ukraine. the proposed measures represent a strong political and economic support for ukraine. i believe that these measures will contribute to the restoration of economic and financial stability in ukraine and to attracting foreign investment, and at the same time to ukraine's own rapprochement with the eu.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the treaty of lisbon and the current decision-making processes already require the european union to ensure the consistency of its external action in the various fields and the consistency of these with its other policies. a comprehensive eu approach implies not only a joint use of eu instruments and resources, but also a shared responsibility of eu actors and member states, i.e. their policy instruments, actions and support should contribute to a more coordinated and effective eu external action. furthermore, a comprehensive approach is an obligation that is shared by all eu actors in the eu institutions, in the eu member states and in third countries. it is important that its observance takes full account of the specific areas of competence of each institution and entity. the european parliament also calls for active engagement and dialogue with citizens and civil society to ensure a comprehensive approach and, more generally, the legitimacy and common understanding of eu foreign policy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because food safety is one of the main concerns of european citizens. european legislation, updated and strengthened as necessary, should therefore ensure their adequate protection. in 1997, following an outbreak of bovine spongiform encephalopathy, the european union adopted strict rules on the identification and traceability of bovine animals. this proposal will enhance the safety of beef offered to consumers. the aim is also to ensure full clarity and applicability of the legislation on the identification and traceability of bovine animals, thus achieving an adequate level of food safety.
in writing. - to ensure that consumers' rights are properly protected and to promote consumer confidence, we must ensure that beef and other meat products are properly labelled and that the product is identifiable and traceable. these provisions should apply not only to products imported from the eu but also to those imported from third countries in order to ensure equivalent protection. the proposals in the report may seem to be an additional bureaucratic burden, but i am confident that the additional checks and the penalties envisaged will help to avoid the risk of fraud in the meat labelling sector and better protect the interests of european consumers.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the protection of the eu's financial interests is an essential part of the eu's political agenda to promote and enhance citizens' confidence and to ensure that taxpayers' money is used properly. the european commission estimates that billions of euros of tax revenue could be lost every year in the european union as a result of tax fraud, tax evasion, tax avoidance and aggressive tax planning. an integrated approach, including anti-fraud and anti-corruption strategies, with effective, coherent and equivalent legal instruments across the eu is necessary to protect the financial interests of the european union and to fight fraud. the european parliament welcomes the commission's action plan to strengthen the fight against tax fraud and tax evasion. i would like to stress that both the commission and the member states should continue to give the utmost attention to the fight against fraud and tax evasion, which requires a better strategy for multifaceted cooperation and coordination between the commission and the member states, which would allow better use of existing instruments, consolidation of existing legislation, adoption of pending proposals and cooperation at administrative level.
