in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal because tax fraud, tax evasion, tax avoidance and aggressive tax planning can result in the loss of around 1 000 jobs in the european union every year. 000[n] the eu has a budget of eur [000] 000[] of tax revenue, which costs each eu citizen about eur [000] 2 000 per year. the protection of the eu's financial interests is an essential part of the eu's political agenda to promote and enhance citizens' confidence and to ensure that taxpayers' money is used appropriately. the lisbon treaty has substantially improved the measures designed to protect the eu's financial interests and obliges both the eu and its member states to combat all forms of illegal activities adversely affecting the eu's financial interests. however, the wide diversity of eu legal systems and traditions makes the protection of the union's financial interests against fraud and any other illegal activities a very serious problem which requires urgent and unavoidable action to address. in order to protect the financial interests of the union and to fight fraud, an integrated approach involving anti-fraud and anti-corruption strategies, with effective, coherent and equivalent legal instruments throughout the union is necessary. the high level of fraud and the scale of tax evasion and corruption in all its forms undermine citizens' trust in the eu and require greater guarantees in the areas of soundness and transparency of public spending.
today we are discussing a really useful commission proposal which will bring together many directives and other documents into one whole and make european union law clearer. i think that the rapporteurs and the amendments tabled by my colleagues have also done a good job. the main topic of the report is animal health and the control of communicable diseases, but animal welfare and the provision of appropriate storage and transport conditions are inseparable from animal health. i therefore urge my colleagues to support amendments to improve animal welfare. i am pleased that the report clearly emphasises the importance of the responsible use of veterinary medicinal products. preventive use of antibiotics should be discontinued as this can prevent the development of antibiotic resistant bacteria. the active educational role of veterinarians is particularly important here. the member states of the european union should help farmers to exchange examples of best practice and encourage continuous learning in this field. i hope that the new parliament will support our position in the second reading and defend it successfully in the trilogue negotiations.
in writing. - the financial crisis has shown that public authorities are ill-prepared to deal with the problems posed by banks operating in today's global markets and experiencing financial difficulties. the functioning of the eu's banking union governance framework, resolution mechanism and resolution fund is therefore crucial. the current separation between national and eu policies should be removed. it should be noted that the single resolution mechanism will concentrate the crisis management capabilities and core resources of banks in the euro area and will allow for the establishment of an integrated resolution mechanism at eu level. it should be noted that the different national rules currently applied in different member states for the resolution of credit institutions reduce confidence and increase market instability, as the possibility of predicting the possible consequences of a bank failure is not ensured. in this context, it is essential to ensure that a neutral approach is applied in the event of bank failures. this would increase the stability of banks in participating member states, prevent spill-over effects of crises in non-participating member states and thus create favourable conditions for the functioning of the internal market as a whole. on the other hand, it is necessary to strengthen the parliamentary scrutiny and democratic accountability of the new eu agency to the ep and the national parliaments of the participating member states. i support the proposal to apply the approval procedure for the appointment of the executive director and the deputy executive director of the board, as well as the regular exchange of information.
in writing. - i agreed to the proposal because i have always been in favour of transparency in public services. access to public utilities should be open to all consumers, regardless of their financial situation. member states should establish appropriate arrangements to be applied to vulnerable consumers. consumer protection is only effective if consumer rights can be properly enforced. an open, transparent and integrated internal energy market is necessary to ensure competitive energy prices, security of supply, sustainability and the widespread deployment of efficient renewable energy, and member states are therefore encouraged to properly transpose, apply and monitor the third energy internal market legislative package. better information for consumers is needed, in particular to improve the services provided and to enable comparison and transparency of tariffs, thereby ensuring non-discriminatory prices.
in writing. - i voted for this report. protecting consumers' rights is one of the eu's policy priorities. i believe that it is necessary to strengthen the protection of consumer rights in the provision of public utilities, since the consumer chooses not whether to buy the service at all, but only from which provider to buy it. this creates a certain relationship of subordination between the service provider and the consumer. it should be noted that in many countries the liberalisation of these sectors has not produced the desired results. on the contrary, prices were increased, contract terms were made more complex and jobs were lost. it should be noted that improving the quality and protection of the rights and interests of the users of public utilities improves both the lives of the population and the living environment. it is therefore appropriate to integrate the rights of consumers of utilities into all economic, management and sectoral decisions. it is important to establish measures at eu level to ensure the availability and quality of public utilities, to increase consumer awareness, to represent their interests and to protect their rights.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the directive laying down technical requirements for inland waterway vessels. this directive lays down the conditions and technical requirements for the issue of navigation certificates for inland waterway vessels, valid on all inland waterways of the community, including the rhine. increased legal certainty and technical adaptation to technical progress within a reasonable timeframe are ensured to ensure that high safety standards are maintained on eu roads and that innovation in the sector is not hindered.
in writing. - the need to move towards common and uniform technical standards is necessary for safety and to ensure a level playing field. the two different sets of rules currently in force do not adequately ensure legal certainty and security. it is therefore essential to have a common set of uniform technical standards across the eu to ensure that high safety standards are maintained in this sector and that there are no additional barriers to innovation. i welcome the efforts to further harmonise the technical requirements for inland waterway vessels and to strengthen cooperation between the river commissions, the unece and the commission in order to increase efficiency and legal clarity in the inland waterway sector, which is dominated by smes. in addition, the commission should implement naiades ii and take concrete action as soon as possible to take account of the specificities of the sector.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the new version of the regulation, which aims to improve the application of eu customs and agricultural legislation in the member states. at present, customs intermediaries have no means of checking whether the declared origin of the goods coincides with the route of the container concerned. the transit system, which is monitored by limited documentary checks and visual inspections, is currently being abused. fraud in the submission of false declarations of origin costs the eu-27 €100 million a year , and this is only the first fraud to be detected. in 2011, member states reported losses of around eur 107,7 million due to false descriptions of goods carried, and it can therefore be concluded that the total amount of revenue lost to the union is significantly higher than the published figures. for the sake of legal clarity, evidence gathered in administrative proceedings should be legally recognised in the member states, whether it relates to aid applications or to aid on its own initiative.
in writing. - to ensure the correct application of customs and agricultural legislation, more effective measures must be provided not only to the commission but also to the customs and other authorities of the member states. it should be noted that the current regulation contains gaps as customs brokers have no means to verify that the declared origin of the goods coincides with the route of the relevant container. in addition, the transit system, which is monitored by limited documentary checks and visual inspections, is frequently abused. in this context, it is necessary to simplify and speed up the mechanisms to combat customs fraud, to avoid unnecessary and repetitive bureaucratic procedures, and to strengthen the internal market and the security of products moving within it, while ensuring a high level of consumer protection. for the sake of legal clarity, i consider that evidence gathered in administrative proceedings should be legally recognised in the member states, irrespective of whether it relates to aid applications or to aid on its own initiative, as there is no reason why these two types of circumstances should be treated differently as regards their legal validity.
in writing. - i agree with this report. in particular, this convention improves the conditions for cross-border activities in the internal market. i support the commission's recommendation and would like croatia to accede to the arbitration convention and related instruments as soon as possible. it should be noted that, in order to remove all doubts as to the possible retroactive application, i consider it appropriate to indicate the date of entry into force of the arbitration convention, not by specifying a specific date, but by providing for entry into force on the day following the date of publication of this decision in the official journal of the european union.
in writing. - i have given my consent to this proposal, which proposes to set up the shift2rail joint undertaking (hereinafter s2r joint undertaking). the s2r joint undertaking will be subject to the standard provisions of the five jti proposals submitted by the joint undertakings in july 2013. its main objective is to help create a single european railway area. the main objective of eu transport policy is to revive the european railway sector so that it can compete successfully with other modes of transport. new technologies can make a significant contribution to the modernisation of european railways, while reducing operational and infrastructure costs and opening up new business opportunities for the european rail supply industry. the high level of investment in research and innovation in the eu rail sector over recent decades has helped the european rail supply industry to remain a leader in the global rail market, in particular with the development of high-speed trains, the european rail traffic management system (ertms), automated metro systems, etc. i believe that there is a need for a coordinated eu approach to research and innovation in the railway sector and the creation of a joint undertaking, thus helping to create a common european railway area to increase the competitiveness of the eu railway sector in relation to other modes of transport and competition from foreign countries. in particular, by developing, integrating, demonstrating and validating innovative technologies and solutions in the areas of rolling stock, infrastructure and traffic management systems, the joint undertaking will help to accelerate and facilitate the market uptake of technological breakthroughs.
in writing. - i welcome the commission's proposal to establish the shift2rail joint undertaking. this is important for the further implementation of the innovation investment package of horizon 2020. it is worth noting that a coordinated eu approach to research and innovation in the railway sector will help to create a common european railway area, increase the competitiveness of the sector in relation to other modes of transport and competition from foreign countries. i believe that the management and operation of joint ventures should be open, transparent, efficient and effective and should allow the various stakeholders to operate in their specific field. it is therefore important to respect the principles applicable to public-private partnerships. on the other hand, the principles of openness and transparency should not only apply to the internal activities and management procedures of the joint undertaking.
in writing. - i voted in favour, as, in addition to their direct function of protecting depositors, deposit guarantee schemes are also essential for the stability of the financial system, especially in times of crisis. the loss of confidence of bank customers in their own credit institution and the simultaneous withdrawal of their deposits (bank run) may not only lead to the collapse of already fragile institutions, but also to the spread of the crisis of confidence to other credit institutions and countries. the amount of liquid assets required to cover such deposits is such that no credit institution can hold such an amount on a permanent basis. to this end, rules will be laid down for the first time at eu level on the financing of deposit guarantee schemes, payment deadlines in the event of insolvency will be shortened, the principle of risk-based contribution will be enshrined, temporary protection for large deposits will be provided, clear and easily understandable information on deposit guarantees will be provided, the funds in the deposit guarantee fund will be primarily dedicated to payments in the event of insolvency and to the protection of insured deposits during winding-up proceedings, and it will be provided that deposit guarantee schemes can lend to each other on a voluntary basis.
in writing. - i voted for this recommendation. the importance of deposits for financial markets is undeniable. without deposit banks, the system could not exist at all. it should be noted that, in addition to their direct function of protecting depositors, deposit guarantee schemes are essential for the stability of the financial system, especially in times of crisis. the rules on the financing of deposit guarantee schemes are welcome for the first time at eu level. in particular, payment deadlines in the event of insolvency will be shortened and disparities in the financing of guarantee schemes in the member states will be eliminated. the principle of risk-based contributions is also established. in order to guarantee the financial security of depositors and the protection of their interests, clear and easily understandable information on deposit guarantees will be provided to depositors.
in writing. - only by working together can we develop alternative fuels infrastructure across the eu. furthermore, a baseline of this infrastructure across the eu will contribute to the functioning of the eu single market. the measures envisaged will help to reduce dependence on oil and accelerate the reduction of co2 emissions from vehicles. however, it is necessary to allow time for this transition until the necessary infrastructure is in place and consumer confidence in alternative fuels is fostered. it should be noted that without eu support in sectors such as transport, it would not be possible to achieve the minimum necessary network expansion, increase the proportionate consumption of these fuels in the transport sector and thus reduce the negative environmental impact of transport. i welcome the proposals as a necessary means of breaking europe's dependence on oil and increasing the diversification and security of energy supply. in particular, the choice of alternative fuels and corresponding fuel stations for alternative fuels vehicles will be increased, information on fuel compliance for the car will be widely available and uniform labelling will allow easy choice of the fuel required in the stations. it will also boost economic growth, enhance the competitiveness of european industry and reduce greenhouse gas emissions from transport. another important aspect is that the direct impact of the directive must be felt by all eu residents. it is therefore necessary to ensure the quality of alternative fuels and to raise consumer awareness of alternative fuels.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is appropriate to reconsider the directive on certain vehicle masses and dimensions. in particular, the provisions envisaged must ensure that road safety rules are respected, that the constraints imposed by the infrastructure are taken into account and that free movement is not impeded. it is essential that cabin profiling not only reduces fuel consumption and greenhouse gas emissions, but also improves the driver's field of vision, thereby saving more lives in europe each year. the implementation of the measures envisaged should improve driver comfort and safety. it should be noted that the proposed directive will allow for mixed transport. in addition, control authorities will be able to better identify infringements and apply uniform control methods and administrative penalties to infringers.
in writing. - i have supported this proposal, which will make it possible to implement common rules on the recovery and resolution of credit institutions and investment firms at union level. to this end, the proposed framework aims to protect the union's banking and financial system and to minimise the losses to taxpayers. the proposal is justified as it provides for the development of a number of measures to address the problems of banking crises, which will be able to combat the crisis of the banking system in the light of the need to protect the core services provided by banks and limit the cases of state aid to failing institutions, taking into account the interests of the market and society. the proposal ensures that, in the event that the financial situation of a bank deteriorates beyond repair, critical functions of the bank can be saved and the costs of restructuring and resolution of failing banks are borne not by taxpayers but by the bank's owners and creditors.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report on the system for the recovery and resolution of credit institutions and investment firms. the banking union is one of the key tools to put europe back on the path to economic recovery and growth. i think this is another important step forward in the creation of a banking union. the new system will help to protect the union's banking and financial system and minimise losses for taxpayers. it will also help to restore the necessary confidence of consumers and businesses and ensure the smooth financing of the economy, reduce the current fragmentation of the single market and ensure a level playing field in the union. it should be noted that the proposal ensures that the costs of restructuring and resolution of failing banks are borne not by taxpayers but by the bank's owners and creditors.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in order to take account of market developments and the experience gained so far by market participants and supervisors, it is essential to harmonise national provisions relating to the obligations and liability of depositaries, remuneration policies and sanctions. a clear obligation should be imposed on the management companies of collective investment undertakings to formulate and implement remuneration policies and practices. it should be noted that investors currently enjoy different levels of protection in different jurisdictions. moreover, the financial crisis has shown that the salary and incentive schemes normally applied in the financial institutions sector have themselves increased the impact and scale of the crisis. the pay policies encouraged short-term decisions and excessive risk-taking. in this context, i believe that common eu standards will help ensure sound and effective risk management, implement the principles of sound remuneration policies and help restore consumer confidence in financial markets.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the directive on the comparability of charges relating to payment accounts, the portability of payment accounts and the availability of payment accounts with basic features. this directive lays down rules on the transparency and comparability of charges for payment accounts and rules on the transfer of a payment account within the union. this directive also defines a framework of rules and conditions under which member states are to guarantee consumers the right to open and use payment accounts with basic features in the union.
in writing. - i agree with the report. access to a bank account is an important requirement for the smooth functioning of the european internal market. a bank account is essential for participating in social and economic life. however, many people have difficulty opening a payment account. the refusal to grant a bank account is due to a variety of reasons and prevents eu citizens from enjoying the full benefits of financial services. in this context, i believe that appropriate measures must be implemented at eu level to ensure that consumers are not separated from their daily lives or from new technological developments. it is not enough to ensure access to the account. it is important to ensure that bank charges are transparent and understandable so that consumers can compare and make an informed decision about their banking service provider. consumers must be able to transfer their bank account in order to protect their economic interests and to promote fair competition between banking service providers in a genuinely functioning single market. it should be noted that the functioning and completion of the single market require the promotion of free and informed consumer choice and the facilitation of consumer mobility.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the regulation increasing the number of judges at the general court. since its establishment, the gradual extension of the jurisdiction of the general court has led to a steady increase in the number of cases currently before it, which has led to an increase in the number of cases before it and an increase in the length of time for which proceedings are to be brought. the regulation provides that, since the geographical balance is already achieved by taking account of the different national legal systems, additional judges should be appointed on the sole basis of their professional and personal suitability and their knowledge of the legal systems of the european union and of the member states. however, in any event, there should be no more than two judges from one member state.
in writing. - i voted for this report. the judiciary must be able to carry out its control functions in a timely manner. this is what citizens expect in order to ensure the rule of law. however, the number of cases left unaddressed by the general court has steadily increased in recent years. in these circumstances, it is necessary to reduce the workload as quickly as possible. i agree that the most effective way to do this is to increase the number of judges. it is therefore appropriate to appoint an additional 12 judges. that's the right number. it should be noted that the requirement of geographical balance must be taken into account in the appointment of judges. the selection of the additional judges should be limited to the order of merit, without regard to nationality. candidates could be proposed by the governments of all member states.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the introduction of this system in vehicles in all eu member states is one of the union's top priorities for improving road safety and intelligent transport systems in europe. the use of ecall could be more effective in reducing road fatalities and preventing the most serious injuries. the system will also help to significantly reduce congestion caused by traffic accidents and thus reduce traffic on european roads in general. i welcome the proposal for a decision on the introduction of the interoperable european emergency call system 'ecall', but in order to allow member states to set up the appropriate bpc infrastructure and for the automotive industry to install technical adaptations in all new cars, i support the proposal to set a later date for the entry into force of this legislation. it is important to note that it should be ensured that all citizens across the eu, and not only those with vehicles equipped with ecall, can benefit from the gnss positioning technologies already widely deployed.
in writing. - i agree with the proposed regulation. it is essential to reduce the cost of deploying high-speed and ultra-high-speed electronic communications networks. in particular, infrastructure should be deployed in a more cost-effective manner and administrative burdens should be reduced through the sharing of existing infrastructure and the coordination of civil engineering works. a common european approach will help to avoid unnecessary duplication and to exploit economies of scale. the eu added value of the proposed measures is evident when compared with the individual measures at national, regional or local level. on the other hand, member states must retain freedom in organisational matters and local and regional authorities should continue to play a key role in coordinating the construction work carried out under their jurisdiction. it should be noted that the development of ict services should be used as a means of improving public services, and it should also promote the activities of smes and stimulate their development. access to civil infrastructure is not a 'magic bullet' that will solve all competition problems in the telecommunications sector, but it can make it much easier for all operators to deploy high-speed broadband networks. particular attention should be paid to attracting private sector investment to rural and remote areas.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it approves the new wording of the regulation on the capacity of the community fleet to promote inland waterway transport. this regulation proposes to extend the scope of the regulation to include training programmes for all crew members and the possibility of using funds for innovation and environmentally relevant adaptation to technical progress on board ships. the characteristic of the inland waterway sector is its structure, which is mainly based on smes, i.e. private operators who own, work and live with their families on board vessels. these smes are particularly vulnerable in times of crisis. it is therefore necessary to provide support to the inland waterway sector to maintain and strengthen what is sought by this newly framed regulation. this parliament's report also sought to highlight its cost-effectiveness and energy and environmental efficiency, and to draw attention to the fact that this efficiency could be further improved. although congestion in road transport is a problem with economic and environmental costs, the inland waterway network has a high capacity to absorb traffic and avoid congestion. we should make better use of these advantages.
in writing. - i support the commission's proposal to extend the scope of the current regulation so that the reserve funds can be used to support the inland waterway sector more effectively and to create conditions for innovation and greater safety and environmental friendliness. a well-designed policy with short- and medium-term objectives to be achieved would be a value for a sector with great potential. the legal certainty is a necessary precondition for attracting or promoting investment in the sector, thus ensuring the sustainable development of the inland waterway market. it should be noted that the structure of the inland waterway sector is predominantly based on smes, which are particularly vulnerable during a crisis, and that it is therefore necessary to provide support to maintain and strengthen the inland waterway sector. in addition, cost-effectiveness and energy and environmental efficiency of inland waterway transport should be continuously improved. i very much welcome the intention to invest more in greening and innovation measures. i think that the european commission, together with the representatives of the sector, should examine how the funds could be used more efficiently and how the financial instruments for the sector could be optimised.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it was the one which gave parliament its approval to the regulation on information on agricultural products and their promotion in the internal market and in third countries. this document regulates the promotion of products in order to respond immediately to agricultural crises caused by price collapses or epidemics. the recent e. coli food scandal has once again highlighted the shortcomings of the cap in terms of the need to implement rapidly measures to restore consumer confidence. the rapporteur proposes to introduce an emergency procedure to be used to launch information and publicity campaigns in the event of a health crisis or market disturbance.
in writing. - in the current economic climate, it is appropriate to increase the value of eu production once again. the implementation of the measures envisaged will allow for a more effective management of the issues related to the globalisation of trade and will help to respond to emerging crises. i think that special attention should be paid to smes. regulation should be flexible to ensure that agricultural crises caused by price falls or epidemics are dealt with promptly. i support the proposal to introduce an emergency procedure to launch information and publicity campaigns in the event of a health crisis or market disturbance. in addition, more support should be given to campaigns on the external market, which are currently underfunded. i propose to maintain the single classification of measures and to extend the list of eligible products. i believe that wine should be included in the list of products under the same conditions as other agricultural products. in order to enhance the protection of eu products against counterfeiting and falsification in third countries, the services related to technical assistance to operators should include consultation and assistance.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the decision by parliament to involve the eu in the active and easier living research and development programme, which is run jointly by several member states. with the expectation of a rise in the number of people aged 65 to 80 years (40% between 2010 and 2030), europe faces the important and urgent challenge of finding solutions to healthy and active ageing and demographic change in the 21st century. in addition, the economy also offers opportunities for the development of innovative services and products that meet social needs and stimulate economic development and new business models. for example, information and communication technologies (ict) can play an important role in improving the quality of life of older people and their carers and in increasing the economic efficiency of care. ict-based solutions can also lead to a more energy-efficient health and social care system.
in writing. - i support the proposed research and development programme for active and easier living. demographic ageing is one of the main challenges facing all member states. the union needs to create favourable conditions for investing in knowledge and innovation in order to ensure smart, sustainable and inclusive growth. it is worth noting that the increased use of new technologies will help to control costs, as well as to increase the well-being of older people, to promote their active participation in society. the programme will contribute to the competitiveness of the union economy and will help improve the quality of life of union citizens and the efficiency of healthcare systems. the measures envisaged should pay particular attention to data protection. the right to privacy must be respected without reservation. furthermore, the r&d&i programme for active and enhanced living should build on the achievements of the previous programme and address the shortcomings of the previous programme.
in writing. - i agree with this report. it is essential to support, at eu level, transnational research activities carried out by smes active in research, and to contribute to the integration, coordination and synchronisation of national research funding programmes. it should be noted that research and development programmes provide added value to european research and development smes and should therefore be of a continuous nature. stronger integration and cost-effective, transparent and efficient administration are needed to realise the benefits for r&d smes. it is essential to ensure that procedures are transparent and that projects are adequately financed. for the purposes of simplification, the administrative burden should be reduced for all parties.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the eu's participation in a european programme of innovation and research in metrology, which is being implemented jointly by several member states. metrology is an essential element of the high-tech world. the main objective of the european programme for metrology innovation and research (empir) is to provide metrology solutions that support innovation and industrial competitiveness, as well as measurement technologies to address societal challenges such as health, environment and energy, including support for policy formulation and implementation. another important objective of the proposal is to establish an integrated european metrology research system with critical mass and active at regional, national, european and international levels.
in writing. - i agree with the motion for a resolution. metrology is an essential element in the world of high technology. it is therefore essential that the eu has an ambitious and more inclusive agenda. the european metrology innovation and research programme should provide for measures to support innovation and industrial competitiveness and an efficient financing mechanism. i believe that this programme will contribute to the harmonisation of metrology throughout europe and the world. i believe that it will be active at regional, national, european and international levels and will maintain the high level of scientific, managerial and financial integration already achieved. it is welcome that the scope of the programme has been extended to include activities in the fields of innovation and industrial applications, research for normalisation, standardisation and regulatory purposes and capacity building.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it supports the eu's participation in the second european-developing country partnership programme in the field of clinical research, which is being jointly implemented by several member states. the objective of this programme is to accelerate the clinical development of effective, safe and affordable medical interventions for poverty-related diseases. cooperation with developing countries, such as those in sub-saharan africa, is essential to overcome the negative effects of poverty-related diseases, which affect one billion people and tragically claim so many lives. the impact of poverty-related diseases on the wider world, and in particular on europe, where many of these diseases have been eradicated, should also be assessed.
in writing. - i voted for this report. cooperation with developing countries is essential to overcome the negative effects of poverty-related diseases. this clinical research partnership between europe and developing countries is one of the key tools for ensuring the clinical development of effective, safe and affordable medical interventions for poverty-related diseases. it is essential to pay particular attention to the best economic offers and to closer cooperation with other public and private sector organisations and voluntary organisations. i think there should be a flexible approach to co-financing, so that countries can participate in a non-cash way. it is important to ensure that the projects with the highest potential are supported and that money is not allocated to projects solely because they are included in the list. it is also essential to increase the number of participants in the programme and to provide information on funding opportunities on a regular basis.
in writing. - currently, a creditor seeking to secure recovery in another member state is facing difficulties due to the applicable rules on the issuing of an account freezing order and the high costs of enforcement. it should be noted that the european account preservation order will allow the application of provisional safeguard measures to accounts of debtors resident in other eu countries. this will facilitate cross-border debt recovery for citizens and businesses, in particular smes. the effectiveness of the enforcement of judgments in cross-border civil and commercial disputes will also be enhanced. on the other hand, the creditor could use this injunction as an alternative to national procedures. it would help to recover unpaid debts.
in writing. - i voted for this. it is essential to improve the relevance, consistency and comparability of non-financial information disclosed by companies across the european union. i think it is appropriate to require large companies to regularly update and publish non-financial information at least on environmental, social and human rights issues, anti-corruption and bribery aspects. it is essential that the information provided is verified by independent verification service providers. on the other hand, member states should be able to exempt certain undertakings from the obligation to draw up a non-financial statement if the undertaking submits a separate report covering the same financial year and containing the same content. in addition, the obligation to disclose diversity policies in administrative, management and supervisory bodies relating to aspects such as age, gender, education and professional experience should be limited to certain large undertakings.
in writing. - i support the proposed directive. legal immigration is an important factor for the eu's economic growth and long-term competitiveness, and it is therefore necessary to develop legal migration measures for highly skilled workers. regulating the legal migration of highly qualified workers will help to activate the mechanisms for concrete and effective joint action in the field of cooperation. the new rules will ensure a gradual simplification of procedures, a clearer harmonisation of the regulatory framework and more flexible working relationships. the measures envisaged will help to avoid any abuse and to link employment opportunities to market dynamics. the directive will also contribute to economic growth and the competitiveness of multinational companies, and will help achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. it should be noted that harmonised, simplified and transparent procedures will remove the constraints of the current system and allow companies to access highly qualified personnel. the harmonisation of procedures will lead to better overall immigration management.

in writing. - i voted for this report. the tripartite social summit on growth and employment is a key high-level event, involving the heads of state or government and the ministers for labour and social affairs of the presidency countries, to discuss social and employment issues in the run-up to the spring and autumn european council meetings. it is also a great opportunity to hear the views and proposals of representatives from both sides of the social dialogue. in order to ensure effective and visible coordination of high-level activities between the eu institutions and the eu social partners, it is essential that this decision be aligned with the institutional changes required by the lisbon treaty, taking into account the recent positive results of the meeting. it would also make a few editorial corrections and ensure a fast review process.
in writing. - i agree with the position in the report. it is regrettable that, both in the procedure for reaching an agreement on the mff 2014-2020 and in the political discussions surrounding these negotiations, there was no common vision on the eu budget and political priorities, and that the eu institutions had widely divergent views and did not fully take into account the increased role and prerogatives of parliament as set out in the lisbon treaty. another important aspect is that the role of the eu budget in overcoming the current economic and social crisis has not been properly recognised. it should be noted that the eu budget is an investment budget with a high leverage effect and enabling the implementation of a large number of projects that would otherwise be difficult or impossible to implement. it is a powerful reform driver for growth, competitiveness and job creation across the union. in this context, it is essential to ensure that any new provisions successfully incorporated into the mff regulation and the interinstitutional agreement are fully implemented during the annual budgetary procedure. the practice of previous financial programmes, where the eu budget was much lower than the mff ceiling, cannot be followed. in addition, the accumulation of outstanding commitments has reached a critical level that could push the eu budget into a structural deficit over time.
in writing. i am very pleased that at the end of this european parliament's term of office we will be adopting a directive to ensure that workers who have worked in at least several eu countries during their careers and have participated in supplementary pension schemes have the right to acquire and retain supplementary occupational pensions. the overly strict rules of occupational pension funds often tie the worker to a particular job for a long time and force him to postpone returning to work in his home country. those who change jobs risk losing their pension rights. this directive addresses those objections. first, the acquisition and waiting period for pension rights, which is currently quite long in some countries - up to 10 years - is reduced to 3 years. the introduction of a strict minimum age of 21 for participation in the pension scheme is a significant improvement, as in some countries workers have only been able to participate in supplementary pension schemes from the age of 28. all these measures will significantly reduce the barriers to accessing supplementary occupational pensions, as long waiting periods and high retirement age increase the risk of losing supplementary social security benefits upon retirement. the directive also contains an important provision that in cases where the worker who leaves the employment relationship has not yet acquired pension rights, he would be reimbursed for the contributions he has paid.
in writing. mr president, about one million european union citizens are seconded to another member state each year. the free movement of workers and the freedom to provide services are the main pillars of the common internal market. therefore, special protection should be granted to this category of workers. this is particularly important in times of economic crisis. the specific characteristics of this category of workers mobility and competitiveness can have an impact on job retention and stimulate economic growth based on the creation of new jobs. the eu's freedom to provide services must, of course, be accompanied by the guarantee and safeguarding of the relevant workers' rights. in this context, it is necessary to develop the harmonised legal instruments provided for in this directive. closer cooperation with the member states is necessary to ensure better protection of this category of workers. the basis of this international cooperation is the optimal reduction of unemployment in the eu member states. it is worth remembering, however, that this objective cannot be the sole focus of our actions. to achieve this, we must focus on the principle of non-discrimination against workers on the basis of their nationality or citizenship. the rapporteur has raised very important issues and has proposed ways of addressing them in a supportive way. the new legislation and amendments to the legislation must not allow employers to circumvent the law in any way and must help to combat unfair treatment of employees.
on behalf of the ppe group thank you, rapporteurs, thank you, commissioner, for the european commission's position on this, i would say, important issue for all of us. first, considering what is probably an important issue from both a consumer and an environmental perspective. we often talk in our countries about whether the european parliament has enough power, how much power it has and how much it influences our daily lives. i think this directive, the proposal we are talking about today, on plastic bags, is a great illustration of the fact that the decisions made here reflect the lives of each of us and, in this case, our habits. we all deal with plastic bags on a daily basis, and the question is often asked how to avoid and reduce their use. on the other hand, we may rarely think about the number and extent of plastic bags we use. the numbers have already been touched and listed a little bit. and as we know, plastic waste and its use cause a lot of environmental problems. plastic waste is highly perishable, and its tiny particles pollute land, water and wildlife. the commission's proposal to reduce the use of plastic bags is very timely and, i would say, ambitious, and i would disagree with the rapporteur, who may think it is too little ambitious and should be welcomed. however, here in the european parliament, after long discussions, the political groups have reached an agreement in principle on the main steps to reduce the use of bags. and we immediately had a question about the definition of what kind of bags we were talking about, and that led to a certain division into these so-called small bags and 10 to 50 micron bags. however, we agreed that any measures must be realistic and proportionate. we have had a lot of discussions in the epp and, while we support the common objective of reducing the use of bags, we are not sure that the measures chosen will be effective. we believe that it may be difficult to reduce consumption so quickly and so drastically, especially given that the choice of base year itself, the reference points, is questionable. for example, not all the national statistics on which it is intended to base itself correspond to the data provided by the commission. another aspect that makes it difficult to agree on is the measures that the state can take, whether it be taxation or some other instrument. one of the proposals under consideration - a total ban on plastic bags - is, in our view, irrational and unworkable, and we therefore propose to abandon the introduction of new bans. the compromise agreed by the groups places too little emphasis on public education measures, which some countries are already implementing. the issue of alternatives while there is general agreement that plastic bags should be reduced, it is physically impossible to completely eliminate them. this seems to be a matter of disagreement not only between the political groups but also between certain national delegations. it is a pity that with the upcoming elections we do not have time for a longer discussion. it is to be expected that the council and the commission will pursue this matter with parliament. and just a quick note on the biodegradable bags. as far as i am aware, the commission was initially of a somewhat different opinion, so it would probably be interesting to hear the commission's observations on this matter again here in this plenary session.
several months ago we did not answer the question whether ukraine was ready for associate membership. a few months ago, we didn't answer the question of whether we could afford to lose ukraine. we have no answer to the question of whether we can stop the war in europe. over the past five years, we've had many conversations about secondary issues, like which city should be the headquarters of the european union, or the invention of the third gender. by devoting our energies to these things, we have shown ourselves to be politicians who are unable to solve not only the problems of growing social exclusion, youth unemployment, but, most importantly, to save a nation and europe from war. madam president, today is still the time when the european union, acting as the europe of peoples, speaking with one voice and agreeing on real solutions, can still stop the madness that is beginning. this is a rhetorical question, but i have to ask, and if we were sitting in kiev tomorrow, that would be a concrete enough step and a weighty argument to avoid the impending disaster.
mr president, this is my last speech in the house as well. the belle époque of european democracy, free will and unity building comes to an end. the epoch of bloody confrontation is reopened in the near east. let us listen, we europeans, to what the kremlin is saying by its action: ‘go away, union, with your childish values, since you lack the will and wisdom to defend them’. the main, if not the only, remaining value and virtue now is military muscle and force. the only international law still valid and observed by non-european and anti-european russia is force majeure. a clear signal came some time ago, when the ruler of russia went to the national tv forum to answer call-in questions. he was asked one by a little boy: ‘mr president, among my classmates there are two boys constantly fighting. which one of them is guilty for that?’ the president answered him then, but also you, ladies and gentlemen: ‘the one who was beaten is guilty’. did you listen? no. that sentence, worthy of the entire ‘mein kampf’, was never debated. maybe it is better understood now. ukraine was beaten, first in crimea, thus ukraine is guilty. tomorrow, if there is a big war in our east, some of you will blame ukraine. but we in europe will be beaten as well. we supported the europe-aspiring ukraine; we annoyed the monster of reborn russian fascism; thus we are guilty and should be struck down as well. why did we behave so provocatively, damning the eastern neighbourhood, not asking the grand master? this is my last speech, with some seconds left. i call on you, my former colleagues, as many will continue to serve here, not to repeat russian formulas in conformity with evil, in a manner approaching slavery. the european democracies and their union must awake and seek their moral rebirth.
in writing. the european union must realise that since the end of february, parts of europe have been living in a completely different era of international relations. forget about the validity of the principles of international law, forget about inclusion policy. it's not working anymore. we cannot expect the aggressor to sit down and take a bow. what is happening in ukraine now is a struggle between different value and political systems, between democracy and authoritarianism. the only effective eu response to these processes is the development of european political and economic rules. the eu must be clear that the only way for the eastern european countries to break out of this cycle is for the eu to open up the prospect of eu membership to them. the 2004 development has shown that it is the most effective tool the eu has at its disposal for promoting democracy and european values. the association process envisaged by the eastern partnership policy must therefore become an eu integration process. it is time to make it clear that the eu will respond to the internal reforms and direction of the partner countries with an open door policy. the eastern partnership policy must become a stepping stone towards full membership of the european union.
in writing. - i voted on this report, which gave its support to an important draft directive at the last session of this parliament. this directive aims to facilitate the mobility of workers between member states by improving the acquisition and preservation of pension rights of participants in supplementary pension schemes. the strict rules of occupational pension funds often tie the worker to a particular job for a long time and reduce his mobility and force him to postpone returning to work in his home country. those who change jobs risk losing their pension rights. the new directive is an attempt to address these contradictions. member states will be required to adopt the necessary measures to ensure that workers who retire can maintain their acquired pension rights in the supplementary pension scheme in which they were acquired and preserve their value. this directive does not provide for the transfer of acquired pension rights, but encourages states to seek ways of doing so. for those who have become entitled to benefits, they will be transferred to the country where the pensioner resides. participants in pension schemes will also be better informed about the possible consequences of termination of employment. the directive must be transposed into national law within four years. it is expected that during this period occupational pension funds will also start to be established in lithuania and that the incentives for those who have left abroad to work will increase.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in the old eu countries, occupational pension schemes are well developed and can provide for the accumulation of a supplementary old-age pension. the strict rules of occupational pension funds often tie the worker to a particular job for a long time and reduce his mobility. the new directive aims to address these contradictions. the reduction of the waiting period for the acquisition of pension rights to 3 years is to be welcomed. in addition, the entry into force of the directive will reduce the age limit for participants in pension schemes to 21 years, it should be noted that these measures should significantly reduce the barriers to the acquisition of supplementary pension rights, as long waiting periods and a high age threshold increase the risk of losing supplementary social security benefits upon retirement. i believe that the necessary measures should be put in place in each member state to ensure that workers who retire can retain their pension rights in the supplementary pension scheme in which they acquired them and preserve their value. in addition, workers who leave the workforce should be given the possibility to maintain their pension rights in the supplementary pension scheme in which they acquired them and to preserve their value, while those entitled to benefits will be transferred to the country where the pensioner resides.
in writing. - i support the proposed amendments to the regulation which aim to establish a common, harmonised system of official control procedures. official controls are important for ensuring a high level of protection of human, animal and plant health and for the proper implementation of comprehensive legislation throughout the food chain and for consumer confidence. it is therefore essential that a single overall framework covering controls of all relevant sectors of the agri-food chain applies in the eu. the proposal contains a major revision of the existing provisions in order to eliminate any duplication of legislation. the new regulation will simplify the common legal framework and provide a coherent approach across all areas. it will also be possible to apply the principle of risk-based controls more consistently and to ensure that controls are financed in a stable and consistent manner. attention should be paid to ensuring that consumers are guaranteed safety by a uniform minimum number of controls.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because this proposal for a regulation is important for public health, since approximately 70% of infectious diseases affect both animals and humans. this regulation aims to establish legislation on the control of animal diseases rather than legislation on animal health. it deals with animal diseases that infect animals and humans. it is important to design the new animal health legislation from the perspective of the need to protect animal health, i.e. by recognising the link between good animal husbandry and good animal health. references to animal welfare are therefore necessary in this respect where they relate to animal health, for example as part of biosecurity measures. it is also appropriate to recall in this regulation the importance of good animal husbandry and the requirements set out in eu legislation on the movement of animals. another important issue in this area is the problem of stray animals. they are also frequently the vectors of infectious diseases and require strict monitoring and control measures by the competent authorities. for this reason, it is necessary to clarify the definition of wild animals in the regulation to include animals previously kept but no longer under the direct control of the owner.
in writing. - i support the proposal which aims to amend some 40 different european legislation in this area. based on the priorities of smart regulation and taking into account scientific evidence and international standards, it is essential that the eu has a single, simplified, transparent and clear regulatory framework. this system will allow for a clear and balanced distribution of duties and responsibilities between the competent authorities of the member states, the eu institutions, animal owners and other entities in relation to the monitoring, prevention and control of different animal diseases. the increased focus on animal disease control is welcome and a clear link between animal welfare and animal and public health has been established. we must constantly stress the importance of good animal husbandry and the requirements set out in eu legislation on the transport of animals. another important issue in this area is the problem of stray animals. they are also frequently the vectors of infectious diseases and require strict monitoring and control measures by the competent authorities. in addition, preventive, proactive measures should be established for the monitoring and control of non-listed diseases. it should be noted that effective control of infectious animal diseases is the best basis for the functioning of the single market in trade in live animals, animal products and food and it is therefore essential that the new legislation is easy to understand and apply.
in writing. - i voted for this report. plant health is a key factor in ensuring sustainable and competitive agriculture, horticulture and forestry. it is therefore essential that common measures against plant pests are applied in the eu. these measures must reduce the phytosanitary risks and ensure a high level of protection, a rational development of agricultural production and a sufficient supply. it is welcome that the new official control regulation provides for an exemption for smes, allowing them to recover the costs of plant health checks. it is also essential to give priority to sustainable biological, physical and other non-chemical methods. however, it is essential to ensure that the level of plant protection products and other measures used does not exceed what is necessary. environmental damage should first be remedied at source and compensated by the polluter. i support the proposal to give the commission the power to prioritise certain quarantine organisms.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report on consumer product safety, as product safety is a key element in ensuring the proper functioning of the single market and thus facilitating the growth of european businesses and the prosperity of the european union. the european parliament wants this regulation to continue to act as a safety net to ensure product safety. the existing legislation, the general product safety directive, emphasises that the directive applies where there are no more specific safety provisions in eu legislation, thus ensuring the safety of all products placed on the market. the alignment of the current provisions with this regulation the safety regulation will act as a shield to close this gap. this new regulation should therefore be a broad framework of legislation of a horizontal nature to address issues and fill gaps relating to existing products or products that could be developed.
in writing. - i agree with the commission's proposal, it is important that this regulation continues to act as a safety net to ensure product safety and to help address the issues and fill the gaps. in this context, the implementation of the precautionary principle, which is of particular importance for consumers, economic operators and market surveillance authorities, plays a particular role. in the single market, we must protect the most vulnerable consumers and increase their confidence in e-commerce. i support the proposal to require the indication of origin on products or their packaging. it is also appropriate to introduce the new ce+ marking to indicate that the marked product has been tested by an independent third party and by a competent body. it is necessary to ensure that eu fines and sanctions are harmonised and applied in a targeted manner, it is noted that cooperation with the product safety and market surveillance authorities of the united states is essential to improve the current situation and the regulatory regime in the union. training on product safety legislation and information to industry and economic operators must be provided on a regular basis.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this draft regulation on product market surveillance, given the increasingly intensive movement of goods within the single market of the european union. the free movement of goods is one of the four pillars of the eu's single market. this pillar and the proper functioning of the single market are based on consumer confidence: european consumers need to be confident that, irrespective of where the product is made and in which member state they can buy it, the products they use are both safe and compliant. this should be ensured by effective and high-quality market surveillance. this supervision should ensure that products do not pose a risk to the health or safety of consumers or workers and that they comply with existing eu legislation, such as rules on energy efficiency, noise levels or emission limit values. at the same time, market surveillance authorities are expected to address new emerging challenges, such as the growth of e-commerce.
in writing. - the free movement of goods is one of the four pillars of the eu's single market. it is therefore essential that the eu has a functioning common european market surveillance system that applies to all products on the internal market, including products entering the eu market. i agree with the commission's proposal and the objectives it pursues. it is essential that market surveillance takes account of all the different aspects and that common safety rules are applied. new, innovative, market-based solutions need to be applied at eu level to complement market surveillance actions. another important aspect is closer, stronger and more effective cooperation between the supervisory authorities of the member states. it is therefore appropriate to establish a european market surveillance forum. in order to enhance the impact of dissuasive sanctions, it is necessary to apply harmonised administrative sanctions in the eu and to establish a public blacklist of operators who repeatedly infringe this regulation.
in writing. - i agree with the proposed regulation. it is essential that the same requirements apply in all member states in relation to the disclosure of trading data to the public, the reporting of transactions, and the trading of derivatives on organised trading venues. it is essential that access to the clearing system and the possibility to trade benchmarks be granted in a non-discriminatory manner. particular attention should be paid to the impact of the proposed regulation on the non-financial corporate sector. it is essential that non-financial corporations are not considered to be traditional financial institutions by their nature, as their activities do not pose any systemic risk and are of crucial importance to corporate business.
in writing. - the markets in financial instruments directive is one of the key pillars of eu financial regulation. a review of this directive is necessary in order to address the shortcomings of this directive and to increase the transparency and regulation of financial markets. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any evidence that the financial institutions have engaged in a systemic risk-based approach to the conduct of their business. in addition, if non-financial undertakings are to be considered financial undertakings, they must fulfil the costly obligations laid down in several financial regulations. risk management will be much more costly for them, and some enterprises, in particular smes, will abandon or reduce their risk management. i believe that there should be a clear distinction between products that can be bartered and that are offered for commercial purposes and do not have the characteristics of other derivatives. the position management approach should be preferred as it is more flexible and better adapted to market characteristics.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is necessary to take a closer look at the current post-trade settlement systems in order to assess what could be improved to best meet the needs of all investors. other objectives of this legislation are to encourage the emergence of new market participants in order to foster a competitive environment, reduce the cost of cross-border settlements and reduce counterparty risk. following the financial crisis in 2008, the european union has for several years carried out a systematic assessment of each stage of the trading cycle in order to strengthen its financial structures and prevent future crises. with the european commission's proposal for a regulation on the improvement of securities settlement and central securities depositories (csds), we can now move to the final stage of this process, which includes the post-trading infrastructure and services. in the past, the focus has been on reducing investor costs and improving efficiency, but in the post-crisis period financial regulators and policymakers have also focused on the post-crisis environment to ensure that our financial infrastructure minimises counterparty risk, is robust and meets the needs of end-investors.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is important to ensure the safety of ships and their crews and to avoid accidents at sea and pollution of the marine environment. this includes products such as navigation aids, life-saving equipment or fire-fighting systems. the new directive thus aims to strengthen the mechanisms for the implementation and enforcement of ship safety rules, thus ensuring the proper functioning of the internal market for marine equipment, while at the same time ensuring a high level of safety at sea and prevention of marine pollution. it is also intended to simplify the regulatory environment by ensuring that the requirements of the international maritime organisation (imo) are applied and implemented in a harmonised manner throughout the union. the shipbuilding industry is a leading industrial sector with a high added value and attracting high investments in research and development. the relevant stakeholders include not only european shipbuilders, many of whom are small and medium-sized enterprises (smes), and shipyards, but also passengers, ship crews and public administrations. the proposal for a directive should therefore aim to improve the safety of ships and their crews, while promoting the marine equipment industry and creating more jobs.
in writing. - i think it is time to focus more on citizens' links with europe and on strengthening the relationship between national parliaments and the european parliament. it should be noted that the relationship between the european parliament and national parliaments must be based not on competition or antagonism but on mutual cooperation. it is therefore essential that political dialogue be based on a european ethical mindset. in particular, national parliaments need to be given greater scope to participate in policy-making at eu level. we need to create new mechanisms to strengthen and intensify cooperation between national parliaments and the european parliament, taking into account their specific roles. legitimation and accountability need to be strengthened through concrete measures.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal for a directive on the rules governing the placing on the market of pressure equipment in order to ensure its safety. the new legislative framework creates a simplified regulatory environment for products and facilitates the consistent implementation of technical standards. this will help to improve the functioning of the single market by ensuring a uniform treatment of non-compliant products and economic operators and a uniform assessment of notified bodies throughout the european union market. in addition, the harmonisation of the new regulatory framework will increase confidence among both producers and consumers, as the obligations of economic operators will be more clearly defined. on the other hand, member states will be given more effective tools to carry out market surveillance, which will reduce the number of non-compliant and unsafe products on the market.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, which lays down the rules governing the action of the european maritime safety agency in response to pollution from ships and marine pollution from oil and gas installations. the agency has a long-term responsibility in the field of pollution response and therefore needs financial security based on a multiannual commitment to carry out the related tasks efficiently and carefully. in carrying out its activities, the european border and coast guard agency shall liaise with experts in the member states in the field of pollution control and shall cooperate with the international maritime organisation (imo) in accordance with existing regional agreements. the agency shall collect, analyse and disseminate information on best practices, techniques and innovations in the field of response to marine pollution. various evaluation activities have been carried out over the past 10 years. the main conclusions drawn from these assessments are positive. the budget for the remediation of the effects of pollution was found to be sufficient. in many cases, it was noted that the measures financed were cost-effective, provided added value and were well managed.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because both the european union and the member states themselves have an important role to play in supporting enterprises and to this end, to increase access to finance for enterprises and to encourage entrepreneurship. unemployment is the biggest social problem facing europe today. so we need to focus on how europe can create the right conditions for job creation. we must also remember the basic principle that it is not the eu growth plans but businesses, especially small and medium-sized enterprises (smes), micro-enterprises, entrepreneurs and innovators, that create jobs. businesses will only be able to create jobs and employ more people if market conditions are favourable, if they can rely on a skilled workforce, if the labour market is sufficiently flexible, if labour costs, including wages, are in line with productivity indicators, if social protection systems ensure the attractiveness of work, and if regulation is proportionate and evidence-based. it is therefore essential that businesses, especially smes and micro-enterprises, have the knowledge and understanding of how to access eu support, for example through the one-stop shop and online portals.
in writing. - unemployment is the euʼs biggest social challenge. taking into account the dire situation in the eu and its member states, it is high time that the eu and its member states acted to improve this. it is especially important now when additional mechanisms from the eu and its member states are required in order to achieve the target of 75[nbsp ]% in active employment by 2020 set by the eu in the europe 2020 strategy. this report lists all the important incentives the eu needs to offer in order to improve the employment situation. it stresses the innovative character of entrepreneurship, it underlines the importance of smes with their huge potential for job creation, and it highlights the significance of eliminating skill shortages and skill mismatches in the eu and its member states. i welcome this crucial report for the eu and consider that the member states should take further steps to tackle high unemployment, especially among youth, the low-skilled and the long-term unemployed. lithuania, as a country with a high unemployment rate, should take all necessary steps to address this challenge and promote entrepreneurship and the right regulatory environment for better job creation.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because, in the light of the current economic crisis and the high level of youth unemployment, one of the increasingly important tasks facing the member states is to provide young people with access to quality education. as regards new technologies, there is still a lack of equal access to digital infrastructure in the european union. moreover, in terms of digital literacy of the workforce, the eu still lags behind the united states and asia. the integration of ict into mainstream practice in european education and training systems is still not possible, as there is a large gap between the everyday use of digital technologies and their use in education and training institutions. cooperation between european education and training institutions should be encouraged in order to facilitate the development of open educational resources. the increased use of digital technologies provides educational and training institutions, teachers, students and pupils with opportunities to teach and learn in innovative ways. in the field of education and training, it is therefore essential to further develop existing methods and to promote innovative teaching and learning methods. open educational resources can play a major role in achieving the europe 2020 goals and making lifelong learning a reality.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. in particular, improved rules on the posting of workers to another eu country will facilitate the fight against fictitious posting and prevent labour law violations. clearer rules will improve workers' rights. the achievement of a proper balance between the freedom to provide services and the protection of posted workers is to be welcomed. the criteria set out in the new rules will help states to prevent the fictitious posting of workers and to ensure that the posting of workers does not make fictitious use of the self-employed status, which provides a worker with lower social protection. i agree with the proposals that a stricter control mechanism should be applied and that a clear system of joint and individual liability should be established. it should be noted that greater attention should be paid to border regions, which have a special role to play in ensuring that secondments are conducted in a manner that respects human rights and dignity and in ensuring the exchange of good practices. they should therefore be given the means necessary to fulfil their tasks. in addition, there should be a regular exchange of good practice and free, clear, transparent and comprehensive information for companies and posted workers on the employment contract requirements, social conditions and occupational health and safety applicable in their territory.
in writing. - i agree with the new version of the regulation. we need to ensure that international trade in wildlife specimens is not threatened and that illegal trade is prevented. it should be noted that one of the major international ecological problems is the trade in wild animals and plants. trade in wild animals and plants crosses national borders and is therefore a global problem, requiring the protection of certain species from over-exploitation worldwide. it is therefore essential to update the provisions of the regulation currently in force in accordance with the treaty of lisbon with a view to replacing the obsolete provisions with new ones, in particular taking into account the rules on comitology. measures should be taken to ensure effective control. member states should strictly monitor compliance with the provisions of this regulation and cooperate closely with each other and with the commission in this regard.
