in writing. - i voted in favour of this partnership agreement establishing a partnership between the european union and the republic of seychelles in the fisheries sector. the economy of the seychelles is based mainly on fishing and tourism. seychelles has signed fisheries agreements on the catch of migratory species with the eu, japan, south korea and taiwan. the first eu-seychelles fisheries agreement was signed in 1984[n 1], which eventually became the fisheries partnership agreement. this agreement is vital to maintain the strategic presence of the eu fleet in this key tuna fishing area in the indian ocean, where other major foreign fleets, notably those from japan, china, south korea, the philippines and taiwan, are also active. this protocol is harmonized and mutually beneficial. the objective of the protocol is to ensure the sustainable fishing of the eu fleet in seychelles' waters, and this objective is enshrined in several provisions of the protocol and the fisheries agreement: they aim to ensure the monitoring, control and inspection of fishing activities, including scientific cooperation, a satellite-based vessel monitoring system, etc. in addition, under this fisheries agreement, some 760 jobs in europe are linked to fleet activities in various parts of the sector.
in writing. - it is worth noting that this protocol is beneficial to both sides, both the eu and seychelles, in view of their interests in the fisheries sector. it will help ensure the sustainable fishing of the eu fleet in seychelles' waters. in addition, monitoring, control and effective scientific cooperation will be ensured to promote responsible fishing and sustainable fisheries in seychelles' waters. another important aspect is that the protocol will have an impact on the creation of jobs on board ships. it is noted that the seychelles fishing authority has an effective vessel control system and is therefore able to combat iuu fishing. in the meantime, european vessels authorised to fish in seychelles' waters shall take on board observers designated by seychelles. the extension of the protocol will allow the eu fleet to continue its activities in deep-sea fishing areas and fishing opportunities in this part of the indian ocean, and will also strengthen cooperation between the two countries and develop a sustainable fisheries policy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this partnership agreement establishing a partnership between the european union and the union of the comoros in the fisheries sector. the main objective of this protocol is to pursue sustainable fishing. the objective is to strengthen cooperation between the european union and the union of the comoros, thereby establishing a partnership framework for the development of a sustainable fisheries policy and the responsible exploitation of fisheries resources in the union of the comoros' fishing zone, taking into account the interests of both parties. the european parliament will continue to closely monitor sectoral policy and support as provided for in the protocol. national authorities are encouraged to improve their organisation of their activities to the maximum extent possible in order to ensure that sectoral support is provided as efficiently as possible. it is also necessary to ensure greater accountability for the use of these funds. sectoral support should ensure the continuous operation of the comorian fisheries monitoring centre and the national vessel register, and the development and implementation of a national action plan to combat illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing in the waters of the union of the comoros and by vessels flying the flag of the union of the comoros. it is also recommended that a regional fisheries monitoring centre be set up, to be chaired by the indian ocean tuna commission and the indian ocean states commission.
in writing. - i agree with the recommendation. i believe that it is necessary to strengthen cooperation between the eu and the union of the comoros, taking into account the interests of both parties. it is important to note that this protocol will benefit both parties and the fishermen in this area. this agreement will help create jobs and prosperity. eu vessels will be allowed to fish in the union of the comoros's fishing zone. in addition, a strong partnership framework will help develop sustainable fisheries policies and the responsible use of fisheries resources. attention should be drawn to the need to continue to monitor closely the sectoral policy and sectoral support in order to ensure that support is delivered as effectively as possible. it is necessary to ensure greater accountability for the use of these funds.
in writing. - i'm in favor of this deal. it should be noted that the fisheries partnership agreement between the eu and the republic of madagascar provides for the possibility for european fishing vessels to fish in malagasy waters. the new protocol will better meet the capabilities and needs of the member states' navies. in particular, the catch shall be increased in strict compliance with the best available scientific advice from the indian ocean tuna commission. in addition, strict external financial control and accountability will be implemented. it is important to stress that the eu has a crucial role to play in ensuring sustainable fisheries management and good governance in third countries. it is therefore essential to implement a more coordinated and coherent policy in the light of the objectives of development cooperation.
in writing. - the republic of korea is one of the eu's most important partners in developing political and economic relations in asia. it is a pity that the process of concluding and ratifying the agreement took almost four years. i think that delays in the future which could jeopardise the development of political dialogue should be avoided. it is worth noting that this agreement will strengthen partnerships in the political, economic, social and cultural fields. the parties will also strengthen and deepen cooperation on readmission, with a view to preventing illegal migration and enhancing cooperation in the area of justice, freedom and security.
in writing. - i'm in favor of this deal. the new framework agreement is based on universally recognised principles ensuring respect for democracy, the rule of law and human rights. this agreement shall strengthen political, economic and sectoral cooperation at bilateral, regional and global levels. it is worth noting that the new agreement will provide a framework for closer cooperation between the two parties on key political and global issues. the eu will be able to assume greater responsibility and influence on the korean peninsula, and korea will be able to participate in the eu crisis management process. this is a welcome step towards strengthening bilateral relations and ensuring overall coherence. the parties shall cooperate in order to ensure the development of a secure cyberspace, to promote sustainable development and economic growth.
in writing. - i agree to this protocol. it should be noted that the stabilisation and association process is a specific eu regional strategy aimed at stabilising and developing both individual countries and the region as a whole. it also contributes to closer bilateral relations and offers opportunities for eu integration. it is therefore essential to establish a comprehensive contractual framework for mutual cooperation. in particular, montenegro should continue to pursue consistent political, economic and institutional reforms and adopt the eu's core values and models. in the main areas of the internal market, it is essential to align national legislation with eu legislation. it is noted that this agreement will facilitate and protect mutual investment.
in writing. - i agree to this protocol. given the importance of cooperation between georgia and the european union, it is essential to establish clear financial and technical rules to facilitate georgia's participation in eu programmes. this protocol will thus further promote contacts in the fields of culture, education, environment, technology and science. this will provide a basis for intensifying people-to-people and sector-to-sector cooperation. it is noted that close political relations between the two sides will contribute to the implementation of the neighbourhood policy action plan. this will help to ensure greater political and economic security in georgia and create an environment conducive to investment.
in writing. - i support this communication authorising portugal to apply a reduced rate of excise duty in the autonomous regions of madeira and the azores to certain alcoholic beverages. the continuing difficulties are having a negative impact on the socio-economic situation of the outermost regions and it is appropriate to extend the validity of the authorisation until 31 december 2020 in order to compensate for the additional costs. it is an effective aid measure that contributes to the sustainable development of these regions and also stimulates growth in the single market. it should be noted that this advantage will not distort competition and will not have a negative impact on the internal market.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in order to increase competitiveness and to compensate for the additional costs, it is appropriate to apply a lower aiem tax or to exempt a number of products produced in the canary islands from such a tax. it should be noted that these regions, due to their remoteness, dependence on raw materials and energy resources, small local market and low level of exports, suffer from high production costs which limit development and sustainable development and also have a negative impact on the socio-economic situation of the outermost regions. the application of this measure is therefore necessary and proportionate. in addition, economic activity and competitiveness in the outermost regions will continue to be promoted.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in order to increase competitiveness and to compensate for the additional production costs incurred by remoteness and dependence on raw materials and energy resources, the application of this advantage should be extended to the french outermost regions. it is noted that these difficulties increase the cost of locally produced products and make them less competitive. in view of the possible negative impact on the socio-economic situation of the outermost regions, the application of this measure is proportionate and justified. it will further promote economic activity and competitiveness in these regions and strengthen local industry.
in writing. - i support the proposed amendments to the rules of procedure of the parliament. it should be noted that only efficient and smooth parliamentary work can ensure the promotion of democratic principles and the legitimacy of the decisions taken. the current arrangements should be reviewed in order to improve the quality of question time. i consider the amendments proposed to be justified and useful. in particular, it will provide greater flexibility and make it easier to plan the agenda of the plenary session, ensuring a good quality of work.
in writing. - i support the amendments to the rules of procedure tabled by the committee on legal affairs and citizens' rights. in order to ensure the efficient work of parliament, i believe it is appropriate to allow committees to deliver opinions on resolutions relating to international agreements. these changes will allow parliament to properly carry out the tasks entrusted to it, ensuring the democratic legitimacy of the eu. it is therefore essential that parliament is fully involved in the union's legislative process. it should be noted that the provisions currently in force do not provide for the possibility of expressing an opinion on the above-mentioned issues. i believe that the amendments proposed will close the existing loopholes and ensure respect for democratic principles.
in writing. - i think that electronic document management should be promoted at all times. in order to reduce the circulation of paper documents and the costs associated with their preparation and storage, the majority of parliament's documents should be prepared, signed and circulated in electronic form. in order to ensure a successful and efficient exchange of information and interoperability, the office should establish as soon as possible the requirements for the technical specifications and the layout of the electronic form. it should be noted that electronic documents are an integral part of the management of information resources. it is therefore essential to ensure effective regulation of their management and consistent quality and efficiency assessment of document management processes.
in writing. - i voted for this recommendation. the eu should have clear, mandatory rules governing the application of noise-related operating restrictions at union airports. in particular, in order to identify the most cost-effective solution to the problem of aircraft noise, the rules should be implemented on the basis of a harmonised approach. the transparency of decisions in the assessment and management of aircraft noise is essential. in addition, noise restrictions must be proportionate to the noise problem identified and adapted to the current requirements of the aviation system. i think it is appropriate to leave the decision on the application of noise-related measures at airports to local authorities.
in writing. - i agree with the recommendation. i think this initiative should continue beyond 2020. it is one of the most effective eu instruments in the field of culture. it reflects the diversity and commonalities of local, regional, national and european cultural development and provides effective long-term support for local and regional culture. in the light of experience, it is essential that the structure and content of the current initiative be maintained. in addition, the selection procedure must be streamlined, the sustainability of infrastructure investments ensured, access to additional sources of eu funding and the possibility for the city to lead the regional proposal. more attention should be paid to the host country of the european capital of culture events. the active participation of local and regional authorities should be facilitated. in addition, while safeguarding the european nature of the initiative, it is necessary to ensure that the cultural capital has a long-term strategy and a positive impact on the cultural sector and cultural capital.
in writing. - the european capitals of culture initiative was created in 1985. since then, this has become one of the eu’s best-known campaigns, during which more than forty cities have been awarded the title. this award and the nomination as a capital of culture have proven very effective in promoting mutual understanding between eu citizens, mobilising public and private investment, and highlighting the richness of europe’s culture. this report is very relevant now in order to make eu action for the european capitals of culture for the years 2020 to 2033 even more significant and efficient. it provides a set of indications to simplify the selection procedure and it makes it possible for a city to lead a regional bid. at the same time it recognises the importance of the member state hosting the capital of culture. i consider that this report is crucial in its added-value for promoting the culture, potential and visibility of regions and cities in the eu and its member states. i believe that this initiative can be economically beneficial as well as able to attract tourism, increase investment and additional funds and unite all eu citizens in its cultural diversity.
in writing. - i agree with the position. whale populations have declined dramatically and tens of thousands of whales die in eu waters every year. in this context, it is appropriate to review the adequacy and effectiveness of the provisions of the regulation currently in force in order to ensure adequate protection of cetaceans and to prevent illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing. in particular, particular attention shall be paid to the use of acoustic equipment to prevent accidental capture of cetaceans. in order to guarantee the stable economic and social situation of the fisheries sector, the regulation needs to be adapted to technical and scientific progress. it is therefore essential that the commission be empowered to adopt delegated acts in order to update the technical specifications and conditions of use of sound-absorbing devices.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report on the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a member state, since it is necessary to ensure the protection of cultural objects and, in particular, of national treasures, since internal border controls were abolished on 1 january 1993. in 2013, the commission proposed a new directive because the 1993 directive was not effective enough. another objective of the new wording is to harmonise the laws of the member states concerning compensation paid to the managers of the object to be returned, to place on them the obligation to prove the origin of the object and to abolish the question of the application of the laws of the member states. the operator of the object will therefore have to prove that sufficient care was taken in acquiring the object, i.e. that sufficient care was taken to ensure that its origin was legitimate. the commission also seeks to improve cooperation between the competent national administrative authorities in the field of the return of cultural objects, in order to reduce the need for judicial procedures. the commission therefore proposes that these authorities make use of the new eu instrument for administrative cooperation internal market information system. this system is better suited to the implementation of the directive than any other existing eu instrument, as it helps to provide information on cultural objects classified as national treasures or stolen, includes secure internet access and can be used in all eu languages.
in writing. - i support the position on the new version of the directive of the european parliament and of the council on the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a member state. it should be noted that the scale of the illicit trade in cultural objects and in particular in national treasures has increased significantly over the past few years. the current directive is not sufficiently effective. it is therefore necessary to ensure the wider use of the drawback mechanism. in order to remove the current obstacles and to facilitate the return of more cultural objects recognised as national treasures, the procedures for the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a member state should be facilitated. it is essential that the rules on compensation are uniform throughout the eu. in addition, cooperation between the competent national administrative authorities should be improved in order to reduce the number of judicial proceedings.
in writing. - i support the proposal for a directive. i believe that the european targets for reducing plastic bags in the new directive, and the introduction of a tax on them, will help to reduce environmental pollution and damage to ecosystems. it is worth noting that the consumption of plastic bags is increasing every year. the proposal to reduce the use of plastic bags by 80% by 2019 is therefore objectively justified and should be implemented. in order to reduce the negative impact on the environment, in particular its pollution by waste, and to promote waste prevention and better resource efficiency, each member state should undertake to dispose of such waste. in particular, the list of measures taken by member states to combat the misuse of plastic carrier bags should be extended. they should use taxes, marketing measures or prohibitions to achieve their objective. however, the choice of implementation policy must carefully analyse the impact on consumers, the retail sector and the environment. on the other hand, specific penalties should be provided for for failure to achieve the objective of the directive.
in writing. - i voted for this report because we need to protect our borders and have an effective border control system. i would like to point out that such external border protection does not have a negative impact on the fundamental rights of migrants arriving on europe's shores. respect for the right to life and the principle of non-refoulement are fundamental rights of the utmost importance and the european union and its member states must ensure that they are fully implemented and fully respected. it is therefore important to ensure additional legal clarity on the respect of fundamental rights in maritime surveillance and the disembarkation of seized or rescued persons at sea, including full respect for the principle of non-refoulement. in this regard, a number of amendments were introduced to enshrine the principle of non-refoulement and to protect human rights, and to underline that all member states participating in such operations are required to comply with obligations under international law and the eu acquis, including relevant case law.
in writing. - i voted for this. it is worth noting that the southern eu member states are currently facing particularly high levels of illegal migration. this problem is not only affecting these countries negatively, but also the eu as a whole. it is therefore necessary to step up efforts to combat and prevent illegal migration to the southern external borders of the member states. it is essential to ensure that the eu has a common legal framework for apartments and that international law is interpreted in the same way in all member states. common practices must be applied across the eu in dealing with detection, detention, search and rescue and disembarkation. full respect for the principle of non-refoulement must be ensured. in addition, member states should be encouraged to cooperate more actively in ensuring full respect for the fundamental rights of asylum seekers and refugees.
in writing. - i support the proposed regulation. it is essential to establish an effective investor-state dispute settlement mechanism. it is also essential that union agreements ensure a high level of protection for foreign and eu investors and ensure the implementation of the division of responsibilities. in order to achieve the objectives pursued, criteria should be established for the allocation of financial responsibility between the union itself and the member state responsible for the regime in place. it is also necessary to ensure that the costs of litigation or other costs do not have adverse effects on the budget of the union or other non-financial resources. the financial liability should therefore be limited in view of the impact on the eu budget, as the essential basis of liability is not limited. i support the proposal to give the union, in exceptional circumstances, a right of reply in disputes relating to the regime applied by a member state. it should be noted that a procedure for the conclusion of peace agreements should be established in order to avoid a costly and unnecessary arbitration process.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this proposal for a regulation, which aims to modernise the anti-dumping and anti-subsidy regulations. the new amendments will have four main effects: the abolition of the lesser duty rule in anti-dumping cases where there is a structural distortion of the raw material market and in all anti-subsidy cases, the obligation for eu producers to cooperate in ex-officio investigations, the reimbursement of duties collected during the expiry review and the warning two weeks before the application of provisional duties. the commission proposal for a regulation is based on three objectives: to increase the effectiveness of the measure, to reduce the risk of retaliation by a third party and to increase transparency and predictability. the european parliament supports these three objectives and believes that they need to be combined to ensure that they are effective and useful for our citizens.
in writing. - i support the proposal to modernise the anti-dumping and anti-subsidy regulations. the eu must have a clear, flexible system of protection against dumped imports and subsidised imports from non-eu countries and ensure the most effective and efficient measures possible, while balancing the interests of the various stakeholders. in order to enhance public confidence, it is important to ensure that the eu has an effective remedy to protect against the threat of third-party retaliation and to increase transparency and predictability. in addition, in order to better address the challenges of the ever-changing trading environment, the sme helpdesk should be given greater attention by providing it with increased support and by strengthening its role.
in writing. - european political parties and european political foundations play an important role in bridging the gap between member states and eu policies. in order to become effective and accountable democratic actors, european political parties and european political foundations need to increase their visibility, recognition, effectiveness, transparency and accountability. it is therefore essential that the eu establishes a specific, harmonised european legal status for european political parties and political foundations. europe's representative democracy will be effective in the year when the voice of citizens is clearly heard. in this context, i believe that more efforts should be made to promote and support european political parties and their associated political foundations. in particular, the conditions for their development should be created by establishing a flexible but rigorous financing system. in addition, a dialogue with european citizens should be developed on a continuous basis in order to link european civil society with the european institutions.
in writing. - it is welcome that a compromise has been reached on the financing of political parties in the union and that the existing regulatory gap has been closed. the new regulation will further enhance the capacity of european political parties to play their role in shaping european political awareness more effectively. it should be noted that, in order to regulate the financing arrangements for political parties effectively, a specific financing mechanism should be established to meet the needs and specificities of political parties, in particular by giving them greater flexibility in financial planning.
in writing. - i voted for this report. in particular, the revision of the regulation is necessary to incorporate the amendments related to the outcome of the negotiations on the multiannual financial framework 2014-2020. i believe that the revision of the financial regulation should include specific provisions on the creation of the performance reserve and the sme initiative. it is also essential that simplifications are introduced in the new financial system. in particular, the joint undertakings should be able to benefit more easily from simplified financial rules. given their nature and existing status, they should continue to be subject to a separate discharge.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because of the importance of reducing emissions from the marine sector, and also because of the importance of monitoring, reporting and verification. the maritime transport sector plays an important role in the european economy. it is responsible for a large proportion of international freight transport between eu member states and other regions of the world, as well as for intra-eu freight transport. however, the maritime transport sector is dependent on the burning of fossil fuels and is one of the least regulated sources of anthropogenic emissions, which contributes to air pollution and climate change. this regulation therefore aims to develop in the near future a policy for reducing emissions from the marine sector in line with the overall climate, air pollution and human health policy. while it is important to ensure that emissions from the marine sector are included in this regulation as consistently as possible, it is also important to focus on the essentials and to reduce the implementation costs and administrative burden.
in writing. - i support the proposed regulation. it is important to note that carbon emissions from maritime transport are increasing and will increase significantly in the future if no action is taken. in addition, the current eu policy is inadequate and the policy ambition needs to be increased, as shipping emissions have health effects. first, we need to ensure that a common policy is in place in europe to reduce emissions from the marine sector. this would be a major step towards achieving the targets for energy efficiency and emissions reduction. i believe that the proposed measures are necessary to establish specific emission commitments in the international shipping sector and to develop appropriate market measures as soon as possible. in order to achieve the objectives set, the scope of the monitoring, reporting and verification system should be extended. in addition, the use of emission measurement techniques should be encouraged.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because invasive alien species threaten biodiversity and ecosystem functions or have negative effects on the environment, human health and socioeconomic development. invasive alien species include animal, plant, fungal and micro-organism species and affect the eu's landmass, water bodies, seas and islands. biological invasions are expected to increase in europe. current trends in the introduction of new species suggest that the problem is far from being under control and its impact on biodiversity is expected to increase, as pressures such as habitat loss, degradation, fragmentation, over-exploitation and climate change increase the number of species concerned and the vulnerability of ecosystems to invasions. the european commission has therefore published a proposal on the prevention and management of the introduction and spread of invasive alien species. the main objective of the proposal is to ensure that coordinated action is taken at eu level in cooperation with the member states. a list of invasive alien species should be established. member states should take measures to ensure that these species are not introduced, sold and bought, stored, bred or released in the eu.
in writing. - i welcome the proposal for a regulation on the prevention and control of the introduction and spread of invasive alien species. this is a major step forward in ensuring the future protection of europe's biodiversity. the principles of prevention, early detection, rapid eradication and long-term management and control provided for in the regulation will ensure the protection of biodiversity and reduce the potential for significant economic, environmental and ecological damage. in addition, a coordinated approach to the management and control of invasive alien species will address the problems of social and economic impact. i believe that it would be appropriate to extend the list of invasive alien species and to provide for additional measures to ensure that border control services are able to carry out effective border controls.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it is important to take the necessary measures and to lay down the technical rules to ensure the effective implementation of the kyoto protocol in the european union after 2012[n 1]. the european parliament considers that the scope of the proposed regulation on the monitoring and reporting of greenhouse gas emissions and other climate change-related information at national and eu level is fully in line with the need to develop specific and coherent rules to ensure the technical implementation of the kyoto protocol in the eu after 2012[n 1]. this requires the provision of the conditions for the effective joint implementation of the commitments of the eu, its member states and iceland. finally, it should be stressed that the formal entry into force of the doha amendment is one of the priority objectives of the european union, as the kyoto protocol makes a crucial contribution to the global effort to combat climate change.
in writing. - i welcome the proposal for a regulation which is necessary to ensure the implementation of the kyoto protocol beyond 2012. it is worth noting that the kyoto protocol makes a crucial contribution to the global effort to combat climate change. it is therefore essential to facilitate the effective joint fulfilment of the commitments of the union, its member states and iceland and to ensure transparency of the results of the parties to the agreement. it is noted that the second commitment period of the kyoto protocol will extend and strengthen the existing global emissions accounting system and their commitment. in this context, i support the proposed amendment to the regulation which will allow for the proper implementation of the commitments of the second kyoto protocol period, as it would allow for the issuance, transfer and transfer of kyoto units of account.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because fraud against the financial interests of the european union is a serious problem, which affects the eu budget and therefore the taxpayers, who urgently call on the european union institutions to ensure that public funds are allocated to structural growth, fiscal consolidation and job creation. there is a well-structured community anti-fraud legislation, but so far it has been implemented in the member states only through the adoption of insufficiently harmonised and harmonised rules and provisions, including sanctions. such a fragmented legal system encourages potential criminals to travel across europe in search of the most favourable legal system. the european parliament considers that the european union and the member states must respond strongly to fraud and other illegal activities affecting the eu's financial interests by applying deterrent measures and must provide effective and equivalent protection in all member states. the european parliament therefore responds with this report to the concerns of citizens and taxpayers and seeks to create a stronger basis for ensuring the protection of the european union's financial interests throughout the european union.
in writing. - i support the proposal for a directive. in particular, the fight against fraud and other illegal activities detrimental to the union budget requires the harmonisation of national legal systems, including criminal law instruments, and the application of a common eu policy. this is a significant step towards the creation of a european area of criminal justice. we must ensure effective, proportionate and dissuasive protection of the financial interests of the union. the framework for the functioning of the future european public prosecutor's office should be established on the basis of this directive. in particular, fraud should be distinguished from corruption and money laundering. the inclusion of vat fraud in the scope of the directive is to be welcomed. in order to protect the financial interests of the union, cooperation should not be limited to cooperation between member states and the commission, but should also include cooperation between member states. it is also appropriate to allow member states to set themselves sanctions other than criminal sanctions for minor fraud.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the right of inquiry is an important part of the european parliament's powers of scrutiny. the right of the european parliament to set up a temporary committee of inquiry to investigate alleged breaches or maladministration in community legislation was originally laid down in the maastricht treaty. this proposed regulation proposes new amendments and improvements by setting up committees of inquiry and giving them powers. it is therefore proposed that committees of inquiry should have the necessary means to carry out their tasks of investigating alleged breaches of eu law and cases of maladministration in the application of eu law. these legal provisions appear to be sufficient and appropriate to ensure that the political control exercised by the european parliament, the only directly elected eu institution, is genuinely serious, effective and in line with the expectations of european citizens for democratic accountability and good governance. however, it should be noted that the european parliament is not a court and does not have the power to impose penalties on individual citizens. however, when all the member states adopted the treaty of lisbon, they also agreed to give the european parliament the right to investigate breaches of law or maladministration in the implementation of eu law. the member states are therefore legally obliged to oblige their administrative system to provide support to all possible ep committees of inquiry.
in writing. - i believe that the role of the european parliament's committees of inquiry should be strengthened and that they should be given specific, genuine and clearly defined powers, in accordance with the principle of proportionality. these are exclusive instruments of political control. in order to be able to carry out their tasks of investigating suspected breaches of union law and cases of maladministration in the application of union law, they need to be provided with certain necessary means. however, the powers conferred on committees of inquiry should be without prejudice to the responsibilities of other institutions and the doctrine of separation of powers should be strictly observed in order to avoid conflicts between investigations of a political nature and investigations of a judicial nature. on the other hand, it is necessary to ensure that research is carried out with full respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms. furthermore, the findings of the investigation should be based only on elements of probative value. i support the proposal to provide for the possibility of effective, proportionate and dissuasive penalties in certain exceptional cases.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, which seeks to highlight one crucial aspect of the process of greater eu integration. it is indeed necessary to renew the citizens' link with europe. the european institutions should also include the parliaments of the eu member states. they are in fact part of the eu's parliamentary system. it is easy to say that they are not only the institutions of their own country but also those of the eu. in essence, their task is to make the institution that can be considered the eu's second chamber, the council, fully democratic and to bring citizens closer to europe. as regards the 'consultative function', which might be better described as 'political dialogue', the european parliament's relations with national parliaments are particularly intense; perhaps their regulation should be more precise and based not on the logic of amending or supplementing decisions but on the desire to provide additional benefits for the ep's political and legislative activities. it is also important to recognise the importance of exchanging information and cooperating between the european parliament and national parliaments. it is clear that mutual information helps national parliaments to make more informed assessments to the european parliament and the european parliament to take decisions that are supported by the general public, and thus enables national parliaments to transpose eu legislation more quickly and effectively.
in writing. - the eu budget is an instrument for the achievement of the european union's objectives. the eu's policy objectives and the means to achieve them must therefore be coherent and coordinated. common eu requirements should apply to own resources, control and supervision and accountability of national authorities. in addition, the new challenges and wide-ranging problems facing the eu require an increase in the european budget and the search for new financial instruments. it should be noted that the establishment of new own resources should pay particular attention to vat. this is a large-scale area of tax evasion. provisions should therefore be laid down to reduce or even eliminate fraud.
in writing. - in particular, the creation of favourable financial conditions for the implementation of commitments under the lisbon treaty, the europe 2020 strategy or the need to take action to stimulate growth. it should be noted that the own resources system is important from a structural and political point of view. this is an integral part of the eu's financial autonomy and a crucial factor in deciding the future of eu integration. in this context, strict democratic control of the own resources system must be applied. the financing system shall be transparent, flexible and clear.
in writing. - the value of the european solidarity fund is enormous: it not only stands for solidarity and help in the event of major natural disasters, it also effectively helps the eu and its member states to deal with the effects of serious disasters. since the creation of the fund in 2002, it has been successfully used for 56 disasters including floods, fires, storms, earthquakes and droughts, and has provided 23 european countries with support. this report highlights the importance of this fund and provides a set of measures to readjust this instrument to current circumstances in the eu, taking into account the economic situation of the eu and its member states. as one measure to improve its functioning, this report clarifies access criteria with regard to regional disasters, sets a general criterion for assessing the severity of damage caused, and makes the administrative procedure required for its mobilisation more effective. i welcome this set of measures that makes the operability of this fund more flexible and credible. in a time of crisis, all these adjustments to the rules will be beneficial for ensuring the eu’s effectiveness, coherence and assistance, which are needed in time of natural disasters.
i'll try to talk about the board's conclusions. the council of 26 and 27 june 2014 adopted decisions which undoubtedly have a major impact on the further development of the european union, on the standard of living of its citizens, and will therefore have an impact on the future of our lives and the lives of citizens of all the member states of the european union. i would like to highlight two issues which are of importance to all the countries of the european union, but particularly to lithuania. first, the council approved the commission's proposal to introduce the euro in lithuania as from 1 january 2015. this decision is of great importance both for lithuania and for all euro area countries. our neighbours, the baltic states of estonia and latvia, already have the euro. this decision will not only strengthen the lithuanian economy, but will increase the competitiveness of our state and will give lithuania greater financial and even political security. however, more than half of lithuanian citizens are still against the changeover to the euro, which poses a serious challenge to the lithuanian government, which must convince citizens of the benefits of this move. and only if or when the majority of lithuanian citizens understand and approve, will such a step be successful. the second council decision relating to the country i represent is the signing of the european union's association agreements with georgia and moldova, as well as the remaining provisions of the economic agreement with ukraine. the association agreement between the european union and ukraine was to be signed in vilnius, but for obvious reasons it was not. it is a good thing that the economic part of this agreement was signed by the same partner who was prepared for signing in vilnius. i think it's symbolic, you know? answer to the question posed by the blue card. one of the basic principles of direct democracy is that all major decisions must be agreed by all states with the majority of their citizens. that is one of the main provisions in this group. that is why i am in this group and why i believe that these positions are correct.
in writing. i congratulate italy on taking over the presidency of the council of the european union from greece. indeed, your presidency begins at an exceptional time with the newly elected european parliament and the commission of the new term still in the making. however, we hope that together we can do a lot for the people of europe and that it is the italian presidency that will give new impetus to several important pieces of legislation that have been delayed for some time in the eu council: the maternity leave directive and the so-called anti-discrimination directive. i am particularly pleased to see that italy has paid particular attention during its presidency to the social policy aspects of the european union - reducing poverty, unemployment, especially among young people, and promoting employability, with particular attention to science, education and exchange programmes. i particularly welcome the focus on combating illegal work. i am pleased to see that the italian presidency intends to continue the work recently started on the establishment of a european platform to combat illegal work. i fully agree that tackling illegal work is one of the key elements of the strategy for jobs and growth and that the role of inspections must be strengthened and their activities given greater attention, including at european union level. i would like to congratulate italy on its ambitious goals and wish it success and strength in achieving them.
in writing. first of all, i would like to emphasise that one of the biggest problems in many eu countries is undeclared work. illegal work has a negative impact on the economy and security of social protection systems in europe and in individual countries, and undermines social benefits and the whole system of financing public services. people forced to work illegally are exposed to insecurity, vulnerability and poverty in their workplaces, both in their working lives and in their old age. the fight against undeclared work was identified by the italian presidency as the most important tool for promoting employment and economic growth in europe. do you think that tackling undeclared work should be one of the eu's strategic priorities for the next term? what specific measures does the commission intend to take, and when, to step up the fight against illegal work in the eu? secondly, do you not think that the european union should take responsibility in solidarity and set up a special solidarity mobility fund to compensate for the money invested in the education and training of emigrant workers in those countries, including lithuania, from which the majority of emigrants have left? this is a very relevant problem because the movement of workers is one-way, i.e. one member state loses the majority of its workers while many others arrive. finally, i would like to ask you whether you intend to appoint a commissioner responsible for women's rights and gender equality and how do you intend to address the gender balance in the new commission?
as regards the situation in ukraine, it is very important to identify correctly what is happening there. i think that there is an undeclared hybrid war going on in ukraine, where one state, i'm talking about russia, has illegally seized part of another state, i'm talking about ukraine and crimea. he was also involved in the 'governmental' activities of the so-called 'government of the donetsk people's republic'. so, although unannounced, although using unusual military methods, the war in ukraine is going on. people are dying, including civilians. it's important to understand that this is not just a war against ukraine. this military conflict is a reflection of the global conflict that russia is currently in with western civilization and europe. if we do not respond properly, the only question is which european state will be next? will it be my homeland, lithuania, or the other baltic states, or moldova, or any other country in the world or in europe? and it will happen only because one of these states or their decisions will not please those who started and today support the war in ukraine. this is why it is so important today for all european states to have a common position and coordinate their actions in order to stop the military action in ukraine and to seek a peaceful solution. our common and firm position is important not only for the integrity of ukraine and its territory, but it is necessary and important first of all for our states, for the citizens of our states. so let us be united and determined to act together today, for tomorrow may be too late. answer to the question posed by the blue card. the signing of the treaty, which was signed by ukraine, was the choice of the legitimately elected authorities of ukraine. i think every country has the right to choose the way i need it. i certainly do not think that these steps, rather than the aggressive stance of another state, namely russia, pose a potentially grave threat to the stability of europe and the security of all our countries.
ukraine's choice, born and raised in maidan earlier this year, to live in freedom, democracy, and to embark on political and economic reforms, did not fit into russia's projected future. russia has taken all kinds of political, diplomatic, economic, military and other actions to undermine the statehood of ukraine and the free choice of the people. he was responsible for the repression of the 'people's republic of donetsk' and the 'crimean people's republic'. we are witnessing a new kind of war: an undeclared war but with a growing number of casualties, no front but with the encroachment of foreign territory, and a relentless information war waged in the name of diplomatic relations. i call on russia to be part of the solution, not the initiator of the conflict. the war against ukraine has dangerously weakened the foundations of european security, re-established mistrust and red herring, and created the risk of regional terrorism and new frozen conflicts.
in writing. the ongoing conflict in ukraine is a matter of great concern and concern. the events surrounding the country have already adversely affected and will continue to affect the prospects for its further development and prosperity. the situation that has arisen confirms that the eu has not paid due attention to the eastern partnership policy in the past and has not properly assessed the political issues of the countries participating in this partnership. russia's actions in the context of events in ukraine clearly demonstrate that it is not a reliable partner for the eu, and its words are far from its actions. the eu's failure to find a unified and clear position on both ukraine and russia is dividing europe itself and weakening it politically. it is because of the eu's delayed inaction that we have today an unstable, fractious, fragmented and destabilised ukraine, which poses a serious threat not only to the security of its closest neighbours, the baltic states, but also to the security of the european union as a whole. the eu must strengthen its external policy, especially in the eastern partnership, make every effort to agree and implement consistent eu action to stabilise the situation at home and take a clear stance on eu relations with russia. the new commission's newly appointed rapporteur, jean-claude juncker, rightly stressed that the eu needs a stronger ukraine, but to achieve this the eu must first of all be united on foreign policy.
mr president, the crimean occupation and continued aggression in eastern ukraine have more than ever displayed the need for even deeper and more integrated cooperation, not only between european countries but between the eu and the us as well. i am sure all of us would agree that the free trade agreement not only boosts economic growth and the creation of new jobs, but at this time can be regarded as a means for ensuring geopolitical stability and peace in europe. that is why we have to ensure relevant political attention and that the negotiations with us will not stall and they will move forward. even more, we can observe how, as if by chance, at every step of the negotiations between the eu and the us some damaging information appears. one could start suspecting that someone is interested in stopping the process as early as possible. that is one more reason why we have to keep pushing forward.
i understand that the 28 countries of the european union are different: they have their own traditions, their own policies and systems for family support, but we are also united by certain common values and rescues. this is, first and foremost, the free movement of persons and workers within the free common market. so in this case, it is absolutely irrelevant where the mother gives birth, works, raises children: whether it is italy, sweden, germany or my native lithuania, but that mother and that father must have minimum social standards, minimum guarantees and maternity leave. that is what we are trying to do with this directive. and so i have great faith and i look to the italian presidency to ensure that we do not give up, and it is a bad sign to start the new european parliament with such a step backwards and to show its impotence both in the commission and in the council and its lack of confidence in the european parliament. i believe that sitting down together at the table can help us find solutions. i wish you would.
for the efdd group in 2004, lithuania joined a different european union. in the last ten years it has changed beyond recognition: unfortunately, i must say that we have not built a european community that citizens can trust, that more than half a billion europeans can become part of. most lithuanians are less optimistic about their future than they were 10 years ago: i am talking about social exclusion, unemployment and people's disappointment. today we are discussing the introduction of the euro in lithuania. i believe that the lithuanian people, which many centuries ago created its state, based its legal foundations on statutes and constitutions, which for centuries has steadfastly defended its freedom and independence, preserved its spirit, native language, script and customs, is not just a simple formality. it's a partial renunciation of statehood. more than half of lithuanians polled in the spring of 2013 had a negative opinion of the introduction of the euro in lithuania. ladies and gentlemen, most lithuanians do not want the euro. and we simply have no right to ignore it. i heard jean-claude juncker speak today. i quote: 'i want the eu to be closer to the people'. mr president, will the introduction of the euro against the will of the majority of people bring the eu closer to the people? madam president, i should like to thank my colleagues for all the nice words that we have heard about my country. today we heard the new president of the commission saying that the euro is saving europe and in lithuania we definitely agree with that. just yesterday it was announced that according to the latest surveys lithuanians are back on the list of the biggest euro optimists. we are looking forward to joining the euro area from 1 january next year. joining means two things for us. firstly, that the path of prudent financial planning and austerity measures and structural reforms really works. it is a great acknowledgement of the difficult process that took place in lithuania. austerity was not easy, it never is, but now we are the ones to say that it was worth it. another reason why euro-optimism is rising is that against the background of the aggression against ukraine, people want more security and it is clear today that europe and the euro area are a geopolitical haven that people seek. so once again the upcoming joining by lithuania of the euro area is a great recognition and a strong message of a united europe.
first of all, i would like to congratulate my country, lithuania, which has worked very hard and has successfully met the necessary convergence criteria and has the opportunity to join the euro area family as of 1 january next year. lithuania is a small country, and therefore participation in the euro area will certainly help our country to expand trade and financial links with other euro area countries, create better conditions for foreign investment, which in turn will promote an increase in the income of lithuanians and quality employment and jobs. in addition, the positive impact of the introduction of the euro will be felt by the population as a result of the reduced interest burden, the disappearance of the litas and the costs of converting to the euro, finally, exports will grow faster in my country, and the investments that our country so needs will certainly come to lithuania faster. in conclusion, the referendum in lithuania was held before the country joined the european union. the lithuanian people then agreed to be in this family and to adopt the euro under certain conditions. i would therefore like to wish that the introduction of the euro in lithuania will also promote economic growth, job creation and the social well-being of the people.
lithuania's accession to the economic and monetary union is a right, logical and decisive step towards further integration into the european union. faced with the challenges of the economic crisis, lithuania took timely action to limit budgetary expenditure and accelerate the necessary social and economic reforms. this has enabled the economy to remain competitive, to start creating new jobs and to restore economic growth. lithuania's economy has been growing for five consecutive years, the best performance on reforms, and this is confirmed by international markets. in the 1980s, when lithuania was breaking free from soviet occupation, we heard a lot of encouragement from the kremlin: don't do it, stay where you are. but, my dear colleagues, the lithuanian people are rational, looking to the future. they know what decisions to make, they don't need advisers in this area. ladies and gentlemen, in january the euro will be speaking lithuanian, and euro coins will be in the pockets of europeans. i expect a sound and necessary decision from this parliament.
i support and congratulate lithuania on taking this very important step. the government has worked hard to meet the criteria for adopting the euro. but unfortunately, it is also being introduced at the expense of the poorest of the poor: pensioners, minimum wage workers, and so on. as a result, today more lithuanians are against the euro than in favour of its rapid adoption. in order to meet the criteria for the introduction of the euro, the minimum and average wages were reduced during the crisis, they have not yet been restored to their previous level and the difference has not been compensated. this is unfair. as lithuania's economy is growing and has already reached pre-crisis levels, i urge the lithuanian government to resolve these problems by the end of this year and hopefully public opinion will change. i think it's a win-win for all of us.

madam president, i think that lithuania joining the euro is a huge achievement. it is an achievement that the whole country is proud of. they actually showed this in the current elections where the pro-european parties dominated. the liberals had a very successful election, as did the social democrats and the conservatives. all these three parties are pro-euro. it is also very interesting to note that lithuania was formerly pegged to the us dollar, and we moved over to trusting the euro a long time ago. we were there. we missed it in 2006 by 0.01%. that is how honest lithuania is. we should be proud of it. we did not fix our figures to qualify in 2006. we took the pain, we worked hard and we suffered a lot. the people of lithuania suffered a great deal and we have to live with that. we need the support of europe. we need investment in lithuania. we can grow. we are pro-european and europe should be proud of lithuania, as i am.
in writing. mr president, the situation in the euro area is currently very volatile and will remain so for quite some time. in such a situation, lithuania's entry into the single currency area would be a mistake. the currency provides a fairly good buffer against the negative effects of the economic and financial crisis. in turn, the rapid introduction of the euro will immediately cause our country the problems that the countries currently involved in monetary union are facing, including the requirement to allocate large sums to rescue failing countries. the euro does not unite, but rather deepens divisions even among those countries that have adopted the single currency. the euro is not strengthening but rather weakening the european union as the only region in the world that is so deeply in recession. today lithuania should ask itself whether the euro is a reliable currency and whether it would not be better to keep its own currency, at least until the situation in the euro area is clear and predictable. in the current situation, a delay in the introduction of the euro seems to be the best solution. and, most importantly, it would be safe for lithuanians, protecting them from the drastic rise in the prices of goods and services that has occurred in almost all countries adopting the euro after possible euro accession. we cannot allow the burden of introducing the euro to fall on the ordinary lithuanian people, who have already been struggling with the crisis for several years. lithuania must preserve its currency, as only an independent monetary policy will allow it to respond flexibly to the economic situation in europe and the world.
i thank commissioner andor for his information on the current situation in the member states with regard to youth employment. you're really working on this. despite the many voices and initiatives raised by the european parliament, there is still very little positive progress in the field of youth employment. are the member states really doing enough to implement the youth guarantee? as the commissioner mentioned, even seven countries have not yet drawn up plans for implementing the youth guarantee. youth unemployment can no longer be seen as a problem of lack of education or unclaimed skills. training, appropriate and early career guidance, and high quality of labour market-relevant science are certainly part of the solution, but job creation - the other side of the problem - must be placed high on the european union's agenda. another important problem is discrimination against young people in the workplace. even when they are employed, young people are discriminated against and exploited, which is why the statistics on poverty and social exclusion among young people are unjustifiably high in our europe. young people also need job security, social protection and social guarantees, as the charter of fundamental rights of the european union enshrines the right to social protection for all: not only for experienced workers but also for those who are starting out. and, again, have we been too focused in our discussions on the economic aspects of youth unemployment? why don't we talk about the impact of youth unemployment on the demographics of our continent? what do you think about the connection between safe jobs, or no jobs at all, for a young person to start a family and raise children? this is so important for our ageing europe.
in writing. - i voted for this report, which is very important for lithuania. first of all, i would like to welcome and congratulate my country, which has successfully, with a lot of effort, met the necessary convergence criteria and has the opportunity to join the euro family. the single currency of the european union is one of europe's greatest achievements. it is unclear what the economic situation would be in europe today if it were not for the euro, because having different currencies would have put us in the grip of banks and fiscal transactions during the crisis. lithuania's accession to the euro area thus demonstrates once again the success and necessity of the single currency in europe. the euro is one of the most important links linking europe, and i am confident that we will continue its successful history. i would like to wish my country that the introduction of the euro in lithuania will continue to promote economic growth, job creation and quality employment, so that the lithuanian people will eventually feel its benefits in their daily lives.
in writing. - i believe that the decision on lithuania's accession to the eurozone could have fundamental consequences for the future of the state and the nation. therefore, at the most important stages of the development of the state, the government should listen to the voice of the people. we cannot ignore the fact that the majority of lithuanians do not want the euro. however, the 'euro train' is coming to our country at a tremendous speed, despite the fact that more than half of lithuanians have a negative opinion on lithuania's accession to the euro area.
on behalf of the alde group. – madam president, the world we live in is far from ideal in spite of continuous and multiple efforts by many of us. the world’s history in general, and europe’s in particular, is full of dark pages of wars, aggression, destruction and other unfortunate events. did we learn any lessons? did we do everything possible to prevent that happening again? it does not look so, even though we have just commemorated the start of world war one and are about to meet the 75th anniversary of the start of world war two. at the same time we have to salute all international efforts to close legal loopholes that still exist in international humanitarian law and the rome statutes by criminalising the crime of aggression. we have to agree that this can be done only by undertaking all possible measures, by accepting necessary legal frameworks for naming the illegal use of force which manifestly violates the united nations charter. in 2010 the states parties, including all eu member states, adopted the kampala amendments to the rome statute which establishes the international criminal court. the above-mentioned amendments mainly provide for adding the crime of aggression to the list of crimes falling under icc jurisdiction, thereby completing the icc jurisdiction and deterring member states from illegal use of force. it is against those who are willing and ready to challenge the peace and security of others. here i should make clear that the kampala amendments do not cover cases of self-defence or a un security council mandate. by now eight eu member states have ratified and completed procedures for enforcing the kampala amendments; it is good but not enough. 2014 is a crucial year to encourage states to start and continue the process of ratification of the amendments, given the long time that ratification of international instruments requires in many countries. the existing momentum should be fully used to push for ratification now. the resolution we are debating tonight explicitly calls for an eu common position around the issue. this is the key part of the document. the resolution in focus is about protecting human rights: this is the ultimate goal of the eu’s policies.
in writing. - the democratic phenomenon of the maidan, which has re-born the hope of the ukrainian people to live in a free, democratic and european country, requires the full and strong support of the european union. the signing of the association agreement between the european union and ukraine on 27 june opened the way to a new type of cooperation. the implementation of the approved free trade agreement is undoubtedly the right step, which meets the expectations of both sides. the new ukrainian government's path of fighting corruption, strengthening the country's governance, improving economic governance and ensuring civil rights, based on strong political power, has received the support of the democratic world and, unfortunately, hostile opposition from the authorities of the russian federation. the unprecedented and disinformation-based propaganda campaign launched by russia against ukraine and the western states is directed against the principles of peaceful coexistence of neighbouring states. the process of direct russian aggression against ukraine, in violation of international agreements and other osce principles, has reached a critical point. he is responsible for the repression of the so-called 'people's republic of donetsk'. he has been involved in the illegal annexation of crimea by the russian federation since 2014. the european union must remain an active and constructive partner of ukraine in helping it to overcome its current difficulties. europe cannot afford another frozen conflict that undermines stability and poses a security risk. the european union's solidarity with ukraine is a commitment by all of us to continue the project of a europe united, secure and prosperous.
in writing. - we are about to mark the 75th anniversary of the molotov-ribbentrop pact, when the two aggressors divided europe. it took 65 years before we reversed the benefits of this pact in 2004. let's not divide europe again, no matter how sweet the offer from russia is - let's not give up ukraine. we must demand clearly and unequivocally that russia withdraw its troops from the ukrainian border, and stop supplying the terrorists with weapons and military equipment. the downing of a civilian airliner only proves that this conflict is not local. no one can feel safe as long as there are foreign-backed terrorists operating in eastern ukraine. europe has the political economic muscle to impose tougher sanctions if russian aggression continues. we have to talk about the european perspective for ukraine, and i understand that this is a difficult subject for many of you here, but believe me, for those fighting in ukraine, this is a matter of life and death. these developments show that we need to strengthen deeper cooperation not only with our eastern neighbours, but also with our strategic transatlantic partners, the us and canada, who share our values of freedom and democracy.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution because it draws the attention of the european parliament to the problems of young people - the high level of unemployment and the increase in poverty among young people. i am one of the authors of this resolution. data provided by the european commission show that in 2012, up to a third of all young people in europe were at risk of poverty and social exclusion. this is due to the deep-rooted discrimination against young people in workplaces across europe. even when young people are employed, they are discriminated against and even exploited, often facing what is known as 'working poverty'. even when young people are employed, they are discriminated against and even exploited. this is why the statistics on poverty and social exclusion among young people are so unjustifiably high in our rich and secure europe. youth also need job security, social protection and social guarantees. the eu charter of fundamental rights enshrines the right to social protection for all - not just experienced workers but also newcomers.
in writing. - youth unemployment is still a particularly serious and difficult problem to tackle in the eu countries, which is why i voted in favour of the resolution. in addition to a detailed description of the situation of youth unemployment in the eu countries, the document also contains specific recommendations for the eu member states, which i believe would significantly improve the unemployment situation if implemented. i think it is particularly important to note that in 2012 small and medium-sized enterprises accounted for as much as 66.5% of all jobs in europe, and eu countries must therefore ensure that they have the right legal and financial conditions to establish and develop. it is also necessary to create favourable conditions for the creation and development of new innovative businesses.

in writing. one good news we heard from the commission this evening is that support for eu farmers affected by the russian embargo will be renewed, and seasonal fruit and vegetable growers can expect support again this year. while the commission has responded effectively by adopting measures for those sectors, the meat and milk sectors are equally affected by the russian embargo. the situation of farmers in some eu member states is further complicated by the swine fever problem, which the commission has not yet been prepared to address and support, unlike the e. coli crisis in 2011, when emergency aid was provided to the large eu member states affected by it. the russian embargo and the recent difficulties within the eu itself are a test to see how the eu upholds the principle of solidarity with its member states. the russian embargo is a serious problem for all eu countries and i hope that europe will stand up to it in solidarity and unity.
in writing. lithuania and the other baltic states are hit hardest by the russian embargo. and we're not just talking about business losses here. the social consequences, which are also significant, will undoubtedly be felt by the most vulnerable social groups in our countries, through falling incomes and even the loss of jobs. i therefore urge the commission to assess the need for additional support to countries whose labour markets will be affected by the russian embargo. in this context, i would suggest that consideration be given to the possibility of support from the european globalisation adjustment fund, so that the funds from this fund can be used more efficiently and effectively to help workers who may be dismissed in the future from companies affected by russian sanctions. i would also like to urge the commission to declare the baltic countries a special needs area and to set up a special fund for these countries to support baltic farmers and compensate them for their losses. the challenges already faced by baltic farmers due to the still low level of direct payments in the european union and the disproportionate impact of the russian import ban make it clear that the agricultural sector in estonia, latvia and lithuania needs specific eu support. it should also be borne in mind that the russian actions are no less important and require a long-term solution to the crisis. eu assistance is also needed to help farmers to reorient themselves to other agricultural sectors and to find alternative markets for their produce.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, commissioners, the present situation in ukraine remains very, and increasingly, complicated. we are witnessing a war going on and war crimes being committed in europe. this has deconstructed the european security system and exposed ukraine to undeclared aggression from russia. the pretext for russia’s military action against ukraine was the choice – by the free will of the ukrainian people and the country’s new leadership – of a european perspective to create new and more transparent governance, to fight corruption and to develop a free-trade exchange with the european union. is that a crime or a danger for the existing system? who can presume to sit in judgment and decide to impose a harsh situation on neighbouring countries? many of us were taken by surprise by the announcement on 12 september that the implementation of the eu-ukraine association agreement was being postponed for 15 months. it is as if a young couple’s parents had decided after the wedding to postpone the honeymoon for 15 months. notwithstanding the format of trilateral talks, we have to respect the autonomy of eu decision-making, the competence of eu institutions and eu procedures. we should be asking the question: is this not ultimately a recognition of the eu’s inability to impose a necessary level of sanctions against russia, and the continuation of a self-imposed policy of restraint? finally, colleagues, by accepting the ‘three seas’ policy of the high representative and, i am sorry to say, the italian presidency, we simply support a ‘no voice, no action’ policy line. i find that most regrettable, and i hope our ratification today of the eu-ukraine association agreement will be a necessary step in the right direction. let us vote together for europe’s future and for a democratic and european ukraine.

in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution because the international community has a greater role to play and african states also have a greater responsibility to play, because the ebola outbreak is a threat to global security and it is not just a west african problem, it is a global problem. the european parliament therefore calls on the commission to step up its efforts and coordinate with the united nations in the fight against the ebola outbreak. the european parliament, by its resolution, asks the un security council to consider, together with partner countries affected by the ebola outbreak, the possibility of using military and civil defence resources under the direction of the secretary-general and in coordination with the office for the coordination of humanitarian affairs. the european parliament also calls on the council and the commission to support and encourage the african union in the necessary comprehensive plan of action, as the situation continues to deteriorate rapidly and affects the economies and public order of the countries concerned. the ebola crisis is complex and has political, security, economic and social implications that will affect the region well beyond this medical emergency.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. the deadly virus that has swept through west africa, although currently localised, could also soon reach europe. especially when we hear about one or two cases of the virus that's been passed on. the world health organization acknowledges that the outbreak has been underestimated and predicts that the number of cases could exceed 20,000 in the next three months. i agree with the resolution that scientific cooperation and technological assistance in the areas affected by the outbreak should be strengthened. it also includes the widest possible involvement of health professionals in education and prevention work to help detect the early signs of this rogue virus. i think one of the key principles of the international community is to focus on coordinating vaccine medical research and production, and to be transparent and fair about vaccine trials.
