in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution because it is important to ensure respect for human rights, democracy and the rule of law in the region. in addition, the protection of human rights is an essential element of the cotonou agreement, which governs relations between the african, caribbean and pacific (acp) countries and the european union (eu). as regards relations between the region and the eu, it should be stressed that the cotonou agreement remains the basis for the eu's partnership with the acp countries. the european parliament therefore takes the view that the current fijian military government, which has taken its own life, has no legitimate authority to take any decision on the application and ratification of the interim economic partnership agreement and that the decision of 17 july 2014 should be considered null and void accordingly. the president of the european parliament shall inform the commission that the delegated regulation cannot enter into force for these reasons.
in writing. i support the assessment of the eu's response to the ebola threat. i would also like to stress that not all member states are directly affected by this. however, physical distance from a threat does not mean greater security, it may just mean less preparedness in the event of a real-life encounter. for example, my country, lithuania, has very little contact with ebola hotspots in africa. and our doctors learn more about this threat from their colleagues or from specialized publications, if not from the media. however, this does not mean that lithuanian doctors are better prepared to deal with the possible spread of this virus in lithuania. on the contrary, the lack of real medical expertise in the case of a potential virus delivery can contribute significantly to the spread of such a threat. to prevent the spread of ebola, as with other dangerous viruses, it is necessary to promote the exchange of information between all member states at eu level, to encourage the exchange of doctors, to enable all those who wish to gain relevant first-hand experience. and here the eu can play a very important role, both in disseminating information and in supporting the exchange of expertise.

i would like to draw attention to one of the major problems in the european union: the constantly increasing poverty, especially child poverty. as many as 27% of children in the european union live in or near poverty. therefore, both the commission and the member states must take urgent measures to eradicate child poverty. the second problem i want to highlight is the poverty of women, because women are now the largest share of the lowest earners. i therefore call on both the commission and the member states to provide specific recommendations to reduce the gender pay gap and, therefore, the gender pension gap. member states should ensure that people have the necessary social protection, access to health and social services, and should increase the inclusion of vulnerable groups in the labour market, reduce poverty at work and promote gender equality, taking into account the recommendations of the european parliament. ladies and gentlemen, social investment is essential to ensure the future of the member states and the european union as a whole.
, in writing. the european semester for the coordination of economic policies is of particular importance for the proper functioning of the economic union. however, it is also worth noting that it has a major impact on social stability and the lives of every eu citizen. this is particularly true in the european union, which has been hit by an economic crisis that has seriously worsened the living conditions of many european citizens, including those in lithuania. the crisis has also led to increased levels of inequality and poverty, and has limited people's access to the good quality public services that should be the norm in europe in the 21st century. the debate on the eu semester provides an excellent opportunity to encourage member states to step up action in areas where citizens are at risk of social exclusion. the phenomenon of social exclusion is a worrying one and is increasingly occurring among young people. the european union, like its member states, must therefore take a more active approach to tackling unemployment, especially among young people who are taking their first steps into the labour market. in addition, coordinated economic policies must take account of support at all levels to create more jobs. it is therefore worth considering a proposal that encourages the commission to focus more on the reforms needed to support the delivery of high-quality public services, with a particular focus on healthcare systems in the member states. for this strategy to be effective, it must be based on development-oriented investments, better use of human resources to make labour markets more flexible and more responsive to economic change.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it is the report by which parliament gave its consent to the european globalisation adjustment fund for the valencia region of spain. the aid was decided to be granted to the employees of the enterprises active in the production of metal products, whose redundancies during the reference period from 1 april 2013 to 31 december 2013 were 633 (of which 300 employees were covered by the support under the egf co-financed measures) out of 142 enterprises in the region. the application submitted by spain met the criteria for the award of a financial contribution under the egf regulation. the commission and parliament also took into account the fact that the metal products sector is a major supplier of raw materials to various manufacturing industries, in particular the shipbuilding, construction and automotive sectors, which have been severely affected by the economic crisis throughout the union. the egf will provide a harmonised package of services tailored to the needs of redundant workers, including career guidance, recruitment, training, entrepreneurship promotion, business start-up support, job search allowance, travel expenses to training and an employment promotion measure.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it was the report parliament gave its approval to the european globalisation adjustment fund for the city of genk, belgium. the parliament found that the application fulfilled the criteria for the granting of a financial contribution under the egf regulation, although the conditions relating to the minimum number of redundancies during the reference period were only partially fulfilled as less than 500 workers were made redundant. the egf regulation provides that an application for a financial contribution from the egf may be considered admissible where the redundancies have a significant impact on the employment level and the local economy. in the belgian region of frontier, two further waves of redundancies are expected in 2014, with more than 11 000 job losses forecast. parliament therefore decided that the speedy implementation of specific measures to assist the first group of redundant workers would improve their chances of finding a new job and ensure equal opportunities for all redundant workers.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it was the report in which parliament gave its consent to the european globalisation adjustment fund to help the workers in charleroi, belgium, who had been made redundant, to undergo retraining. the workers at carsid sa (carsid) were made redundant as a result of the major structural changes in the world trade system caused by globalisation and the rapidly decreasing market share of the union continuous casting steel industry on a global scale, as well as the continuously decreasing production of cast steel in the union. this is related to the decreased demand for steel in the automotive and construction sectors. the redundancies at carsid are expected to significantly increase unemployment in the charleroi area (a former coal and steel-producing area with a high dependence on traditional heavy industry for employment), which already has a high proportion of long-term job seekers and low-skilled workers. the commission notes that the measure was granted by the region to the region of charleroi, which is the largest private investor in the region.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it was the report in which parliament gave its consent to the european globalisation adjustment fund providing aid to workers made redundant by the french car manufacturer peugeot citroën automobiles. a company producing motor vehicles, trailers and semi-trailers has made 6 120 redundancies. the main redundancies were in the île-de-france (one plant) and brittany (renault plant) regions. during the reference period from 1 october 2013 to 31 january 2014, 2 357 workers are to be supported under measures co-financed by the egf. the parliament found that the redundancies of 6 120 workers would lead to an even greater increase in unemployment in the regions of île-de-france and brittany, where the two plants were located, since one was the eighth largest employer and the redundancies reduced employment in the region by 13,6%. it also noted that global trends in the automotive industry in western europe were of great concern, as it was estimated that while global vehicle production increased by 44% between 2000 and 2012, production in western europe decreased by 25% over the same period. in addition, production in third countries (south korea, china, turkey, indonesia, iran, malaysia, thailand and south america) has increased significantly and now accounts for 47% of the world production of vehicles (compared to 15% in 2000).
the rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on foreign affairs. - first of all, i would like to thank the rapporteur for her efforts to reach a compromise with the council on the budget for the coming year. as rapporteur for the afet committee, i cannot agree with the council that the so-called global european budget should be cut at a time when europe and the world are facing the enormous challenges of the ebola virus, with military threats to international peace and stability. the council's reduced resources must therefore not only be rebuilt but also increased in priority areas, i.e. additional funding must be devoted to strengthening the european neighbourhood policy so that the european union can properly implement its commitments in both the eastern and southern regions. insufficient funding for humanitarian aid is hampering the smooth implementation of this policy and undermining the prestige of the european union and its ability to respond to humanitarian crises. we want to see adequate funding in next year's budget for both the middle east peace process and the palestinian settlement of the crises in iraq, syria and gaza.
rapporteur. - first of all, let me thank all of you who supported ukraine during our meetings in the committee on international trade and here in plenary. i am pleased we have a broad and firm understanding that europe has to stand by ukraine by granting the unilateral extension of the autonomous trade measures (atms). as we speak, ukraine continues to struggle on all fronts at the same time. the ceasefire is not being respected; ukraine is continuing to defend its territorial integrity against attacks from outside; it faces enormous economic and financial difficulties; and it has to undergo deep internal reforms. europe has made a commitment to help ukraine – and with today’s decision we will turn our words into deeds. this should help ukraine to withstand the pressure from russia, which is willing to obstruct the implementation of the association agreement between the eu and ukraine and to interfere in our bilateral relations and sovereign decisions. there was a time in history when europe abandoned one eastern european country: 75 years ago poland was left on its own against aggressors, despite previous statements offering great support. we all know what happened later. what could have been stopped at poland’s borders finally engulfed the whole world. ukraine is today’s front, and strong statements and words of support are simply not enough. the extension of the regulation is part of the eu commitment that was made at the same time as it was decided to postpone implementation of the deep and comprehensive free trade agreement (dcfta) for 15 months – in the hope of peace. the ukrainian side favours the atms and says that they have helped to increase ukraine’s exports to the eu and, indeed, to compensate for the fall in exports to countries in the customs union. the atms provide a much-needed opportunity for ukrainian producers and businesses to divert their exports away from the increasingly restrictive russian market, and also an incentive to bringing their operations and standards more into line with those of the eu. i would note too that member states have already supported the extension of the regulation, so there is every chance of ensuring that it will remain in effect without any break. taking into account the call from some colleagues for close monitoring of the implementation of the atms and for more reassurance on deadlines, i will urge the commission to continue to monitor the effects closely. these atms have been in place since april 2014, with parliament’s approval, and we know exactly what they mean in terms of entitlements. all the relevant calculations have been done. moreover, as parliament ratified the association agreement last month it was expected to come into full force immediately, meaning that the tariff agreement would have been in place since last month. given the current situation, our timely decision will prove that the european parliament can act swiftly and decisively. it will also be proof of our political will and recognition of our moral obligation: we are ready to help the ukrainians and we are not leaving them alone. colleagues, i count on your firm support for this decision.
member of the commission. - madam president, honourable members of the european parliament, at parliament’s plenary session of 16 september, commissioner de gucht explained in detail the content and context of the outcome of the trilateral consultations between ukraine, russia and the eu on 12 september. the commission agreed, as part of the outcome, to propose to the council to delay the provisional application of the trade title of the eu association agreement with ukraine until 31 december 2015, and this in the event that ukraine ratified the association agreement. the proposal by the commission for such a delay was based on a clear request from ukraine to take an initiative that would allow ratification of the association agreements to go ahead while leaving more time to discuss ways of addressing russian concerns arising from the implementation of the dcfta. this outcome must be seen in the bigger political context. it is part and parcel of a comprehensive peace process in ukraine and secures a commitment from russia to maintain its preferential trade regime with ukraine. four days later, on 16 september, ukraine went ahead with the ratification of the eu-ukraine association agreement, which was endorsed simultaneously by the european parliament here in strasbourg giving its consent. many of you, honourable members, expressed satisfaction when the association agreement was ratified. it was truly historic, as it was the first time in the eu’s history that such a simultaneous vote had taken place. as part of the 12 september outcome, the commission also agreed to submit a proposal to extend, until the end of 2015, the autonomous trade preferences currently granted to ukraine, as they will otherwise cease to apply as of 1 november. let me recall the basic elements of these autonomous trade preferences. the unilateral market access granted to ukraine corresponds to the first year of application of the schedule of concessions negotiated under the dcfta. the current proposal simply extends the duration of those autonomous preferences by 15 additional months, thus maintaining the same preferences. suspended duties would present an amount of up to nearly eur 500 million. preferences are granted to ukraine subject to its effective cooperation in customs matters and require ukraine not to change in any way its tariffs towards the eu. in addition, safeguard controls are in place to prevent market-distorting surges impacting adversely on european companies and industry, including the agriculture sector. eu phytosanitary rules remain fully applicable to ukrainian products. in order to address concerns expressed by parliament, the extension of the autonomous trade preferences is subject to ukraine’s respect for the essential elements of the association agreement. most importantly from an economic point of view, the autonomous trade preferences have a very limited impact on the eu markets, and yet at the same time they represent a gesture of tangible economic and political support to ukraine in a difficult situation, helping to address ukraine’s difficult economic, political, security and territorial challenges. i would like to thank parliament for the efforts already made to pave the way for a swift conclusion of the legislative procedure for this file in order to ensure that trade flows are not disrupted. parliament is therefore asked to endorse the commission’s proposal so that these important preferences for ukraine continue to apply after 1 november.
for the verts/ale group although it is late, i think the issue is important enough for europe and ukraine. the commission has also been asked to provide a report on the implementation of the eu's 'sustainable development goals' and to provide a report on the implementation of the 'sustainable development goals' and the 'sustainable development goals'. today, it is only regrettable that, under pressure from russia, ukraine was forced to ask for a delay in the full and complete application of the fta. i therefore think that it is necessary to adopt the resolution which has been tabled. we can't put it off. we must continue to support ukraine's economy, help it to strengthen internally and ensure its stability. we must send a clear signal to the people of ukraine, as well as to business, that we support ukraine's economy and its integration into the european union processes. by supporting the commission's proposal, we will demonstrate that the european union is committed to its partners in the eastern neighbourhood and is able to find the right response to the challenges, while demonstrating that it is committed to its values.
rapporteur. - madam president, i would like to thank all my colleagues who participated in the debate. it clearly sends a very strong message to our ukrainian friends that all the main groups in parliament support the decision. i would like to make a few points. regarding certain deadlines, i am sure that, in cooperation with the commission, we can sort these things out. regarding russia, i have a huge question: why do we want to involve a third country in this type of decision? to me it is clear that the euromaidan people made a decision when they protested against the regime. they made a decision to be part of europe and we have to respond to that, without involving any third parties, whether russia or any other country. i strongly believe that on thursday we will support this decision, and i call on all those members who are still in doubt to support the decision without the amendments.
in writing. – i want to express my strong support for the reduction or elimination of customs duties through autonomous trade measures (atms) on goods originating in ukraine. i believe that it is time to unite and to show our respect for the pro-european choice made by the ukrainian people. i see the extension of atms as one of the ways to stabilise the countryʼs economy and politics. it would not only help ukrainian businesses re-orientate from the russian to the european market but would also help adapt their functioning and standards to eu ones, which will help in preparing for the full implementation of the dcfta from january 2016. ukrainian exports to the eu increased by 25 % in the first half of 2014 and will keep increasing if we open our market while ukraine respects our sanitary and phytosanitary standards.
despite the differences of opinion on this commission, i would like to congratulate the new commission and wish it a good day's work. i think that the commission will be confirmed and the new commissioners will be able to start work as soon as possible, because time and, what is particularly important, problems are not waiting. the commission must work as one well-coordinated instrument, as one team, and pursue the common objective of bringing the european commission's decisions and all the activities of the european union closer to the expectations of citizens in all the countries of the european union, including lithuania. i remember juncker saying: 'we had to fix a burning plane in flight, so sometimes we had to burn our fingers.' i hope that those finger burns are not a hindrance, but an incentive for future success. it is now essential that the new commission focuses on investments in job creation, poverty reduction programmes and support for small and medium-sized enterprises. the european union should make more progress and move towards sustainable economic growth, job creation and poverty reduction. another important objective is a unified approach to problems, especially in the areas of a unified energy and foreign policy. the european union's institutions and countries must, as far as possible, adopt a common position, because only in this way can we achieve the desired result. i am particularly pleased to welcome lithuania's newly appointed commissioner vytenis povilas andriukaitis and wish him well in his work for the european union and lithuania.
in writing. i congratulate mr juncker on presenting the new commission to us in the european parliament after lengthy hearings and intensive negotiations with the commissioners-designate. it is very important that the commission starts work on 1 november this year, because europe cannot wait. millions of people who are still suffering from unemployment today cannot wait. youth, whose unemployment rates are still at record highs in some member states today, cannot wait. and finally, people trapped in poverty by the financial and economic crisis can't wait either. so let's get to work. we social democrats have confidence in your promise to maintain the social direction of the european economic recovery. we believe that the economic recovery will be sustainable and that the new jobs created will be secure. to fight poverty, especially among children and the elderly. that we do not allow the impoverishment of the working class to take hold when even the working people in our countries cannot survive. to combat energy poverty by increasing energy independence from unpredictable large neighbors. this is particularly relevant for the baltic states. i am particularly pleased with your promise to treat all member states equally and fairly. i hope that this promise will also find an echo in agricultural policy, where the baltic countries are still discriminated against today because they receive the lowest direct payments in the european union. the commission's new term will certainly be challenging, so i wish you all the best and look forward to the start of our cooperation.

in writing. unemployment remains high in the european union, especially among young people. i would therefore urge the member states to implement the youth employment initiative as soon as possible, which should now be one of the top priorities in all countries, in order to help young people get a job, especially their first job. in addition, the youth employment initiative should be considered as an incentive for all member states to use the european social fund (esf) to finance broader projects related to youth, and in particular poverty and social inclusion. the economic and financial crisis, as a result of austerity measures in the member states, has led to a significant increase in poverty in europe, and continues to do so, in particular child poverty. we also need to focus on women's poverty, as women currently make up the largest proportion of those on the lowest incomes. the commission and the member states should therefore ensure in their policy programmes that all people have the necessary and adequate social protection, access to health and social services, increased labour market inclusion of vulnerable groups, reduction of poverty at work, promotion of gender equality, taking into account the recommendations made by the ep. it is also necessary to promote health and safety at work, strengthen the rights of workers with non-standard employment contracts and provide for greater social protection for the self-employed.

in writing. today we voted not only on the european semester for the coordination of economic policies, but also on the composition and political programme of the european commission under jean-claude juncker. one aspect commitment to increase eu investment by €300 billion, thus contributing to a faster recovery of the european economy very good news from the new commission. on the other hand, member states' economies are growing at a very disproportionate rate, and unemployment remains high, especially among young people. the european semester represents the commitment of member states to economic solidarity, and therefore all member states have a responsibility proportionate to the respective contributions of the member states to the overall economic performance of the eu. i believe that greater coordination of economic policies requires the creation of favourable conditions for economic growth in the member states, the exchange of good practices in reducing youth unemployment, which some member states have done better than others, and increased investment in innovation and research, especially in the long term.

on behalf of the efdd group. – mr president, the berlin wall was demolished 25 years ago. it seemed that this was the end of the cold war and the victory of democracy, the free and united world. it seemed that not only the wall but also cold war ideology, flushed with the blood of innocent people, collapsed. yet 25 years after the fall of the berlin wall i ask myself and all of us: can we really say that the cold war as a political concept belongs to the dump of history? what does the russian president, vladimir putin, mean by saying that the break-up of the ussr was the greatest geopolitical tragedy of the 20th century? what does the military occupation of sovereign territories of georgia and ukraine mean? are they not attempts to restore the great russia and the soviet union? does that not look like building a new wall between the west and russia? let us celebrate the historical events in berlin that we witnessed 25 years ago, but do not let anyone build the new walls of a cold war today. that is our duty and responsibility as politicians.
in writing. tax avoidance schemes set up in some eu countries cast a shadow over the community and the european institutions as a whole. the president of the european commission must therefore ensure that the necessary draft legislation is submitted to the european parliament's committees as soon as possible to prevent further tax manipulation. even if these proposals are not perfect, we cannot delay the process and start to consider substantially new proposals this year. today, in the face of the ongoing economic crisis, it is essential to restore citizens' confidence in europe. it is therefore necessary to take all possible steps to dismantle tax haven systems in individual member states. it is necessary to examine the tax systems in each country, to establish a public register to identify the uncertain beneficiaries and to prevent money laundering, and finally to publicly identify certain countries and pressure them not to block decisions in the council of europe that could contribute to limiting tax avoidance.
in writing. i would like to draw attention to the court's balanced 2013 annual report, which clearly sets out the problems faced by member states in allocating eu funds and provides recommendations on how to ensure better use of these funds. i commend the court for its principled stance to improve the accounting of eu funds and to reduce the rate of payment errors, which remains very high at 4.7%. the main problem remains that member states provide inaccurate and incomplete information on the use of eu funds. in the new funding period, member states must be obliged to provide better quality information both on the control of the use of eu funds and on the results achieved. as for the european commission, it is unacceptable that it has ignored the recommendations of the court of auditors for years. even about 20% of all recommendations are not fully implemented. the commission should monitor eu funds more closely and ensure better application of eu financial rules.
in writing. i thank the president of the european court of auditors for presenting the 2013 annual report. in this report, the court of auditors assessed that the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) had been very effective in enabling redundant workers to re-enter the labour market as quickly as possible during this period. the court also concluded that, where the fund was used to co-finance services that were not normally available in the member states, such as training for redundant workers, a great deal of help was given to our people. in the light of this report, the commission notes that the support provided by the egf should be seen as financing a set of interrelated instruments which, taken as a whole, contribute to the successful implementation of the operations financed. indeed, the egf supports workers by providing a range of support, including benefits, to ensure that workers have sufficient income during the period of application of the incentives, while allowing them to benefit from the various instruments financed, such as training programmes. lithuania has successfully used the european globalisation adjustment fund on several occasions, for example, alytaus tekstilė, ab snaigė, and financial assistance has also been provided to redundant workers in the construction, furniture and clothing sectors. together with state funding, the fund helps them to change their qualifications, set up a business or find a new job.

rapporteur. - mr president, mr corman, speaker of the parliament of moldova, ladies and gentlemen, it is indeed my great honour and pleasure to hereby bring to the attention of the european parliament, and ask for its consent to ratify, the association agreement between the european union and the republic of moldova. today we are embarking on a new european future for moldova and its people, the future that has actually always been destined for moldova. there is no doubt that moldova is a european country and a part of european culture. it makes me extremely happy to be in a position to say that moldova fully deserves this historic shift. today’s ratification is the culmination of the work started by moldova and the european union nearly ten years ago with the initiation of the eu-moldova action plan. however at that time the implementation of this plan did not look very bright and promising in moldova’s case. five years ago the eastern partnership was launched by the eu and its partners, and even at that time moldova’s record was considered to be rather lagging behind. i am particularly proud to be able to say that now moldova stands as a front runner among our european partners and as an example of very well drafted and implemented political and socio-economic reforms. this is even more impressive when we know that this has been accomplished in only the last few years, thanks to the political determination of the moldovan government. certainly any government needs the trust and support of its own people. it is therefore quite symbolic that our ratification today comes just a few weeks ahead of the general elections in moldova. we will be looking very closely at these elections, and we call on the moldovan authorities to ensure that the upcoming electoral process takes place according to the highest european standards. we all hope that the choice of the moldovan people will be one that leads to the further building of a free society and a market-based economy. by implementing the european-moldovan association agreement, this part of europe will be more stable and prosperous. in principle this has to be the ultimate goal and in the interest of everyone in the region. it is to my deep regret, however, that this is not a strategy shared by everyone. just as we state in the accompanying resolution – which has been adopted by the committee on foreign affairs and for which i also ask the consent of the european parliament today – we are concerned about russia’s actions aimed at undermining the association process of the eu’s eastern partnership. we once again reiterate our conviction that the association process is not a threat to russia’s political and economic interest, and it is to be truly regretted that the russian leadership wishes to regard it as such. as we all know, the association agreement is an extremely complex document that brings responsibilities for both sides. in other words, it is equally the obligation of the european union to assist moldova in fighting corruption, fostering an independent judiciary, strengthening media freedom, protecting all minorities, and doing everything needed so that the moldovan people are able to see the benefits of their european choice, such as new jobs created or the increased trade preferences that are already being afforded to moldova as countermeasures to the use of the trade embargo by russia. trade volumes between the european union and moldova have risen by 33% in the last five or six years and that is already a real example of the benefit to people. the eu-moldovan association agenda, if duly implemented, will open up new possibilities and is very encouraging to all of us. in conclusion, allow me to sincerely thank my colleagues in this house for their kind – and sometimes rather intense – cooperation and for being the real partners in the preparation of this report. i look forward to the discussion and today’s positive vote. (applause)
today is a very important day for moldova and for the european union as a whole. by ratifying this treaty, we are sending a very clear signal to all the eastern partnership countries that the european union is firmly committed to and will continue to honour its commitments towards these countries and to continue signing association agreements despite the current rather difficult situation with russia. agreement with moldova first after the signing of the agreement with ukraine. this shows that the eastern partnership countries, despite the difficult situation and the economic and political pressure from russia, are choosing for themselves a european path that is currently much more difficult but more economically and politically stable in the long term. i am confident that this treaty will be mutually beneficial and will pave the way for fundamental changes in moldova, strengthening democracy and civil society, ensuring more sustainable economic and social development and a better life for the moldovan people. i once again welcome the rapporteur and moldova's choice to pursue the path of peace and european integration.
rapporteur. - mr president, today’s debate is just more proof that we are about to take the right decision. the european parliament’s vote is about the free choice of moldova towards its future. it is a confirmation of a commitment for further political and economic reforms. it is a sign of european solidarity with the people of moldova. by our vote, we will embark with moldova on the european path of cooperation, bilateral debate and mutual support. the association-based partnership between the european union and moldova is not against any third party. we should not be a hostage to the past. along with many central and eastern european countries, moldova was a victim of the molotov-ribbentrop pact. today we are witnessing the political funeral of the molotov-ribbentrop pact. if you like, we are destroying one of the last sections of the berlin wall and authoritarian history. that is why we are opening new political horizons for moldova. today we are witnessing once again how the well-designed eastern partnership policy delivers excellent results for the european continent. it means stability and security for moldova, new possibilities and a better future for the moldovan people. that is what our ratification of the association agreement between the european union and moldova is about. i would like to thank all colleagues for their cooperation and an excellent debate. i hope for an excellent vote. (applause)
in writing. this agreement will allow moldova to implement deep reforms and will promote all-round development. moldova's progress in implementing european reforms is undeniable, for example, moldova is the first eastern partner to achieve visa-free travel with the eu. in the current geopolitical context, especially with regard to the situation in ukraine, a clear signal from the european union is needed that moldova's pro-european path is supported and supported by the eu as a whole. the bilateral opening of markets to the movement of goods and services will increase moldovan exports to the eu by 16% and imports from the eu by 8%, boosting annual economic growth gdp growth is expected to be around 5,4%. the moldovan agricultural sector alone will be able to save eur 46 million in customs duties. it will open up new opportunities for people, civil society, business and the modernisation of the economy in general. in addition to the economic benefits of meeting our mutual commitments, we will be able to work together towards common goals in the areas of energy, regional stability and prosperity. i would like to see moldova continue to implement the necessary reforms, and at the same time urge that greater attention be paid to public awareness and that the eu institutions and the eu member states do their utmost to provide moldova with all the necessary technical and financial assistance. at the same time, the eu must be prepared to respond appropriately to the likely russian economic and political pressure on moldova for its free and independent choice to become an integral part of the european cultural, economic and political fabric.
in writing. i agree with the report and the conclusions of the legal affairs committee not to waive the parliamentary immunity of ms gomes. the european parliament has a special role to play. it reflects the essential principles of modern democracy and must ensure the free and open development of parliamentary will. the member of the european parliament is directly elected. in the performance of his duties, he shall be guided by the will, the interests and the conscience of his constituents. freedom of speech and expression shall not be restricted, in particular where this is connected with the duties of a member of the european parliament. no member of the european parliament may be punished or prosecuted for the performance of his duties. the corresponding legal liability should only apply where the acts are harmful to the interests of individuals, society or the state.

in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it endorses the granting of support from the european globalisation adjustment fund to workers made redundant during the reference period from 21 october 2013 to 21 february 2014 by the company andersen ireland limited, engaged in the manufacture of jewellery, jewellery, jewellery and similar articles. 171 employees were dismissed. this is the first application for financial assistance from the egf concerning redundancies in the jewellery, jewellery and related products sector, a sector which is experiencing difficulties due to the growth of online trade and which could therefore be further weakened. these redundancies will further worsen the unemployment situation in the region, as andersen ireland limited is a very important employer in this predominantly agricultural region, where the unemployment rate (39,3%) was already twice the national average (19%).

in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at mobilising the egf for the needs of greece in order to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant in the retail company sprider stores . the greek economy has been in deep recession for six consecutive years. since 2008, greek gdp has decreased by 25,7%, public consumption by 21%, private consumption by 32,3% and unemployment by 20,6%. since 2008, thousands of companies have suspended operations, closed down and laid off their employees. the opening of 'sprider stores' in all regions of greece has led to redundancies throughout the country and will further increase the unemployment rate. the package of services supported by the egf for redundant workers will include consultancy, advice, language courses, entrepreneurship training, standardised courses and training programmes, entrepreneurship grants, etc. i approve the mobilisation of eur 7 290 900 to support the reintegration into the labour market of 1 311 redundant workers.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it supports the granting of support from the european globalisation adjustment fund following the dismissal of 761 workers at sprider stores s.a. (retail clothing and textiles) in greece. the commission has examined the events that led to the redundancies as a result of the decline in household disposable income due to the increased tax burden, the decline in wages (private and public) and the increase in unemployment, the consequent sharp decline in purchasing power and the drastic reduction in lending to companies and individuals by the greek banks due to the cash shortage linked to the economic crisis.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal for several reasons. in particular, it is important to ensure that people who have lost their jobs are able to recover, receive stable income and contribute to public finances, but it is also important to ensure that the greek economy is brought to a halt. i think that the action programme for business start-up support is a good way to revive the greek economy, but i would like to stress that a well-developed business infrastructure would bring even more benefits in this case.
in writing. i support the ratification of the eu-moldova association agreement. the agreement provides for the eu to completely eliminate all import duties and prohibit exports of all goods. only certain agricultural commodities (mainly products of animal origin, sugar and cereals) which are considered sensitive in the eu will be subject to a trade restriction. it also provides for the immediate abolition of customs duties on imports of most products. for certain sensitive products and commodities (mainly agricultural and textiles/clothing), a gradual liberalisation process (ranging from 3 to 10 years depending on the product) is envisaged. the market for other products is not liberalised and is subject to a tariff quota system (poultry and pigmeat, certain dairy products, processed meat products or sugar and related products). i believe that the free trade area will provide new opportunities for economic growth and will directly benefit moldovan businesses and citizens, while the eu will benefit from smoother trade flows and better conditions for investment in moldova. the agreement with moldova reaffirms the eu's commitment to the eastern partnership countries and the continuation of the process of signing association agreements with these countries.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, as the association agreement and the resulting gradual integration of the republic of moldova into the european union's policies should not abolish the traditional, historical and economic links of the republic of moldova with other countries in the region, but should, on the contrary, allow the country to fully exploit all its potential. the association agreement shall apply to the entire internationally recognised territory of the republic of moldova and shall benefit all of its inhabitants. i would like to stress the need to address the social implications of the relevant reforms in a timely and appropriate manner. it is important that the republic of moldova ensures that the reforms are embedded in the institutional framework. it is also necessary to strengthen the stability, independence and effectiveness of the institutions responsible for ensuring democracy, the rule of law and good governance and to strengthen the system for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms. in addition, a comprehensive public information campaign should be organised on the objectives and content of the association agreement and on the direct tangible benefits to citizens of the implementation of the association agenda.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the conclusion of an association agreement between the european union and the republic of moldova. i have no doubt that eu has to support the wish of people from moldova to go forwards the european future. this time is particularly important as moldova and its neighbouring countries face increasing pressure from russia. the moldavian government and people have put in a lot of effort, implemented the major reforms and now are ready to go for further integration into the eu.
in writing. it is welcome that moldova has made great progress and intends to apply for membership of the european union in 2015. each country has the right to choose its own political path to promote the well-being of its citizens and political stability. no one has the right to pressure or otherwise influence her decision. in this context, it is essential that the country does not fall behind and continues to pursue its objectives in a systematic manner through the necessary reforms. in particular, the country must have an effective system for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms. in addition, the implementation of the principles of democracy and the rule of law and good governance should be strengthened. it is also essential that moldova makes further progress in implementing the energy efficiency action plan and aligns its climate change policy and objectives with eu policy. violence and aggression must be prevented. the crimean scenario cannot be allowed to be repeated in transnistria. delaying the decision could have serious negative consequences. it is therefore essential that the authorities take concrete action to ensure the safety of the people of transnistria and improve their living conditions.
in writing. i voted in favour because i believe that this treaty is an important step forward for the eu. as a member of the eu-moldova parliamentary cooperation committee, i understand the importance of the association agreement. it provides for support for the modernisation of moldovan institutions and economic stimulation. as the experience of other european countries and regions shows, good governance and stable economic growth are essential conditions for peace and stability. the document itself can only be accompanied by a remark that it does not sufficiently emphasise the external causes of moldova's deeply difficult situation the frozen transnistrian conflict, the destructive influence of russia, the fact that most of the industry remains on the side of transnistria. the internal reasons for this lack of good governance, the excessive centralisation, which means that the local government and the autonomous region of gagauzia have very limited powers to deal with local issues related to specific, sometimes specific, needs, are also insufficiently analysed in the document. it is important to add that the treaty is being adopted at a time when europe is pressed by its own problems. the russian embargo, the african swine flu, all hit agriculture hard. so before we expand our markets by helping other countries, we should find solutions to local challenges.
in writing. i'm in favor of this deal. i would like to emphasise my positive assessment of the progress made by the republic of moldova in the area of justice and law enforcement, in particular in the implementation of the human rights action plan and in the fight against corruption. however, i would like to stress that these reforms are only the first step on a difficult path and that such reforms will have to be implemented over a long period of time. it is also important to make every effort to resolve any possible disagreements in the transnistrian region peacefully, through dialogue.

in writing. i voted in favour of this recommendation to approve the association agreement between the european union (eu) and moldova negotiated under the eastern partnership programme. the agreement contains trade provisions which, if adopted together with the eu, will create a full and comprehensive free trade area. a comprehensive and inclusive free trade area will open up new opportunities for trade and economic growth, directly benefiting moldovan businesses and citizens, and the eu will benefit from smoother trade flows and better conditions for investment in moldova. the comprehensive and progressive free trade agreement (cfta) includes several provisions aimed at reforming moldova's trade and trade-related policies in line with and on the basis of the eu acquis. they will allow the economy to modernise, consolidate eu investment in the country and create a better and more predictable political environment. the european parliament's endorsement of this association agreement will give moldova the opportunity to build a prosperous future in europe. the recommendation stresses that the success of an association agreement depends on its thorough and balanced implementation by both partners.
in writing. – i voted for the association agreement between the eu and the european atomic energy community and their member states, of the one part, and the republic of moldova, of the other part. i see this as an official recognition of the implemented reforms and ambitions of moldova. i feel proud of moldovans, who face the increasing pressure from russia and still move towards moldova’s future goal – european union.
in writing. i supported the ep's position in favour of the conclusion of the agreement. at the same time, i hope for swift ratification by the parliaments of the eu member states, for the full operation of the association agreement, including a comprehensive and complete free trade area, and for moldova not to slow down its reforms. the most important thing is that all moldovan citizens should be able to benefit from the agreement as soon as possible.
in writing. i am in favour of an agreement that will benefit both parties. we must help moldova to become a prosperous country. the eu has also been active in the field of human rights, including the fight against corruption and organised crime. it should be noted that the implementation of the provisions of the agreement will lead to the creation of a full and comprehensive free trade area. this will allow the economy to modernise, eu investment to be anchored in the country and a better and more predictable political environment to be created. it should be noted that not only moldova but also the eu have to implement the terms of the agreement in depth and in a balanced manner. otherwise, the objective of this agreement will not be missed.

in writing. turkey has issued a statement stating that a large area of cyprus is reserved for seismic research. these seismic surveys should be carried out in the areas allocated by the government of cyprus to the italian company eni for the exploration of potential hydrocarbon deposits on the seabed, but turkey is preventing the company from carrying out these activities. cyprus has the sovereign right to explore natural resources in its exclusive economic zone and i therefore support calls for turkey to respect international law and to withdraw vessels operating in the exclusive economic zone of cyprus. it is important that the european external action service and the commission closely monitor turkey's activities in the exclusive economic zone of cyprus.
