in writing. south sudan is one of the most vulnerable countries in the world, with more than 10 000 people killed in the fighting. the peace talks have made little progress, and the fighting between president kiir's forces and the rebels loyal to riek machar is gradually resuming and will intensify if no solution is found. i support calls for the international community to honour its funding commitments to south sudan in response to the deteriorating humanitarian situation. the eu should adapt its development assistance programmes to meet the essential needs of the people of south sudan and support the transition to peace and stability. the capacity of the un mission in south sudan (unmiss) to investigate human rights needs to be strengthened. all parties must participate in the peace negotiations in order to fully implement the ceasefire agreement. i support calls for the eu to continue to provide material and financial assistance to igad (intergovernmental authority on development) and to provide staff to ensure the functioning of the ceasefire monitoring and verification mechanism.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the resolution on humanitarian aid in south sudan as it is our duty to protect human rights and to ensure their dignity. we cannot close our eyes when repeated violence causes deaths and injuries among the civilian population. the european union has to do everything in its power to make sure that people in south sudan are able to return to their deserted farms, reopen markets and rebuild homes.
in writing. i voted in favour of the resolution expressing concern at the humanitarian situation in south sudan. south sudan the youngest and most vulnerable country in the world, ranking second in the european commission's overall vulnerability and crisis assessment index. the situation in the country is extremely complex, with grave violations of human rights in the context of a state of war, the brutal recruitment of children into the armed forces, and the country at the epicentre of a humanitarian crisis, famine and infectious diseases. it is difficult to identify the culprits in such a situation, but violations of human rights, violence against children, sexual abuse cannot be ignored. the eu's tightening of sanctions, arms embargoes and the activities of the eu special representative are processes that need to be monitored and are likely to contribute, at least in a small way, to the stabilisation of the situation in south sudan in the future.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. first, the country must put an end to hostilities as soon as possible and find political solutions and implement the principle of a democratic rule of law. strengthened control of armed forces is needed to reduce violence and increase compliance with peace agreements. fighting and aggression only exacerbate the conflict and create instability in the region and further deepen the humanitarian crisis in the country. relations between the humanitarian community and all parties to the conflict are also deteriorating. executions and mass killings and attacks must be prevented. it is necessary to review development assistance programmes in the light of the needs of the country and its people and to provide increased support to the country in order to ensure a faster transition to peace and stability. particular attention shall be paid to the most vulnerable. humanitarian and food assistance should be given priority. it is also essential that all parties to the conflict ensure and respect the neutrality, independence and impartiality of humanitarian workers. humanitarian aid shall not be provided to armed groups. it should be noted that humanitarian aid is essential but cannot solve political problems. the government therefore has a special role to play, and it must take immediate and lasting decisions to achieve national reconciliation for the benefit of all south sudanese.
in writing. the resolution, which i fully support, paints a grim picture of reality. the document is accurate, applicable, well-informed and at the same time shocking. the situation is indeed bad, but we cannot give up. europe can make a difference and the european parliament has a duty to show europe the right way to solve this crisis. this document perfectly reflects all that south sudan needs for lasting reconciliation, peace and social and economic development.
today we are going to talk about europe 2020 again, and life will seem to be going its way again, as it has done many times before. the lisbon strategy also addressed the reduction of social exclusion and various measures, but the objectives set were not achieved. poverty and social exclusion are increasingly starving the eyes, and it's not just the blind who can't see it. millions of people in europe live below the poverty line. i believe that the european union should establish common criteria for a minimum income for each person, and that this minimum income should be guaranteed by a special european fund, which the commission could set up. the commission could also take a legislative initiative to establish a minimum wage corresponding to living standards throughout the european union.
achieving the employment targets and reducing poverty and social exclusion by 2020 requires united political will and real cooperation, both between and within member states and between the eu member states and the eu institutions. the slow recovery was undoubtedly affected by the economic and financial crisis, the unprecedented austerity measures that have had a negative impact on social protection systems, the increase in the number of people suffering from poverty and social exclusion, in particular the increase in the number of children living in poverty, and the worsening of working poverty. it is no wonder that in some lands people have chosen to emigrate. i urge member states to implement the youth employment initiative as soon as possible and the review of the eu budget requires more funding for youth employment. strong links between education, the economy and labour market policies are needed. only then will we be able to deliver real results for the citizens of our european union.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at the ratification of the rail protocol. the railway protocol aims to facilitate the financing of railway rolling stock by creating a particularly strong international guarantee for creditors. this will not only stimulate capital investment in the railway sector but also the creation of a market for mobile rail equipment rental in europe. the railway protocol also establishes a register of international interests relating to railway rolling stock, which will complement the european vehicle identification number system. i believe that the adoption of the protocol will stimulate innovation and investment in the european union's railways and will support the transition to greener and more sustainable modes of transport, namely rail transport.
in writing. i voted in favour of this recommendation to endorse the council decision on the rail protocol. the railway protocol is compatible with european union law and the council has therefore taken advantage of the commission's proposal and decided to exclude provisions in areas where eu legislation itself is considered appropriate. the commission proposal states that the purpose of the rail protocol is to facilitate the financing of high-value railway rolling stock by creating a particularly strong international guarantee for creditors, whether creditors are sellers or institutions that credit such sales. the commission emphasises that this should not only stimulate capital investment in the railway sector but also the creation of a genuine market for the rental of mobile railway equipment in europe. this commission proposal is therefore closely linked to the 4th railway package, one of the objectives of which is to encourage more innovation and investment in the eu railways. the proposal notes that the railway protocol is also compatible with the objective of supporting the transition to greener and more sustainable modes of transport, such as rail.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the convention on international interests in mobile equipment on matters specific to railway rolling stock. i do believe that after this convention comes into force it will be much easier to attract investments into the railway sector. more investments into this area will help to modernise this important industry. the system itself will be more transparent and reliable for the investors.
in writing. i voted in favour of this recommendation, which proposes to approve the protocol, which is beneficial for the european rail industry, banks and governments. i believe that a strong international guarantee would make it easier for creditors to finance very valuable railway rolling stock. the uniform rules on cross-border interest will allow creditors to obtain an international guarantee. they will thus be granted absolute priority over these assets in the international register. it should be noted that this protocol also establishes an international system of unique and unchanging registration of international interests and supplements and supports the european vehicle identification number system. it should be noted that the protocol will benefit not only operators but also society. in addition, capital investment in the railway sector will be encouraged, contributing to the creation of a genuine rental market.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to endorse the hague convention on arbitration for the purposes of the settlement of disputes in the field of justice. this convention on private international law shall apply to agreements on exclusive jurisdiction in civil and commercial matters. these are agreements whereby both parties agree that any dispute between them on a particular issue will be heard in the courts of that state. i agree that the european union should participate in the implementation of this convention, which was signed in 2009. i support the proposals to encourage the us to ratify the convention, as its widespread application would help to ensure greater legal certainty in the international settlement of exclusive jurisdictional agreements, and would thus promote trade between the participating countries.
in writing. i voted in favour of this recommendation to approve the draft council decision excluding from the application of the convention insurance contracts concluded with consumers, thereby preventing consumers from inadvertently objecting to the choice of jurisdiction clause. the declaration annexed to the draft decision formally sets out this exception and preserves the freedom of the main commercial insurance and reinsurance contracts. the hague convention on arbitration agreements is a convention in the field of private international law drawn up by the hague conference on private international law. this convention shall apply to agreements on exclusive jurisdiction in civil and commercial matters. these are agreements whereby both parties agree that any dispute between them on a particular issue will be heard in the courts of that state. the widespread application of this convention would help to ensure greater legal certainty in the form of exclusive jurisdiction agreements at international level, thereby promoting trade between the parties. this convention should therefore be approved.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the hague convention of 30 june 2005 on choice-of-court agreements, because i believe that it will encourage the united states of america to proceed with the ratification. moreover, i believe that eu companies will have more legal certainty when doing business with companies outside the eu. in this way, they can be sure that the judgment given by the chosen court will be recognised and enforced in the countries which applied for it. it will give more clarity and simplification in the business environment.
in writing. i voted in favour of this recommendation, which proposes to approve the convention. i believe that uniform rules on jurisdiction and the recognition and enforcement of foreign judgments in civil and commercial matters will promote closer links in international trade and investment matters and strengthen judicial cooperation in this field. this is particularly important for eu companies trading outside the eu, as legal certainty is an important element in risk assessment. it should be noted that the autonomy of the countries should be guaranteed not only within the eu but also outside the eu, at international level. it is therefore essential to enhance legal certainty for companies trading with third countries. it should be noted that the eu's efforts alone are not sufficient to achieve greater legal certainty in the form of international agreements on exceptional jurisdiction. the united states should be encouraged to ratify this convention. the more countries that are major trading partners of the union ratify this convention, the greater the benefits for business.
in writing. i am voting in favour of eu participation in the hague convention of 30 june 2005 because it gives greater legal clarity to international agreements on the choice of court and at the same time promotes trade between the participating countries and, in particular, helps small and medium-sized businesses feel safer when expanding outside the european union. i also support the exemption from the convention for insurance contracts, as this protects consumers from choosing the court without their knowledge. it is essential that consumers are protected against disadvantages arising from unequal power relations.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at mobilising the egf for the needs of finland in order to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by stx finland oy as a result of the major structural changes in the world trade system brought about by globalisation. the company was engaged in the shipping industry. the eu's shipbuilding market share was 5% in the first three quarters of 2013 compared to 13% in 2007. the corresponding combined market share of china, south korea and japan was 86% in the first three quarters of 2013 and 77% in 2007. the package of services for redundant workers will include upskilling-oriented training, employment and business services provided by the service centre, retraining, entrepreneurship promotion, etc. i support the allocation of eur 1 426 800 from the egf to finland's needs to speed up the reintegration into the labour market of 565 redundant workers.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, which deals with the application of finland stx rauma to the european globalisation adjustment fund. in view of the major structural changes in the world trade system brought about by globalisation, the commission adopted on 14 october 2014 a proposal for a decision on the mobilisation of the egf in finland to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by stx finland oy in its nace (other transport equipment manufacturing) activity. the aid is necessary because, as the finnish authorities argue, the global shipping industry has changed significantly in recent years and that in this global context the eu's share of the shipbuilding market has decreased. in many asian countries, shipbuilding has become an industry that creates jobs and foreign exchange. due to the global nature of the shipbuilding industry and its impact on employment, it is supported in asian countries. as a result, europe, which has developed a modern shipbuilding industry, has shifted a large part of its market to asian countries with lower labour costs. the finnish authorities state that the unemployment rate in the rauma region will increase by more than 2 percentage points from 11,1% to 13,1% after the closure of stx. in addition, low education and relative age are likely to increase the risk of long-term unemployment.
in writing. – i voted in favour of mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund: application egf/2014/008 fi/stx rauma. i believe that the eu should protect and support its citizens from any crisis. we should provide financial support for people in need and give them a chance to reintegrate themselves into the labour market after they were dismissed because of force majeure.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report. the eu must be proactive and effective in helping member states to address the short-term negative effects of globalisation. this is also a way of showing solidarity with the workers who have been made redundant. it is worth noting that the financial support from the european globalisation adjustment fund is a vital tool that increases the chances of finding a job in sometimes rather difficult circumstances. financial support from stx finland for the redundant employees is therefore necessary. it will provide opportunities for reintegration into future-oriented and sustainable sectors of the economy. it should be noted that support should be given in particular to the implementation of social assistance measures for the most vulnerable and low-skilled workers. on the other hand, in view of the number of applications submitted in the shipbuilding industry, i believe that the shipbuilding industry in different member states could be supported in the preparation of european-wide guidelines.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at mobilising the egf for the needs of france in order to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by gad société anonyme simplifiée as a result of the major structural changes in the world trade system brought about by globalisation. the company was engaged in the production of food products. the financial and economic crisis in europe has led to a decline in pork consumption, which in turn has reduced pork production and the productivity of slaughterhouses such as gad. in 2007, the per capita annual consumption of pork was 43 kg, in 2013 it was 39 kg. total revenues fell from eur 495,1 million in 2008 to eur 445,8 million in 2009 and the company did not recover. the tailored services to be provided to redundant workers consist of a single action: the provision of a consultancy and guidance service for redundant workers by a team of consultants. i support the allocation of eur 918 000 from the egf to the needs of france to speed up the reintegration into the labour market of 760 redundant workers.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, which concerns the decision to mobilise the egf for the needs of france in order to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by the gad société anonyme simplifiée, an undertaking active in the economic sector (food production) as a result of the global financial and economic crisis. the french authorities argue that the global financial and economic crisis has led to a decline in pork consumption in europe, which in turn has reduced the production of pork and the productivity of slaughterhouses such as gad. the decline in consumption caused by the global financial and economic crisis is also common to other types of meat, but pig producers have been the most affected, as the price of pig meat has increased faster than that of other meats, in particular beef. in addition, the costs of feeding the pigs are compensated, thus increasing the final price of the pigs, which is ultimately borne by the consumer. with the eu still suffering from the effects of the crisis, consumers were unwilling or unable to buy as much pork as before. the slaughterhouse and meat processing company gad were in a price pressure zone farmers faced the difficulties of rising feed prices and consumers the problems of reduced incomes. this pressure lasted for five years or more and eventually put the company in a very difficult financial situation.
in writing. – i voted in favour of mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund: application egf/2014/005 fr/gad. as i was elected by the nation, marked by longstanding agricultural traditions, i felt the need to vote in favour of supporting the french abattoirs. the issue, which french authorities encountered, is very close to the situation that lithuania experiences at the moment due to the russian embargo.
in writing. it is essential that the european globalisation adjustment fund continues to demonstrate the european union's solidarity with workers during this new programming period. rapid and effective assistance will help to avoid devastating consequences for human lives and the economy. in this context, i support this resolution which proposes to mobilise the egf in order to support the reintegration of workers made redundant in a french slaughterhouse and meat processing plant as a result of the global financial and economic crisis. it is essential that the implementation of the measures envisaged respects the principles of equal treatment and non-discrimination and is directly linked to the provision of services tailored to individual needs. it is worth noting that the egf improves the economic situation of regions by helping workers to re-skill and to acquire a new profession, and by updating and improving their skills in finding a job. it also builds self-esteem and self-confidence. the measures, if properly implemented, therefore have a positive long-term impact.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which seeks to obtain the opinion of the court of justice on the compatibility of the eu-canada agreement on the processing and transfer of passenger name record (pnr) data by air carriers with eu law. in 2005, the eu concluded an agreement with canada on the processing of passenger name record data. the european legal basis for the transfer of pnr data to the canadian border guard agency ceased to exist on 22 september 2009 when the relevant commission decision expired. as there is currently legal uncertainty as to whether the draft new agreement is compatible with the provisions of the eu treaties and the eu charter of fundamental rights relating to the rights of individuals to the protection of personal data, i support the decision to request the opinion of the court of justice on the compatibility of the agreement with the eu treaties.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the opinion from the court of justice on the compatibility with the treaties of the agreement between the european union and canada on the transfer and processing of passenger name record (pnr) data by air carriers to the canadian border services agency. i voted in favour, because i do agree that the court of justice should express an opinion on the compatibility of the agreement with the treaties regarding pnr data usage. we should not forget that the transfer of pnr data amounts to a violation of eu data protection. i truly believe that all of us should respect the fundamental rights of citizens of the union.
in writing. given the importance of this agreement, it is essential that legal uncertainty be removed. in order to ensure adequate protection of personal data, all doubts as to the compatibility of this agreement with the eu treaties must be removed. the transfer of passenger data shall strictly respect the principles of necessity and proportionality. in particular, confidential data shall be deleted by means of an automatic process for the detection and deletion of confidential data. it is also important to limit the categories of pnr data to be processed and to specify the specific canadian authorities to which pnr data may be transferred. in order to minimise the possibility of access to personal data, it is necessary to provide for a targeted mechanism for the assessment of pnr data against risk criteria. impartial monitoring of compliance with the agreement shall be carried out on a regular basis. the agreement should therefore provide as a matter of urgency that no other canadian authority should have direct access to pnr data and should not be able to obtain it upon request. this will prevent misuse to circumvent the provisions of the agreement and ensure adequate protection of personal data.
in writing. this proposal by the alde group is welcome. it is wise to seek the opinion of the european court of justice on this matter. this is a sensitive issue and its legal basis is confusing. as this agreement with canada is likely to become a model for similar agreements, it is essential that any possible incompatibilities with the eu treaties be resolved before parliament takes a decision. i consider that the wrong legal basis is based on articles 82 (1) (d) and 87 (2) (a) of the treaty on the functioning of the european union (police and judicial cooperation) and should be based on article 16 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union (data protection). we must ensure that any transfer and processing of personal data is carried out in compliance with the fundamental rights of our citizens as enshrined in the treaties of the european union and the charter of fundamental rights.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to set development policy priorities for the period after 2015. in 2000, the un adopted the millennium declaration and set specific goals to be achieved by 2015. while there are some positive results, such as the halving of extreme poverty and the good results achieved in the fight against malaria and tuberculosis, there is still a need to reduce poverty, increase food security, education, promote gender equality, sustainable energy, sustainable growth, etc. i support the proposal to focus development policy on poverty reduction, the fight against inequality, the protection of human rights and the sustainability of health systems. the year 2015 is a symbolic one, as it marks the completion of the millennium development goals agreed by the united nations in 2000.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it is necessary to adopt a new development programme for the period after 2015. the eradication of poverty is one of the priorities of this programme and the new programme will therefore address the multiple dimensions of poverty and inequality, which are linked not only to income deprivation but also to high unemployment. secondly, it is essential to ensure human rights and conflict prevention, peacebuilding and the promotion of lasting peace. the eu recognised that sustainable development can only be achieved in the context of peace and security, and that, conversely, without development and poverty, peace cannot be sustainable. it is also important to ensure health care and education. in the new development agenda, the eu should focus on promoting equitable and universal healthcare, ensuring quality costs and access to quality healthcare. it is particularly important that health and education services reach the most vulnerable groups in society. it is also important to give priority to the elimination of all forms of violence against women and girls.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the report on the eu and the global development framework after 2015. mdgs adopted in 2000 have reached a lot of successful goals and made a huge impact on people’s lives. i agree with the human development report 2014 that stresses increasing global challenges due to poverty, human rights violations, corruption, etc. therefore, we need a strong and cohesive eu position as well as all stakeholders united to be able to resist these challenges.
in writing. about 1.5 billion people are affected by poverty, lack of healthcare, education and living standards, and 162 million children are still suffering from malnutrition. the eradication of poverty at international level must therefore be a priority issue on which the eu should express a unified position. it should be underlined that poverty eradication, the fight against inequalities and sustainable development should be the priority development themes for the post-2015 agenda, with human rights at the heart of the agenda. in order to implement this programme, it is necessary to focus on areas such as sustainable economic growth, job creation and the efficient use of resources. i supported the european parliament resolution on the eu and the post-2015 development agenda because the aim of reducing the number of people living at or near the poverty line is important and, with the right measures chosen and based on sound data, is both feasible and achievable.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution. given that the challenges of poverty eradication and sustainable development are of global concern and concern all countries and all people, they must be addressed jointly on a global scale. a strong political commitment and decisive action are needed to ensure a decent standard of living worldwide. in particular, the eu and the global development agenda post-2015 must be based on the principles of human dignity, impartiality, equality and sustainability. it must be a catalyst for change, a new global partnership and a response to a changing world. it should be noted that the programme should provide for the responsibilities of the parties, taking into account the different situations, capacities and levels of development of the parties. i support the proposal to give greater attention to strategies to promote the non-discharge of coal-based compounds. it is essential that all developing countries establish a national minimum social protection and ensure universal access to comprehensive health and education services. the least developed and other vulnerable countries must not be left behind. the commission should continue to work closely with other donors in developing and implementing innovative financial mechanisms.
in writing. i voted in favour because it encourages all eu countries to review their previously set millennium development goals and to set even more ambitious ones. it is particularly important that the document covers the three dimensions of sustainable development - social, economic and environmental. without sustainable action, we will not be able to address the root causes of the world's poverty, violence and discrimination. we cannot remain indifferent when 1.5 billion people are suffering from poverty and multiple deprivations in health, education and living standards. today we are talking about poverty not just in some distant place, but here in europe. more than ever, therefore, a new development agenda must be global and applicable to all countries. i welcome the fact that this document calls for the eu to take action and to indicate what commitments the community is prepared to make and to offer at international level. i think it's time to realize that we can't make a difference unless every country is involved in this process, unless we have continuous funding and consistent policies and clear criteria by which we can measure progress.
in writing. i voted in favour of this document, but i would like to highlight a few key points which i believe are the most important in achieving the objectives set. first, it is the fight against corruption, which like a cancer destroys all the good work that has been done. secondly, we must act as an example to other countries - particularly in the field of environmental protection - to show that development based on clean energy, produced in a sustainable way, is possible.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to review the employment and social aspects of the eu 2020 strategy. since the start of the europe 2020 strategy in 2010, the unemployment rate has continued to rise, with 24.6 million unemployed in the eu today. since 2008, the number of people at risk of poverty or social exclusion has increased from 10 million to more than 122.6 million, or one in four. i agree that the role of social protection systems as key instruments for economic and social stability and poverty reduction is not sufficiently taken into account. i support the proposals set out to encourage member states to pursue broader ambitions when they translate eu objectives into their own national objectives. i believe it is necessary to call on the commission and the member states to ensure that employment growth is only achieved by creating new quality jobs in the european economy, and it is also important to create a platform of social partners that brings together the interests of employers and workers.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because there is still a lot of progress to be made in the member states to achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. it remains to be regretted that in some member states, the growth outlooks and country-specific recommendations are not sufficiently aligned with the employment, poverty reduction and education objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. the role of social protection systems as key instruments for economic and social stability and poverty reduction is not sufficiently taken into account. unemployment remains high, and people often opt for open-ended contracts, fictitious self-employment or low-wage work as a way out. the european parliament therefore calls on the member states to respond as actively as possible to the needs of the labour market, in particular by ensuring strong links between education and work. calls on the commission to ensure that the european social fund and the european structural and investment funds are closely aligned with the policy priorities of the europe 2020 strategy, in order to strengthen the role of these funds in providing financial support for the implementation of the strategy;
in writing. – i voted in favour of the resolution on employment and social aspects of the eu2020 strategy. i strongly support the expressed regrets that current eu policies focus solely on economic growth, which does not necessarily lead to sustainable society. i see an urgent need for member states’ active role implementing eu2020 strategy goals, especially by helping to create quality jobs within the european economy.
in writing. it is recognised that the eu has not achieved the employment and poverty reduction targets set out in the europe 2020 strategy. unemployment rates in some member states continued to rise, reaching an alarming 10.1% in 2014 across the eu28, with particularly worryingly high levels of youth unemployment. measures such as professional mobility, the eures programme in the member states must be easily accessible to all, and a broad information campaign must reach even socially sensitive eu citizens. the introduction and application of new measures may be linked to a mid-term review of the strategy, integrating the specific youth employment objective, as well as public consultation, consultation of civil society on the proper and effective implementation of the europe 2020 strategy, and focus on key employment and social aspects.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution. it is important to note that the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy have not yet been achieved and that stronger measures are needed to close the current gap. social systems are key tools for increasing economic and social stability and reducing poverty. it is therefore essential that employment and social issues are treated on an equal footing with macroeconomic issues. in particular, in pursuit of the poverty reduction objective of the europe 2020 strategy, the commission should, as a matter of urgency, present a proposal for the establishment of a minimum social security, with particular attention to minimum income systems. this would improve living standards and further boost competitiveness, help to ensure greater economic, social and territorial cohesion and help to mitigate the social consequences of the crisis, protect fundamental human rights. it is an urgent stabilisation measure to guarantee a balance between economic and social objectives and to reduce social disparities between the community's population. as unemployment rates continue to rise in some member states and disparities between member states increase, member states need support to carry out the necessary structural reforms, to increase labour market flexibility and to remove administrative and bureaucratic obstacles. in addition, a special role should be given to the multiannual financial framework, which currently provides for insufficient funding to achieve the employment and social objectives of the europe 2020 strategy.
in writing. i voted in favour because i fully agree that current policies remain focused on economic growth alone, without regard to whether that growth benefits all sections of society. the economic crisis, the constant attempts of certain groups to profit at the expense of the common people, have led us to the fact that more than 6 million people live in poverty. obviously, poverty has affected not only those who are unemployed but also a portion of those who are employed. high workloads, demands for free after-hours work, and extremely low wages are just some of the aspects that many workers experience on a daily basis. i fully support the idea put forward in the document that it is necessary to remove the tax burden from the working population and to introduce other forms of sustainable taxation in order to stimulate economic growth and job creation. together, we must review the poverty indicator system and monitor the evolution of poverty levels on an ongoing basis in order to identify and assist those at risk of exclusion.
the question asked when the blue card is raised. i would like to ask you, mr president, if it is not as if everything is standing on its head instead of on its feet. that we negotiate jobs first, then we have a huge bill, then we all suffer, and then we decide how to solve the problem. shouldn't we put everything in place and agree on funding first, and then talk about how we're going to implement those projects?
last week, in my home country of lithuania, a 20-year-old student was raped and brutally murdered, a student who, according to her mother, would not dare to harm a bug. and so one in three women in our developed, democratic europe experiences violence because of her gender. 7 women die every day in europe from violence. can we still ignore the scale of this human rights violation? is that acceptable to us? the answer is absolutely no. that is why we, the members of the european parliament, will never stop calling on the commission to submit a proposal for a european union directive to combat violence against women. i am personally convinced that one measure alone will not eliminate violence against women. we must take coordinated action in all areas: legal, judicial, enforcement, infrastructure, cultural, educational, social. and that's the only way to get results. i also call on the commission to establish a european observatory on violence against women and girls. this could be done not by creating new institutions but by extending the scope of the european institute for gender equality to fulfil this function.
i agree that risk prevention and the promotion of safer and healthier working conditions are of great importance not only for improving the quality of work, working conditions, but also for promoting competitiveness. but i want to emphasize that this is not an automatic work tool or a machine. this new strategy has to be for the person who works hard, works a lot and works more and more as the life span increases. it is therefore necessary to take account of demographic changes in europe, as employers will have to recognise the changes in the workforce and increasingly use older workers. it is therefore necessary to take into account that the health risks of older workers are very different from those of younger workers. therefore, in order to enable people to work longer, occupational health and safety measures need to be adapted to workers of this age. another problem is that the psychosocial risk aspect, i.e. the intimidation of employees, is still not addressed. for example, that they could lose their jobs at any time, because now there's a crisis, because there's a shortage of jobs, because there are hundreds, thousands of people who can replace them. we also underestimate the stress of our workload. and these questions are becoming more and more important, because the work environment is changing dramatically with the introduction of new technologies, materials, work processes, design, and different ways of organising and managing work. not all employees can adapt to new changes at the same pace, and this can have a significant impact on their mental and physical health. thank you very much, and i urge the commission to take all the proposals into account, because it really matters what people's health will be.
indeed, ensuring equal opportunities for all in employment and protecting citizens from discrimination remains a major challenge for member states. the equality directive affects the daily lives of everyone: young people, disabled people, women, the elderly. i would like to emphasise that the demographic changes in europe, as well as the increase in the retirement age, have made age discrimination in employment more and more relevant. therefore, if we are already extending the retirement age, we must also ensure that these people have the opportunity to remain in the labour market as full-fledged employees. secondly, women also face many inequalities in employment, social protection and so on. there are still huge wage differences for the same work, and women are much more likely than men to work in temporary, socially insecure jobs, all of which have a bearing on lower pensions for women and an increasing number of women entering poverty in old age. and thirdly, i want to stress that we are still failing to protect the most vulnerable members of society, the disabled, from discrimination. it is sad to hear that some member states are violating this directive by failing to adapt jobs to disabled people, by failing to provide them with suitable working conditions, by failing to ensure equal opportunities in education and vocational training. i therefore urge the commission not to be indifferent to the most vulnerable groups in our society, because directives should not just sit on the shelves of the institutions. it should become a tool for everyday life in the member states, protecting and improving the lives of all people.
in writing. i congratulate the commission on the presentation of the investment plan. europe needs investment now to ensure long-term and stable prosperity. it is difficult to say anything more positive about the plan presented. it is not balanced in the way it is expected to be, and it is not clear how it plans to raise the eur 315 billion. the so-called innovative financial engineering measures, which are expected to increase the value of eu investment by up to 15 times over 7 years, appear more like speculation in securities in optimistic emerging markets than long-term investment in regional growth. i would also point out that these plans are being announced now that the eu budget does not have the funds to compensate the union's commitments for projects for the period 2007-13. ideas can be drawn from the investment plan prepared by the green paper, which is based on the realistic potential for mobilising public sector funds. we are realistic and therefore do not think that one euro of public spending will attract more than two from the private sector. the impact of the measures we propose will be far more sustainable: a cleaner environment, reduced pollution, a more sustainable and resource-efficient economy. the greens propose a fundamental reorientation of the economy and the promotion of social and environmental innovation, and the creation of an effective energy union independent of the external environment. in this way, the plan will be used to tackle social exclusion, poverty and create decent living conditions for all citizens.
for the efdd group the lima conference should become a stepping stone towards a legally binding, ambitious international agreement in paris in 2015. not only the european union, but the rest of the world needs to start taking global warming seriously, rather than keeping its head in the sand. the risks posed by climate change are very serious and have negative consequences for long-term economic growth and pose a real threat to human existence. the european union's efforts alone are not enough and there is a risk that the safe climate threshold may be exceeded after 2020. it is therefore necessary to agree on global action against climate change after the expiry of the kyoto protocol. a development strategy based on carbon reduction is fully compatible with economic growth. all parties must do their homework and meet the targets set. action plans must be drawn up, setting out specific commitments by states and ensuring clarity and transparency in action at national level. in pursuing ambitious climate change policies at the global level, we need to work together and promote joint action, in particular by governments, business, financial institutions, industry, society and all together to look for all possible sources of funding, because this is of paramount importance.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to take account of the european central bank's recommendation to amend regulation (ec) no 2533/98 to take account of the ecb's new supervisory tasks relating to the prudential policy for the supervision of credit institutions. the proposal provides that the ecb may use confidential statistical information in the performance of its new supervisory tasks. i support the rapporteur's proposal to limit these new powers to allowing only those confidential data to be transmitted which are necessary for the ecb to carry out its new supervisory tasks under the ssm regulation. furthermore, the european system of central banks should allow for the transmission of confidential statistical information to the authorities of the member states and the eu responsible for the stability of financial institutions, markets and infrastructure and to the european stability mechanism in the detail necessary for the performance of their respective tasks. i agree with the rapporteur's proposal that such an important power should be granted by requiring the receiving authorities to take all necessary regulatory, administrative, technical and organisational measures to ensure the protection of the relevant confidential statistical information.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, which amends the recommendation of the european central bank (ecb) on statistical information. the proposed amendments to the ecb recommendation provide that the ecb may access confidential statistical information in the performance of new supervisory tasks in a similar way to the national central banks, which are entrusted with specific prudential supervisory functions and which are therefore able to access confidential statistical information for prudential purposes under the current regulation. the european parliament proposes that the new powers be limited to allowing the transmission of confidential data only to those necessary for the ecb to carry out its new supervisory tasks under the regulation on the policy on the supervision of credit institutions. the new powers under that regulation are conferred on the ecb, not on the european system of central banks. the european parliament therefore considers that such an important power to transmit confidential data to certain authorities must be balanced by the requirement that the receiving authorities take all the necessary regulatory, administrative, technical and organisational measures to ensure the appropriate physical and logical protection of confidential statistical information.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the report on the draft council regulation concerning the collection of the statistical information by the european central bank. i believe that the statistical information has to be exchanged within the european system of central banks in order to be able to supervise and to ensure a stable financial sector.
in writing. i think it is important to have clear rules on the information circulating in the european central bank, but even more important to ensure that the countries receiving this information have the appropriate legal framework, technical preparation, administrative and organisational matters in place to ensure the protection of sensitive and confidential information. i believe that these new rules can also help to reduce the administrative burden, as it is envisaged that the same data will only need to be collected once.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, which proposes amendments to the council regulation on the powers of the european central bank (ecb) to impose sanctions. the financial crisis has highlighted the need for better regulation and supervision of the eu's financial sector. new rules have been adopted to ensure that all financial entities, products and markets are properly regulated and effectively supervised. financial markets and all relevant users of financial markets need to have confidence that the supervisory authority will be able to take action in the future if banks do not comply with the rules. i agree that the ecb should be given clear and unambiguous powers to impose sanctions if banks do not comply with the rules.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, which proposes amendments to the council regulation on the powers of the european central bank (ecb) to impose sanctions. the financial crisis has highlighted the need for better regulation and supervision of the eu's financial sector. new rules have been adopted to ensure that all financial entities, products and markets are properly regulated and effectively supervised. these rules form the framework for all 28 eu member states and are the basis for a well-functioning single market in financial services. it is widely agreed that the crisis was contributed to by a lack of regulatory credibility, as implementation proved to be seriously flawed. traditionally, the financial sector has placed great reliance on market discipline, but transparency is essential for the proper functioning of the market surveillance system. this means not only having the necessary information, but also ensuring that this information reaches the market effectively and is also correctly interpreted by market participants and used in decision-making. the european parliament notes that there is no qualitative impact assessment, which is necessary, and that some of the shortcomings could be effectively addressed by applying the impact assessment methodology. parliament also proposes to improve some of the recommendation's elements and to make the following main changes.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the report on the powers of the european central bank to impose sanctions. after surviving the financial crisis, i see an urgent need for better supervision and regulation of financial sector. giving the power to impose sanctions is one of the ways to ensure that the european rules are respected and followed. as member states are closely related and spill-over effect is mostly unavoidable, we cannot act with an individual mind any more. we have to feel responsibility for each other.
in writing. everyone will benefit from proper supervision of banks in europe and from the knowledge and compliance of all with the rules. of course, it is always best to have goodwill in agreements, but there are sometimes abusive ones, and it is important that the european central bank has the power to punish such institutions. i therefore hope that this instrument will be implemented swiftly and properly. another tool to be looked at in the future is not the sanctioning authorities but the individuals responsible for the decisions taken.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution on climate change, which notes that in order to achieve a global temperature rise of below 20°c, it is essential to use the lima conference to outline the main objectives of an international agreement that can be reached in paris in december 2015. it is important that the eu and its member states keep in mind their commitment to increase contributions to the unece fund, with a target of usd 100 billion per year by 2020. the agreement must provide for clear mitigation, adaptation and implementation measures to halve carbon dioxide emissions by 2050 compared to 1990 levels. the agreement on climate finance, technology transfer and capacity building will be a key element of support to developing countries. it is also important that the international maritime organisation (imo) and the international civil aviation organisation (icao) take action to achieve sufficient results in the fight against the effects of climate change.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because climate change is an unavoidable and potentially irreversible threat to human health and survival, biodiversity and the planet, and must therefore be addressed by all countries at national, eu and international levels. if we are to achieve the european union's environmental, development, humanitarian and disaster risk reduction, economic, foreign, security and human rights policy objectives, we need concrete and tangible results in tackling climate change. the european parliament hopes that the eu and the member states will play a constructive role at the meeting in lima in order to successfully agree a binding global climate agreement in paris in 2015. we should remember that governments around the world have a shared responsibility, including to future generations, to take the right climate action. the european union should therefore substantially strengthen the eu's climate policy and reach an agreement on binding ambitious targets for emissions reductions, energy efficiency and the use of renewable energy sources by 2030, with the exception of biofuels that are socially and environmentally harmful.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the resolution on the climate change conference in lima, peru. i absolutely agree that governments of the world share a collective responsibility for future generations, therefore they have to take adequate actions. decisions made in lima are extremely important in order to be able to reach climate change goals in paris 2015.
in writing. climate change is a global problem that threatens the stability of the biosphere, communities and societies. scientific evidence shows that climate change is not a myth climate warming is real, and human activities are the main contributors. without specific measures to mitigate climate change, the average temperature is likely to be 5°c higher by the end of the 21st century. it is therefore essential to find international consensus and strengthen measures to reduce global greenhouse gas emissions and to limit global warming to 2°c. research shows that economic growth and the fight against climate change are compatible and can be achieved by countries of all economic capacities. it is important that the paris climate change conference in december 2015 agree on coordinated action on climate change beyond 2020 at international level, in line with the 2°c target and the right to sustainable development.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution. the 20th conference of the parties to the unfccc will be held in lima. this is the final and decisive step towards the paris meeting in 2015. in this context, the main elements of this agreement should be agreed and the draft agreement should be negotiated in lima. we must act quickly to address the risks and ensure that the climate does not warm by more than 2 degrees. not only the eu but also the rest of the world must make commitments to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. it is therefore essential that developing countries are also given the appropriate support to join this global fight. only by working together can we tackle climate change much more effectively and prevent potential threats to the entire world's population.
in writing. - i voted in favour because it is clear to me that tackling climate change is one of the greatest challenges facing the world. it is essential to recognise that change requires the abandonment of old, unreliable technologies, especially coal, and the implementation of effective programmes to reduce consumption. the european union must take all measures to promote the development of renewable energy, thus creating green jobs and added value for people living in the regions. the european union can no longer delay decisions on the phasing out of fossil fuel subsidies. we cannot turn a blind eye to the construction of new nuclear power stations, both in the european union and in its neighbourhood, under the guise of climate change mitigation. we can no longer wait for other countries to set ambitious goals. if we want change, we have to start with ourselves. every person, every member state, has to take action. the european union must make it clear that decisions must be taken now, not before the next un climate change conference in paris.
in writing. the fight against global warming one of the priorities on the european union's agenda. global warming is a huge loss - some regions feel it more, others less, but it is particularly acute in the face of extreme natural events. this resolution is the basis on which we will have to work in lima in december. the lisbon summit is the key moment to make important decisions that will be taken at the paris climate change conference next year.
