in writing. a single resolution board is to be established as an eu agency as from 19 august 2014. the smooth functioning of the board as from 1 january 2015 requires that its financing structures be established by 1 january 2015. i support the regulation on the establishment of the board's financing structure.
in writing. — i express my sincere concern regarding the delay in adoption of the operational programmes and delays in implementation of cohesion policy for the period of 2014-2020. it is expected that only about half of the total programmes will actually be adopted by the end of this year and the rest will face a long delay in the start of implementation because of the appeared requirement of technical revision of the multiannual financial framework (mff). in 2015 europe might face a significant downturn in growth, a missed opportunity for investments and creation of jobs. this is a risk which must be avoided. thus i call upon the commission and the member states to streamline internal procedures in order to ensure that as many programmes as possible are ready for adoption by 31 december 2014. furthermore, the adoption of the mff revision must be conducted as early as feasible and the solutions for limiting the damage must be envisaged very clearly. this scenario cannot happen again in the future. the price is too high and the lessons must be learned.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to encourage the commission and the member states to draw up and adopt the cohesion policy programmes for 2014-2020 as soon as possible. cohesion policy is the eu's main policy for investment in the real economy and a tool to boost growth and jobs in the eu, with a budget of over €350 billion by 2020. today, not only is the implementation of the 2014-2020 cohesion policy delayed, but also the payments for the 2007-2013 programming period are delayed - both of which undermine the credibility of the cohesion policy. i support calls on the commission and the member states to adopt as many action programmes as possible in 2014. it is necessary that the commission endeavours to simplify its internal procedures in order to allow for the adoption of programmes more quickly.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because cohesion policy is a key instrument of the european union (eu) strategy to reduce regional disparities and inequalities, to contribute to increasing diversity and adaptation to industrial change, and to achieve economic, social and territorial cohesion. the policy is the eu's main instrument for investment in the economy, growth and jobs, with a budget of over €350 billion until 2020. the european parliament is particularly concerned about the serious delays in the implementation of the 2014-2020 cohesion policy. it also recalls that the current delays are creating difficulties for national, regional and local authorities in terms of their ability to effectively plan and implement the european structural investment funds for 2014-2020. the european parliament therefore calls on the commission and the member states to act responsibly and to take all possible measures to ensure that as many programmes as possible are drawn up and adopted before the end of the year.
in writing. cohesion policy the eu's main policy for investment in the real economy and a tool for boosting growth and jobs in the eu, with a budget of over €350 billion until 2020. that is the opening sentence of the document we are voting on today. it should also be stressed that cohesion policy is the main source of investment in some member states. the new president of the european commission is deeply concerned about the delay in implementing an important eu policy instrument, but maintaining a high quality of the action programme at the beginning of its implementation can easily avoid future errors, fraud and the lack of transparency in the management of funds in the member states. the aim of reaching agreement on a timetable for implementing the measures by the end of 2014 and of supplementing the cohesion policy fund with the eur 315 billion in investment funding provided by the new european commission is therefore welcome.
in writing. i voted in favour because i fully share parliament's concern about the delays in the approval of cohesion programmes. despite the fact that cohesion policy programmes were due to start at the beginning of this year, there is a real risk that a large part of them will not start in 2015. it must be acknowledged that the information provided by the european commission that member states may not wait for the approval of programmes and obtain them if they do not raise any doubts in the ec is not acceptable. member states may not risk funds that may later be declared ineligible. it is well known that cohesion policy, combined with the co-financing of the member states, covers a large part of the public expenditure related to growth in the european union. how can we boost job creation, fight the crisis, while at the same time cutting the branch we're sitting on? the delays in the approval of cohesion policy programmes, the delays in the payment of cohesion policy action programmes for 2007-13 are contributing to economic stagnation in europe. i don't think we want that. in this context, i call on both the european commission and the council to address these problems.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because the delay in the implementation of the 2014-2020 cohesion policy means that programmes financed by the structural funds may not be approved. we need to ensure that we have as many action programmes as possible and that we can speed up their implementation. further delays in the implementation of cohesion policy may threaten jobs and infrastructure improvements, which are the prerequisite for a programme financed by the structural funds.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to revise the commission's impact assessment (ia) guidelines. as a tool at an early stage of the legislative development process, impact assessment plays an important role in the eu decision-making process in order to provide detailed facts about the nature of the problem being addressed. adequate and independent impact assessment is crucial for smes, which often face more difficulties than large companies in adapting to new legal and administrative requirements. i support the proposals to establish clearer procedures for the impact assessment board, to strengthen its independence, and to ensure that the iab is composed of highly qualified persons. it is important that the sme impact assessment considers all economic, social and environmental aspects.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because impact assessment, as a tool at an early stage in the development of legislation, plays a crucial role in the commission's advanced regulatory agenda to provide transparent, comprehensive and proportionate facts on economic and social impact. in addition, impact assessments are an important tool for decision-making in all eu institutions and an important part of the better regulation process. i would like to stress that the impact assessment must be thorough, thorough and based on the most accurate, objective and comprehensive information available, with an analysis that is proportionate and focused on the objective of the proposal, in order to enable a sound policy decision to be taken. it is therefore important to consult all relevant stakeholders at an early stage of the impact assessment process so that their input can be taken into account in the preparation of the impact assessment before its publication. the european parliament therefore calls on the commission to present a new revised draft impact assessment guidelines, taking into account the issues highlighted in this resolution and the new structure of the commission, in particular the role of the new vice-president of the commission responsible for better regulation.
in writing. — a few weeks ago we celebrated the 25th anniversary of the un convention on the rights of the child, which is the most widely ratified human rights convention worldwide, including ratification by all eu member states. progress towards safeguarding children’s rights has been made since its adoption but new challenges have emerged and children’s rights continue to be breached in many parts of the world as well in the eu. many children are still victims of violence, war, poverty, social exclusion and rights violations. also more needs to be done in our relationship with the third world countries to see how we can better harness the influence and resources of the eu to protect children in the conflict zones. we must continue to push children’s rights globally and ensure that all children have the right to a childhood free from war. thus we cannot cease and must work further to ensure full implementation of the un convention on the rights of the child in policy and in practice and to take additional measures to ensure respect for the rights of every child everywhere, especially the most vulnerable. i call on the eu institutions and the member states to join forces and cooperate to improve the situation of children.
in writing. this year marks the 25th anniversary of the united nations convention on the rights of the child. i voted in favour of this proposal, which seeks to ratify all the optional protocols to the un convention on the rights of the child. the protection and promotion of children's rights are clearly stated objectives of the eu. standards for the promotion and protection of the rights of the child as defined by the un convention on the rights of the child a comprehensive set of international legal standards for the protection and well-being of children. i support the calls for the eu and its member states to give the millennium development goals (mdgs) top priority in their internal policies and in their relations with third countries. it is also important to give a clear focus to children in the implementation of the european semester, the preparation of the annual growth survey and the review of the europe 2020 strategy. it is essential that the eu and its member states invest in public services for children, including childcare, education and healthcare.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution on the united nations convention on the rights of the child, which i helped draft as a co-author. as i said yesterday in the debate, we would like to celebrate the greater achievements and even more the beautiful anniversary of the convention on the rights of the child, but the reality is that we have not yet achieved much of what we promised the world's children 25 years ago. this session also discussed child hunger, malnutrition and malnutrition in developing countries. after all, many children are now living in poverty, not only in developing regions of the world but also in the rich countries of the european union. of course, child poverty and hunger are associated with high unemployment in all regions of the world, with low family incomes, with family poverty and exclusion, with the inability to support large families. so i hope that in the future, we will be able to join the efforts of governments, ngos and individuals and not leave any child to his or her fate.
in writing. safeguarding children's rights must be a priority for all our work, both in the eu member states and beyond, especially in non-democratic countries, in war zones. even when the latest report by the united nations children's fund (unicef) on child poverty in rich countries shows that since 2008, 2.6 million children in the world's richest countries have been pushed below the poverty line - a worrying situation, and children are the most vulnerable segment of society. investing in children, policies specific to children, improving their social situation in the ways chosen by member states through educational programmes, education, community support, support for families, would undoubtedly have yielded results in the near future. on the other hand, the world's humanitarian crises continue to do exceptional harm to children. in 2014, over 59 million children were directly affected by conflict-related crises, and today there are around 250 000 child soldiers, of whom 40% are girls. an integrated policy for children, in conjunction with the eu's neighbourhood or development policies, can therefore be effective. the fight against child poverty, child exploitation, has to start today. accession to the un convention on the rights of the child by countries such as somalia or south sudan should be done without delay, perhaps even under pressure from the international community. in the european parliament, we constantly draw attention to children's rights and their violations, and last year's sakharov prize was an illustration of this. malala, who fought for the right of children to education, won not only the sakharov prize but also the nobel peace prize.
in writing. i support this resolution and would like to emphasise one aspect which i consider particularly important. it is essential for the european union to start working towards improving joint cooperation in the search for missing children. child abduction is a common and very serious crime that endangers not only children but also parents and entire communities.
in writing. i voted in favour of this non-binding resolution, which aims to discuss plans for the creation of a digital single market. in some areas of the digital single market, there are weaknesses caused by dominant operators. i support calls to ensure the implementation of the rules of the internal market for urgent services and to ensure the implementation of rules such as the consumer rights directive, alternative dispute resolution and electronic dispute resolution mechanisms. it is necessary to ensure a level playing field for companies operating in the digital single market, as this is essential for their ability to compete. it is important to adopt a new modernised data protection package as a matter of urgency in order to ensure the right balance between a high level of protection of personal data, consumer safety and control of personal data, and a stable and predictable legislative environment. the commission and the member states must allocate the resources needed to combat cybercrime through legislative instruments and law enforcement cooperation at both national and eu level. all internet data flows should be subject to uniform, non-discriminatory conditions.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because the digital market not only provides economic benefits but also has a particularly significant impact on the daily political, social and cultural lives of consumers and citizens in the european union. the digital single market is one of the most innovative sectors of the economy and is therefore crucial for the competitiveness of the european economy and contributes to economic growth through the development of e-commerce. however, it is recognised that there are shortcomings in some areas of the digital single market. the european parliament therefore calls on the commission to ensure the implementation of the rules of the internal market for urgent services and the implementation and enforcement of provisions such as the consumer rights directive, the alternative dispute resolution and electronic dispute resolution mechanisms, while ensuring a reduction in the administrative burden. calls on the commission and the member states to further develop and implement eu and national regulatory frameworks in order to create an integrated and secure market for online and mobile payments, while ensuring the protection of consumers and consumer data;
in writing. the digital single market creates millions of jobs and could create an additional 4% of gdp in europe by 2020. a competitive digital single market cannot exist without high-speed, high-capacity broadband and telecommunications networks in all eu regions. it is encouraging that lithuania continues to lead europe in terms of broadband penetration via fibre lines (33.9%). lithuania ranked 7th in the world in this indicator. the commission notes that the measure is aimed at improving the competitiveness of the lithuanian economy and promoting the development of the economy as a whole. so we have a great opportunity to develop this area further, bringing a lot of economic value. at the same time, it is desirable to draw attention to the fact that cybersecurity and the security of electronic communications and networks are priority issues in the digital single market that need to be addressed urgently and with common capabilities.
in writing. i am in favour of a digital single market and i believe that the time has come to act on it. the creation of a digital single market would undoubtedly stimulate innovation and technology in europe and would be a major driver of economic competitiveness. i believe it is important for the european union to develop a strong and modern infrastructure.
in writing. nearly one billion people suffer from hunger and some 225 million children under the age of five suffer from severe chronic malnutrition. in developing countries, 2.6 million such children die each year. the causes of child malnutrition are many: inefficient economic structures, unequal distribution and misuse of resources, poor management, over-reliance on individual crops, discrimination against women and children, inadequate health care systems, and inadequate education. i agree with the calls for governments to ensure that food availability is possible by directly working the land; that accessibility is ensured by economic and physical access to food; and that food is safe and adequate for the nutritional needs of each person. it is necessary to integrate nutrition, food security and sustainable agriculture into all its development policies. i support calls on the commission and the member states to pursue coherent strategies for sustainable development and to work towards reducing the scale of hunger, including in the context of emergencies and humanitarian intervention.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because almost one billion people still suffer from hunger and at least 225 million children under the age of five worldwide suffer from severe chronic malnutrition or from developmental disorders caused by chronic child and maternal malnutrition. in addition, an estimated 2.6 million such children die each year in developing countries. malnutrition or malnutrition in such lands leads to an increase in birth defects, the development of a multitude of diseases, and, of course, an increase in mortality, especially among children. there are many causes of childhood malnutrition, but most of them are caused by human activity, and they can be prevented, and we need to do so as soon as possible. these include inefficient economic structures, unequal and disorderly distribution of resources, discrimination against women and children, and poor health due to inadequate health care systems, as well as inadequate nutrition education. the fight against child malnutrition and the achievement of universal access to adequate nutritious food should therefore remain at the top of the post-2015 development agenda.
in writing. available data indicate that almost one billion people worldwide suffer from hunger, and at least 225 million children under the age of five suffer from severe chronic malnutrition or developmental disabilities. in developing countries, 2.6 million such children die each year. micronutrient deficiency is also a major problem, accounting for 7% of all diseases worldwide, especially among newborns and infants. women and girls are the most vulnerable group, accounting for 60% of the victims of chronic hunger. although women produce 60 to 80 percent of the food in developing countries, and do 80 percent of the farming work, they officially own only 2 percent of the land. the solution to child malnutrition requires an integrated approach in the implementation of development cooperation and in the context of humanitarian and natural crises, taking coordinated action in the areas of health, education, agriculture, water, access to energy resources and sanitation that affect child malnutrition.
in writing. i voted in favour because i support the resolution which calls for the necessary action to save 2.6 million children every year. that's the number of children in developing countries, or 30 children under the age of five, who die every minute from developmental disorders caused by chronic malnutrition. i support the objectives of the resolution, which provides for concrete support of 3.5 billion to improve nutrition. it is also important that the resolution makes clear that climate change will have a negative impact on agricultural production. so we have to think bigger when we're dealing with hunger. without the protection of marine life and other ecosystems and biodiversity, deforestation and climate change mitigation, sustainable and secure access to energy, decent employment, product redistribution, we will not be able to achieve the ambitious goal of eliminating all forms of malnutrition by 2030.
madam president, mr vice-president of the commission, your remarks confirm this: the problem is not to revise the rules which were put in place at the height of the crisis to clean up the economic and budgetary policies in the union, but to apply these rules, to apply them fully in the letter and in the spirit. it is a matter of credibility: the credibility of the union and, more particularly, of the governance of the euro area, which has set itself even more ambitious objectives in this area with the budgetary pact, and the credibility, mr vice-president, of the new commission. i would like to follow my colleagues in the epp, and in particular mr theurer, in issuing a warning here: the rules must apply in the same way in all member states, regardless of their size. i would remind you that the reason why new legislation - six-pack and two-pack - had to be adopted is that the original rules - the stability and growth pact - were not applied. in 2003, the two largest economic powers in the union - germany and france - were found to have emerged from the crisis at the same time. they have asked for - and obtained without much difficulty - flexibility and interpretation of the rules, which have emptied them of their content. the conclusion was that the union could not function on the basis of an unconfessed principle which is not, for the states, too big to fail but somehow too big to be blamed too big to be sanctioned. if we do it again today, if the commission is inflexible with greece and portugal but trembles in the face of countries larger than spain, then the whole system will collapse. because, in the end, we will inexorably return to the situation we wanted to avoid at all costs, in which financial operators and rating agencies decide the fate of our economies instead of politicians. (the president agreed to answer a question on the 'blue card' (rule 162 (8) of the rules of procedure))
the question asked when the blue card is raised. the situation in each country is quite different, but i would like to ask you this question: shouldn't the principle be legalised to protect society, that first of all, when the economy recovers, when the budget is restored, social benefits, wages, tax refunds should be restored, and only then talk about new programmes, only then implement new programmes?
mr president, commissioner, the committee on internal market and consumer protection (imco) at the moment is working on a report for better integration of the single market pillar in the european semester. i believe the single market is a key to achieve growth and is something we have really lost focus on, as the member states have failed to implement some very good directives, very good recommendations, and this is where we are failing. also, the annual report on single market integration was not provided by the commission. where is it? the european parliament would like to propose a number of analytical tools: barometers – we have to measure our performance, everyone has to see how we are performing; methodology to measure reduction of administrative burden. this is where we are failing. too much red tape – it is too difficult. let us measure it to find out what needs to be done. furthermore, i would like to stress the importance of the implementation of the european semester, otherwise there is no need to work on a single market pillar. i also want to mention a crucial aspect of the role of national parliaments. in writing. ‒ the european ombudsman investigates complaints of maladministration by the institutions and bodies of the european union. it is open to eu citizens and residents, businesses, associations and other organisations registered in eu countries. i have endorsed the irishwoman emily o'reilly for the post of ombudsman for the 2014-2019 term.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the candidate ms emily o'reilly for the post of the european ombudsman. taking into consideration the fact that the independent european ombudsman plays an important part in ensuring that eu institutions adhere to the principles of good administration and respect for eu citizens' rights under the treaties and the eu charter of fundamental rights, i am sure that mrs emily o'reilly is a suitable candidate for the post of european ombudsman. she has underlined the dangers of an over-reliance on the law as being detrimental to the requirements of good administration. she has also emphasised that the fundamental values and principles on which the eu is based, including fairness and justice, need to complement the law.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this proposal to repeal directive 93/5/eec on the scientific examination of questions relating to food. at the end of 2012, the commission launched the eu regulatory quality and performance programme to provide businesses, workers and citizens with a simple and stable regulatory framework. the commission thus committed itself to abolishing unnecessary or outdated rules. the aim of the council directive on the scientific examination of foodstuffs was to ensure the smooth functioning of the scientific committee for food. since the tasks assigned to that committee have subsequently been taken over by the scientific committee and the european food safety authority, i agree that that directive has become obsolete and should be repealed.
in writing. ‒ i voted for this report because it supports the repeal of an outdated directive adopted in 1995. the purpose of this directive was to ensure the smooth functioning of the scientific committee for food by encouraging member states to provide scientific support to the commission and to ensure cooperation with national authorities on matters relating to food safety. since 1995, the functions and objectives of this directive have been taken over by other, newer legislation, and the 1995 directive has therefore become obsolete. the commission has therefore decided to repeal the legislation adopted almost 20 years ago under the current refit programme, which aims to simplify the eu legislative framework so that citizens, workers and businesses can benefit from a simple, clear, stable and predictable regulatory framework.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour for the proposal for a directive on assistance to the commission and cooperation by the member states in the scientific examination of questions relating to food. i believe that it is necessary to ensure smooth cooperation with the relevant national bodies on scientific issues regarding the safety of foodstuffs.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to confirm the eu's accession to cites. cites is the main international instrument for protecting endangered species of fauna and flora by controlling international trade in specimens of these species. the convention entered into force in 1975 and has 178 parties (including all eu member states). it covers about 35 000 species. import, export and introduction from the sea of these species shall be authorised through a licensing system. at the end of 2013, the eu was given the opportunity to become a cites party. i agree that the eu's accession to cites is a necessary step to ensure that the eu is fully capable of achieving its environmental policy objectives. i believe that accession would give the commission the opportunity to express a consistent eu position on cites issues on behalf of the eu and give the eu a greater role in the negotiations at cites conferences.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report because it supports the european union's accession to the convention on international trade in endangered species of wild fauna and flora (cites). this international trade in endangered species of wild fauna and flora, adopted as early as 1975, is the main international instrument for the protection of endangered species of fauna and flora by controlling international trade in specimens of those species. the convention has 178 parties (including all eu member states) and covers some 35 000 species of flora and fauna. these species are classified according to the level of protection they require and the convention aims to ensure that international trade in specimens of these species does not endanger their survival.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to sign a cooperation agreement on a civil global navigation satellite system (gnss) with morocco. morocco has a lot of practical experience in space technology and would like to expand the use of satellite navigation on its territory and in the surrounding region western mediterranean and west africa. the agreement provides for cooperation in the fields of satellite navigation and timing, research, industrial production, training, service and market development, trade, and issues related to radio spectrum, integrity, standardisation and certification. as the agreement will strengthen joint eu-morocco activities in these sectors, i believe it will be beneficial for the citizens, industry and scientific communities of both countries. i'm in favor of the settlement.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the conclusion of a cooperation agreement between the eu and the kingdom of morocco on a civil global navigation satellite system (gnss). the purpose of the agreement is to encourage, facilitate and enhance cooperation between the parties through the contribution of europe and morocco to the development of a civil global navigation satellite system (gnss). the eu and morocco have concluded this agreement in the light of their shared interest in developing a civilian global navigation satellite system (gnss) and the growing expansion of gnss applications in morocco, europe and elsewhere in the world, and in recognition of the importance of the galileo programme for the navigation and information infrastructure of europe and morocco.

in writing. ‒ croatia’s admission as a new contracting party to the european economic area should be congratulated as an essential milestone event for the country to become a fully-fledged member state and participant of the union. looking back on croatia’s accession to the eu, one must take into account that its integration process entails both political and economic dimensions which needless to say are highly interconnected. this means that only by being economically engaged can a country be fully politically involved in eu decision making. hence, making these new economic ties between the eu and croatia results not only in free flow of goods and services but also in joint economic and trade policy and shared expertise and cooperation in such areas as innovation, development and consumer protection, which in turn will benefit not only croatia but the european union’s economy as a whole.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to ensure that croatia joins the european economic area (eea). in order to facilitate the participation of croatia in the eea, the following agreements are hereby concluded: the agreement on the participation of the republic of croatia in the eea, the norway-eu agreement on the additional protocol to the financial mechanism of norway for the period 2009-2014 and the additional protocol to the agreement between the european economic community and iceland. the eea agreement provides for the application of eu law in all eea states, covering the four freedoms (free movement of goods, services, persons and capital). it also covers cooperation in other areas such as research and technological development, education, social policy, environment, consumer protection, etc. i believe that croatia's participation in the eea is a normal consequence of its accession to the eu and therefore i support it.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its consent to the conclusion on behalf of the eu of the agreement on the participation of croatia in the european economic area (hereinafter referred to as the 'eea agreement') and three related agreements. the eea agreement was signed in 1992 and croatia, in accordance with its act of accession, undertakes to accede to it under the conditions laid down in that act. under the eea agreement, the eu member states and the three members of the european free trade association iceland, liechtenstein and norway are brought together in a single market, with free movement of goods, services, persons and capital across the eea. the european parliament adopted a resolution on the situation in the former yugoslavia on the basis of the conclusions of the european council in brussels on 21 and 22 june.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour for participation of croatia in the european economic area. i support croatia’s participation in the european economic area, because as croatia is a member of the eu, it is natural consequence that it becomes a member of the eea.
in writing. ‒ the eea agreement provides for the application of eu law in all eea states, covering the four freedoms (free movement of goods, services, persons and capital). the agreement also covers cooperation in other important areas such as research and technological development, education, social policy, the environment, consumer protection, tourism and culture. in favour of deeper eu integration, i voted for croatia's participation in the european economic area as an inevitable step after its accession to the eu.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish a new legal framework for the dock dues applicable in the french outermost regions from 1 january 2015 to 31 december 2020, by approving products that may benefit from exemption or reduction. france is authorised to apply a reduced dockage tax to certain products produced in the french outermost regions. this is to compensate for the disadvantages faced by such regions: remoteness, dependence on raw materials and energy resources, and a small local market. since this measure promotes the economy and competitiveness of the outermost regions without undermining the coherence of the internal market and the eu legal system, i support this proposal.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report because it was the report parliament adopted in favour of the decision on the procedure for applying the dock levy in the french outermost regions. the purpose of this decision is to establish a new legal framework governing dock dues in the french outermost regions, payable from 1 january 2015 to 31 december 2020, and to adopt a list of products eligible for exemption or reduction. since, in the parliament's view, this measure can be considered justified and intended to promote the economy and competitiveness of the eu's outermost regions without damaging the coherence of the internal market and the eu's legal system, parliament gave its unamended consent to the agreement.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour concerning dock dues in the french outermost regions. i am sure that we have to provide a support for the outermost regions in order to make them more competitive and provide a possibility to develop economically and socially. i do believe that the application of exemptions or reductions to the ‘dock dues’ tax will boost the level of the small sized export activity of a small market.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to mobilise the egf for the needs of italy in order to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by the electrical equipment manufacturer 'whirlpool europe'. the global financial and economic crisis led to a severe economic downturn in the 28 eu member states and italy in 2009. the italian market for large household appliances decreased from 3,174 billion in 2010 to 2,649 billion in 2013, i.e. by 16,5%. a total of eur 1 890 000 is to be granted from the egf to 608 redundant workers. a tailored package of services will include guidance and counselling, skills assessment, retraining, vocational training and entrepreneurship advice. i am in favour of the allocation of funds from this fund to redundant staff of whirlpool europe.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report because it was the one where parliament gave its support to the decision to grant aid to 608 workers made redundant in 2014 by the italian firm whirpool and five supply and downstream companies. the italian authorities argue that the global financial and economic crisis has had a significant impact on the consumption choices of italian households, which have reconsidered their purchasing decisions, in particular those concerning the purchase of consumer durables, including household appliances. as a result, the market for large household appliances decreased by 16,5%. the granting of this aid to the redundant workers also took into account the fact that the redundancies would further worsen the unemployment situation in the province of trento, where the unemployment rate has doubled since the beginning of the crisis and the decline in employment has affected both the construction and industrial sectors.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund: application egf/2014/010 it/whirlpool. i do believe that the eu should protect and support its citizens from any crisis. we should provide a financial support for people in need and give them a chance to reintegrate themselves into the labour market after they were dismissed due to a significant downturn in the economy. i do believe that the eu has to help the redundant workers to come back into labour market.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at allocating a total amount of eur 1 259 610 from the egf to poland to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by fiat auto poland. since 2007, the european automotive industry has lost market share. in that year, european passenger car production accounted for 32,2% of total world production, compared to 23,2% in 2012. the impact of globalisation was further exacerbated by the financial crisis, which led to an unprecedented drop in sales of new passenger cars in the union. the package of services for the 777 redundant employees will include entrepreneurship training, apprenticeship grants, traineeship grants and grants for those wishing to become self-employed. i support the allocation of funds from this fund to the redundant employees of fiat auto poland.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report because it was the parliament's decision to support the 1079 workers made redundant in 2013 by fiat auto poland and its suppliers and downstream companies. the decision to grant aid underlines that the european automotive industry has lost market share since the start of the crisis and that the situation is even worse at national level. parliament therefore agrees that the redundancies at the fiat plant in tycho and at its suppliers and downstream companies are linked to the fundamental structural changes in the world trade system brought about by globalisation and that the impact of globalisation has been exacerbated by the financial crisis, which has led to a sharp drop in sales of new passenger cars in the union. in this case, the redundancies at the fiat plant in tycho will have a negative impact on the whole region, as the former employees of fiat auto poland, its suppliers and downstream companies represent 10% of all unemployed persons in these areas.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour for the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund: application egf/2013/006 pl/fiat auto poland s.a. i do believe that the eu should protect and support its citizens from any crisis. we should provide a financial support for people in need and give them a chance to reintegrate themselves into the labour market after they were dismissed due to major structural changes in world trade patterns due to globalisation. moreover, there are 6 workers with longstanding health problems or disabilities who definitely need support to overcome the crisis.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to mobilise the egf for greece in order to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by the retailer odyssefs fokas sa. since 2008, greek gdp has decreased by 25,7%, public consumption by 21%, private consumption by 32,3% and unemployment by 20,6%. the egf is intended to provide a total of eur 6 444 000 to 600 beneficiaries. the package tailored to the individual needs of the dismissed employees will include career guidance, training, retraining, business start-up support and mobility allowance. i support the allocation of funds from this fund to the redundant workers of odyssefs fokas.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report because it was the one that gave parliament its support for the decision to grant aid to 551 workers made redundant in 2014 by the company odyssefs fokas s.a.. it is alleged that the two events which initially triggered the redundancies were: a decline in disposable household income due to an increased tax burden, a decline in wages (in both the private and public sectors) and rising unemployment, and a consequent sharp decline in purchasing power. the commission notes that the bank of greece has not provided any information on the amount of the loan to the bank of greece.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour for mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund: application egf/2014/013 el/odyssefs fokas. i am in favour of the mobilisation of the fund concerning this request, because the application relates to 500 young persons not in employment, education or training. considering the fact that youth unemployment rate is dramatically high in the eu, thus i strongly support all financial measures that promote entrepreneurship and business initiative that might lead to create more workplaces.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to mobilise a total of eur 25 937 813 from the egf for the needs of france. international air transport has suffered from severe economic shocks and a particularly sharp decline in its share of the eu market. the redundancies of 'air france' are linked to the major structural changes in the world trade system due to globalisation and to the decline in the union's air transport market share. the package of services tailored to the individual needs of the 3 886 redundant workers includes staff counselling and career guidance services, training, business creation support, retraining and mobility benefits. i approve the allocation of funds from this fund to the redundant employees of air france.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of this report because it was the report by which parliament approved the granting of aid to 5 213 workers made redundant by 'air france' in 2013. it is noted that the redundancies of 'air france' are linked to the major structural changes in the world trade system due to globalisation and to the decline in the union air transport market share, in particular the impressive growth of the three main gulf carriers, which was further exacerbated by the global financial and economic crisis. the aid was also granted because the redundancies of 'air france' would have a negative impact on the île-de-france region, which will be the subject of another collective redundancy, as the closure of the 'peugeot citroën automobile (psa) ' car plant at one is still ongoing this year.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the proposal for a decision of the european parliament and of the council on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund: application egf/2013/014 fr/air france, from france. i do believe that this application fulfils the eligibility criteria laid down in the egf regulation. to my mind the eu has to support the companies suffering the consequence of the economic crisis. the eu has to take all possible measures to ensure that the dismissed workers have the ability to reintegrate themselves into the labour market, to get appropriate advisory services and vocational guidance, training etc.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to appoint the chair, the vice-chair and the members of the single resolution board responsible for the functioning of the single resolution mechanism. this mechanism will complement the single supervisory mechanism, which will be directly supervised by the european central bank in the euro area and in other member states that decide to join the banking union when it becomes operational at the end of 2014. a common bank resolution mechanism would ensure that, in the event of a bank subject to the ssm experiencing significant difficulties despite enhanced supervision, it could be effectively resolved at the least cost to taxpayers and the real economy. the members of the board shall be appointed on the basis of their merits, competence, knowledge of banking and financial affairs and experience in financial supervision, regulation and banking resolution. i support the proposal to appoint the candidates proposed by the commission.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report because it was the report by which parliament gave its consent to the appointment of the members of the general board of the bank. the general board of resolution part of the european banking union. the european banking union is a single banking supervisory mechanism designed to enhance the soundness and safety of the financial sector. it was created to improve the regulation, supervision and governance of the financial sector so that taxpayers do not have to pay for bank mistakes in the future. most of the banking union's rules have either already entered into force or are in the process of being finalised.
the question asked when the blue card is raised. i wanted to ask my colleagues: during the elections and now, the citizens of the european union who have lower incomes are really hoping and expecting that both the new commission and the new parliament will take many decisions that will give a better perspective. do you think that the new programme should include a minimum wage within the european union in order to guarantee the minimum necessary income for work?
in writing. madam president of the commission, ladies and gentlemen. it is difficult enough in these discussions to evaluate in detail the work programme presented by the commission, which has not yet been presented to us. in your speech you mentioned that the commission's work programme for the coming year will build on the commission's communication on the guidelines for investment. i fully agree that europe needs investments to enable projects of european interest to be implemented in the areas of energy, transport, the digital agenda, research and other very important areas, and i am pleased that we will already be receiving important proposals in these areas next year. i welcome the commission's detailed investment plan for the european union, which will have to be approved by the european council in the coming days.
written. the commission emphasises that its work programme for the coming year is people-centred, with a view to regaining europeans' trust in europe. unfortunately, i think that the social dimension is missing from this programme and that social policy, among all the other priorities, appears only as an adjunct, or worse, as an administrative burden, to the economic tasks of the single market. first of all, i would like to stress that we must strengthen the social dimension of the european union. the commission must pursue policies that help improve the daily lives of our people, especially those who have suffered the most from the effects of the recession and austerity. after all, the last elections made it clear that there are a lot of people who are not happy with the eu, that people want change. unemployment remains one of the most acute problems in the european union. at present, 25 million europeans are unemployed. people need to have not only jobs but also decent working conditions and a fair wage. it is necessary to establish a subsistence minimum wage system in the european union, which would ensure that workers receive a wage above the poverty line. the pay gap between men and women in the same job needs to be reduced more rapidly. eu legislation on working conditions needs to be improved to improve worker safety. the fight against poverty, especially child poverty, must be a priority for europe as a matter of urgency.
the european council failed to reach agreement on the 2015 budget. the european parliament took a very clear position on the 2015 budget that the european union's commitments must be based on adequate funding, especially in the most important areas. i was responsible for the drafting of the opinion in the afet committee and it is therefore welcome that the agreement on increased funding for european neighbourhood programmes, humanitarian aid and the common foreign and security policy is particularly relevant in the light of current events. however, i am not in favour of reducing the funding for the european agricultural guarantee fund, as this will affect many farmers in the european union who are in a particularly difficult situation due to the prolonged russian embargo and who need financial assistance from the european union.
in writing. mr president, the draft general budget of the european union for the financial year 2015 presents a major challenge to the european institutions, as its adoption is taking place in the context of the continuing economic crisis which has disrupted the community's finances as a whole. the financial institutions, the member states, especially in southern europe, are largely responsible for the current crisis, but it is also worth remembering that the eu bureaucracy has reacted too slowly and inadequately to the situation. the draft budget for the following year is therefore of crucial importance in view of the prospects for economic recovery. it is therefore essential to include in the 2015 budget additional funds to boost the economy while protecting the most vulnerable social groups. it is good that the maximum allocation for this purpose is planned to be around eur 67 billion, which will be used to promote growth and employment. support for improving social, regional and economic cohesion should become an important element. the european parliament should support these budgetary provisions, which will also increase funding for education, research and innovation, with a 38% increase in funding next year compared to last year. but what seems to be most important, especially for countries in central and eastern europe like lithuania, is the special attention paid to farmers. they are the ones who suffer the most from the effects of a balanced food market. the union should therefore allocate the available surplus from the special reserve to compensate for european agricultural losses.
on behalf of the alde group.– madam president, exactly a month ago, this parliament voted in favour of the association agreement with moldova. i hope we are all convinced that, by embarking on the implementation of the eu-moldova association agenda, our aim is to make this country and the whole region more stable and prosperous. however, this is not shared by ‘big brother’ to the east, which chose to ban a long list of moldovan agricultural products. this is in violation of russia’s world trade organisation commitments. it clearly harms not only the moldovan people, but the russian people too, as the prices in russia’s empty shops have gone up considerably. our decision today is one more act to show our political solidarity with the people of moldova – first and foremost with those 250 000 people who are living from agriculture in that country. as we know, agriculture represents around 40% of moldova’s economy. by extending additional quotas, and doing so at this juncture – when the moldovan people have just reconfirmed their trust in the country’s european future at the recent elections – we are making a correct and timely step in all regards – political, economic and, to a certain degree, even humanitarian. we should have no concerns about a dramatic inflow of moldovan grapes, plums or apples into the european union market. i can assure you that they are tasty and good for our stomachs.
i would like to support the amendment proposed in principle, since moldova is a country that is very dependent on agricultural production and, by helping its agricultural production to enter the european union, we will also help to strengthen its economy. but of course, it must be ensured that the benefits that this region of moldova will receive reach all the actors, the whole chain, including apple, plum and grape growers. and of course the problem is that the crisis has been going on for almost six months, there has been a lot of talk about the search for third markets, and today it can be firmly stated that nothing has been done, although it has been announced that funds will be found for the search for markets. but i would say that if the plum and grape problem is less, the apple situation in the european union is in fact very much in need of a solution, and i think that the commission should work more quickly on this issue.
madam president, during the past few weeks talk has started to spread that the eu should start debating a common trade area with russia that starts at vladivostok and goes all the way to lisbon. in this debate i would like to ask whether it is not a better time to pay more attention to the other trade area that starts at vancouver and goes all the way to chişinău or kiev or even tbilisi. that is why today’s decision is very important because atps are additional and complementary to the liberalisation regime for goods in the dcfta. now countries like moldova and ukraine, especially after successful european elections, are showing a lot of political will and resolution to continue on a path to much needed reforms. so this is the best time to stand with moldova and show our support on their way towards europe.
, in writing. it is clear that the safety of the citizens of the european union is a priority. but when it comes to cybersecurity, i have my doubts about how far the institutions of each particular country can go to prevent potential threats. more recently, the cybersecurity law was adopted in lithuania, which allows an individual user, i emphasize an individual user, whose computer may only be infected with a virus, to disconnect the internet for 2 days, suspecting the possible impact of a cyber incident. it doesn't require a court order. this can be done by specialists from the national cyber security centre. the same law allows the police to instruct an internet provider to store for 90 days all information about a person's pages visited, payments made using online banking, postal and geographical addresses, etc. this legitimizes surveillance in a country infamous for its wiretapping of between 12 and 14 thousand people a year. members of parliament, do we really want more and more people to be followed? i'm not saying that this resolution is unnecessary. i say that it is necessary to ensure that this resolution does not become a basis for the violation of human rights and freedoms.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims at duty-free entry of fruit from moldova into the european union. the proposal aims at temporarily exempting from eu customs duties a certain quantity of fresh apples, grapes and plums imported from moldova. the new proposal would allow moldova to export 40 000 tonnes of fresh apples, 10 000 tonnes of fresh grapes and 10 000 tonnes of fresh plums to the eu duty-free. this concession would complement the eu-moldova free trade agreement currently in force. this measure is intended to mitigate the effects of the ban on the import of fruit from moldova into russia and i therefore support the proposal.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it supports the amendment of the autonomous trade preferences agreement with moldova. political and economic relations between the eu and moldova have strengthened in recent years, notably with the conclusion of the association agreement, which builds on the establishment of a comprehensive and inclusive free trade area, complementing the agreement on autonomous trade preferences. as the economic and political relations between the eu and moldova have strengthened, russia has reportedly imposed an import ban on moldovan agricultural products. this ban is seriously damaging to the moldovan economy. in seeking help and support in these difficult circumstances, the moldovan authorities have turned to the eu to mitigate the negative economic impact of the russian ban, in particular on the main products of moldovan fruit growers. in the interest of solidarity, the commission has therefore proposed to amend the autonomous trade preferences agreement to establish three additional duty-free tariff quotas for fresh apples, fresh table grapes and fresh plums.
in writing. — i voted in favour of this proposal to introduce autonomous trade preferences for the republic of moldova. agriculture represents around 40% of the moldovan economy and it is now highly affected by the politically motivated import ban imposed by russia. i see this as an act of solidarity and a step forwards the creation of a ‘deep and comprehensive free trade area’.
in writing. i would like to ask my colleagues to support the proposed amendment to regulation (ec) no 55/2008. moldova is a country that is highly dependent on agricultural production, and by helping its agricultural production to enter the eu, we will help to strengthen its economy. however, i am very unhappy that the autonomous trade preferences are being proposed for apples. i would remind you that apple growers in many member states have been hit very hard by the russian embargo. by legalising this concession, we will be dealing a severe blow to our apple growers. therefore, i do not think it appropriate to introduce such a concession for apples at this stage. the other two sectors mentioned in the regulation - plum and wine - have not been affected by such severe sanctions. when the common european interest is at least theoretically to be defended will the commission show the real work and results of its search for new markets for agricultural producers affected by the russian embargo, such as european apple growers? once we start to address the issue of finding these new markets, we can even talk about much higher duty-free import quotas for other products of the moldovan agricultural sector.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to establish a customs tariff regime for goods originating in ecuador. negotiations with ecuador on its accession to the trade agreement concluded between the eu and colombia and peru were concluded in july 2014. following these negotiations, a protocol on the accession of ecuador to the agreement was initialled. in order to avoid unnecessary disruption of trade pending the completion of the procedures for the application of the protocol of accession, the present proposal maintains the level of the customs duties applicable to ecuador at the date of initialling of the protocol.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it involves parliament in the legislative procedure alongside the council for the adoption of a regulation on the tariff regime for goods originating in ecuador. negotiations with ecuador on its accession to the trade agreement between the eu and colombia and peru were concluded on 17 july 2014. the ecuadorian government took this decision after coming under considerable pressure as some of its main export products were in a very unfavourable competitive position compared to the same products from colombia and peru. in order to avoid unnecessary disruption of trade until the provisional application of the eu-ecuador free trade agreement and to alleviate the burden on eu importers of goods exported from ecuador, the commission has adopted a proposal to ensure that the duties applied to products originating in ecuador on the date of accession of ecuador to the european union and the conclusion of the free trade agreement with colombia and peru are not increased and that no new duties are applied to these products. the aim is to apply this regulation from 1 january 2015.
in writing. — i voted in favour for the proposal for a regulation on the tariff treatment for goods originating in ecuador. i see it as clearly intended to be a transitional measure for ecuador and the european union before signing an eu-ecuador free trade agreement.
in writing. i voted in favour of all the draft amending budgets for 2014 and in favour of the agreement reached between the european parliament and the council on the 2015 budget. i congratulate the european parliament's negotiators for having achieved an immediate payment of eur 4.25 billion in urgent debts from this year's eu budget and for having obliged the european commission to draw up a graduated plan for the reduction of outstanding accounts and to set a sustainable debt limit for the future. the parliament will allocate €0.5 billion more to commitments than the eu council proposed. more money will be allocated to the eu research programme 'horizon 2020' and the student and youth exchange programme 'erasmus+'. the increased funding for european neighbourhood programmes, humanitarian aid and the common foreign and security policy is welcome and highly relevant in the context of the current situation in europe. however, i am opposed to cutting funding to the european agricultural guarantee fund, as this would be a response to the many eu farmers who are in a particularly difficult situation due to the prolonged russian embargo and who need eu support.

in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it gives parliament its support for the decision to mobilise the european union solidarity fund. the funds will be allocated to italy (sardinia) for the floods of november 2013, to greece (islas cavalonas) for the earthquake, to slovenia for the ice storms and to croatia for the ice storms and the floods that followed. in 2013, heavy rains in sardinia, italy, caused many rivers to overflow their banks and caused massive flooding. in 2014, a strong earthquake (measuring 5.8 on the richter scale) followed by several strong aftershocks left 3,000 people homeless and several dead in the north-east of the island of kefalonia. slovenia has been hit by one of the worst winter storms in decades, devastating parts of europe and causing damage in several countries. slovenia was hit hardest in 2014, when almost half of the country's forests were destroyed by ice, and a quarter of homes were left without electricity after heavy snowfall cut power lines and uprooted trees. croatia suffered from the same meteorological phenomenon that led slovenia to apply for assistance from the eu solidarity fund. the north-western regions and parts of the northern adriatic region were particularly affected. in addition, the melting of the ice and snow led to flooding, which further damaged important public infrastructure and private and public property.
in writing. — i voted in favour of the urgent need to release financial assistance to italy, greece, slovenia and croatia, which were affected by the natural disasters in 2013 and 2014. i see it as the duty of the european union and the main purpose of the eu solidarity fund (eusf).
