in writing. — i voted in favour of the motion on the eu solidarity fund. this fund is created to respond to major natural disasters. this expresses the solidarity of all the member states, which i think is absolutely necessary for the union. it is important to ensure that money reaches the affected member state rapidly, in order for the citizens of the region to feel the support.
in writing. i voted in favour of all the draft amending budgets for 2014 and in favour of the agreement reached between the european parliament and the council on the 2015 budget. i congratulate the european parliament's negotiators for having achieved an immediate payment of eur 4.25 billion in urgent debts from this year's eu budget and for having obliged the european commission to draw up a graduated plan for the reduction of outstanding accounts and to set a sustainable debt limit for the future. the parliament will allocate €0.5 billion more to commitments than the eu council proposed. more money will be allocated to the eu research programme 'horizon 2020' and the student and youth exchange programme 'erasmus+'. the increased funding for european neighbourhood programmes, humanitarian aid and the common foreign and security policy is welcome and highly relevant in the context of the current situation in europe. however, i am opposed to cutting funding to the european agricultural guarantee fund, as this would be a response to the many eu farmers who are in a particularly difficult situation due to the prolonged russian embargo and who need eu support.
in writing. — i am satisfied with the general budget deal. taking into account the really complicated negotiation process and often colliding council and ep positions, it is extremely important that the budget was finally adopted in a comparably timely manner. moreover, the increase of eur 244.2 million in the overall level of commitment appropriations compared to the initial proposition of the council is a significant achievement which will help to stabilise the current fiscal debt levels. in addition, the bigger part of funds allocated to competitiveness and innovation is a promising sign for steady growth and job creation. i believe this will contribute to the overall europe 2020 strategy and induce the overall economic performance of the eu.
in writing. i voted in favour of the agreement reached between the european parliament and the council on the 2015 budget. i congratulate the european parliament's negotiators for having achieved an immediate payment of eur 4.25 billion in urgent debts from this year's eu budget and for having obliged the european commission to draw up a graduated plan for the reduction of outstanding accounts and to set a sustainable debt limit for the future. the parliament will allocate €0.5 billion more to commitments than the eu council proposed. more money will be allocated to the eu research programme 'horizon 2020' and the student and youth exchange programme 'erasmus+'. the increased funding for european neighbourhood programmes, humanitarian aid and the common foreign and security policy is welcome and highly relevant in the context of the current situation in europe. however, i am opposed to cutting funding to the european agricultural guarantee fund, as this would affect many eu farmers who are in a particularly difficult situation due to the prolonged russian embargo and who need eu support.
in writing. i voted in favour of the agreement reached between the european parliament and the council on the 2015 budget. i congratulate the european parliament's negotiators for having achieved an immediate payment of eur 4.25 billion in urgent debts from this year's eu budget and for having obliged the european commission to draw up a graduated plan for the reduction of outstanding accounts and to set a sustainable debt limit for the future. the parliament will allocate €0.5 billion more to commitments than the eu council proposed. more money will be allocated to the eu research programme 'horizon 2020' and the student and youth exchange programme 'erasmus+'. the increased funding for european neighbourhood programmes, humanitarian aid and the common foreign and security policy is welcome and highly relevant in the context of the current situation in europe. however, i am opposed to cutting funding to the european agricultural guarantee fund, as this would affect many eu farmers who are in a particularly difficult situation due to the prolonged russian embargo and who need eu support.
in writing. i voted against this proposal, which calls on the commission to withdraw the draft implementing regulation on the list of serious infringements of eu rules. this regulation establishes the specific infringements of eu rules that deprive carriers of their good repute status. those rules help to raise the level of professional competence of road transport operators, to rationalise the market and to improve the quality of the services provided, for the benefit of road transport operators, their customers and the economy in general, while improving road safety. the proposal aims to reject this implementing regulation because the commission has not included 'illegal cabotage' in the list of infringements. however, the commission has indicated that such 'illegal cabotage' is not included because the regulation on the basis of which this act is being prepared establishes a clear list of categories and does not contain a category of illegal cabotage, i.e. the implementing regulation cannot extend the scope of the basic regulation. i therefore vote against the proposal to reject this regulation.
in writing. this document is of particular importance for those member states of the union which are close to external borders or have a well-developed road transport sector. the regulation specifies the specific infringements that deprive carriers of their good repute status. the tran committee rejected this proposal because the commission did not include 'illegal cabotage' among the infringements. the commission, for its part, explicitly stated that such 'illegal cabotage' was not included because the original regulation established a clear list of categories. it does not fall within the category of 'illegal cabotage' and therefore the implementing act of the original regulation cannot extend the scope of the basic regulation. if the ep wants to include an additional 'illegal cabotage' provision in the implementing regulation, the basic regulation should be supplemented first. i suggest that we do not politicize the cabotage issue and support the commission's proposal. the commission's studies (e.g. com ((2014) 222 final) show that illegal cabotage accounts for no more than 2% of international transport activity, while other offences (such as non-compliance with driving time arrangements) have a significantly higher negative impact on both competition between carriers and road safety. it is these other infringements which are the subject of a commission regulation which will allow the elimination of the malicious carriers from the transport business on their basis. in turn, a separate discussion could be launched on the definition and fight against illegal cabotage and the commission could be asked to draw up a separate document on this subject.
in writing. i want to support the proposal to approve the draft directive. the quality of fuel is a very important issue because it directly affects the air we all breathe. however, the proposed modifications are minimal and will not have a significant environmental impact. on the other hand, the proposal will have a significant economic impact, as it will create significant opportunities for a wider range of fuel sources. this is particularly important in view of the need to diversify fuel sources. the competitiveness of eu economies such as estonia, which has a long history of using local shale hydrocarbons, must also not be undermined. i therefore urge my colleague to vote unequivocally against the rapporteur's draft resolution calling for the rejection of the commission's proposal.
in writing. i voted against this resolution. the purpose of the proposal is to suspend the adoption of a commission delegated regulation establishing a discard plan for the baltic sea. this commission regulation aims to reduce the size of cod caught in the baltic sea, allowing fishermen to supply fish for trade of at least 35 cm, which would contribute to reducing unnecessary discards and to better protecting the baltic sea resources. the current minimum size is 38 cm. i fully support the new commission proposal and reject the resolution opposing it.
in writing. i voted against this resolution because it was intended to prevent the entry into force of a commission decision favourable to fishermen in the baltic sea and lithuania. in october, the commission decided to reduce the size of the cod market from 38 to 35 cm. the commission's decision, which will enter into force as from 1 january 2015, will allow fishermen to sell more cod already caught to the food industry and to reduce the quantities already caught but still discarded. at the same time, the scientists say that this change in the fishing regulations will not in any way undermine the recovery of fish stocks in the baltic sea and will therefore in principle allow for a more responsible and environmentally friendly fishing in the baltic sea.
in writing. — i voted against the resolution on commission delegated regulation (eu) establishing a discard plan in the baltic sea. i do support the idea of reducing the minimum conservation reference size for cod in the baltic sea from 38 cm to 35 cm as this would reduce the amount of disposable fish and would not harm the biological recovery. all in all, this would create better fishing conditions in the baltic sea.
in writing. — taking into account today’s changing security environment and emerging new security threats, a timely and prompt revision of common eu internal security strategy (iss) is needed. new threats are becoming, as never before, diversified, asymmetrical and international, meaning that international crime, radicalisation of religious and nationalistic groups, terrorism and cybercrime are on the rise and the traditional security measures and combat forms no longer serve as effective measures to tackle these challenges. therefore, we must call upon the ep, the council and commission to work closely to make the iss more coherent, univocal and targeted. thus, a motion for the revised internal security strategy (iss) including improved working methods and alternated structure is a very welcome and needed initiative. only by implementing the new iss will europe be able to preserve its legal and political order and defend itself against the new challenges on the rise.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to update the internal security strategy. the 2010-2014 ssm is due to expire and a new ssm for the period 2015-2019 is necessary. the security situation in europe has changed dramatically in recent years as a result of new conflicts in the eu's neighbourhood. i agree with the suggestions that in preparing the new ssp, europol should examine the security threats faced today and those that are likely to emerge in the future. the new vms shall be easily adaptable to the changing situation. the rapid increase in the number of eu citizens who travel to conflict zones to join terrorist organisations and then return to the eu is a cause for concern. i therefore agree that appropriate measures must be taken to prevent recruitment for conflict, to prevent the provision of financial support to terrorist organisations and to individuals who wish to join them, and to provide for criminal prosecution. it is also important to provide adequate resources for the proper implementation of the gbs.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because it seeks to advance parliament's contribution to the new eu internal security strategy for 2015-2019, which the commission plans to present in spring 2015. the future strategy is of particular importance in view of the dramatic change in the security situation in europe in recent years, due to new conflicts and upheavals in the eu's immediate neighbourhood, the rapid development of new technologies and the rise of radicalism, which has led to violence and terrorism. in particular, in the new eu internal security strategy, parliament called on the commission to reflect the eu charter of fundamental rights and its values, as security measures must always be applied in accordance with the rule of law and guaranteeing all fundamental rights. parliament also called for a focus on security threats both existing and emerging, and for an integrated approach to areas such as cybersecurity, trafficking in human beings and the fight against terrorism, or to interrelated issues such as organised crime, money laundering and corruption. in the resolution, parliament also stressed that a clear division of tasks between the eu and the national level was necessary for the proper implementation of the new internal security strategy.
in writing. threats of the 21st century are transcending traditional perceptions, threats are becoming asymmetrical, wars, labelled as hybrid, are taking place in the information space. the internal security strategy 2015-2019 needs to be thoroughly reviewed and identify emerging threats. this requires cross-border and inter-disciplinary cooperation, a constant exchange of information, cooperation with third countries and constant monitoring of the information field. the eu must become a flagship in the fight against terrorism and crimes against humanity, and properly assess threats from both its immediate and more distant neighbourhoods. as regards the eu's neighbourhood, i would like to stress once again that no 'green man' can arbitrarily occupy the territory of an independent state and that the eu must be prepared to respond appropriately to any act of aggression.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution. the eu has repeatedly reaffirmed its support for an israeli-palestinian solution that respects the 1967 borders, jerusalem is the capital of both states and both states coexist peacefully and safely. i support calls for political leaders to cooperate and take action to alleviate the tensions that have arisen. i welcome the commitment of the high representative of the union to actively promote a positive process aimed at facilitating real progress in the peace process. i believe that the eu must become a more active player and mediator in the middle east peace process and develop a comprehensive strategy for resolving the israeli-palestinian conflict.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to promote a policy of industrial production in all member states in order to preserve jobs in the eu. the eu steel industry is currently facing a significant drop in demand, which is leading to a loss of jobs and a decrease in competitiveness. the closure of dairies has resulted in the loss of 60 000 jobs since 2007 and a decrease in production from 210 million tonnes in 2007 to 166 million tonnes in 2013. i believe that an industrial policy action plan is urgently needed to revive european industry on the world market. i support the commission's proposals to study the strategic position of the european steel industry in the world, to develop a comprehensive tool for analysing the steel market, which would provide information on the balance between supply and demand for steel and to report on the main challenges facing the european steel industry, taking into account social, economic and environmental aspects. it is necessary to allocate part of the new investment package to promising long-term infrastructure projects and to innovation linked to large-scale industrial projects.
in writing. i voted for this resolution because it was the one in which parliament spoke about the protection of workers and industry in the eu steel sector. the resolution stressed that the steel industry in europe is vital for economic growth and prosperity, but that it is currently experiencing a significant drop in demand, leading to a loss of jobs and a decline in competitiveness, which unfortunately does not bode well for the recovery of the european economy. parliament also called on the commission to speed up the work on the industrial policy action plan, which should be published in the first half of 2015, in order to revive the competitiveness of european industry on the global market and ensure an effective level playing field, while ensuring high social and environmental standards in the eu.
in writing. the eu steel sector is a major source of employment, creating 350 000 direct jobs and several million jobs in related industrial sectors, including the supply chain for products for processing. support to the steel sector is of great importance, including the strategic development of new core steel-using sectors such as energy, transport, construction. i support this document primarily because it is about developing and promoting innovation, not only by supporting research and development and the transfer of knowledge, but also by promoting market access and innovation clusters, by encouraging public-private partnerships in strategic sectors such as the steel industry.
in writing. i voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to address the issue of strengthening the eu's border policy and security and promote solidarity between member states in the area of asylum. at least 3 072 people died in the mediterranean in the first nine months of 2014. this further demonstrates the importance of member states taking action to save lives at risk and to fulfil their international obligations in the field of rescue at sea. i agree that the eu must assume its fair share of responsibility and solidarity towards the member states receiving the highest numbers of migrants and asylum seekers. it is also important to reconsider the issue of strengthening border policies and security, and to consider how to improve the future role of frontex and the european asylum support office. i agree with the need to apply criminal sanctions against trafficking in human beings and smuggling of migrants and to examine the whole strategy of cooperation with third countries in terms of humanitarian, financial and political assistance.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because it deplores the tragic deaths in the mediterranean off lampedusa, where at least 3 072 people lost their lives in the mediterranean in the first nine months of 2014. in the resolution, parliament also calls on all eu member states to shoulder their fair share of responsibility and solidarity towards the member states receiving the highest numbers of migrants and asylum seekers. the resolution also states that it is necessary to consider strengthening border policy and security issues, as well as to consider how to improve the future role of frontex and the european asylum support office (easo).
in writing. the lampedusa tragedy has revealed the flows and scale of immigration. so we need to talk nonstop about legal migration and its ways, about the implementation of asylum policy. human life and its preservation, and therefore member states must do everything possible to avoid similar catastrophes at sea. according to the international organisation for migration, at least 3 072 people died in the mediterranean in the first nine months of 2014, and we must do everything we can to reduce these numbers as much as possible and to ensure that all member states act in a coordinated way in managing migration flows.
mr president, tomorrow’s ratification will mark an historic move for future georgia-eu relations. the country which is furthest to the east remains committed to its european choice, made back in 2003. i would also like to emphasise the remarkable trust in the eu by the georgian people. a lasting commitment to a european course is of utmost importance in overcoming the remaining pressures from russia. the so-called russian-abkhazian partnership alliance is yet another attempt to legalise the annexation of georgian territory. it has to be deplored. the ratification of the association agreement is not a christmas gift for georgia. it is a huge task that will require both good skills and solid speed from the georgian authorities. for instance, in 2015 the legal harmonisation will embark on some 72 legislative initiatives. equally, georgia is expected to continue with democratic reforms, strengthen the market economy, and increase the living standards of its people. we also expect the georgian judicial system to pass the test of being transparent and independent, and it must not be misused against political opponents. georgia needs national reconciliation no less than it needs legal approximation. here too, europe is going to be both a cooperative and a demanding partner. i wish georgia a happy new year.
mr president, tomorrow, with the ratification of the association agreement with georgia, the eu marks the final step towards the commitment it gave more than a year ago at the vilnius summit. the european union promised that the three countries that matched the criteria would be able to come one step closer to the eu: georgia was one of the first and most active countries from the region to set itself on the european path, and it has paid a steep price for its choice. despite everything today, we should proudly rejoice that we have reached this important landmark. however, there are still some alarming developments. people who laid the foundation for the georgia-euro-atlantic path are being persecuted on political motives, and georgia is increasingly demonstrating indications of selective justice. the current government should note that this has been taken into account very seriously here in parliament. georgia has to stay on the reform track, while not allowing the erosion of democratic achievements which took it a considerable number of years to reach. it should be clear that the political elite of the country has to stay united when it comes to euro-atlantic integration, the rule of law and the independence of the judicial system. only when these conditions are met can the real georgian dream come true and not evaporate as an empty promise.
first of all, i would like to congratulate my colleague mr mamikins on a very important document. georgia became the third eastern partnership country, after ukraine and moldova, to conclude an association agreement with the european union. this is a very important step forward in the development of relations between the european union and georgia and this association agreement confirms georgia's commitment to pursue political and economic integration. we all know that the eastern partnership countries have had and still have to face many difficulties in their decision-making, and i commend georgia for choosing the path of european integration despite the difficulties and pressure it has faced. this agreement will be mutually beneficial and in the long term, stronger political and economic ties will ensure greater stability and security in europe and a better life for the people of georgia.
for the ecr group the political and social situation in sudan is becoming increasingly unstable. this country reached a critical point in its history when the state institutions began to use particularly repressive police methods. their aim is to restrict freedom of speech and expression. it also seeks to prohibit free political debate. this activity is illegal. this is in violation of the norms and standards of international law. as a member of the committee on civil liberties, i would like to draw attention to the disturbing incident of the arrest of human rights defender dr medani. the european union must respond strongly to protect the fundamental rights of the sudanese people. persecution for the faith should also be considered. freedom of religion must be guaranteed, it cannot be a reason for persecution. let us send a clear signal, let us not be silent in the face of open discrimination.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to prevent the philippines from being granted bls+ status until the commission revokes the yellow card it has been given for illegal fishing. the commission granted them this card after it found that the philippines was not complying with the rules on combating illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing. allowing the philippines to join gsp+ would mean allowing fishery products from the philippines to enter eu markets duty-free and without guaranteeing that those products are derived from legal fisheries. i agree with the proposal to wait for a commission decision to withdraw the yellow card granted to the philippines before granting it gsp+ status. this resolution sends a message to eu citizens and fishermen: we cannot accept that the eu faces unfair competition from illegal products.
in writing. in order to ensure a smooth and timely implementation of the bank recovery and resolution directive by 1 january 2015, it is necessary that the national resolution authorities start calculating and collecting contributions to the resolution financing structures by 1 january 2015 at the latest. to achieve this, the regulatory delegated regulation needs to enter into force as early as 2014, so i support this proposal.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because it seeks to enable the commission to apply effectively the so-called bank recovery and resolution directive. for the purposes of the directive, member states must ensure that contributions are collected at least once a year from the relevant credit institutions and investment firms authorised in their territory. those contributions from credit institutions and investment firms are to be adjusted in line with the risk profile of those institutions and such adjustment can only be made by the commission by means of delegated acts whose adoption has been approved by the european parliament.
in writing. i voted in favour of the conclusion of the association agreement with georgia. this association and free trade agreement aims to strengthen economic and political relations between the european union and georgia. it will be the third such agreement to be ratified by the european parliament with the eu's eastern neighbours. the ep has already ratified a similar treaty with ukraine in september this year and with moldova in november. the establishment of a full free trade area with the eu should increase georgia's market access to the eu economic bloc through binding commitments on standards and common values, while the eu will benefit from smoother trade flows and better investment conditions in georgia. it is important that both parties engage in a constructive dialogue in order to confirm the main reforms and commitments envisaged in the association agreement. the priorities set out in the agreement should be adequately supported technically and financially in order to provide georgia with the means to pursue its democratic and economic reforms.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because it is a reiteration of the european parliament's support and assistance to georgia, while at the same time endorsing the conclusion of the eu-georgia association agreement on behalf of the european union. in this resolution, parliament warmly welcomed the signing of the association agreement by georgia as an important step forward in the development of bilateral relations and as a commitment to pursue a path of political association and economic integration. the resolution stresses the importance of a constructive dialogue involving all parties in order to adopt the main reforms and commitments contained in the association agreement, based on a common choice to pursue european integration. the resolution also calls on georgia to ensure that reforms are firmly anchored and deeply rooted in the institutional structure in order to create a society of pluralism, non-discrimination, tolerance, justice, solidarity and gender equality.
in writing. — i welcome the association agreement with georgia. following such countries as ukraine and moldova, it marks a turning point for georgia-eu relations. the agreement embodies not only the mutual benefits parties will get from a deep and comprehensive trade area and a common security and defence strategy but most importantly it marks georgia’s commitment to a complex reform and cooperation process, which requires a lot of effort and political will to align georgia’s laws and judiciary to eu legislation and involves public sector reform, in other words measures which will help georgia to keep up with eu standards. i am convinced this agreement will lead to positive developments at all levels of state and civil society in georgia and enhance bilateral eu-georgia relations, providing georgia with a modern european perspective.
in writing. i voted in favour of the conclusion of the association agreement with georgia. this association and free trade agreement aims to strengthen economic and political relations between the european union and georgia. it will be the third such agreement to be ratified by the european parliament with the eu's eastern neighbours. the ep has already ratified a similar treaty with ukraine in september this year and with moldova in november. the establishment of a full free trade area with the eu should increase georgia's market access to the eu economic bloc through binding commitments on standards and common values, while the eu will benefit from smoother trade flows and better investment conditions in georgia. it is important that both parties engage in a constructive dialogue in order to confirm the main reforms and commitments envisaged in the association agreement. the priorities set out in the agreement should be adequately supported technically and financially in order to provide georgia with the means to pursue its democratic and economic reforms.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it gives the european parliament its consent to the conclusion of the eu-georgia association agreement on behalf of the european union. the eu-georgia association agreement is the most effective instrument of bilateral cooperation to help georgia strengthen its political and economic stability, increase its resilience to external threats and respond appropriately to the people's choice to link their future with the european union.
in writing. i strongly support the motion for a resolution and urge my colleague to vote in favour of it. georgia may not be as important economically, but it is a very important outpost of european values in the east. this country has voluntarily chosen the path of european integration and has remained committed to european values even under pressure from russia. the eu's support to georgia is currently limited, but the way georgia is using the eu's good practices to modernise itself is a great example for many other eu partner countries. georgia is one of the clear leaders of the eu's neighbourhood policy and we must support it. i would also like to make a few comments on the text of the resolution. firstly, i think it does not sufficiently address the importance of public information on eu affairs. secondly, while paragraph 43 attempts to suggest a solution to regional economic integration, it unnecessarily ignores the potential of turkey and the eu to encourage economic cooperation with georgia.
